# TeaTree Blueprint

The product spec. Code is an artifact; this file is the product.

If the entire `src/` and `tests/` tree were deleted, this document — plus every architectural appendix rostered in § "Architectural Appendices" and the skills in `skills/` — should be enough to regenerate the project without ambiguity. That table is the sole roster; the count is stated nowhere else.

**Tone.** This blueprint is functional and architectural. It names classes and files where the structure carries the design, but it is deliberately language-agnostic — a competent engineer should be able to reimplement teatree in another language with this file alone. Prose-of-code (paragraph descriptions of method bodies, ticket-history rationale, line-by-line walkthroughs) does not belong here. The code itself is the source of truth for implementation; this file is the source of truth for architecture.

**Change policy.** Every code change to teatree must keep this file consistent with the architecture. Implementation details — new flags, new error messages, why a particular regex was tightened — belong in docstrings, commit messages, or the issue tracker, not here. Before modifying this file (or any appendix) please ask the user for approval — this is the source of truth and the user validates every change.

**Status:** current architecture under [#541](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/541). All phases (0–8) shipped.

- Statusline file is the always-on persistent UI surface; an on-demand HTML admin dashboard (`teatree.dash`, [#3162](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3162)) is served by `t3 admin` on a local gunicorn
- Code-host + messaging Protocols unified; backends are selectable per overlay
- Fat loop + scanners + dispatcher; the headless executor runs in-process via `claude-agent-sdk`
- No-overlay-leak grep gate keeps the platform tenant-agnostic

The §17.1 Invariants list is parsed by `scripts/hooks/check_blueprint_invariant_numbering.py` (gapless 1..N — do not renumber, do not delete). Its CI cross-PR mode (`blueprint-cross-pr` job) is **fail-closed**: when BLUEPRINT.md is in the PR diff but the merge-base §17.1 snapshot cannot be read (unresolvable merge-base, or the file introduced since the branch-point), the gate reds rather than delegating to the approximate common-prefix fallback, which would silently approve the exact `[1..N]`-on-both-sides cross-PR collision the gate exists to catch. The §5.6 Loop Topology summary is scanned by `tests/test_blueprint_loop_epic_alignment.py`.

---

## 1. What TeaTree Is

A personal code factory for multi-repo projects. It turns a ticket URL into a merged pull request by coordinating the full lifecycle — intake, coding, testing, review, shipping, delivery — across multiple repositories, worktrees, and agent sessions.

**Target:** service-oriented projects with databases and CI pipelines (any language). Not for docs-only repos or CLI tools.

**Operating mode.** TeaTree runs as a long-lived interactive Claude Code session orchestrated by a fat `/loop` (~10–15 min cadence). The loop fans out to in-session subagents per tick to sweep PRs, auto-review PRs assigned to the user, intake assigned issues, watch messaging mentions/DMs, and render a multi-line statusline that is the **always-on UI surface**. An on-demand HTML admin dashboard (`teatree.dash`) is served by `t3 admin` on a local gunicorn. The loop runs in the same session the user types into, so debugging stays direct.

**Code-host neutrality.** Pull requests are the canonical concept. Both **GitHub** and **GitLab** are first-class in core; GitLab MRs map onto the PR abstraction at the Protocol layer.

**Messaging-backend pluggability.** Mentions, DMs, and outgoing posts go through a `MessagingBackend` Protocol declared per overlay. Slack (Socket Mode bot) is the first implementation. A `Noop` default lets overlays opt out.

**Core principle.** Infrastructure is deterministic code; development work is skill-guided. State management, port allocation, provisioning, task routing, code-host sync, and messaging integration are Python code with >90% branch coverage. The actual development — coding with TDD, debugging, reviewing, shipping — is driven by skills that encode methodology, guardrails, and domain knowledge.

**Core stays generic.** No customer-, tenant-, or product-specific names appear in the scanned roots (`src/teatree`, `docs`, `skills`, `agents`, `tests`, `scripts` + top-level `README.md`/`BLUEPRINT.md`/`AGENTS.md`); per-overlay specifics live in the overlay package and the DB `ConfigSetting` store. A gate (`scripts/hooks/check_no_overlay_leak.py`) enforces this — cited as `BLUEPRINT § 1` from `tests/test_no_overlay_leak_hook.py`. It runs two passes: the configured term list (whole-token via `teatree.hooks.term_match`) and an always-on opaque-ID pass (`teatree.hooks.opaque_id`) flagging a real-shaped Slack/forge id (`C0…`/`U0…`/`T0…`) — a leak class with no dictionary word — while an obviously-synthetic placeholder and any `leak-scan:allow` line are exempt. As a pre-commit hook it sees only staged files; the full-tree CI job `overlay-leak-tree` walks every root and passes `--require-terms`, making a missing `TEATREE_OVERLAY_LEAK_TERMS` secret a LOUD MISCONFIGURED failure (exit 2) rather than a silent no-op.

---

## 2. Architecture Principle: Code-First, Not Skills-First

Infrastructure and orchestration are code; development methodology is skill-guided prose. The split is load-bearing:

1. **Skills are prose, not code.** Prose produces different results depending on model, context, and what else is loaded. Anything that must be deterministic — state transitions, port allocation, provisioning, sync, the `/loop` tick — is code.
2. **Coordination needs transactional guarantees.** An FSM + ORM provide atomic transitions and row-locked workers. Coordination through JSON files cannot.
3. **Code is testable; prose is not.** Core logic must reach >90% branch coverage.
4. **One ABC with a handful of methods beats thirty thin extension points.** Overlay customization goes through `OverlayBase` — typed methods with defaults, no priority system, no plugin registries.
5. **Terminal today, SDK/API-portable by design.** Teatree is driven through Claude Code's interactive terminal app today, but every architectural decision is made so the same flow can be driven through the Anthropic Agent SDK / API. Don't adopt a mechanism that only works in the terminal CLI when an SDK/API-portable equivalent exists. The terminal-driven flow keeps working; SDK/API-portability is the binding constraint when the two pull apart. This is why the headless executor (`src/teatree/agents/runner.py`) is kept deliberately slim — it is the swap point for an SDK runtime — and why dispatch, state, and skill-loading route through code rather than terminal-only affordances.

---

## 3. Package Structure

Package name: `teatree` (double-e). Repo/CLI: `teatree` / `t3`. Python: 3.13+. License: MIT. Build: `uv`. Entry point: `t3 = t3_bootstrap:main` — a top-level shim that bootstraps Django settings before importing `teatree.cli`.

Top-level layout under `src/teatree/`:

```
cli/         # Typer CLI — bootstrap commands (no Django needed); cohesive groups are subpackages (cli/eval/ cli/review/)
core/        # Django app — models, FSM, managers, sync, runners, management commands; backend_protocols hub at root + packages models/ gates/ merge/ runners/ selectors/ views/ modelkit/ and the clustered leaf packages cleanup/ worktree/ provision/ factory/ intake/ review/ evidence/
agents/      # Headless executor (in-process claude-agent-sdk)
loop/        # /loop topology — tick, scanners, dispatch, statusline
mcp/         # Structured-search + gate-preserving-write MCP server (serializers + search + command_catalogue + per-service groups + write_tools + MCPServer wiring); `t3 mcp serve`. 13 read tools: command_search (CLI discoverability) + ticket_get/ticket_list/ticket_search/worktree_status/pr_for_ticket/task_list/question_list/loop_stats/incoming_event_recent/config_setting_get/gate_status/factory_signals(+factory_score dark) — plus the per-service read groups and the gate-preserving write suite (#3076). `config_setting_set` refuses to write a safety-posture key by declared EFFECT — membership in `config.SAFETY_POSTURE_KEYS` (a delegation key whose write IS an authorization: `substrate_auto_merge_authorized_by`, `substrate_self_signoff`, `trusted_issue_authors`, `send_proxy_allowlist`, `autonomy`, `enforce_regulated_path`, …), not a name-glob — so a shell-denied MCP agent cannot self-grant a substrate-merge delegation or widen an intake allowlist; a conformance test fails closed if any delegation-shaped field is classified as neither refused nor explicitly MCP-settable
backends/    # Pluggable external service integrations; per-forge subpackages github/ gitlab/ slack/ msteams/ notion/ (+ flat sentry, figma, sharepoint)
config/      # Settings load + overlay discovery
utils/       # Pure utilities (git, git_remote remote-URL parsing [#2404], ports, db, secrets, compose contract, ...)
skill_support/  # Skill metadata, loading policy, deps resolution, schema/ref validation
overlay_init/, contrib/, docker/, templates/overlay/
```

Plus top-level: `agents/` (phase sub-agent definitions), `skills/*/` (workflow skills), `hooks/` (plugin hooks), `tests/` (pytest suite), `scripts/` (utility scripts), `.claude-plugin/`, `apm.yml`, `settings.json`.

**Runtime platform access is connector-free (direct official APIs).** Every runtime read/write of an external platform goes through that platform's official REST API with a stored token, never a claude.ai MCP connector: Slack (`slack.com/api`), Sentry (`sentry.io/api`), Figma (`api.figma.com`), and Notion page status (`api.notion.com/v1`). `core.sync.fetch_notion_statuses` reads each in-flight ticket's `extra['notion_url']` page into `extra['notion_status']`, token-gated on `get_notion_token()` (`notion_token_pass_key`) and a clean no-op until a token is configured; the opt-in reverse write (`push_notion_status`) is gated behind `notion_write_back` (default off). The `connector_manifest` / `mcp_connectivity` diagnostics serve the human's interactive claude.ai session only — no registered overlay declares a REQUIRED connector, so the loop never `SystemExit`s on one.

**Headless Notion documents (`t3 notion`).** The page-status sync above is the narrow read; `teatree.backends.notion` is the full document surface an unattended run needs, and `t3 notion` is the only way agents reach it. Auth is a Notion **internal integration** token resolved through the `Credential` machinery (env `NOTION_TOKEN`, then the overlay's `notion_token_pass_key` entry injected as `pass_path_override`, then `notion/integration-token`), failing loud rather than degrading to an unauthenticated read. The integration must additionally be **shared onto each page/database** — a Notion-side grant no code can perform — and the failure taxonomy (`backends/notion/errors.py`) keeps that condition distinguishable: token missing (3), token rejected (4), capability not granted (5), object not shared (6), not a Notion object (7), rate-limited (8), write-reported-but-not-landed (9), ambiguous section (10), unrepresentable Markdown (11), property not on the page (12), property unwritable from plain text (13), page not live (14), each its own exit code. "Not shared" is separated from "bad token" by probing `/users/me` on a 404, so the diagnostic names the integration a human must grant. `t3 notion setup` is how the token gets there without reading any documentation: it opens the integrations page (`webbrowser.open`, never a Claude-specific browser tool the headless factory does not have), prints the capability checklist Notion grants none of by default, takes the secret on a hidden prompt (no `--token`, so it never reaches argv), writes it to the overlay's own `pass` entry with a timestamped backup of any prior value, verifies it by resolving it BACK through `build_notion_client` — the readers' own path, which is what catches an exported `NOTION_TOKEN` shadowing the entry just written — and reports one line per `--page` through the same `page_verdict` triage `t3 notion doctor` exits on. `t3 doctor check` gates the same credential for every overlay declaring a `notion_token_pass_key` (`cli/doctor/checks_notion.py`), distinguishing absent / rejected / **shared onto nothing** — the third read via `POST /v1/search`, whose empty result is the only precise signal that a valid token was never granted anything — because each has a different fix, and silent where Notion is unrouted. The deployed stack resolves it from the box's `pass` store at boot (`deploy/entrypoint.sh`, beside `TEATREE_GH_TOKEN`) plus a per-service `environment:` declaration so a `docker exec` inherits it too; it is deliberately never written into `deploy/teatree.env`, which the deploy workflow regenerates wholesale and keeps secret-free. <!-- skill-symbol-ref: teatree.env is gitignored — the deploy workflow writes it on the box, so it is absent from the tree by design -->

**An archived page is not a source, so the read refuses (`backends/notion/liveness.py`).** An archived Notion page renders as COMPLETELY current — full acceptance criteria, a `Status` still reading "In Progress", live comment threads — so an agent handed one builds against a superseded spec, and an acceptance criterion only the dead page states produces the WRONG work rather than merely wasted work. `PageLivenessProbe` is the predicate every page-scoped command goes through (`cli/notion_support.live_page`, the single chokepoint): `archived`/`in_trash` on the page object is the only signal that CONCLUDES death and is decisive; membership of the page's own parent database is the corroboration, honest about not being an independent detector (the probe queries the parent the page itself declares) and earning its place by turning an unverifiable read into an explicit UNKNOWN and by supplying the SUCCESSOR — the live row sharing the page's title, named in the refusal, or plainly reported unresolvable rather than guessed. Death is a disjunction (any signal suffices) and life a conjunction (every applicable signal evaluated AND agreeing), so a parent database this integration cannot query fails CLOSED at exit 14 rather than reading as fine. `t3 notion doctor` reports the liveness as its own third stage — `OK`/`DEAD`/`UNKNOWN`, never a bare OK it did not establish. The one escape is a READ escape and its OWN command rather than a flag on `fetch`, so it cannot be reached by appending something to the read you were already typing: `t3 notion audit-fetch <page> --reason '<why>'` takes written prose (blank does not unblock), announces itself on stderr, and stamps the emitted Markdown, so an audited body cannot travel as current; there is no audited WRITE. Beyond the CLI, `core.sync` skips a non-live page rather than recording its status onto a live ticket and refuses the write-back, and the `notion_page_status` MCP tool refuses instead of answering — both through the boolean `NotionPageClient.page_is_live` seam, so core honours the rule without importing across the core → backends inversion. The claude.ai connector is outside this gate entirely and the rule is carried there as prose (`skills/ticket/SKILL.md` § "An Archived Page Is Not a Source"), pinned by `evals/scenarios/ticket.yaml::archived_notion_page_is_not_a_source`. `backends/notion/sections.py` is the idempotency primitive the PRD/BDD skills own a named H2 section with: **block-scoped only** — the new body is appended under the matched heading, only that section's enumerated blocks are archived, and a legacy heading is patched in place so its id and its discussions survive. There is deliberately no whole-page replace anywhere on the surface, because re-creating every block destroys the block-level comments attached to them. Two further primitives cover what a block tree cannot express, both under the same verify-by-re-read rule: `backends/notion/comments.py` posts a page comment **at most once per marker** (the dedup is the default, keyed on the caller's marker or the body itself, and reports `duplicate` at exit 0 rather than double-posting — the callers are dedup-driven skills, so a forgotten flag must under-post; `allow_duplicate` is the deliberate second copy, and the check sees only Notion's unresolved discussions), and `backends/notion/properties.py` reads any page property as plain text and writes one **shaped by its own live type**, so a caller names a value and never a Notion type. Every write re-fetches and refuses to report success unless what it asked for is what the page now carries.

Per-overlay Slack bot setup (`t3 setup slack-bot --overlay <name>`) — and the one-command `t3 setup slack-provision` that runs the whole lifecycle (manifest scopes, OAuth URL, channel join, tokens) idempotently ([#1686](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1686)) — are detailed in [docs/blueprint/configuration.md](docs/blueprint/configuration.md) §10.1 "Slack bot setup", cited from `src/teatree/backends/slack/bot.py`, `src/teatree/cli/slack/setup.py`, and `src/teatree/cli/slack/provision.py` as `BLUEPRINT §3.6`.

---

## 4. Domain Models

Five core lifecycle models in `teatree.core.models/`: **Ticket**, **Worktree**, and **Task** are FSM-driven (`django-fsm`); **Session** is a plain per-phase quality-gate tracker and **TaskAttempt** an immutable execution-history row. Around them, supporting rows under the same package: `AttachmentManifest` (the intake attachment-fetch manifest, below), `BotPing`, `ConsolidatedMemory`/`DreamQaProbe`/`DreamRunMarker`/`InstructionComplianceSnapshot`/`InstructionComplianceRecord` (memory-consolidation, below), `DailyDigestThread`/`DailyDigestMessage`, `DbApproval`, `DeferredQuestion`, `EvalRunRecord`/`EvalScenarioResult` (eval-run ledger), `SessionAuditRecord` (eval audit layer), `HonestyEscalation`, `IncomingEvent`, `IntentClassification`, `LandscapeArtifact` (the intake landscape survey the planner consumes, below), `LivePostApproval`, `LocalStackQueueItem`, `LocalStackReaperMarker`, `Loop` (DB-configured autonomous loop — name, exactly one of prompt-FK/script, run_in_sub_agent/description/overlay, nullable delay, enabled, §5.6), `LoopLease`, `LoopState` (durable per-loop control plane, §5.6), `MergeClear`, `OnBehalfApproval`, `OutboundClaim` (drift verifier), `PendingChatInjection`, `PendingPullRequest` (the durable obligation a pre-push `ensure-pr` deferral owes — drained by the `pending_pr_drain` dispatch scanner, aged into a doctor FAIL, §5.6; the invariant needs two terminal conditions or it accrues obligations nothing can discharge, which is how sixteen of them reached ~12,000 drains apiece and turned `t3 doctor check` into sixteen permanent FAILs that buried its five real findings, [#4577](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4577) — a deferral from a checkout under a temp root owes NOTHING, since a cold review's scratch clone is deleted when the review ends and the pre-push hook has no teatree caller to ask, leaving the path the only knowable signal (`utils/disposable_checkout.py`, roots overridable via `TEATREE_DISPOSABLE_CHECKOUT_ROOTS`); and an obligation whose checkout has since gone is RETIRED by the drain rather than retried, announced once as a `pending_pr.retired` signal, because `ensure-pr` cannot run in a directory that is not there. Neither weakens the guard the invariant exists for: a branch that was pushed stays covered by the orphan-branch guard on the remote, one that was not is already gone with its directory, and a LIVE checkout with unpushed work still owes and still nags — the load-bearing case, pinned explicitly rather than inferred from the filter's narrowness), `ProjectLearning` (durable per-repo knowledge store, below), `Prompt`/`PromptVersion` (first-class reusable prompt with templated `params` + edit-history snapshots, the `Loop.prompt` FK target and the `/prompts` trigger, §5.6), `PullRequest`, `RedCardSignal`, `ReplyDispatch`, `ReviewAssignment`, `ReviewEvidence` (per-ticket review-evidence artifact — cold-review + integration-review kinds — gating review-request and cross-repo close, below), `ReviewRequestPost` (one row per posted review request — the Slack channel + thread it landed in, plus `nag_count`, which widens the re-ask interval as `base_interval * fib(nag_count)` so a long-ignored MR is re-asked less and less often, and `resumed_at`, the single-use stamp claimed by a conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE resumed_at IS NULL` before the in-thread "now ready for review" reply, so a paused request can never be resumed twice), `ReviewVerdict`, `ScannedBroadcast`, `SelfImproveFiring`, `SessionHandover`, `SweepSkipStreak` (one row per PR the sweep keeps skipping — the streak ages into a doctor WARN so a permanently-skipped PR is surfaced rather than silently re-skipped every tick; a row is discarded once the PR is settled or has left the swept open set, since `resolve` fires only on a live `pr_sweep.*` signal and so cannot retire the row of a PR that closed outside the sweep, [#4518](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4518); `SKIP_REASON_DISPOSITION` classifies every emittable reason as a deliberate park or a stall in ONE place, so a parked draft never pages the owner however long it runs, and the doctor reports ONE standing finding naming the count rather than a line per row, [#4523](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4523)), `TicketTransition`, `UnshippedWorkRecord` (one row per checkout a reaping pass found holding staged/unstaged/unpushed work, written BEFORE the disposition so a KEPT or force-deleted checkout's work is observable rather than silent — the on-disk salvage bundle it points at is captured with `git diff HEAD`, never a bare `git diff`, which sees only the working tree, and VERBATIM through `git.run_strict_verbatim` rather than the stripping `run_strict` — a patch missing its trailing bytes is one `git apply` rejects as `corrupt patch`, and a bundle exists only to be applied ([#4435](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4435)); untracked-only content is in scope via `full_worktree_diff`'s throwaway-index `add -N`, since a `force` delete would otherwise keep the filename and drop the file; a read this venue could not complete writes its cause to a distinct `.unreadable` key and leaves the last good capture and the row's delta intact, rather than overwriting real content with zero bytes; `t3 <overlay> workspace restore <checkout> --into <checkout>` (`core/cleanup/unshipped_restore.py`) reads a bundle back, so the round-trip is exercised by the suite instead of first by an operator recovering real work; `t3 doctor check` reads those rows back as an advisory WARN carrying each checkout's age via `cli/doctor/checks_unshipped_work.py`, since a recorded row nothing surfaces leaves the work exactly as unobserved as no capture at all), `WaitingItem` (the manual entries of the durable waiting-on-you lane — questions/merge-authorizations/review-requests are computed live, §17.1), `WorktreeEnvOverride`. Further supporting rows back the loops and gates that own them: `KnownIssue` (health-registry), `StandingGoal` (§17 verified-green stop-gate), `PendingTriageRecommendation` (needs-triage assessor), `AssessSweepRun`/`AssessFinding` (assess-sweep ledger), `TicketFacet` (per-ticket decomposition), `ScannedFailedE2E` (failed-E2E watcher dedup), `LoopScheduleSlot` (loop schedule presets, §5.6), `PendingReinstall` (self-update reinstall queue), `SlackSelfAckReaction` (self-ack dedup), `Rubric`/`RubricCriterion` (§17.4.3 rubric-done gate), and the audit rows `DbAudit`/`OnBehalfAudit`/`E2EBypassAudit`. This roster is representative, not exhaustive — the authoritative model set is the `teatree.core.models/` package; each name above is cited from §5.6, §17.1, §17.4, or §4 prose where it is load-bearing.

**Memory-consolidation data layer ([#1933](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1933)).** The idle-time "dreaming" pass clusters session feedback and distils each cluster into one imperative rule, backed by durable rows: `ConsolidatedMemory` (the cited-rule consolidation ledger with a `CANDIDATE → VERIFIED → PROMOTED` status ladder AND a `disposition` ladder that makes the ledger a queue that DRAINS a detected core-gap to a merged fix rather than a pile that grows), `DreamQaProbe` (the retention/interference/monotonicity QA corpus the acceptance gates replay), `DreamRunMarker` (staleness marker), and the `InstructionComplianceSnapshot`/`InstructionComplianceRecord` accountant rows. The engine ranks a bounded extract → an injected `Distiller` clusters by root cause → upserts by `cluster_key` (rejecting uncited clusters, never overwriting a BINDING rule), and the LIVE file-side phases (cross-link / merge / re-index / decay, each fault-isolated behind its own kill-switch) maintain the on-disk `~/.claude` memory corpus under a set of acceptance gates (retention, interference, consolidation-happened, index-budget, monotonicity, no-loss, compliance-non-regression) — a failing gate marks the pass attempted-not-succeeded, never stamps success. A cold-tier `MEMORY_ARCHIVE.md` recall hook resurfaces an archived rule as advisory `additionalContext` for one turn. The full phase mechanics, disposition/escalation ladders, decay budget tiers, and eval-derivation loop are in [docs/blueprint/memory-consolidation.md](docs/blueprint/memory-consolidation.md).

`Ticket` also carries a `Role` enum (`AUTHOR` / `REVIEWER`) orthogonal to its state. The reviewer role drives the §5.6 `reviewer_prs` scanner (`Ticket(role="reviewer", issue_url=<pr_url>)`) and short-circuits to the dedicated reviewer terminal `review_posted` once the review work completes; the author role is the default lifecycle. `delivered` means ONLY author work merged to main, so a reviewer ticket never reads as author-merged "Landed" work on the board.

**Repo-namespaced ticket key ([#2293](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2293)).** `Ticket.repo_namespaced_key` is a collision-free `<repo-slug>#<issue-number>` computed from `issue_url` on save (issue-only — GitLab issues and merge requests are separate numbering sequences in the same project, so a PR/MR URL never feeds this key), backed by a partial unique constraint mirroring `issue_url`'s. `TicketManager.resolve()` and `t3 <overlay> ticket context show|add|edit` (the durable per-ticket context store, [#627](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/627)) accept it alongside pk / bare issue number / full issue URL, so `owner/repo#42` never risks landing on a different repo's issue #42.

**Per-project learnings, DB-placed ([#2892](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2892), #550 item 3, epic #2557).** `ProjectLearning` is the DB-placed analogue of gstack's per-project `learnings.jsonl` — durable, intent-bearing knowledge scoped to a *repo* (`owner/repo`), not a ticket, so it survives across every ticket on that repo. Keyed on `repo_slug` (unique), computed via `teatree.utils.url_slug.project_slug_from_ref` — the SAME repo-slug extraction #2293 introduced for `repo_namespaced_key`, reused rather than duplicated (a literal slug or a full issue/PR/MR URL both resolve; unlike the issue-only `repo_namespaced_key`, project scope has no numbering-collision risk to guard against, so a PR/MR URL is a valid ref here). `append_learning` mirrors `Ticket.append_context` — append-only, timestamped, refuses a blank entry. `t3 <overlay> learnings show|add|edit <repo-ref>` is the read/write CLI — the round-trip affordance the DB-only cache-vs-intent rule ([docs/blueprint/configuration.md](docs/blueprint/configuration.md) §10.5) requires for a DB-only user-intent field. Skill-flow consumption: `workspace ticket`'s intake summary renders any recorded learnings for the ticket's repo as a collapsed `<details>` block (`render_project_learnings`, mirroring `render_ticket_context`), so a fresh session sees prior project-scoped lessons before doing work with no explicit lookup.

**State machines** (one row per `Ticket` is the unit of work):

| Model | States |
|---|---|
| `Ticket` | `not_started → scoped → started → planned → coded → tested → reviewed → shipped → in_review → merged → retrospected → delivered` (plus terminal `ignored`, and the reviewer-role terminal `review_posted` for external-review tickets) |
| `Worktree` | `created → provisioned → services_up → ready → torn_down` (plus the reversible `services_up`/`ready → provisioned` `stop_services` demotion, [#2190](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2190): docker down only, DB + worktree preserved) |
| `Session` | per-phase quality-gate tracker keyed `(ticket, phase, agent_id)` |
| `Task` | `pending → claimed → in_progress → completed / failed`; carries a human-readable `subject` (+ `created_at`) that `Task.display_subject()` resolves (stored subject → work-item title → `#N phase`) so the pending-tasks scanner and statusline show readable titles, never the bare phase token |
| `TaskAttempt` | execution history rows (immutable); each carries the [#2009](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2009) repair-loop budget fields `iteration` (1-based per ticket + normalized-phase, spanning re-queued `Task` rows) and `error_fingerprint` (normalized hash of the terminal reason); each also carries `lane` ([#657](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/657), `TaskAttempt.Lane`: `subscription` \| `metered` \| unattributed `""`) — the Layer-2 lane (subscription OAuth vs metered API key/OrcaRouter BYOK, [#2887](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2887)) that dispatch authenticated through, resolved in `src/teatree/agents/runner.py`'s `_resolve_dispatch_lane()` (`PydanticAiHarness` always metered; `ClaudeSdkHarness` attributed only under an explicit `agent_harness_provider` pin) and always `subscription` for an interactive `record-attempt` (rides the user's own Max seat) |

**A failed task records WHY ([#3957](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3957)).** `Task.fail(*, reason)` takes the reason as a REQUIRED keyword, so no failure path can record nothing; it writes `failure_reason` plus a `failure_kind` drawn from `core/modelkit/task_failure_taxonomy.py`, and `TaskAttempt.failure_kind` is stamped on save beside `outcome`. The kind separates an ENVIRONMENTAL failure (a lost lease re-claimed by another worker, a harness crash) from a DETERMINISTIC one (invalid config, missing phase evidence). What to DO about it is read off the SAME row: `task_failure_taxonomy.RECOVERY` maps every kind to a `RecoveryStrategy` (`retry` \| `corrective_retry` \| `halt_and_escalate`) plus that environmental column, and a kind added without a row is a red test ([#4505](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4505)). Before that table the requeue decision was a SECOND hand-maintained list keyed on error TEXT that never consulted the classification, so `harness_crash` was named environmental and still never retried — eleven tasks dropped in one day, and a PR that reached the owner unreviewed because its reviewing task died that way. The columns stay distinct under a one-way invariant, `retry ⇒ environmental` and never the reverse, so a requeue decision cannot re-run a deterministic failure into the same wall; text markers now feed `classify_failure` only. The reason reaches the surfaces an operator reads: `tasks list` (JSON + a "Failed because" column) and the dash ticket card as a kind chip. Without it a card shows a cause-less error and a genuine defect is indistinguishable from a transient fault.

**A failing ticket is repaired, not only a frozen one ([#3958](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3958)).** The hourly `loop/stuck_ticket_redispatch.py` sweep ran, but its candidate set excluded the population that actually fails: it filtered `role=AUTHOR`, and the failures are dominated by `reviewing`, which lives on REVIEWER tickets. It also keyed on FROZEN — in a work-state, nothing in flight, idle past a threshold — which finds a ticket that stopped, never one that keeps failing and so is never idle. Both are widened on ONE path. A reviewer ticket has no state→phase map (minted at `NOT_STARTED`, held until `REVIEW_POSTED`), so `_implied_phase` reads the phase off its own most recent task, which also preserves the codex review variants a plain `reviewing` would collapse; the open-PR exclusion stays AUTHOR-only, because a reviewer ticket's PR is its SUBJECT and excluding on it would re-narrow the sweep to author-only. A FAILING class is OR-ed alongside the frozen one — latest attempt for the implied phase failed, nothing in flight — minus any failure whose output `phase_landing_evidence` proves landed, which is a dead artifact rather than work to redo. Both classes and both roles reduce to one `_Candidate` through `_redispatch_one`, so no re-dispatch reaches a scheduler without the [#2009](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2009) repair-loop verdict. Neither class reaches a ticket whose newest task an operator CANCELLED ([#4105](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4105)) — see the cancellation paragraph in the intake section. What makes the widening safe rather than a repair storm is a second circuit-breaker: `repair_loop.is_kind_stalled` applies the same two-consecutive-identical rule to the recorded [#3957](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3957) `FailureKind` names, because a fingerprint compares free text and one deterministic defect carrying a varying detail yields two fingerprints that never match. Environmental kinds are dropped rather than compared — an outage is the environment's fault, so it says nothing about the work and stays retryable to the cap — and `unclassified`/`unrecorded` are dropped too, being the absence of a name rather than a shared cause. CAUSELESS kinds — `no_result_envelope` and `runtime_ceiling`, where the run reported nothing at all ([#4075](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4075)) — are dropped from BOTH checks by that absence-of-a-cause test, not by whether the text repeats: `no_result_envelope` is a compile-time constant and so also collides on the fingerprint side, but `runtime_ceiling`'s reason interpolates the breach and so does not — for it the kind-level drop in `task_failure_taxonomy.stall_kinds` alone does the work, which is why the pinned corpus asserts that asymmetry directly ([#4276](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4276)); four real tickets halted on the `no_result_envelope` fingerprint and three later ran the same phase and succeeded.

**One agent per checkout ([#3952](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3952)).** The [#3903](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3903) dedupe stops two `Task` rows for one ticket+phase; it cannot see two agents that ALREADY hold checkouts. Under the autonomous posture the loop mints its own work while operator-dispatched lanes are live and both resolve one ticket to one worktree path — observed on a single branch: a factory agent committing the other's uncommitted edits under its own commit, a `git add -A` staging the other's in-progress files, an unpushed local merge left behind mid-verification. The contended resource is the worktree, so the claim lives on the `Worktree` row: `core/worktree/occupancy.py` carries the same #786 compare-and-swap as `task_claim` (ONE conditional `UPDATE … WHERE <grantable>` whose row count IS the decision — a read-then-write races on SQLite, where `select_for_update` is a silent no-op), keyed on the fully-qualified `(holder, holder_session)` pair so a same-holder re-acquire refreshes while a rival is refused. `run_headless` holds the claim for the whole run (renewed by the existing task-lease heartbeat, released on every exit path) and records a refusal as a FAILED attempt naming the incumbent; `workspace ticket` refuses to hand back an occupied checkout unless `--take-over`; `t3 <overlay> worktree occupancy` / `claim-occupancy` / `release-occupancy` is the surface a hand-driven lane claims through, and `doctor check` reports every live claim. **Advisory only** — the gate refuses a SECOND requester and never evicts the first: nothing here deletes, reaps or kills anything, and a lapsed lease grants the next requester without touching the previous holder's process, files or branch (this repo has already reaped live work by inferring absence from one execution context). `worktree_occupancy_gate_enabled` is the never-lockout kill switch; `worktree_occupancy_lease_seconds` (default 1800) bounds a claim whose holder died without releasing.

**A lost lease is evidence about the LEASE, never about the WORK ([#3982](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3982)).** A shipping task that pushed its branch, opened its pull request and advanced its ticket to `in_review` had completed the phase's entire purpose; recording it `failed` / `lease_lost` because the heartbeat's renewal found the claim generation moved on feeds the auto-repair sweep a "re-do this" signal for work that already landed, and inflates the environmental-failure rate capacity decisions read from. `core/models/phase_landing.py` answers "did this phase's output land?" from the DB alone — the ticket's position on the FULL author ladder (`task_phase_disposition.phase_output_reached`, which runs to `delivered`, unlike `Ticket.has_completed_phase`'s deliberate stop at `shipped`), plus the phase's own artifact: for `shipping` an attached non-closed `PullRequest`, and for a REVIEWER-role ticket's review phase a `ReviewVerdict` recorded at the head that task reviewed ([#4100](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4100) — reviewing carries most of the recorded lease losses, and a reviewer ticket has no author-ladder position at all, being minted at `not_started` and held until `review_posted`, so the ladder half can never speak for it; the reviewed head comes from the `AutoReviewDispatch` contract the task was dispatched under, else the ticket's `reviewed_sha`). Reader and writer are keyed on the SAME head ([#4126](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4126)): `agents/attempt_recorder` records the returned envelope's verdict at the dispatch head, and REFUSES a reviewer's divergent self-asserted one — recording it there hid the verdict from this guard (which reads the dispatch head), while recording a divergence at the dispatch head would vouch for a tree nobody reviewed, so a reviewer that judged a different tree than it was dispatched for fails the task as the finding it is. An UNDISCLOSED head is refused on that same reasoning ([#4168](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4168)): reading an omitted `reviewed_sha` as agreement enforced the rule only against reviewers that disclose a head, so one that said nothing got `merge_safe` recorded at the dispatch head with no check performed at all — the shell sibling `t3 <overlay> review record` already refused an empty `--reviewed-sha`, and `build_review_contract` hands the reviewer the literal 40-char head, so disclosing it costs a compliant reviewer nothing. `RESULT_JSON_SCHEMA`'s `required` list for `review_verdict` and the recorder's refusals are ONE fact, pinned by a test that walks the declared list, so a field declared required but never enforced cannot read as a guard that guards nothing. The artifact half is trusted only when the caller can attribute the row's failure to the lease itself, not to the work — a REQUIRED `trust_phase_artifact` keyword (un-omittable, so no call site opts in by omission) — because a pull request can exist on a REVIEWED ticket independently of a successful `ship()` (the no-orphan pre-push gate and the `PendingPullRequest` drain both open one regardless of the FSM transition) and a verdict at a head can be another reviewer's, and trusting either for a genuinely deterministic failure would silently swallow a real defect. Three consumers read the one predicate: `src/teatree/agents/runner_interruption.py` records an exit-0 attempt carrying the evidence instead of the false failure (landing the row COMPLETED only via `UPDATE … WHERE status=pending`, so a live successor's claim is never stolen) and passes `True` unconditionally, since its call is already gated on `outcome.lease_lost`; `loop/transient_requeue.py` and `loop/stuck_ticket_redispatch.py` each evaluate an already-recorded row with no live outcome to consult, so they pass `True` only when that row's own `failure_kind` is `LEASE_LOST`, and retire (rather than reopen, escalate, or re-dispatch) only then. A watchdog runtime/turns breach is a real runaway and stays a failure regardless. The lost-lease reason itself is read back from the row (`task_claim.describe_lease_loss`) rather than assumed: a single worker driving every loop routinely loses leases to its own `reclaim_orphaned_claims` sweep, and reporting that as "re-claimed by another worker" sends the operator hunting for a second worker that does not exist.

**An interruption noticed after the row COMPLETED records nothing at all ([#4100](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4100)).** The heartbeat outlives the work: the task reaches COMPLETED, the next lease renewal finds the claim generation gone — because the row moved on by FINISHING — and the interrupted run wrote a `failed` / `lease_lost` outcome over a completed row. Roughly half of all recorded failures on the live box were work that had succeeded, which inflated `repair_burn` past its red threshold and had the auto-repair sweep re-dispatching reviews that were already done (three concurrent review tasks for one PR). `src/teatree/agents/runner_interruption.py::_record_stuck_outcome` therefore consults the LIVE row before recording anything: a COMPLETED row leaves the interruption as an exit-0 attempt naming it and touches the row itself not at all — the run had, in the diagnosis's own words, nothing left to hand over. What the run had already PRODUCED is a separate question ([#4464](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4464)): a reviewing agent that emitted its verdict envelope and only then lost its lease had that envelope dropped for a summary-only attempt, leaving a `completed` row with nothing persisted behind it — indistinguishable from a reviewer that silently declined to record, which is the shape that made [#4308](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4308) so hard to pin down. The produced envelope therefore rides onto the attempt beside the interruption, making the work durable and inspectable without re-driving an FSM the rival run owns.

**Repair-loop budget + stall detection ([#2009](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2009)).** The verify→diagnose→patch→re-verify retry loop has a *visible* per-ticket-phase iteration budget, not just the time-based 24h stale-task expiry. Each `TaskAttempt` is auto-stamped on insert (the single `TaskAttempt.save` chokepoint) with `iteration` (its 1-based attempt number for the `(ticket, normalized-phase)`, spanning re-queued `Task` rows) and `error_fingerprint` (a stable hash of its terminal reason, normalized so transient noise — timestamps, pids, hex ids, paths, bare numbers — does not defeat the identical-failure check). `t3 <overlay> ticket show` surfaces `attempt N/max` per phase against the configurable `MAX_PHASE_ITERATIONS` cap (default 5). The re-queue chokepoint `TaskQuerySet.reclaim_orphaned_claims` consults `Task.check_requeue_allowed` (pure policy in `teatree.core.repair_loop`, model orchestration in `teatree.core.models.task_repair`) before returning a failed/orphaned task to PENDING: at the cap it raises `MaxIterationsExceeded`; two consecutive identical fingerprints on the same phase raise `IterationStalled`, which escalates to the user via a durable `DeferredQuestion` (§17.1 invariant 9) and refuses the re-queue — so a doomed phase neither re-runs forever nor burns another attempt on the identical failure. Every `last_two_fingerprints` builder passes through `task_failure_taxonomy.stall_fingerprints`, and every `last_two_deterministic_kinds` builder through its kind-side sibling `task_failure_taxonomy.stall_kinds` — the two places a CAUSELESS attempt is withheld from a stall comparison ([#4075](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4075)): `no_result_envelope` / `runtime_ceiling` are the ABSENCE of a cause — the run reported nothing about why the work is unfinished — so two of them are one silence repeated, not one defect recurring, and the corrective retry the loop itself schedules supplies the second strike, making the halt an artifact of the repair rather than an observation. `stall_fingerprints` withholds one further kind — `outage`, whose repeated TEXT still says nothing about the work, so two identical ones are one outage repeated — and that set is deliberately NARROWER than the operator-display `is_environmental` axis: `result_error` / `provision_failed` / `landing_unverified` / the lease kinds carry defect-specific text, and `harness_crash` carries a traceback naming a code path, so a verbatim repeat of one is a defect recurring; `transient_requeue`'s reopen branch passes no `last_two_deterministic_kinds`, so the fingerprint check is its only stall check. The attempt still counts toward `iteration_count`, so a phase that keeps reporting nothing still escalates at the cap.

**Deterministically-correctable config breaches self-repair instead of paging ([#3665](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3665)).** A repair-halt whose failure has exactly one valid resolution carries no decision, so it is corrected rather than escalated to a person. The criterion is exact: a failure is *self-correctable* iff its error text is recognised by a registered resolver AND that resolver yields **exactly one** setting→value assignment that makes the configuration valid — zero candidates (nothing to correct) and two or more (a genuine choice) both page through the ordinary `DeferredQuestion` path, unchanged. For an invalid `agent_harness`/`agent_harness_provider` pair the candidate set is the harnesses under which the operator's EXPLICIT provider pin is valid; correcting the *provider* is deliberately not attempted (every harness admits two providers, so picking one would invent a credential decision the operator never made), and a future harness sharing a provider makes the set ambiguous so the condition pages again — by construction, not by a second rule. Over-suppression is foreclosed on three sides: `ConfigRepair.apply` logs at WARNING naming the setting, the value and why it was unambiguous; a durable `[self-repaired] <setting>=<value>` stamp on the task is rendered by the `/dash/settings/` *Self-repairs* readout; and that stamp is also the idempotency guard, so a breach surviving its own repair escalates normally. The split is by layer — `core.config_self_repair` owns the pure criterion and the correction it yields (over an injected valid-provider table), `loop.config_self_repair` resolves it against the live harness registry, because the agents-layer registry and the core-layer config store are domain siblings that cannot import each other.

**§4 invariant — worker enqueue pattern (load-bearing).** Transitions that own long I/O follow one rule:

- Transition body stays pure: state change + metadata only, then `transaction.on_commit(lambda: execute_X.enqueue(self.pk))`. The state change and the queued work land atomically.
- Workers take a row lock (`select_for_update()`), re-check the source state, run the runner, and on success call the next transition. On the production SQLite engine that clause is a **silent no-op** (`has_select_for_update` is `False`, so Django drops it and Django's own outside-a-transaction guard — itself gated on that feature — never fires): the exclusion actually comes from `transaction_mode: "IMMEDIATE"` (§4.3), which only a `transaction.atomic()` block takes. So the locked re-read must sit INSIDE `atomic()`, and `skip_locked`/`nowait` are unavailable — `IMMEDIATE` blocks where they promise to return. `tests/conformance/test_select_for_update_is_backed_by_immediate.py` re-derives the census of every call site on each run and fails on either shape ([#4226](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4226)).
- At-least-once delivery is safe because the state guard makes redelivery a no-op.
- `post_transition` signals are reserved for lossy cross-cutting side effects (audit log, Slack reactions) — never for the main work of the transition.

**Review states name WHOSE review they record.** `REVIEWED` is the maker's own pass (self-review) and `IN_REVIEW` is peer review in flight, so a ticket that has cleared its own review is not yet a ticket a colleague has looked at — the review-request gate reads `has_passed_review` for the first and the broadcast lifecycle for the second. The labels say so rather than leaving the two readable as one "reviewed".

Auto-scheduling chains the phases: `start → provision worker → schedule_planning`, `plan → schedule_coding` (gated on `PlanArtifact` DB record — no artifact raises `NoPlanArtifactError`; the only path from STARTED to CODED passes through PLANNED), `code → schedule_testing`, `test → schedule_review`, `review → schedule_shipping` (gated on `has_shippable_diff()`). Each forward transition threads the completing task as the next phase task's `parent_task` (PR-12), so the phase-boundary brief is warm — `agents.prompt._parent_result_summary` reads the parent's last attempt (summary, files, next steps) instead of dispatching cold. The runner classes live in `core/runners/`; the workers in `core/tasks.py` and `core/worktree/worktree_tasks.py`. The PLANNED `plan_text` also carries an `E2E test plan / Acceptance scenarios` section for UI-visible tickets (prose-gated on `frontend_repos`, mirroring `is_ui_visible`) so behaviour-level TDD is planned up front (#2240).

**Intake landscape survey baked into the FSM ([#2541](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2541)).** The provision worker (`execute_provision`) is the ticket-intake (info-fetch) step: once the worktrees materialise and before `schedule_planning`, it surveys what is already in flight or already settled — open PRs/MRs, in-flight local worktrees (uncommitted/unpushed), and a per-issue close/merge/supersede recommendation — and persists it as an append-only `LandscapeArtifact` (the survey shape gathered django-free by `teatree.core.intake.landscape` and composed by `teatree.core.intake.landscape_gather`; mirrors `PlanArtifact`, latest governs). The planner CONSUMES that persisted survey rather than re-deriving it (a headless planner sees it inline in its `INTAKE LANDSCAPE SURVEY` system-context block; any planner reads `LandscapeArtifact.latest_for`). Persistence is best-effort and never a gate — a forge outage during provision degrades to a log line and never blocks provisioning or planning; the planner then falls back to the `workspace landscape` fetch. `info artifacts` surfaces the latest survey alongside a ticket's other artifacts.

**Pre-flight attachment-fetch gate (PR-15).** The same intake step (`execute_provision`, after the landscape survey and before `schedule_planning`) refuses to hand off to the planner while any attachment the ticket references is still un-fetched — a GitLab upload, a linked Notion file, a Slack-thread file. `teatree.core.intake.attachment_manifest` extracts every referenced attachment URL from the issue body + comments (deterministic regex, code-host text via an injected seam, fail-open), reconciles them against the on-disk cache under `<ticket_dir>/.attachments/` (ground truth: the file exists), and persists an append-only `AttachmentManifest` (mirrors `LandscapeArtifact`, latest governs). The gate is vacuous on a zero-attachment ticket; a hold is transient (re-running provision after a fetch hands off) and never a lockout — the `attachment_gate_enabled` kill-switch, a `--fetch`, or manual placement all release it. CLI: `t3 <overlay> ticket attachments <ref> [--fetch]`; fetch transport is injected via a core registry backends populate at app-ready (Notion wired, other kinds report a manual-download hint). Companion intake-source check: `teatree.verification.url_check.check_url` (HEAD-first, ranged-GET fallback, typed `OK`/`UNRESOLVED`/`NETWORK_ERROR`) gates `PendingArticleSuggestion.record_candidate`, dropping a fabricated/404 candidate URL and recording-anyway on a transient network error.

**Who drains the queue.** No always-on `db_worker` is assumed; a won-owner tick drains a bounded batch of `DBTaskResult` jobs in-process (`teatree.loop.queue_drain`), standing down while a live worker holds the `worker` (`WORKER_SINGLETON`) flock — the one singleton every worker entry point acquires — and first expires READY jobs older than `T3_QUEUE_STALE_HOURS` (default 24h) to the reversible `FAILED` state so a fresh drainer never fires stale heavy jobs.

**Clean-worktree preflight (#884).** `code()`, `test()`, `review()`, `ship()` refuse the transition if any worktree has tracked uncommitted changes. The refusal raises a `DirtyWorktreeError` (an `InvalidTransitionError` subclass, **not** `TransitionNotAllowed`) inside the caller's outer atomic block, so the FSM state change is rolled back and the lease reaper returns the CLAIMED task to PENDING on the next tick. No auto-stash — worktrees share one `.git`, so a stash is repo-global and could clobber an unrelated branch's work.

**DoD local-E2E preflight ([#88](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/88)).** `ship()` runs a second preflight (`core/gates/dod_gate.check_local_e2e_dod`) after the clean-worktree one: a UI-visible ticket (scoped `repos` intersect the overlay's `frontend_repos`) needs a green local-stack E2E artifact (`Ticket.extra['e2e_recipe'].last_run` with `result == "green"` and `env == "local"`) before leaving the implementation lifecycle. Refusal raises `DodLocalE2EError`; hatch `dod_e2e_override` with a non-empty reason; an overlay without `frontend_repos` is silent. `is_ui_visible` fails **closed** for an unregistered overlay ([#1426](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1426)), while a known **path-only** TOML overlay is answered from its `[overlays.<name>]` table ([#733](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/733)).

**Mandatory-E2E gate for customer-display-impacting changes ([#1967](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1967)).** `core/gates/e2e_mandatory_gate` runs at `pr create` and §17.4 `ticket clear`: a change the fail-closed classifier `overlay.review.classify_customer_display_impact` marks impacting needs a satisfier — a green AND **posted** SHA-bound `E2eMandatoryRun`, a single-use **user** `E2EBypassApproval` (maker/agent/loop refused), or the `e2e_mandatory_gate_enabled` kill-switch. Deny names both remedies. Additive to #88 (which keys on frontend repos; this keys on diff content).

**Per-(repo, ticket) open-PR budget gate (north-star PR-2).** `core/gates/pr_budget_gate.check_pr_budget` runs at the two core PR-creation seams — `_run_ship_gates` (`pr create`, right after the shipping gate) and the orphan-branch path (`_ensure_pr.create_or_defer_pr`) — refusing to open a PR when the ticket already has `max_open_prs_per_repo_per_ticket` open (not-merged) PRs in that repo. The knob is a durable `UserSettings` field (`OVERLAY_OVERRIDABLE`, **not** a `FEATURE_FLAG`) read as data via `get_effective_settings(overlay)`; its **shipped default `1`** (D9) enforces at-most-one-open-PR-per-repo-per-ticket out of the box — the generic core shape a max-one-MR-per-repo directive resolves to, with no overlay-local branch in core (the N=2 litmus) — while a per-overlay `ConfigSetting` value of `0` is the explicit **unlimited opt-out** (the empty-table escape). The count unions the ticket's non-merged `PullRequest` rows with the synchronously-written `extra["pr_url_by_branch"]` index (reconciler-lag safe); the refusal names the offending PR URLs and the `config_setting set … 0` escape. **Forge-authoritative third source (fleet-safety Stage 3).** The local union only sees PRs THIS instance opened — a sibling fleet instance's PR is invisible until its own reconciler imports it a tick later, the window in which two instances each open a PR for one ticket (invariant I2). When a caller supplies the repo's code host (all three seams do), `check_pr_budget` adds a live forge read (`core/gates/pr_budget_forge.forge_open_pr_urls_for_ticket` over `host.list_my_prs` — the client the PR scanners already use, no new forge client), scoped to the ticket's repo and attributed by the same `<slug>#<n>` close-keyword join the tick reconciler uses, unioned with the local set and deduped by URL (so a PR both sources know is never double-counted). It **fails OPEN**: a forge/network/rate-limit error, an unresolvable identity, or a missing host degrade to the local-only count so a forge outage never blocks all shipping; the read is memoised per `(ticket, repo)` for a short window so the two ship seams in one flow share one `list_my_prs` call.

**Directive intake front-end (north-star PR-6).** A `Directive` (`core/models/directive.py`) is a first-class guarded-row FSM in the `OuterLoopExperiment`/`MergeClear` family that turns a plain-language directive about teatree's own behaviour into a ratified, typed `MechanismSketch` (`core/models/mechanism_sketch.py`: setting key + neutral default, core policy chokepoint, overlay activation, acceptance tests, and the recorded **rejected alternatives** — the N=2 litmus). PR-6 implements the intake arc `CAPTURED → CLARIFYING → INTERPRETED → RATIFY_PENDING → ADMITTED` (post-`ADMITTED` implement/configure/verify states are declared but a later PR's). Interpretation is a headless `directive_interpreting` phase (the `CriticDispatch` idiom — `DirectiveDispatch.enqueue`) carrying its own `SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE`/`_TOOLS_BY_PHASE`/`PHASE_REQUIRED_EVIDENCE["directive_interpreting"] = ("directive_interpretation",)`; the server-side recorder (`core/gates/directive_interpret_gate`) validates the sketch (structural checks in the model, the overlay-registry scope check in the gate) and writes it, or records clarifying `DeferredQuestion`s — maker≠checker. **DEFAULT BEHAVIOUR FIRST ([#4181](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4181)).** The kind catalog is three: `default_behaviour` (the DEFAULT — the constraint ships as the unconditional behaviour at the core seam, `setting_key` and `activation_scope` both empty), `setting_policy_gate` (the EXCEPTION), and `activation_only` (the duplication-check branch). A setting is minted only when a SECOND overlay demonstrably needs a different value, and the recorder REFUSES a `setting_policy_gate` that names no such consumer — an empty `activation_scope`, or an `activation_value` equal to the `neutral_default` — naming `default_behaviour` as the reshape. The refusal is what closed the shipped-inert class: the previous "constraint as data with a NEUTRAL default (core inert)" doctrine had queued 31 sketches to mint 31 settings that would never run. Ratification is one human `DeferredQuestion`; `Directive.admit` is the ONLY writer of `ADMITTED` and **raises without a consumed ratify question** (no auto-admit, mirroring the outer loop). **UNDECIDABLE NEVER TERMINATES ([#4184](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4184), [#4187](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4187)).** Every reader of a ratify answer — this loop, the outer loop, and the #119 dial's decline metric — shares ONE classifier (`core/models/ratification.py`, in core because core cannot import a loop), pinned by `tests/conformance/test_one_ratification_classifier.py` so a fourth copy cannot appear silently. It reserves `DENIAL` for a bare refusal standing alone or an unnegated denial verb stated up front; a NEGATED approval ("not approved yet", "cannot approve until …") and a self-contradicted bare denial ("Nope, actually approve it") re-ask instead, because the rejected state is terminal with no recovery transition and a re-ask costs one round trip. `directive_loop_enabled` ships ON ([#3895](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3895) graduated it DARK → SETTLING under the owner-authorised autonomous-by-default posture), so a captured directive IS interpreted out of the box; what bounds the arc is structural rather than a flag — the `DIRECTIVE` intent is unrouteable at intake (ambient directive detection is deleted, #105), so the router DROPs a DIRECTIVE-classified event and nothing writes a row unless the explicit `t3 directive capture` CLI is used (`Directive.objects.capture` is the ONLY producer), and every effectful step sits behind the human ratify gate. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Net-new-tech-debt merge gate (north-star PR-3).** `core/gates/debt_delta_gate.check_debt_delta` runs at the same two core PR-creation seams as the budget gate — `_run_ship_gates` (`pr create`, right after the budget gate) and the orphan-branch path (`_ensure_pr.create_or_defer_pr`), both through one `evaluate_debt_delta` orchestration — refusing a ship whose diff introduces **net-new** tech-debt suppression: a new `noqa` / `type-ignore` / `pragma-no-cover` comment, an unreferenced `pytest.mark.skip`/`xfail`, a new `per-file-ignores` entry, or a lowered `fail_under` coverage floor. The pure scanner (`teatree.quality.debt_delta.scan_debt_delta`, reusing `gate_relaxation.parse_diff` + `blank_string_literals`) reads only diff-**added** lines of `git branch_diff` (merge-base..HEAD), so pre-existing legacy debt is exempt and removing a suppression is always allowed — **delta, not absolute**: a shrink-only ratchet like the deferred-import-peg / module-health ledgers. The escape is an **audited plan-manifest waiver**: an `approved_debt` entry (`pattern` + non-empty `reason`) on the ticket's latest `PlanArtifact.adequacy`, mechanizing "no tech debt without explicit approval" as a recorded artifact rather than a silent bypass. Gated by the `require_debt_delta` DARK `FEATURE_FLAG` (`UserSettings` default `False`, `OVERLAY_OVERRIDABLE`): ships core inert, opt-in per overlay; its own kill-switch (`config_setting set require_debt_delta false`) is the never-lockout escape, and an unverifiable diff (no real repo / git error) is a no-op, never a confirmed block. Diff-computed — no migration. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Merge-quality critic gate (north-star PR-4).** The SEMANTIC quality judgment on top of the deterministic debt gate: "clean + tested-enough WITHOUT bloat" becomes a real merge gate, so a merely-green auto-change that is not well-engineered does not merge. Two LLM-advisory rubric items at `transition="merge"` on the existing transition-keyed critic registry (`critic_rubric`) — `test_value` (anti-vacuity AND anti-bloat, anchored to the ratified `test_strategy`: the `MechanismSketch.acceptance_tests` for a directive ticket, the plan's `test_strategy` adequacy section for an ordinary one) and `cleanliness` (the CLAUDE.md bar) — are judged by the headless critic on the shared `critic_reviewing` phase, recording a `CriticVerdict(transition="merge")` at the shipped head. Judging is split from gating in time (the critic-gate doctrine — no LLM in the blocking path): `core/gates/merge_quality_gate.assert_merge_quality_verdict` runs a DETERMINISTIC row-check at `core/merge/execution.execute_bound_merge` (the single chokepoint both merge paths cross), refusing unless a verdict covers the exact shipped head and affirmatively PASSes every merge item (a FAIL, an uncited pass, or an OMITTED item all block — the anti-vacuity floor). Fail-closed like "CI must be green", and satisfiable — the gate arms the async critic when no covering verdict exists. DIRECTIVE tickets are gated UNCONDITIONALLY (self-modification gets no benefit of the doubt); ORDINARY tickets only under the `require_merge_quality_verdict` DARK `FEATURE_FLAG` (`UserSettings` default `False`, `OVERLAY_OVERRIDABLE`), whose kill-switch is the never-lockout escape. No migration. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Design critic — mechanism_placement + transition=plan generic-vs-hack judgment (north-star PR-5).** The anti-tech-debt tooth at DESIGN time: for a directive-implementation ticket, the plan must declare its generic-shape decision and a coder is never dispatched against a hack. Two layers. **Deterministic teeth:** a fifth plan-adequacy section `mechanism_placement` (`core/models/plan_adequacy.py`: `MECHANISM_PLACEMENT_SECTION` + `mechanism_conforms`) that a directive ticket's plan must carry — a CORE-seam chokepoint (never an overlay package — reusing `mechanism_sketch.is_core_seam_chokepoint`, the no-overlay-leak core-seam predicate), the constraint as the ratified setting + its neutral default, the ratified per-overlay activation, the N=2-litmus `rejected_alternatives`, and every refactor the sketch names. `mechanism_conforms` (whose own presence check is the 5th-section requirement) is enforced inside `plan_currency_gate.check_plan_current` (at `code()` + `schedule_coding`), so a non-conforming plan raises `NoCurrentPlanError` and BLOCKS coder dispatch. These directive teeth are checked UNCONDITIONALLY (H3): the `mechanism_conforms` call sits ABOVE the `require_plan_adequacy` early-return, so a directive-linked plan is held to its ratified sketch even when the general plan-adequacy flag is off — consistent with `merge_quality_gate`'s "directive tickets unconditionally" doctrine. It is a no-op for ordinary work (no linked directive), so ordinary tickets are never over-blocked. Type-aware value comparison keeps a JSON `false`/`0` (bool-vs-int) neutral-default/activation-value a drift. Because the teeth are unconditional, neither the `require_plan_adequacy` kill-switch nor the trivial-skip marker disables them; the never-lockout escape is the audited all-reasoned-negative plan-bypass manifest (`mechanism_conforms` waives it) — a section-level `none_reason` does NOT waive (it is only reached once a mechanism was ratified, so a "mechanism-less" waiver is contradictory). **Semantic net:** four LLM-advisory rubric items at `transition="plan"` on the transition-keyed `critic_rubric` — `generality` (the N=2 litmus), `sketch_conformance`, `convention_fit`, `refactor_honesty` — judged by the headless critic on the shared `critic_reviewing` phase (`core/gates/design_critic_gate`), armed at `Ticket.plan()` for directive tickets and recording FAIL items as `CriticFinding(transition="plan")`. ADVISORY-only (the deterministic `mechanism_conforms` holds the block); the advisory-only design critic carries no independent switch — it is armed by the `directive_loop_enabled` `FEATURE_FLAG` (#104: the critic fires only for directive-linked tickets, so the directive-loop flag arms it), now SETTLING/default-ON (#3895) — a strict no-op (one settings read) for a ticket with no linked directive, and OFF restores the pre-graduation no-op everywhere. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Directive loop runtime (north-star PR-7).** `loops/directive_loop/` drives the post-`ADMITTED` arc `IMPLEMENTING → CONFIGURING → VERIFYING → FULFILLED | REVERT_PENDING → REVERTED`, mirroring `loops/outer_loop/` module-for-module (guarded FSM rows, single-step `run_tick`, injectable `TickSeams`, QUADRUPLE-OFF). **IMPLEMENTING** schedules a real coder against the ratified `MechanismSketch` via `Ticket.schedule_coding` (the maker≠checker pipeline: plan gate → design/quality critic → cold review → critic-gated merge keystone), linking the synthetic ticket back with `extra["directive_id"]`; a `default_behaviour` sketch builds code the same way, and only an `activation_only` sketch skips straight to configure. **CONFIGURING** applies the overlay `ConfigSetting.set_value(key, value, scope)` only when byte-identical to the ratified sketch (the drift guard), confirmed by a `get_effective_settings` read-back; a global (empty-scope) mechanism needs no per-overlay row, so configure is a no-op that advances to verify. **VERIFYING** judges the five evidence classes — activation live, acceptance node-ids green at the merged tree, behavior probe clean (`loops/directive_loop/probes`), `no_collateral_regression` (shared with the outer loop), and zero open `CriticFinding` rows — reaching `FULFILLED` only when all pass; a setting-less `default_behaviour` sketch has no activation to read back, so evidence 1 is carried by the other four rather than failed against a setting deliberately never minted; anything less rolls the config back instantly and parks in `REVERT_PENDING` for a human-asked revert (`t3 directive resolve-revert`) to terminal `REVERTED`. The guard chain reuses the outer loop's probes and is ARC-SCOPED (#3643): the pre-admission INTAKE arc (interpret → clarify → ratify-ask → admit) runs flag → critic-live → signal-trust → budget, while the post-admission EXECUTION arc adds the factory-score requirement — the arc that actually changes config and measures against the metric. Interpreting owner intent therefore does not depend on the DARK `factory_score_enabled` subsystem, and it still STOPS at the structural human ratify gate (`Directive.admit` raises without a consumed, answered ratify question). A guard refusal is logged at warning level and printed as `WARN` by `t3 directive tick`, so a refused tick is never indistinguishable from an idle one; The OFF stack lost its flag leg when #3895 graduated `directive_loop_enabled` to SETTLING/default-ON: the remaining THREE are the seeded-disabled `Loop` row (seed migration `0035`) + `off_live_tick` + the code guards, so the loop is still inert on a fresh install until an operator enables the row, and the EXECUTION arc additionally needs the DARK `factory_score_enabled` and a live critic. Ambient directive detection is DELETED (#105): the intake scanner (`incoming_events.IncomingEventsScanner._execute`) DROPs a `DIRECTIVE`-classified event as an unrouteable intent, so the ONLY `Directive` producer is the explicit `t3 directive capture` CLI (`Directive.objects.capture`, `source=CLI`); the #116 firewall substrate (provenance stamping, reader profile, taint floor) is retained for other untrusted inbound processing and for #119's dial. CLI: `t3 directive tick|resolve-revert|history` on the `t3 outer` shape. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Context firewall / lethal-trifecta mitigation (#116).** Once the directive intake becomes ambient (an inbound untrusted message minting a directive), it assembles Willison's lethal trifecta — private data (repos, Slack history, the teatree DB, worker-env tokens) + untrusted content (inbound Slack/MR/issue text) + external write (Slack posts, forge comments). #116 severs the untrusted-content leg from the other two ARCHITECTURALLY, at the single narrowest choke — the `IncomingEvent → Directive` boundary — in three structural (never prompt-based) layers, all shipping fully DARK. **Layer 1 — quarantined reader profile** (`src/teatree/agents/reader_profile.py`, WIRED into the headless spawn at `src/teatree/agents/runner.py` + `src/teatree/agents/_runner_options.py`): any stage that ingests untrusted content runs under the `directive_reading` phase, which the `phase_tools` SSOT maps to the EMPTY toolset (`_NONE`), so Lane A denies the EXHAUSTIVE `llm.builtin_tools.KNOWN_BUILTIN_TOOLS` registry (the binary-validated 28-tool set — every known CLI built-in including the external-effect `PushNotification`/`RemoteTrigger` and tool-acquisition `ToolSearch`, not a hand-rolled subset) and Lane B filters to nothing. Because an empty `allowed_tools` is a no-op in the SDK transport (the flag is omitted when the list is empty), the residual tool-acquisition channels are closed at the source too: the reader spawn loads NO settings (`setting_sources=[]`) and NO MCP config (`strict_mcp_config=True`, empty `mcp_servers`), so no MCP-server tool or custom slash command from user/project config is reachable — the reader physically cannot read, shell out, fetch, write, spawn, or obtain ANY tool. Defense-in-depth env: `_resolve_child_env_or_failure` filters the reader's `options.env` through `reader_child_env` (an ALLOWLIST — inference credential + minimal runtime only), and the spawn runs inside `reader_env_hermetic` which reduces `os.environ` to the same allowlist for the spawn window (the SDK merges `{**os.environ, **options.env}` and cannot delete an omitted key, so the scrub is the belt and the filtered `options.env` the suspenders). An allowlist — not a denylist of known secret shapes — is load-bearing: it drops `DATABASE_URL`/`SENTRY_DSN`/`AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` and any unanticipated secret by construction. **Layer 2 — schema-validated emission** (`core/models/directive_candidate.py`): the reader emits ONLY a typed, length-capped, single-statement `DirectiveCandidate` (mirroring `mechanism_sketch.py`), never free-form text; `validate_candidate_structure` is deterministic first-finding-wins (is-directive, length cap, no control chars / code fences / injection markers, well-formed scope). **Layer 3 — fail-closed recorder** (`core/gates/directive_candidate_gate.py`): `record_returned_directive_candidate` (maker≠checker) cross-checks the reader's ECHOED provenance against the true source event (the reader cannot upgrade its own trust) and runs the schema gate; on ANY finding it writes ZERO `Directive` rows and the raw attacker text stays inert on `IncomingEvent.body`. On success `Directive.raw_text` is the SANITIZED candidate, so every downstream tooled stage (interpreter, implementer) works from validated text by construction and never touches raw content. **Taint + floor.** Provenance (`core/models/provenance.py`: `owner`/`trusted_colleague`/`public`/`web`, fail-closed to `public`) is stamped once at the `persist_incoming_event` chokepoint and carried as `Directive.taint`; `core/models/approval_policy.py::approval_policy(action_class, taint, *, dial)` checks the taint FLOOR FIRST and short-circuits — any taint ≠ `owner` is `ASK` before any dial is consulted, so no dial can auto-approve an untrusted action. Ratification of an ambient directive is PAYLOAD-VISIBLE (the inert verbatim source + provenance + concrete mechanism facts). **Per-action-class dial (#119, PR-11).** The dial `#116` left empty is now `core/models/approval_dial.py::policy_dial` — a per-action-class trust table (DB-home `ConfigSetting` row `approval_dial`, a `{class: "ask"|"auto"}` JSON dict resolved global-then-overlay; classes `directive_admit`/`outer_loop_keep`/`on_behalf_post`/`public_issue_create`/`gate_or_policy_change`) consulted at ask-time in `ask_ratification`/`ask_keep`/the on-behalf gate (`require_on_behalf_approval`). It returns `AUTO_APPROVE` for a class ONLY when three gates all pass: the class is not a **never-fades** class (`public_issue_create` non-templated public bodies + `gate_or_policy_change` — the other never-fades case, free-text external posts from untrusted input, is the taint floor above the dial); the operator **graduated** it to `auto` (`t3 <overlay> approval_dial set <class> auto`); and its trailing-window **metrics** (`core/models/approval_metrics.py`, off #117's `SendAudit` rows + the `DeferredQuestion` ledger — decline rate, defect escape, rework) have not breached, which **auto-re-tightens** a graduated class back to ASK. Graduation is an AUDITED auto-answer, never a bypass of the structural raises: `auto_answer_by_policy` consumes the recorded `DeferredQuestion` single-use with `resolved_via='policy'` + a `DeferredQuestionAudit` receipt (the on-behalf path records a single-use `policy` `OnBehalfApproval`), so `admit()`/`record_kept()`'s consumed-question guards still hold. **Ships inert** — the table is empty, every class ASKs, byte-identical to before. Any error reading table/metrics fails CLOSED to ASK. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Directive dogfood + eval (north-star PR-8).** The capability's capstone evidence — the proof that a natural-language directive drives a clean, verified self-modification end to end. `tests/integration/directive_dogfood/` runs the WHOLE arc `CAPTURED → FULFILLED` on REAL components (the delta vs the PR-6/PR-7 unit suites, which fake every seam): real settings resolution from `ConfigSetting` rows, the real `directive_interpret_gate` recorder, a real ratify `DeferredQuestion`, a real admission `FactoryScoreSnapshot`, a real byte-conformant `ConfigSetting` activation + `get_effective_settings` read-back, a real `pr_budget_violations` probe over real `PullRequest` rows, the real `no_collateral_regression` fold over two real snapshots, the real `CriticFinding` count, and one real `run_acceptance_tests` subprocess; only the two host-wide guard seams (G3 signal-trust, G4 budget) stay injected. Four journeys: the capstone `CAPTURED→FULFILLED` proof-case, keep-only-if-verified + instant rollback on a probe violation, the merge-quality (PR-4, unconditional for directive tickets) and debt-delta (PR-3, per-overlay) teeth firing on the loop-created ticket, and — the no-prod-flip pin — quadruple-OFF at DEFAULT resolution (the router DROPs, the seeded `Loop` row is disabled, the tick refuses `directive_loop_disabled` with zero mutation), so merging PR-8 is a proven no-op in production: the harness enables the flags ONLY as `ConfigSetting` rows inside the pytest-django test transaction, never the host store. The evals live on the existing metered catalog (`evals/scenarios/directive_interpretation.yaml`): a `clean_room` interpretation-quality scenario (duplication-first → `activation_only`, deterministic field-level matchers pinned to the checked-in exemplar) and an `under_load` ratify-conformance scenario (never hand-apply a value that drifts from the ratified sketch — the skill's byte-law section), each with `_pass/_fail/_noop` replay fixtures and anti-vacuity/contract-drift/exemplar-conformance pins so a doctrine or exemplar edit reds for free on every PR. No new model, migration, flag, or eval-runner — the delta is test/eval evidence, one skill-prose section, and this paragraph. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Snapshot-baseline attestation gate.** The `snapshot-baseline-gate` prek hook (`scripts/hooks/check_snapshot_baseline` over the pure `teatree.quality.snapshot_baseline` engine) refuses a commit that stages a Playwright visual baseline (a file under `__snapshots__/` or `<spec>-snapshots/`) unless the commit's worktree ticket carries a recorded visual-verification attestation — a green + posted `E2eMandatoryRun` read via `E2eMandatoryRun.has_visual_verification` (a per-ticket, any-SHA variant, since the tree the baseline lands on has no SHA yet at commit time). It rides the existing mandatory-E2E attestation row rather than a second store. Sibling never-lockout shape: the `snapshot_baseline_gate_enabled` kill-switch, the `ALLOW_SNAPSHOT_BASELINE='<reason>'` per-commit marker, and fail-open on a Django/DB error or an unresolvable ticket (crash ≠ deny). The live-post approval matcher (`cli/review/live_approval._has_approval_phrase`) additionally masks quoted spans so a quoted approval phrase (reported speech) never authorizes a `--live` publish.

**Default browser tool (chrome-devtools-mcp).** `core/evidence/browser_diagnosis.resolve_browser_diagnosis` gates Google's `chrome-devtools-mcp` server behind the `chrome_devtools_mcp_enabled` setting (default on — teatree's default browser tool, replacing the legacy Claude-in-Chrome extension; it drives its own Chrome over CDP with no claude.ai account or extension pairing); `t3 mcp browser-diagnosis` prints the `claude mcp add` registration line, which passes `--headless=true` (upstream defaults to a visible Chrome) unless the `chrome_devtools_headless` setting is turned off to explicitly opt into a headed browser.
