It drives and inspects a page (navigate/click/fill/upload, network/console/DOM) before any root-cause claim; a diagnostic and interaction aid only — perf/trace enforcement stays in the deterministic Playwright lane.

**Read-before-overwrite gate for tracked config/dotfiles ([#2661](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/pull/2661)).** `core/gates/config_overwrite_guard` (decision core) + `hooks/scripts/config_overwrite_guard.handle_block_config_overwrite` (the PreToolUse hook) refuse a blind destructive write to a user config/dotfile (a `dotfiles`-repo file, `.zshrc`, any file under an XDG `.config` dir regardless of suffix): a `Write` or `Edit` that overwrites an existing one from content the agent assumed rather than read, or a `git checkout`/`git restore` that restores a tracked one from a committed version — when the path was NOT read this session (the predicate consumes the existing `<session>.reads` capture `handle_read_dedup` maintains). The on-disk content is authoritative even when it diverges from the committed version (uncommitted edits beat git), so reading it first clears the gate. A sibling over-deny gate (invariant 10): per-call `[config-overwrite-ok: <reason>]` token, the `config_overwrite_gate_enabled` kill-switch (`t3 <overlay> gate config-overwrite disable`, a `self_rescue` allowlist entry), and the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` chain (crash-proof — fails OPEN when `teatree` is unimportable, never tracebacks).

**Main-clone working-tree guard ([#2836](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2836)).** `core/gates/main_clone_guard` (pure git-command classifier) + `hooks/scripts/main_clone_guard.handle_block_main_clone_mutation` (the PreToolUse hook) deny a working-tree mutation of any REGISTERED (teatree-managed) main clone — a primary `.git`-*dir* checkout, distinguished from a linked `.git`-*file* worktree by the shared `running_from_worktree` helper. Blocked: an `Edit`/`Write` to a path under it, or a `git checkout`/`switch` to a NON-default branch / `reset --hard` / `restore` / `stash pop` in a main-clone cwd. ALLOWED (never locked out): `git fetch`, `git pull --ff-only`, `git checkout <default>`, `git worktree add/remove/prune/list`, and all read-only git — so `t3 update` and worktree creation keep working. The deny is UNIVERSAL (even the owner branches a worktree) and FAIL-LOUD, pointing at branching a worktree off origin/main. The managed-repo / worktree-vs-clone path toolkit (`resolve_branch_and_root`, `repo_root_is_teatree_managed`, `is_agent_state_path`, `default_branch`, …) is shared with `handle_protect_default_branch` via the `hooks/scripts/managed_repo` sibling. Sibling over-deny gate (invariant 10): per-call `[main-clone-ok: <reason>]` token, the `main_clone_guard_gate_enabled` kill-switch, and the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` chain. Its companion `core/stale_clone_notice` makes the housekeeping self-update scanner and `t3 update` emit a DURABLE user-facing notice (a `BotPing`-backed bot→user DM, idempotent per clone/reason/HEAD) when they must skip a dirty/detached clone — naming the repo path + manual remediation, never auto-stashing/auto-checking-out to recover.

**Blank-sub-agent dispatch gate.** `hooks/scripts/general_purpose_agent_gate.handle_block_general_purpose_agent` (the PreToolUse hook, registered right after `handle_enforce_skill_loading`) refuses an `Agent`/`Task` dispatch whose `subagent_type` is `general-purpose` — or absent, which defaults to it — when the brief names a repo the harness manages. Such a sub-agent starts with ZERO context, so it rediscovers the stack from scratch and works outside the workflow; the rule shipped as an advisory `additionalContext` injection, read as a suggestion, and was overridden about a dozen times in one session before it became a gate. The refusal names the typed agents that carry the context (`t3:coder` / `t3:debugger` / `t3:tester` / `t3:e2e` / `t3:reviewer` / `t3:bughunter` / `t3:planner` / `t3:shipper`, or `Explore` for read-only fan-out) so the caller can re-dispatch immediately. WHICH repos count is overlay knowledge, read from `managed_repo.overlays_registry`'s repo slugs rather than named in core, so the module stays overlay-agnostic and an overlay declaring no repos disables the gate for itself; matching is word-boundary anchored on each slug and its path segments. Scoping is by EXACT tool name — `TaskCreate`/`TaskUpdate` carry a description and no `subagent_type`, so a prefix match would deny them and block task tracking. Sibling over-deny gate (invariant 10): per-call `[general-purpose-ok: <reason>]` token, the `general_purpose_agent_gate_enabled` kill-switch (`t3 <overlay> gate general-purpose disable`), an unreadable registry (no tokens → no deny), the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` chain, and every internal error fails OPEN.

**Silent-no-op guard for `glab mr create`.** `hooks/scripts/glab_stale_base_remote_guard.handle_block_glab_stale_base_remote` (the PreToolUse hook, registered right after `handle_validate_mr_metadata`) refuses a `glab mr create -R <slug>` that `glab` would answer with exit 0, no output and no MR: `glab` reserves the remote name `glab-base` for its own `-R` override and persists one into the cwd repository, so a later create aimed at a DIFFERENT project hits the stale override and bails silently. A must-fail call and a should-succeed call become the same empty result, which reads as a swallowed hook rather than a tool that gave up. It fires only on a real create (verb detected on the quote/heredoc-stripped skeleton) with an explicit `-R`/`--repo` target, run in a directory whose `glab-base` points elsewhere; a matching or absent remote, no `-R`, and a non-git cwd all ALLOW. Sibling over-deny gate (invariant 10): per-call `[glab-base-ok: <reason>]` token, the `glab_stale_base_remote_gate_enabled` kill-switch (`t3 <overlay> gate glab-base-remote disable`), the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` chain, and every internal error fails OPEN. Its sibling in posture is the MR-metadata gate's typed skip (`gate_result.GateSkipped` + `warn_gate_skipped`): a call the gate RECOGNISES but does not evaluate names itself on stderr, so only "not an MR mutation" and a clean PASS stay silent.

**Schema-readiness admission gate ([#3901](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3901)).** `SelfUpdateScanner` advances a live worker's HEAD on a cadence while applying the schema is a separate boot-time `init` step, so a merge that lands a migration left the process running code whose models the control DB did not carry. `core/schema_readiness` answers that in three values — `CURRENT` / `BEHIND` (named unapplied migrations) / `UNKNOWN` (the probe itself failed) — over the same `pending_migrations` the #869 pre-flight reads, memoised per alias for 60 s because the claim chokepoint consumes it. Both non-`CURRENT` states refuse admission at `claim_next_pending` and `_claimable_for_target`, exactly where `worker_quiescing` already gates: `UNKNOWN` cannot be laundered into a green light, and the never-lockout escape is the DB-home `schema_readiness_gate_enabled` kill switch (default ON), not a softer verdict. In-flight leases renew through a different path and are untouched, so the gate defers new work without killing a live agent. The paired write half is `loop/scanners/self_update_schema.reconcile_schema_after_pull`, run the moment a clone advances, with `retry_pending_reconcile` re-attempting it on the ticks where nothing advanced — HEAD has already moved, so without the retry a reconcile that failed once (a transient locked SQLite is enough) would park the claim gate until a human intervened. The retry is gated on the memoised verdict, so a `CURRENT` control DB never walks the graph for it.

**Process-freshness admission gate ([#4387](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4387)).** The MIRROR of the schema-readiness gate, and the destructive direction: a long-running role that imported its model classes before a migration landed keeps claiming, an agent runs to completion, and the INSERT that records the result omits the new `NOT NULL` column — so `Task.complete()`'s single `transaction.atomic()` rolls back and a run that finished is stored as `failed`. `core/process_freshness` freezes the `max_migration.txt` head of every installed app in `CoreConfig.ready()` (no DB access, no migration graph) and compares that FROZEN snapshot against the applied head now, memoised 60 s. It never re-reads disk: `MigrationLoader.load_disk()` reloads the migrations package, so a process that re-read it would see the files the reinstall drain fast-forwarded under it and report `CURRENT` precisely when it is broken. Only a proven skew refuses (`claim_admission_block_reason`, the one composition both claim sites call); an absent snapshot, a snapshot that froze no head, or an unreadable probe ADMITS and warns (never a green `CURRENT` record on a comparison that never happened, [#4411](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4411)), because a fail-closed UNKNOWN here has no self-heal path — unlike schema-readiness, which clears the moment `init` migrates. Each reading is PUBLISHED to `$DATA_DIR/process-freshness-<role>-<pid>.json`, and `gates/schema_guard.doctor_check_process_code_freshness` reads those records rather than measuring itself: the watchdog runs `t3 doctor check` in a fresh process whose own snapshot is always current, so a self-measuring check reports healthy exactly while the stale worker is destroying results. The paired schema-level backstop is `db_default=""` on `Task.owner_pid_namespace` / `LoopLease.owner_pid_namespace` ([#4379](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4379)) — the gate protects processes running the gate's own code, the DB default protects every writer including ones older than it.

**Stranded-quiescing self-repair ([#4359](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4359)).** The third term of the same admission composition is `worker_quiescing`, and unlike the two schema directions nothing self-clears it: `deploy/deploy.sh`'s stage-2 drain sets it and its stage-7 `resume_admission` clears it, so a convergence killed in between halts ALL admission while `t3 worker status` reports RUNNING, the loops tick green, and the outage is visible only by reading the flag. `cli/doctor/self_heal_quiescing` therefore CLEARS that gate rather than only printing about it — the same `set_worker_quiescing(value=False)` write the deploy would have done, confirmed by re-read so an env or file layer outranking the config store reports a FAIL instead of a claimed heal. Deadness is established from LIVENESS, never from the gate's age alone: `cli/doctor/deploy_liveness` answers `LIVE` / `GONE` / `UNKNOWN` from the deploy's own in-progress record (`<pid> <epoch>` written into the lock FILE under the flock — `/proc/locks` is pid-namespace-filtered, so the flock itself is invisible to the containerised doctor) corroborated by the host process table, and `GONE` requires BOTH to answer. What the venue cannot establish it refuses, falling back to the pre-existing report-only FAIL. Clearing this gate cannot admit work onto a mismatched control DB — the schema-readiness and process-freshness gates above are separate terms of `claim_admission_block_reason` and are untouched.

**Pre-merge migration-fork probe ([#995](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/995)).** Two branches each adding a migration off the same parent both pass per-branch CI (each graph is linear), then the post-merge `migrate` fails with `Conflicting migrations detected`. `core/migration_leaf_probe.sha_forks_migration_graph` predicts the merged tree via `git merge-tree --write-tree` (no worktree mutation), parses every migration's `dependencies`, and refuses at §17.4 `ticket clear` when any app's graph would have more than one leaf, with a `makemigrations --merge` hint. Fails open on an uncomputable merge-tree (the merge-time CI re-check backstops). **Commit-time layer ([#2277](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2277)):** `django-linear-migrations` records a per-app `max_migration.txt` sentinel; a fork rewrites it with a conflict marker surfaced by `manage.py check --tag models` and a pre-commit hook (`scripts/hooks/check_linear_migrations.py`), so the conflict shows at commit time.

**Destructive-reinstall headroom gate ([#4338](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4338)).** `uv tool install --editable … --reinstall` deletes the working tool venv before rebuilding it, so a filesystem that fills mid-build leaves neither install — measured: 391 MB free, `typer` present, `click` absent, every `t3` invocation dead at import and the worker crash-looping. `utils/install_headroom` answers "is there room?" for both venues that run it: `deploy/entrypoint.sh`'s boot install (`require_install_headroom`, the bash twin of the same 2048 MB default) and `self_update.reinstall_running_editable`, which returns `deferred=True` WITHOUT invoking the runner so the previous venv is untouched. `loop/self_update_reinstall` maps that to `DrainOutcome.DEFERRED` and leaves the `PendingReinstall` row PENDING — marking it failed would let one transient low-disk moment disarm self-update for good. Fail CLOSED on a measured shortfall (proceeding is irreversible), OPEN on an unmeasurable filesystem (an absent reading is not evidence of no room). The boot install is then VERIFIED: `t3 --help` runs the whole import chain, and a CLI that cannot start fails init where it happened instead of surfacing downstream as an unrelated traceback.

**Fix-ticket FixRecord DoD gate ([#1661](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1661)).** `Ticket.kind` is `feature` (default) or `fix`. For `kind=fix`, `mark_delivered` (RETROSPECTED → DELIVERED) runs `check_fix_record_dod`: refused (`FixRecordDodError`) unless `Ticket.extra['fix_record']` carries `root_cause`, `evidence`, `regression_test`, `observed_red`, and `recurrence_fingerprint` — done = root cause + verified, not a merged manifestation patch. Hatch `fix_record_override` with a reason; `feature` tickets pass. **The positive path ([#4520](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4520)).** That artifact is written from the agent result envelope, not a self-attesting CLI (rejected on `repro_waiver`'s own reasoning): the fixing agent returns a `fix_record` object, and `agents/fix_record_recorder.record_returned_fix_record` — chained into `attempt_recorder._record_returned_envelopes` beside the review/critic/directive recorders — validates it and writes it through `ticket.merge_extra`. One definition feeds every side: `core/models/types.FixRecord` yields `FIX_RECORD_FIELDS`, from which the gate's required set, the envelope JSON schema, the recorder's validator and the fix-ticket brief block (`phase_blocks`, injected on `coding`/`debugging` for `kind=fix` only) all derive. Compatibility on a cross-overlay schema is deliberate and asymmetric: an **absent** record is a no-op — an overlay that never emits one keeps today's behaviour and still reaches DELIVERED via the override — while a **malformed** one is refused at attempt-record time with `envelope_refusal.MALFORMED_FIX_RECORD_PREFIX`, so it classifies as a recorder refusal and earns `transient_requeue`'s one-shot corrective retry rather than being silently dropped. `fix_record`/`fix_record_override` are declared in `TicketExtra` because `validated_ticket_extra` strips undeclared keys on every `_extra()` write-back, so an undeclared record would not survive `Ticket.test()` on the way to the gate. The exception beside that route is `t3 <overlay> ticket fix-record-override <id> --reason '<why>'` (`_close_commands`, writing through `Ticket.record_fix_record_override`) — the gate's refusal has prescribed that command since #1661 while nothing implemented it, so its documented exception was unreachable until now.

**Forced-repro gate ([#118](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/118)).** The *executed* upgrade of `fix_record_dod`'s self-attested `observed_red` field: for `kind=fix`, `ship()` runs `core/gates/repro_gate.check_forced_repro` (registered via `register_gate`, invoked right after `local_e2e_dod`). Refused (`ForcedReproGateError`) unless `ReproEvidence.objects.has_valid_repro(ticket)` — a harness-recorded RED→GREEN pair with `provenance_ok=True` — or a `ReproWaiver` exists. The anti-fabrication proof lives in the guarded `ReproEvidence.record_red`/`record_green` factories: the harness (`t3 <overlay> repro record-red|record-green`) stamps both SHAs from `git rev-parse HEAD` and runs the command itself, so a RED must actually fail (exit ≠ 0), a GREEN must actually pass (exit 0), and `record_green` refuses unless the RED tree is a **proper ancestor** of the GREEN tree (`merge-base --is-ancestor red green` with `red != green`) — a RED captured at/after the fix proves nothing. The ancestry result is frozen into `provenance_ok` at record time so the gate is a pure DB read. It sits in `ship()` (not `mark_delivered`) because the record commands need the live worktree, which `mark_merged` tears down. The `ReproWaiver` escape is **human-authorized** (`ReproWaiver.record` refuses a maker/coding-agent/loop `approver_id`), deliberately stronger than `fix_record_override`'s self-authored reason — a self-waivable repro gate would defeat itself. Ships DARK behind `require_executed_repro` (default OFF → a total no-op at `ship()`); the operator's kill-switch is flipping it back off; `feature` tickets never fire.

**Per-ticket spec-coverage DoD gate ([#2232](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2232)).** The same `mark_delivered` chokepoint runs `check_spec_coverage` after the fix-DoD check. Opt-in `require_spec_coverage` (default NO-OP); when on, every acceptance criterion in `Ticket.extra['spec_coverage']` must name a backing test, else `SpecCoverageDodError` naming the uncovered ACs. A MISSING manifest is itself a block (zero proven ACs is the partial-subset claim), so the gate is only satisfiable because a writer exists: `t3 <overlay> ticket record-spec-coverage <id> --ac '<label>=<test>,…'` (`_spec_coverage_commands`, upserting by label through the locked `merge_extra`), with `--override-reason` stamping the `spec_coverage_override` hatch. Registered DARK in `FEATURE_FLAGS` tracking #2232: automatic AC extraction from the spec is still a follow-up, so every AC is entered by hand.

**Review-state FSM chain (PR-08).** Four gates around the review-request/close/review flow, each opt-in or threshold-scoped so a normal reviewed-and-cleared PR still proceeds, backed by the new `ReviewEvidence` model (two kinds `cold_review`/`integration_review`; guarded factory with maker≠checker + full-SHA validation; recorded via `review record-evidence`). (a) **Review-request REVIEWED-state gate** (`core/gates/review_request_state_gate`): `review_request_post` refuses a broadcast unless the ticket has passed the `REVIEWED` milestone (`ticket_review_state.has_passed_review` — `REVIEWED` or any later maker state; the broadcast fires at `request_review` time so a progressed ticket is `SHIPPED`/`IN_REVIEW`, not the momentary `REVIEWED`, when it goes out — PR-08b) with a review-evidence artifact — a `ReviewEvidence` cold-review row OR an existing `ReviewVerdict` from the cold-review step (so the artifact stays recordable by that step). Pre-review states are still refused. Opt-in `require_reviewed_state_for_review_request` (default NO-OP). (b) **Cross-repo integration-review DoD gate** (`core/gates/integration_review_gate`): the same `mark_delivered` chokepoint runs `check_integration_review` — a ticket touching ≥ 2 repos cannot close without a `ReviewEvidence` integration review covering the combined changeset (`IntegrationReviewError`). Opt-in `require_integration_review`; single-repo tickets never fire; hatch `integration_review_override`. (c) **No-bulk-close gate** (`core/gates/bulk_close_gate` + `ticket bulk-close`): a single close action over `bulk_close_threshold` (default 5) tickets is refused without an explicit per-item confirmation token — the deterministic replacement for the backlog-sweep prose ask-gate. (d) **Review-shape gate** (`cli/review/inline_shape_gate`, in the pre-publish chain): refuse an MR-level draft note when inline drafts already exist on the same MR (stay inline once inline); `--force-general` escape.

**Merge-evidence FSM gate (self-catch, work-tracking-truth).** The terminal `MERGED` state is unreachable without real merged-SHA evidence. `Ticket.mark_merged()` and `reconcile_merged()` each run `core/gates/merge_evidence_gate.check_merge_evidence` (registered via `register_gate`, invoked exactly as `ship()` invokes `local_e2e_dod`): the transition is refused (`NoMergeEvidenceError`) unless a `MergeAudit` row with a real `merged_sha` exists for the ticket's PR, OR the overlay's live forge merge-state probe (`fetch_pr_merge_state`) confirms the PR merged. This kills the believe-done-not-done class — the ungated `_advance_ticket` walk (and the loop's `complete_ticket` action) could previously mark a committed-and-tested-but-unpushed ticket done. **Fail-closed:** an inconclusive/erroring forge probe yields no evidence, never a false pass. **Never over-blocks the keystone:** `merge.execution.record_merge_and_advance` writes the `MergeAudit` row atomically BEFORE calling `reconcile_merged()` in the same transaction, so an authorised merge always passes. **Never-wedge:** the forge fallback passes a genuinely-merged PR whose `MergeAudit` row is absent (a manual merge), and the `require_merge_evidence` kill-switch (per-overlay overridable, default OFF; enabled for the teatree overlay) is the operator's audited escape if a forge outage would otherwise wedge a ticket.

**Plan-adequacy + late-bound-plan gate (self-catch, SELFCATCH-3).** Forecloses the named root cause of the 26-bug integration campaign: thin-spec-as-plan and stale-base coding. `PlanArtifact` gains `base_sha` (the target HEAD the plan was authored against) and an `adequacy` four-section manifest (`design`, `integration_seams`, `edge_cases`, `test_strategy`; each section substantive OR an explicit reasoned negative — the proven `no_new_tests` shape, so silence never passes; pure validators in the leaf `models/plan_adequacy`). Under opt-in `require_plan_adequacy` (default NO-OP; strict-bool, `OVERLAY_OVERRIDABLE` + `FEATURE_FLAGS` DARK), `PlanArtifact.record()` refuses a new row without a 40-char `base_sha` and a complete manifest — a scope+acceptance thin spec structurally fails (the audited `record_bypass()` sibling writes an all-negatives manifest so `plan()` still advances). `core/gates/plan_currency_gate.check_plan_current` (registered via `register_gate`) runs on `Ticket.code()` (PLANNED→CODED) AND at the top of `schedule_coding()` — covering the **normal author PLANNED→CODED flow** (not corrective/synthetic re-entry: the loop's `_handle_red_card` / `_handle_skill_drift` synthetic tickets and the review-loop author leg mint a `coding` task directly and are intentionally exempt, since those tickets carry no plan of their own). **Stale-is-absent** — a plan whose `base_sha` moved off the live target HEAD AND whose intervening commits touch a declared seam (`git log base_sha..HEAD -- <seam paths>` via `branch_currency`'s fetch machinery) is treated as ABSENT (`NoCurrentPlanError`). **Never-lockout:** the error names the exact remediation, distinct per case — a STALE-but-adequate plan reaffirms with `plan-reaffirm <id> --base-sha <new> --disposition <…>` (carries the adequate manifest forward, one disposition per intervening seam commit); an INADEQUATE/legacy plan supplies a fresh manifest with `plan-reaffirm <id> --base-sha <new> --adequacy-json '<…>'` (carrying-forward an inadequate manifest cannot make it adequate); the `require_plan_adequacy` kill-switch, `trivial-skip`, and `plan-bypass` escapes stay. **Fail-open** on any inconclusive git probe (no worktree, offline fetch, unresolvable range). Declared seams are plumbed into the coding-task prompt + review context (data only).

**Critic gate — autonomous user-proxy critic (self-catch, SELFCATCH-5).** The unifying runtime of the self-catching layer: at the final done-claim `Ticket.mark_delivered()` (RETROSPECTED→DELIVERED, beside `fix_record_dod`/`spec_coverage`/`integration_review`), `core/gates/critic_gate.check_critic` (registered via `register_gate`) walks the seeded rubric `core/critic_rubric.CRITIC_RUBRIC` and records a `CriticFinding` per failing item. The rubric is the frozen-dataclass + dotted-path-resolve + registry-walk-test idiom of `chokepoint_registry`, seeded with the 8 classes the human had to point out this session, in **two honestly-distinct kinds** — do NOT claim all 8 are deterministic predicates:

- **3 DETERMINISTIC blocking teeth** — `spec_not_plan`, `done_not_done`, `completeness`: a pure predicate over REAL artifacts that REUSES its sibling gate (`plan_currency`/`plan_adequacy`, `merge_evidence_gate.has_merge_evidence` + `ticket_worktree_checks`, `spec_coverage_gate` — no-manifest is a FAIL, matching `check_spec_coverage`) and fires on ABSENCE. These are the ONLY items that can block. No LLM in the blocking path.
- **5 LLM advisory items** — `coherence`, `duplication`, `deferred`, `ignored_input`, `unenforced_guarantee`: determinism cannot read them, so (when live) the gate ENQUEUES a headless critic on its OWN `critic_reviewing` phase (`CriticDispatch.enqueue`, a clone of `auto_review_dispatch`) that judges them against the REAL delivered artifacts (plan text, the PR diff, the intake `AttachmentManifest`) and RETURNS a `critic_verdict` envelope. The dedicated phase is load-bearing: it carries its own `PHASE_REQUIRED_EVIDENCE["critic_reviewing"] = ("critic_verdict",)` + `_TOOLS_BY_PHASE` (read-only+web) + `SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE` entry, so `attempt_recorder.record_result_envelope` measures the critic result against the critic evidence contract and reaches `record_returned_critic_verdict` — under the reviewing phase a critic result (only `critic_verdict`) would fail `check_evidence("reviewing")` and never record. It records the `CriticVerdict` server-side (maker≠checker — `grader_identity` refused if a maker/loop role, mirroring `ReviewVerdict`), then mirrors the verdict's FAIL items into `CriticFinding` — ADVISORY, never blocking. They do NOT read any self-declared `extra` key.

**Tri-state posture, cost-safe while off** behind the `critic_gate_mode` DARK feature flag (`off|advisory|blocking`, `OVERLAY_OVERRIDABLE` + `FEATURE_FLAGS`, #104): the cheap deterministic findings are recorded on every delivery (advisory evidence), but the mode gates BOTH the BLOCKING raise AND the EXPENSIVE async LLM dispatch. `off` (default) arms no `claude -p` critic (no Session/Task/CriticDispatch created), so a customer overlay that never opts in incurs no recurring token spend; `advisory` arms the async critic and records its `CriticVerdict` rows but never blocks (the posture that accumulates critic-liveness evidence pre-enablement); `blocking` refuses a blocking deterministic finding (`CriticGateError`, ticket stays RETROSPECTED). Enablement is per-overlay, the teatree/dogfood overlay first (like `require_merge_evidence`/`require_plan_adequacy`). **Enforcing-mode rollback safety:** `mark_delivered` runs inside `transaction.atomic()`, so the block's raise would roll back the findings — `CriticGateError` carries the specs and `execute_retrospect` re-records them on a fresh transaction after the atomic unwinds. **Anti-theater:** a predicate that raises OR an uncited LLM pass is recorded as `instrumentation_gap` (a FAIL, never a silent pass); a now-clean item's stale finding is deleted so the row set is the latest verdict. Dropping enforcement back to `advisory` (recording continues) is the never-lockout escape. Deferred (tickets filed): the `plan()`/`review()` bindings, the dream-promotion of RedCardSignals into rubric candidates, and the `critic_catch_rate` rubber-stamp signal.

**Reviewing-phase review-skill evidence gate ([#1539](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1539)).** When `review_skill` is set, recording the `reviewing` attestation is refused (`ReviewSkillEvidenceError`) unless `Ticket.extra['review_skill_run']` attests the skill ran (`lifecycle record-review-skill-run`). Empty (default) is a NO-OP. Distinct from the `architectural_review_skill` cadence scanner. Both names must resolve to an installed skill for the gate/cadence to do anything, so `t3 doctor` (`_check_configured_review_skills`, [#3352](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3352)) resolves the effective `review_skill` / `architectural_review_skill` against the canonical skill set (`ref_validator.canonical_skill_names`) and hard-FAILs on any configured name that resolves to no `SKILL.md` — the `ac-reviewing-skills` → `ac-reviewing-codebase` incident class caught at the live config site, not just the `.teatree-skills.yml` / `agents/*.md` sites `validate-skill-refs` covers.

**Declared-dependency provisioning gate ([#3652](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3652)).** The general form of the check above, and epic [#3445](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3445)'s acceptance principle: a dependency the configuration declares REQUIRED but nothing provisioned must be a loud `t3 doctor` FAIL, never silence. `teatree.provisioning.declared` enumerates the mandate from the declaration surfaces themselves — `apm.yml` `dependencies.apm` (mandated skills), `pyproject.toml` `[tool.teatree.provisioning].required_binaries` (required tools), `~/.claude/settings.json` `enabledPlugins` (enabled integrations) — so declaring a new mandate is the whole change and a checker-local list can never drift behind it. `teatree.provisioning.probes` answers provisioned-ness against the surface the consumer really reads (an installed `SKILL.md` on a skill search dir, never the eval-fixture corpus; a binary on PATH; a plugin whose registry entry resolves), and `_check_declared_dependencies_provisioned` hard-FAILs each gap with the dependency, its declaring surface, and the remediation — the runnable `apm install <spec>` line built from the manifest's own pinned spec (`declared.skill_remediation`), so the fix is pasteable rather than a shape the reader still has to resolve back to a source. Surfaces are enumerated independently, so one unreadable surface neither hides another's mandates nor passes as "nothing is mandated" — it is reported as its own unverified-surface WARN. The provisioning half is `t3 setup`'s `MandatedSkillProvisioner`, which installs each declared-but-absent skill from the source its declaration names, idempotently. **The mandated skill set is `architecture-design` plus the three `souliane/skills` companions `ac-reviewing-codebase` / `ac-python` / `ac-django`.** Only `architecture-design` is carried in this repo's `skills/` tree; the companions are owned elsewhere and arrive solely through the declaration, because the plugin-first install path means an in-tree copy would satisfy the mandate locally and hide an unreachable source. `ac-reviewing-codebase` is the load-bearing one — it is the `architectural_review_skill` default, so a box without it dispatches its periodic architectural review with no skill guidance at all, which is precisely what the gate keeps loud.

**Skill-supply gates.** Two failures the readers above cannot see, because both live on surfaces they do not enumerate. `_check_dispatched_overlay_skills` covers ABSENCE at the dispatch site: an overlay's `stage_skills` / `companion_skills` / `pr_review_companion` name skills the lifecycle loads on every ticket reaching the phase, and one that resolves to no `SKILL.md` loads nothing and says nothing — the phase proceeds without the discipline the skill carried. Enumeration comes from the overlay config itself, so a skill wired in later is gated with no change to the check. `_check_skill_source_drift` covers STALENESS: skills install as dereferenced physical COPIES, and a copy cannot announce that its source moved on, so a merged fix reaches nobody until each person separately re-installs. `teatree.provisioning.skill_drift` compares installed bytes against the source clone's reviewed ref OFFLINE (`git ls-tree` / `git grep` read a ref directly — no fetch, no network). Both hard-FAIL inside `_check_enabled_but_unprovisioned`.

**Skill-pin freshness.** The third staleness question, and the only one the two gates above structurally cannot ask: they compare an install against the PIN, so a fresh install at a months-old pin is clean by both while work merged after that commit reaches no consumer at all. `teatree.provisioning.skill_pin` compares the PIN ITSELF against its source's current head — the pins read from `declared.skills_declared_in_apm_manifest` (never a checker-local list), one `git ls-remote --symref <url> HEAD` per source repo rather than a fetch or clone, and an entry carrying no `#<ref>` skipped because it declares no pin to bump. A source that cannot be reached — no network, no auth, rate-limited, no resolvable HEAD — is UNMEASURABLE, never current. The answer needs the network and `t3 doctor check` is the offline lane, so the work is split: `t3 setup`'s `SkillPinAuditor` takes the measurement, suggests each bump, and RECORDS the verdict with its timestamp, and `checks_skill_pins._check_skill_pin_freshness` reads that record offline. A recorded verdict ages, so past a 14-day horizon the finding becomes UNVERIFIED again rather than staying green — an absent, aged, or unmeasurable record all report themselves, and only a recent measurement finding every pin at head is silent. Unlike its two neighbours this **never gates**: a pin may be held deliberately, so a trailing pin is an INFO carrying the sha to move to and the pasteable `declared.skill_bump_remediation` line (the manifest entry first, then `apm install <spec>` at the new sha — fixing only the install would leave the mandate behind).

**Recommended (not mandated) skills ([#3668](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3668)).** The counterpart surface to the gate above: a skill teatree SUGGESTS but does not require. `teatree.provisioning.recommended` carries a curated `RecommendedSkill` list — each with a pinned `apm` source, a provider caveat, and a scope note — and `checks_recommendations._check_recommended_skills` surfaces every absent recommendation as a `t3 doctor` **INFO** that never gates the exit code (it is not installed by default and its absence is not a FAIL). The first entry is the agent-platform vendor's `claude-api` architecture skill: its guidance is load-bearing on Claude/Anthropic API primitives and its own frontmatter directs the reader to SKIP it for non-Claude providers, so it is offered — with an explicit Anthropic-specific caveat and the install command — only to operators running on Claude, rather than shipped by default onto a box that may run another provider. The overlap with the local `architecture-design` skill is resolved in that skill's `SKILL.md`: local is scoped to this repo's conventions, the vendor skill to provider-level agent architecture.

**Cold-hook settings readability gate ([#3499](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3499)).** The CLI and the cold hooks read the same `ConfigSetting` store through DIFFERENT interpreters: `t3` runs from a uv-tool venv that can import `teatree`, while the hooks run under whatever `hooks/scripts/run-hook.sh` selects off `PATH` (the first `python3.13|3.12|3.11|python3`), which on many hosts is a bare system interpreter with no teatree installed. When the hook-side reader cannot import `teatree`, every DB-home flag in `hooks/scripts/teatree_settings` degrades to its compiled-in default — so `t3 <overlay> gate <name> disable/enable` writes the DB and the gates never see it, and `autoload` reads OFF while the store says on. The failure is silent by construction: a value alone cannot distinguish "never opted in" from "cannot read the store", since both resolve to the caller's default. `read_cold_setting_status` therefore returns `(value, status)`, and two consumers branch on it — the SessionStart advisory (`hooks/scripts/engagement_advisory`) names the read failure and points at `t3 doctor check` instead of telling the operator to set a flag that may already be set, and `checks_cold_hooks._check_cold_hook_settings_readable` asks the HOOK's own interpreter (through `run-hook.sh`, since the interpreter SELECTION is half the fault) what it resolves, hard-FAILing on an unreadable store or on any CLI/hook disagreement. Fail-closed is unchanged throughout: an unreadable store still never auto-engages, and an unaskable probe WARNs rather than reddening the run.

**Review deep-retrieval constraint — path-independent.** Reviewing carries the same responsibility as implementing: a verdict from the diff alone is not a review. `Ticket.review_context_satisfied()` is a `django_fsm` condition on `review()` and `mark_reviewed_externally()`, so when `require_review_context` (default off) is on, every verdict path is refused until `review_context_gate.is_complete` accepts `Ticket.extra['review_context']` (the work item fetched from source, links followed, referenced docs analyzed against the diff). Stamped via `lifecycle record-review-context`; `mark_review_no_action` (bot/no-diff) is exempt.

**Anti-vacuity attestation gate ([#1829](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1829)).** `core/gates/anti_vacuity_gate` extends the §17.4 CLEAR machinery so a maker cannot request-review/merge an MR whose new regression test is *vacuous* (green even with the bug present). Opt-in `require_anti_vacuity_attestation` (default NO-OP); on, **merge** and **request review** refuse unless `Ticket.extra['anti_vacuity_attestation']` maps the diff to the acceptance criteria and proves each new test anti-vacuous (revert fix → RED) or claims `no_new_tests`. SHA-bound like `MergeClear.reviewed_sha`; stamped via `lifecycle record-anti-vacuity`. `autonomy = full` does NOT collapse it.

**Rubric→verifier done-gate ([#2241](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2241)).** `core/gates/rubric_gate` adds a sibling §17.4.3 merge precondition: a ticket's `Rubric` must be graded PASS by an INDEPENDENT verifier (≠ maker). Opt-in `require_rubric_verification` (default off), **fail-closed**. Full spec: [docs/blueprint/rubric-done-gate.md](docs/blueprint/rubric-done-gate.md).

**Falsifiable acceptance criteria ([#3762](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3762)).** The anti-vacuity rule raised from *tests* to *feature-level acceptance criteria*: a criterion with no state of the world that makes it FAIL is not a criterion. A silently-skipped implementation phase was certified by "the existing resolver test suite passes UNMODIFIED" — satisfied precisely by never touching the resolver, so the skip scored as a success. `quality/falsifiable_criteria` is the mechanical half of `skills/rules` § "An Acceptance Criterion That Cannot Fail Is Not a Criterion" ([#3759](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/pull/3759), the prose half): it detects the absence-satisfied shape ("X stays unchanged", "passes unmodified", "no changes to Y", "does not change") and `Rubric.populate` refuses a checklist whose criteria are ALL of that shape — the same reason it already refuses an empty one, since both pass the verifier vacuously. An absence criterion PAIRED with a positive one is accepted: the pairing is the rule, not a ban. Refusal is at population time, so it can never strand a merge.

**Review-verdict merge gate ([#2829](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2829)).** `merge.authorization.assert_review_verdict_gate` runs at the TOP of `execute_bound_merge` — the single chokepoint both autonomous merge paths (keystone CLEAR + solo-overlay bypass) cross before the forge squash PUT — so no merge reaches the forge without a non-stale, INDEPENDENT `merge_safe` `ReviewVerdict` at the live head. Always-on, **fail-closed**. The semantic is **newest-wins** (`ReviewVerdict.objects.effective_state_at`): the effective verdict is the most-recent non-stale verdict by `recorded_at` — a later `merge_safe` overrides an earlier HOLD, an even-later HOLD re-blocks. The head-SHA bind is free via `reviewed_sha` + `is_stale_at` (a force-push staleness-invalidates every prior verdict). The solo-sweep companion `has_independent_cold_review` shares the same predicate so it flags a HOLD-topped PR instead of diving into a refusal. The real `ticket clear` keystone records this `merge_safe` verdict as a by-product, so the keystone flow passes unchanged.

**Diff-scope gate on a blocking finding ([#4251](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4251)).** A cold reviewer can diff three-dot correctly and still take every RUNTIME measurement against the branch checkout, which reports what `main` did to a file since the branch was cut — a docs-only PR was held at high severity on `src/` code it does not touch, and every prescription was already on `main`, so applying them would have turned an `rc=0` merge into a conflict. `ReviewVerdict.record` therefore refuses a blocking-severity finding (`modelkit.diff_scope.BLOCKING_SEVERITIES`) citing a path outside the PR's own changed-file set, unless the reviewer attests a merge-result re-take. Both recording seams supply the set from `CodeHostQuery.pr_changed_paths` via `core.review.diff_scope_probe` — `t3 <overlay> review record --merge-result-retake` and the orchestrator's returned-envelope recorder (`review_verdict.merge_result_retake`) — and only when a finding is blocking AND cites a file, so a clean verdict pays no forge round-trip. The changed-file set is **three-valued**: an empty or sentinel-marked fetch is UNAVAILABLE, never a proven no-op diff (the rule `ci_rollup.attach_touched_paths` already reads), and an UNAVAILABLE set never fires the gate — "outside the diff", asserted without having read the diff, is the same unbacked claim the gate exists to stop. `t3 review merge-tree` is the one-step extract a re-take runs on: `git merge-tree --write-tree` + `git archive` into a PLAIN directory (never a git worktree — `resolve_data_dir` auto-isolates one onto a per-worktree DB), `git init`-ed with the source clone's real `origin` URL (`resolved_repo_slug` returns `""` for a local-path origin).
**A recorded HOLD is readable and reaches its PR ([#4476](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4476)).** A verdict's findings were persisted and rendered by nothing, so `findings_count: 4` stood in front of content only a database read could reach: the author could not fix what they could not read, and a later reviewer had to reach a fresh judgment rather than check the findings were addressed. `core/review/verdict_findings.py` is the strict read — a stored row that is not an object, or carries no summary, is REFUSED rather than dropped, closing the gap where `review status` counted the RAW JSON rows while `ReviewVerdict.structured_findings` silently discarded the malformed ones, so the count and the content could disagree. `--findings-json` refuses a non-object element for the same reason. Three surfaces read it: `t3 <overlay> review findings <pr-url>` (`--json`, `--sha`), `review status --json` (the record now carries `findings`), and the PR itself — `core/review/verdict_findings_publish.py` posts one comment through `send_proxy.route_forge_write` (public-repo leak scan + audit/allowlist) and then the on-behalf pre-gate, idempotent against a hidden per-verdict marker so a re-record never duplicates it. `review record` publishes as a by-product and `review publish-findings` retries or backfills. The publish action `post_review_findings` is deliberately NOT in the shipped `on_behalf_auto_actions` default — on a customer overlay a review critique under the user's name is a colleague voice, not self-documentation — so the shipped mode WITHHOLDS it, reports the block on the record result, and DMs the owner the findings, naming both solution-oriented exits (enable per overlay, or approve once). All five review leaves (`record`, `record-evidence`, `status`, `findings`, `publish-findings`) are registered in `core/factory/capabilities.CAPABILITIES`, so a front end reads the findings through the same declared `--json` contract as the rest of the machine seam rather than scraping `--help`. Discharging a hold needs no new state: verdicts are newest-wins, so a later `merge_safe` at the same head supersedes it.

**Per-MR review-dispatch lock ([#1405](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1405), `MRReviewLock`).** Keyed on `(slug, pr_id)` with an explicit state machine `idle -> review_dispatched -> resolved`, acquired atomically (get-or-create + CAS on state). Both review-dispatch paths — the manual `Agent()` spawn (`t3 <overlay> review lock-acquire <mr-url>`, run before spawning `t3:reviewer`) and the loop's `AutoReviewDispatch.enqueue` — acquire the lock before dispatching; a second dispatch attempt on an already-held lock is a deterministic no-op naming the holder. `merge.authorization.assert_no_active_review_lock` runs alongside `assert_review_verdict_gate` at the SAME `execute_bound_merge` chokepoint: a merge is refused while the lock is actively held (`review_dispatched`, not yet stale) even when a recorded `merge_safe` verdict already exists at the head — a concurrently-dispatched review could still be about to record a HOLD. `ReviewVerdict.record` resolves the lock as a by-product (merge_safe or hold, either way the review concluded) — refused only for a MISMATCHED holder (`resolve(..., holder=)`). Five code paths produce a `Task(phase="reviewing")` and only these two acquire the lock, so an unqualified release let a verdict from a lockless path (codex / self-PR review, the two ticket schedulers) free a lock a DIFFERENT, still-running reviewer held — reintroducing the very race #1405 exists to prevent. The holder check is anti-theft, not proof-of-ownership: a caller that NAMES a lock identity is taken at its word and cannot release another identity's lock, while a caller that names none releases. The asymmetry is load-bearing — the recorded holder is a DISPATCHER identity (`LOOP_SCANNER_HOLDER`, or a manual `lock-acquire --holder`) whereas the releaser is the REVIEWER that concluded, and a reviewer shelling `t3 <overlay> review record` cannot learn the dispatcher's; demanding a match would hold the lock for its full `deadline` on every CLI-recorded verdict, and a stranded lock is what leaves a PR unmergeable and escalating ([#3920](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3920)). A `deadline` set at acquire time makes a crashed reviewer's lock self-healing: it is treated as unlocked once its deadline passes, both by a fresh `acquire` and by the merge gate's consult; `MRReviewLock.reconcile_stale` is the explicit sweep that resets an expired row back to `idle`.

**Sync writers honor the same gate ([#1426](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1426)).** The DoD decision is factored into `dod_gate.sync_gate_allows` so every sync writer — not just `ship()` — respects it, closing the bypass where sync wrote a post-ship state directly. A **post-ship** state is at or past `SHIPPED`. A *workflow* state inferred from a live open PR is *capped* — a gate-refused state demotes to `STARTED`; a *terminal* state reflecting an external fact (merged PR → `MERGED`, board "Done" → `DELIVERED`) is **never demoted** but records a durable `dod_e2e_violation` audit marker and logs loudly, so the gap is auditable not silent.

**Concurrent-stack cap (#1397).** `max_concurrent_local_stacks` caps the distinct tickets whose worktrees can be in `services_up`/`ready` at once per overlay. The gate (`local_stack_gate.check_local_stack_limit`) runs before `Worktree.start_services()` and raises `LocalStackLimitExceededError` naming the blockers. Default `1` (a single in-flight stack, the headless-safe cap; `0` restores unbounded); sibling worktrees of one ticket count as one stack; each blocker is reconciled against docker first (a zero-container row is a phantom and excluded; a mid-restart row stays counted, fail-safe).

**Provisioning time-box + loud alert ([#2220](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2220)).** A long-blocking provisioning subprocess (DSLR restore, `migrate`, `--create-db`) must fail loud, never grind silently. `provision_timebox.run_timeboxed_step` bounds each step by a *fast* (`provision_fast_step_timeout_seconds`, default 120) or *heavy* (`provision_step_timeout_seconds`, default 1800) ceiling — `ProvisionStep.heavy` opts a step (a DB import, a frontend build) into the long one; every other step (symlinks, settings, a compose override) defaults fast, so a hang on an ordinary step surfaces in seconds instead of burning half an hour. On the ceiling it aborts and DMs the user, while a heartbeat distinguishes slow-but-moving from a hang. `detect_migration_conflict` matches a running `migrate`'s conflict output and surfaces the `makemigrations --merge` remedy. Best-effort.

**Parallel provisioning + resource-aware admission ([#2949](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2949)).** `workspace provision` runs a ticket's worktrees under a bounded subprocess pool (`_workspace_provision_parallel.run_worktree_provisions_in_parallel`) instead of one serial `for` loop — each worktree's provision runs as its OWN `python -m teatree worktree provision` subprocess, never in-process threads, because `WorktreeProvisionRunner`'s DB-import path mutates process-wide `os.environ`. The pool size defaults to an nCPU-derived cap (`teatree.utils.ram_probe.default_provision_concurrency`, halved core count) unless `provision_max_concurrency` pins one. Before each new subprocess is admitted, `provision_admission_gate.check_provision_admission` HOLDS it when host RAM is at/above `provision_ram_ceiling_percent` (default 85); a held request re-checks on the next poll and drains automatically once RAM frees, bounded by `max_hold_seconds` (default 600s) past which admission is overridden (logged loud) rather than blocking the CLI invocation forever. Within one worktree, `ProvisionStep` is a typed DAG node (PR-27): `requires`/`produces` tokens order the steps and steps with no dependency path between them run concurrently (the `subprocess_only` ones on a bounded thread pool, ORM steps serially in-process) via `run_provision_steps`; a `requires` token no step `produces` fails the run loud (fail-closed). `ProvisionStep.post_condition` is a truth check evaluated after the callable — a step that "ran" but did not produce its resource is recorded FAILED — and its aggregate (`teatree.core.provision.provision_postconditions.aggregate_provision_post_conditions`) is what `worktree status` evaluates to prove a worktree is really provisioned. `ProvisionStep.skip_probe` lets a step short-circuit to a near-zero `StepResult(skipped=True)` when its precondition is already satisfied. Every provision persists a `ProvisionReport` (steps + total duration + success) to `Worktree.extra['provision_report']` — no schema change — rendered by `workspace provision --report` and `worktree status`; a total exceeding `provision_slow_threshold_seconds` (default 600) fires the same best-effort user alert as a single step timeout.

**Wrong-repo worktree guard ([#2276](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2276)).** `find_clone_path` resolves a ticket's repo by basename, so a sibling clone of the same name (different `origin`) could be cut silently into the worktree. `WorktreeProvisioner._create` calls `git_guard.guard_repo_remote_slug` before `git worktree add` whenever `repo_name` is an `owner/repo` slug: a mismatch between the clone's `origin` slug and the expected slug raises (a missing `origin` fails closed). A bare basename carries no canonical slug, so the legitimate `--repos <basename>` flow is untouched.

**Per-repo branch provisioning ([#33](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/33)).** A ticket whose repos live on DIFFERENT branches (an unmerged multi-repo change — one repo on a feature branch, a sibling on another) maps each repo to its own branch in `Ticket.extra['branches']` (repo → branch); the `WorktreeProvisioner` (#2589) checks each repo out on its mapped branch while the ticket DIR stays the single `extra['branch']`, so the repos provision as SIBLINGS in one dir. The CLI surface is the `--repos` token: a `repo:branch` form (e.g. `--repos 'backend:fix/x,frontend:main'`) supplies the override — `resolve_repo_names` strips the suffix to the bare name and `parse_repo_branch_map` extracts the map from the same string; a bare `repo` token (no `:branch`) falls back to the shared ticket branch. The map merges across `workspace ticket` reruns so adding a sibling repo's branch never drops the others.

**One ticket, one workspace dir.** `core/worktree/ticket_workspace.ticket_workspace_dir` is the single predicate for "where do this ticket's worktrees live": the parent shared by every materialised `Worktree`, or `None` when the ticket has none yet or its existing ones already disagree. The provisioner reads it to co-locate a repo added later as a sibling; `adopt_worktree_for_ticket` reads it to REFUSE a checkout from a foreign root (`TicketWorkspaceDivergenceError`). A split ticket cannot resolve its own siblings, so overlay code scanning the worktree's parent dir generates a compose override with the sibling service missing — and provisioning reports success. Adoption also records the repo from the clone's leaf (`canonical_repo_root`), not the directory's name, so a hand-made `git worktree add` on a branch-named dir no longer stores the branch slug as `repo_path`.

**Idle-stack reaper + acquisition queue ([#2190](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2190), [#44](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/44)).** Four composed parts replace the hard `SystemExit` at the cap; all are global, mechanical-only, and individually kill-switched.

- **Reaper** (`IdleStackReaperScanner` → `reap_idle_stack`): demotes an IDLE running stack to `provisioned` via `stop_services` (reversible — DB + worktree preserved). Idle (`core.gates.idle_stack.preserve_reason`) = no live `Session`/`Task`, `last_used_at` past `idle_stack_idle_minutes`, not the CWD, no live external-delivery lease, no recent E2E run, not `reaper_pinned`; fail-safe ⇒ KEEP. `classify_running_worktrees` logs preserved-vs-reaped so a reap is never silent (#2227).
- **Queue**: at the cap `acquire_or_enqueue` reaps → retries → enqueues a `LocalStackQueueItem`; `LocalStackQueueDrainerScanner` re-checks the cap each tick with Fibonacci-minute backoff and re-fires `start` once a slot frees — never tearing down another ticket's stack.
- **Stale-stack reaper** ([#2207](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2207)): teatree-provisioned compose projects (named `<repo>-wt<ticket-pk>`) with no `Worktree` row, older than `stale_stack_min_age_minutes` (default off), are downed via `workspace reap-stale`; unknown age ⇒ KEEP. Ownership is gated in the shared candidate seam (`teatree.docker.reap._reapable_candidates`), so neither reaping flavour can select the deploy stack or an unrelated user project.
- **Safe disk reclaim** ([#2246](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2246)): `workspace reclaim-disk` runs only three zero-data-loss prunes (build cache + dangling-only images + unreferenced-only volumes) via a fixed argv, so `-a`/`system prune` can never enter the set — the sanctioned path so the agent stops hand-rolling raw `docker image prune -af`.
- **Checkout liveness is venue-relative; UNKNOWN never reaps** ([#3583](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3583), [#3912](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3912), [#3853](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3853)): a linked checkout records its admin dir as an ABSOLUTE path written by whatever execution context created it, so read from a context that reaches the clone elsewhere `git rev-parse` fails with the SAME wording it uses for a directory that never held a repository. `core/worktree/checkout_liveness.py` supplies the discriminator, and `probe_checkout` (`core/worktree/worktree_roots.py`) is the one probe the reapers, the setup-time warning and the `t3 doctor` worktree-health check share, so they can never disagree. It is three-valued: `NOT_A_CHECKOUT` demands positive proof — the dir carries no `.git` at all AND git agrees there is no repository, so no pointer exists that another context might resolve; `CHECKOUT` covers a checkout git resolved here and one the caller-supplied source clone still holds an admin entry for (proof of life resolved by NAME, which is context-free, rather than by an unusable absolute path) — that upgrade applies only when the CALLER can resolve the clone, and clone resolution is a separate pre-existing seam (`clone_paths.resolve_clone_path`) that answers `None` for a checkout whose stored clone path was written elsewhere, in which case the verdict stays UNKNOWN; everything else is `INCONCLUSIVE`, the UNKNOWN a fail-open pass keeps. The three values are verdicts on what a caller may DO, never claims about what it can RUN: a clone-vouched `CHECKOUT` still fails every git command run inside it, and `NOT_A_CHECKOUT` authorises releasing a registry ROW and never removing a directory. `_workspace/broken_worktrees.py` (run by `workspace clean-all`) therefore REPORTS unresolvable dirs and removes none — it needs no dry-run branch, since a preview and a live run emit the same lines. `core/worktree/worktree_roots.py` is also the single answer to "which roots hold teatree worktrees?" — the canonical per-overlay `worktree_root()` is the only root ever written to, while the SCANNED set additionally covers every root existing registered worktrees actually live in, so an alternate root stays visible rather than accumulating unseen (the scanned set is sampled BEFORE the row reaper runs, so a row it releases cannot take its root out of the sweep). The doctor check FAILs on a registered worktree PROVED never to have been a checkout, WARNs UNVERIFIED on an unresolvable one — naming no destructive remedy, because the same evidence is produced by live in-flight work — and WARNs on a namespace split across roots. The same evidence bar governs CREATION, not just reaping ([#3967](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3967)): provisioning's reconcile step used to clear its worktree slot on an INFERENCE — a directory the acting clone held no registration for was a partial leftover, so it was removed before `git worktree add` — and a checkout created in another context is unregistered here for exactly the reason its gitdir is unresolvable here, so a container-view actor re-created a live host worktree underneath its writer. `core/worktree/checkout_disposal.py` is the one seam every removal in that path asks first: it authorises disposal only on positive proof — the directory carries no `.git` of its own, or the acting clone vouches for it by admin-entry NAME — and refuses everything else with the operator's next action named, a gitdir naming a root absent here being reported as a VIEW MISMATCH rather than a dead checkout. Occupancy is the independent second signal, read from the same `Ticket.has_active_work` the reapers use, so a checkout the acting clone DOES vouch for is still never torn down out from under a live writer (the requesting ticket is excluded, so its own re-provision is never wedged by its own liveness). `tests/conformance/test_checkout_disposal_deciders_refuse_a_foreign_view.py` is the forward ratchet: every decider that can authorise removing or re-creating a checkout is driven against one real foreign-view checkout, so a new path that reads an unresolvable pointer as proof of death goes red there.
- **One owner for a dead registered checkout** ([#3583](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3583) follow-up): `core/worktree/broken_checkout.py`, consulted by `reap_done_worktree`, owns a registered `Worktree` whose dir is provably not a repo. It closes the deadlock where the row reaper kept the row (its dirt probe runs git INSIDE the dead dir, so it could only fail closed) while the broken-DIR pass skipped the same dir as row-tracked — making the doctor's prescribed `workspace clean-all` a structural no-op for 100% of what the doctor flagged. For a dead checkout the question moves off the unreadable dir onto the BRANCH in the source clone, where any recoverable git work actually lives. The source clone is resolved BEFORE the checkout is probed, since it is what separates a checkout that is live in another context from one that is dead everywhere. Release needs proof on both halves: `NOT_A_CHECKOUT` (never a probe that merely errored, and never a pointer this venue simply could not follow) AND the #706 standard on the branch — nothing that exists on no remote whose content is also not upstream. An unresolvable clone, an unreadable push state, or genuinely unrecoverable commits all KEEP the row and name why. The DIRECTORY is nobody's to remove: no evidence available to a single venue proves a checkout dead, so disposing of its files stays an explicit operator decision reached through `workspace salvage`.
- **A branch tracks its OWN remote ref, or nothing** ([#4225](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4225)): `git worktree add -b <branch> <path> origin/main` — the recipe this repo published for an ad-hoc worktree — makes git configure the new branch to track `origin/main`, so `branch.<branch>.merge` reads `refs/heads/main` (measured: 28 branches on one box, 20 of them at `main`). `push.default = upstream` would then aim a routine `git push` on any of them straight at `main`, leaving forge-side branch protection as the only remaining barrier; under git's unchosen `simple` default the same config instead makes `git push` refuse over the mismatched names, which is the live friction each agent has to diagnose. Conformance is deliberately two-valued — `utils/git_upstream.py` accepts the branch's own merge ref or NO upstream, and repairs a mistracked branch to whichever applies (its own ref when the remote holds one, otherwise untracked). Always-set is NOT the third option it looks like: pointing a never-pushed branch at its own absent ref makes git render it `[gone]`, the exact signal `_workspace/cleanup.prune_branches` force-deletes on, so the repair would shred unpushed work. `utils/git_worktree.worktree_add` normalises the branch it CREATES (the `autoSetupMerge = inherit` config makes even the no-start-point fallback inherit the default branch's upstream); an existing branch's config is the repair command's business, never a side effect of checking it out. `core/worktree/branch_upstream.py` sweeps every clone `clone_paths.known_clone_paths` names — the same enumeration `candidate_clones` uses — behind `t3 <overlay> workspace repair-branch-upstreams`, and `t3 doctor check` hard-FAILs on any remaining mismatch with the per-branch remedy named. FAIL rather than WARN on purpose: a box already carrying a score of benign WARNs cannot carry a single-layer push hazard as one more. Neither seam reaches the recipe an agent COPIES out of a skill, so the published text is its own surface: `quality/worktree_recipe_tracking.py` reds on any `git worktree add` under `skills/`/`docs/` that creates a branch from a remote start point without `--no-track` — line-scoped to the invocation, so a `-b` belonging to another command quoted in the same sentence cannot leak, with a `worktree-tracking: allow` pragma for prose quoting the defective shape deliberately.

---

## 5. Agent Execution

The agent layer is `teatree.agents` (headless executor + prompt + skill bundle + structured result schema) and `teatree.loop` (the /loop topology). The orchestrator-as-keystone contract is §17.8 — every implementation, review, test, debug, and ship action is dispatched to a sub-agent; the orchestrator's job is synthesis and dispatch, not execution.

**Headless executor (`src/teatree/agents/runner.py`).** Drives the agent in-process behind the `Harness` seam (`src/teatree/agents/harness.py`) — a REAL-environment run, with the resolved phase model, the worktree `cwd`, and the parent session via the SDK `resume=` option. The **transport switch (`agent_harness`, [#2565](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2565)).** `resolve_harness()` reads the DB-home `agent_harness` setting and returns the backend that opens the session behind the narrow `Harness`/`HarnessSession` protocols (`open` / `query` / `receive_response` / `interrupt`): `claude_sdk` (default — the `ClaudeSdkHarness` wrapping `ClaudeSDKClient`, byte-identical to before the seam) | `pydantic_ai` (the `PydanticAiHarness`, [#2885](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2885) — a `pydantic_ai.Agent` targeting OrcaRouter's BYOK, OpenAI-compatible, metered endpoint via `OpenAIChatModel`/`OpenAIProvider`; `PydanticAiHarnessSession` adapts the streamed output BACK into the same `claude_agent_sdk` `AssistantMessage`/`ResultMessage` vocabulary `ClaudeSdkHarness` yields, so `runner.py`'s driver never special-cases the transport). **Lane-B tool layer ([#2512](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2512)).** A PHASED `pydantic_ai` dispatch (`resolve_harness(task, phase=…)`, threaded from `run_agent`) wires the phase-scoped, gated tool/MCP layer (`agents/lane_b`) onto the `Agent` (`toolsets=`/`tool_timeout=`) — otherwise the un-phased harness stays text-only, byte-identical to before the port. Adopts pydantic_ai's OWN native primitives (`FunctionToolset`/`CombinedToolset`/`FilteredToolset`/`WrapperToolset`/`approval_required`/`MCPServerStdio`/`FunctionModel`): teatree-owned File System (read/write/edit/search, worktree-jailed against path traversal) and Shell (denylist/timeout, via `teatree.utils.run`) capabilities, phase-scoped by the shared `core/modelkit/phase_tools` least-privilege table (keyed on teatree's provider-neutral capability names, which each lane maps to its model-visible vocabulary at its own boundary: Lane B names its `FunctionToolset` tools with the skill/SDK vocabulary — `Bash`/`Read`/`Write`/`Edit`/`Grep` via `agents/lane_b/tool_names` — so a skill instruction saying `Bash`/`Read` names the actual tool, and Lane A's headless `_build_options` injects its COMPLEMENT as `ClaudeAgentOptions.disallowed_tools`, mapped from teatree's capability names to the SAME SDK tool names by `agents/sdk_tool_map` — on top of the unconditional external-contact FLOOR (`_runner_options._EXTERNAL_CONTACT_BUILTINS` — `AskUserQuestion`/`Monitor`/`PushNotification`/`RemoteTrigger`/`SendMessage`) denied on EVERY headless phase, so no headless agent can reach the user or an external endpoint via a built-in (the only sanctioned user-contact path is `needs_user_input` → `DeferredQuestion` → Slack DM; the floor names no capability tool, so a work phase keeps Read/Write/Edit/Bash, and the #116 reader denies the full `KNOWN_BUILTIN_TOOLS` superset) — so a verdict-producing review-phase dispatch (`phase_tools.VERDICT_REVIEW_PHASES` — `reviewing`/`codex_reviewing`/`codex_adversarial_reviewing`/`e2e_reviewing`, one shared grant so the entries cannot drift apart) keeps the shell it needs to cold-check-out the head and RECORD its verdict, while denying `Write`/`Edit` and the spawn tools so it terminates at that verdict, while a write phase's empty complement leaves the SDK options byte-identical); a `HardDenyToolset` consulting the SAME `core/gates/main_clone_guard` + `hooks/quote_scanner` functions Lane A's PreToolUse hook wraps, so a hard-deny (main-clone mutation, privacy leak) fires IDENTICALLY on both lanes; the native `approval_required` deferral for the soft ask-gate; teatree's read-only MCP server as an `MCPToolset` (guarded on the `fastmcp` extra, degrades to none when absent); and context compaction (`compact_history`) trimming `message_history` before each turn. `PydanticAiHarnessSession` maps each turn's `ToolCallPart`/`ToolReturnPart`/`RetryPromptPart` into the same `ToolUseBlock`/`ToolResultBlock` vocabulary the `claude_sdk` lane yields, so tool calls flow through the seam un-special-cased. `agent_harness` is paired with the Layer-2 credential setting **`agent_harness_provider`** ([#2887](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2887), `AgentHarnessProvider`: `subscription_oauth` | `api_key` | `openai_compatible`, the retired `orca_router_byok` spelling aliased on read), constrained by `AgentHarnessProvider.valid_for(harness)` — `claude_sdk` accepts `subscription_oauth`/`api_key`, `pydantic_ai` accepts `openai_compatible` and (#3157 E1b) `anthropic_api` — the latter drives a NATIVE Anthropic Messages-API binding (`PydanticAiBinding.NATIVE_ANTHROPIC`, `pydantic_ai_config.resolve_native_anthropic_model`, one branch in `_resolve_model`) instead of the OpenAI-compatible router client, so real `cache_control` is reachable. **Open harness registry ([#3157](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3157) E1).** The backend set is no longer a closed enum: `agents/harness_registry` holds a name→`HarnessSpec` registry populated from the `teatree.harnesses` entry-point group (an overlay ships a third transport with ZERO core edits) plus the two built-ins registered at import; `resolve_harness` looks the resolved name up in it and an unregistered name fails loud (`UnknownHarnessError`) at dispatch, not config parse. Each backend declares a typed `HarnessCapabilities` (`hooks`/`mcp`/`cache_control`/`server_resume`/`structured_output`, PLUS the dispatch-lane hints `spawns_cli_child`/`metered_lane` — #3157 AH-5), which `runner.py`'s dispatch reads through the protocol's typed `capabilities` attribute instead of `isinstance`- or untyped-`getattr`-branching on the concrete harness class. A `HarnessSpec` also carries the backend's `valid_providers`, consulted by `resolve_harness` to validate the configured `(agent_harness, agent_harness_provider)` pair (#3157 AH-6 — the open parallel of `AgentHarnessProvider.valid_for`, so an overlay backend's own provider constraint is enforced). Past the `open` boundary a provider-agnostic backend consumes the NEUTRAL `HarnessOptions` (`agents/harness_options`), adapted once from the vendor `ClaudeAgentOptions` at `open` (#3157 AH-2), so the vendor type never threads into pydantic_ai/Vertex logic; the boundary signature stays the vendor type until the deferred SDK port-surface migration. **Context/cache API ([#3157](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3157) E2).** `src/teatree/agents/context_plan.ContextPlan` is an ordered set of stability-tagged segments (`static`/`per_repo`/`per_task`/`volatile`) with cache boundaries; `cache_control_plan` maps each boundary to a `cache_control` breakpoint (≤4) with a TTL, and the cacheable head is byte-stable (no timestamps/uuids before the last breakpoint, test-enforced). The `ContextPlan` core type ships and is proven through the `teatree.overlay_sdk` surface (for the Factory to build against), but the harness-side application of the breakpoints onto a live request is DEFERRED to the Factory's native-binding option-builder (which will emit pydantic_ai `CachePoint` markers) — the never-fed harness passthrough was removed as dead (#3157 AH-1). `lane_b/compaction.CompactionPolicy` replaces the hardcoded head+tail trim (per-phase `keep_recent`, DB-configurable). `t3 cost` gains a per-lane/phase cache-hit ratio and flags price-table estimates distinctly from a reported figure (`TaskAttempt.cost_is_estimated`, the metered router's own reported cost passed through when present — #3157 E5). `agent_harness_provider` DEFAULTS TO UNSET (`None`): only an explicit operator pin forces eager credential resolution on a `ClaudeSdkHarness` dispatch — unset inherits the ambient env unchanged, preserving the `claude` CLI's own native auth. Only `ClaudeSdkHarness` spawns the bundled `claude` CLI child and needs an `agent_harness_provider`-keyed Anthropic credential — `PydanticAiHarness` resolves its own OpenAI-compatible credential lazily inside `open` (`teatree.llm.openai_compatible.resolve_openai_compatible_backend`, env `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_API_KEY`/`OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_BASE_URL` then `pass`; the retired `ORCA_ROUTER_*` vars are reported loud, never read), caught by the same "fail loud, record it" `CredentialError` contract. **Cached-resume parity ([#2886](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2886)).** `claude_sdk` resumes a parked headless run cheaply via the SDK's own `--resume <session_id>`; the provider-agnostic transport has no server-side session, so `teatree.agents.pydantic_ai_resume` re-homes the same continuation in the harness: on a `needs_user_input` PARK, the live `PydanticAiHarnessSession.history` (`list[ModelMessage]`) is JSON-dumped (`ModelMessagesTypeAdapter`) and stored durably under `Ticket.extra['pydantic_ai_threads']`, keyed by the parked `Task`'s own pk (single-use — a resume pops its entry) via the locked `Ticket.merge_extra`; `resolve_harness(task)` rehydrates it on resume by walking `task.parent_task` exactly like `_get_resume_session_id` does for `claude_sdk`, so both backends land on the same ancestor. Resending the rehydrated history is the whole prompt-cache mechanism (no manual `cache_control` — semantics differ per provider behind OrcaRouter and are opaque to teatree); a cache hit shows non-zero `cache_read_tokens` on the resuming `TaskAttempt`, a miss simply re-pays the context as ordinary input tokens, and the resume never refuses. A refusal BEFORE the harness genuinely opens — a budget breach (checked before `resolve_harness` runs at all) or a failed OrcaRouter credential resolution inside `open` — must never permanently destroy the popped ancestor thread: `resolve_harness` records the popped ancestor as `PydanticAiHarness.resume_source`, and `run_agent`'s `CredentialError` handler restores it via `persist_parked_thread` before returning the failure ([#2916](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2916)). **Regulated-path model allowlist ([#2887](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2887)).** A lane that carries regulated client/bank data (a future regulated lane) restricts inference to a compliance-vetted model set — an EU data-residency & regulatory-compliance requirement (GDPR, data residency, processor jurisdiction), NOT a model-origin question. `PydanticAiHarness._resolve_model` calls `src/teatree/agents/regulated_path.assert_model_allowed_on_regulated_path()` BEFORE credential resolution, so when the DB-home `enforce_regulated_path` setting is `True` (default `False` — the teatree factory lane carries no regulated data and runs unrestricted, incl. cheap open-source models) a model whose id is NOT on the explicit `regulated_path_model_allowlist` (a per-overlay, BYOK / residency-controlled allowlist) is refused as a config-policy violation. The gate is CLIENT-SIDE only (best-effort): with the default `orcarouter/teatree-factory` router handle the OrcaRouter SERVER-SIDE bandit can still route to a model not on the allowlist, so an operator needing a HARD regulated-path restriction must ALSO constrain the OrcaRouter dashboard (Allowed-models glob) or pin explicit model ids. **OrcaRouter dark call-site wiring (ships OFF; `agent_harness` default stays `claude_sdk`, so nothing routes to Orca until an overlay flips `agent_harness=pydantic_ai` AND the wallet is funded).** Four seams complete the binding: **(1) model-id normalization** — teatree's abstract tiers resolve (via `TIER_MODELS`) to Claude DASH-form ids that OrcaRouter's catalog does NOT carry (Orca serves PROVIDER-PREFIXED ids — `anthropic/<dotted-id>`, the open-source pool, named router handles), so `src/teatree/agents/model_tiering.resolve_pydantic_ai_model` normalizes a teatree-native id UP to the OrcaRouter router handle from the SEPARATE `PYDANTIC_AI_TIER_MODELS` catalog (all abstract tiers collapse to one handle — the router's own bandit does the mundane-vs-hard tiering; overridable per tier via the DB-home `agent_pydantic_ai_tier_models` map, and PER-OVERLAY via the DB-home `openai_compatible_model` setting so the per-overlay endpoint/model split is config/overlay-driven, not hardcoded — the override applies only to the normalize-UP branch, so an explicit Orca-native pin still wins), while an explicit Orca-native pin passes through and still hits `assert_model_allowed_on_regulated_path`; **(2) step cap (cheap-model guardrail #1)** — the DB-home `pydantic_ai_request_limit` (default 40 — a real turn budget above the ~16-request Lane-B reality, per-overlay overridable, `0` = uncapped, a positive caller `max_turns` winning over it) becomes a `pydantic_ai` `UsageLimits(request_limit=…)` on every `PydanticAiHarnessSession` run so a cheap-model maker cannot drift on a long tool loop (the FSM chunks work into phases + the orchestrator re-dispatches, so a per-run cap composes with orchestration); **(3) verifier pinned to Claude (cheap-model guardrail #2)** — `src/teatree/agents/model_tiering.PHASE_HARNESS`/`resolve_phase_harness` pin the VERIFICATION phases (`reviewing`/`requesting_review`/`testing`, `VERIFICATION_PHASES`) to `claude_sdk` regardless of the `agent_harness` setting — overridable per-phase via the DB-home `agent_phase_harness` key, an EMPTY override keeping the pinned defaults byte-identical — so when a MAKER phase rides a cheap open-source model the checker in the maker≠checker pipeline stays on the trusted Claude lane (the reliability backstop is a Claude verifier + the test-pass-before-merge keystone gate, never the cheap model checking its own work); **(4) `x-lane` header + key path** — the OrcaRouter provider is built from an `openai.AsyncOpenAI` client carrying a default `x-lane: <factory|eval|bulk>` header whose value is the DB-home `openai_compatible_lane` setting (`factory` default = the headless factory dispatch, `eval` = the eval CI job via `T3_OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_LANE=eval`, `bulk` = a secondary overlay's cheap bulk legs, which a routing rule keying on `headers["x-lane"] == "bulk"` picks up; informational until such a rule matches it), and the DB-home `openai_compatible_credential_entry` points teatree at an existing per-account `pass` entry with NO copy (`OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_API_KEY` env still wins). All five OpenAI-compatible DB-home call-site knobs (`openai_compatible_lane`, `openai_compatible_model`, `openai_compatible_base_url`, `pydantic_ai_request_limit`, `openai_compatible_credential_entry`, plus the `agent_harness`/`agent_harness_provider` pair) are resolved SYNCHRONOUSLY in `resolve_harness` (a `get_effective_settings` read from inside the async `open` fails safe to defaults under Django's async-unsafe guard) and threaded through `OpenAICompatibleLaneConfig`. That resolve reads at the dispatched TASK's OVERLAY scope (`get_effective_settings(task.ticket.overlay)`), NOT global/active-only — whether an overlay runs Lane B and its endpoint/credential/cap are per-overlay overridable and a headless dispatch runs per-task, so a per-overlay override for a non-active overlay applies (a task-less `resolve_harness()` keeps the active-overlay resolution).
