The pydantic_ai lane also carries a binding-AGNOSTIC per-request output ceiling — the DB-home `pydantic_ai_max_tokens` (default 16384, per-overlay overridable, `0` = the binding's own default) is resolved synchronously in `resolve_harness`, carried on `PydanticAiModelConfig` (NOT `OpenAICompatibleLaneConfig`, since it is not endpoint-specific), and merged by `build_model_settings` as the base `max_tokens` `ModelSettings` key on BOTH bindings (router AND native Anthropic); pydantic_ai's Anthropic binding otherwise defaults to 4096, which truncates a long result envelope mid-JSON, so a `'length'` finish reason on the final `ModelResponse` is surfaced by `PydanticAiHarnessSession.receive_response` as an error (`ResultMessage(subtype="error_max_tokens")`) rather than a silently-amputated success. **Enabling later is a config change, not code:** `t3 <overlay> config_setting set agent_harness pydantic_ai --overlay <name>` (+ `agent_harness_provider openai_compatible`), `openai_compatible_base_url` set (or `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_BASE_URL` in the worker env), and either `OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_API_KEY` exported or `openai_compatible_credential_entry` pointed at the `pass` entry. The teatree factory overlay keeps the `factory` lane; a secondary/regulated overlay flips to its own endpoint with `config_setting set openai_compatible_model <id> --overlay <name>` (+ `openai_compatible_lane bulk` for its cheap bulk legs); the eval CI job sets `T3_OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_LANE=eval` in its env. Reasoning EFFORT is HARNESS-SCOPED (`src/teatree/agents/model_tiering.HARNESS_EFFORT_SCALE`): the shared abstract-tier default (`xhigh`) is valid on both harnesses' vocabularies, but an operator `[agent.tier_effort]` override outside the ACTIVE harness's scale (e.g. `claude_sdk`-only `"max"` reaching a `pydantic_ai` spawn) is dropped, never forwarded. The typed `ResultMessage` maps to a `TaskAttempt` through the shared `attempt_recorder` chokepoint (#2204; the prior `claude -p` subprocess is gone). A heartbeat-driven `LoopWatchdog` interrupts the session on a turn/cost/runtime breach so an in-flight agent flushes its final status ([#997](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/997)); a per-ticket `TicketBudget` caps cost; a `ResultMessage(is_error=True)` naming a usage/weekly limit lands FAILED, never a silent success. **Sub-agent fan-out ceiling (`src/teatree/agents/subagent_ceiling.py`).** The watchdog bounds a run's DURATION, not its fan-out, so a delegation runaway is invisible to a wall clock. `_build_options` arms a per-dispatch `SpawnCeiling` as a `ClaudeAgentOptions` `PreToolUse` hook matched on the SDK tools the `dispatch_subtask` capability grants (derived from `src/teatree/agents/sdk_tool_map.CAPABILITY_TO_SDK_TOOLS`, never a spelled-out tool name), counting delegation calls for the run and refusing the ones past the DB-home `subagent_spawn_ceiling` (default 20, per-overlay overridable, `0` disables the gate — the watchdog's own `0 = disabled` convention). It refuses a SPAWN, never the run: the verdict is a `deny` on the single tool call, so sub-agents already running finish and the parent keeps its context and its remaining turns, with the refusal naming the ceiling and telling it to complete the work in its own loop. Every refusal is loud on four surfaces — the reason the agent reads, a `systemMessage` in the transcript, a `WARNING` log line, and a `refused`/`breached` counter on the instance. The hook is armed on EVERY phase rather than a maintained delegating-phase list; a phase without the grant already carries those tools in `disallowed_tools`, so it is inert there. **Envelope Stop gate (`src/teatree/agents/envelope_stop_gate.py`, [#3905](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3905)).** The brief teaching the result-envelope contract (`src/teatree/agents/envelope_contract.py`) is the primary control; this is the net under it. `_build_options` arms a per-dispatch `EnvelopeStopGate` as a `ClaudeAgentOptions` `Stop` hook (unioned with the spawn ceiling's `PreToolUse` entry — distinct event keys, so neither shadows the other) that refuses a turn-end whose assistant output carries no envelope the recorder would accept. Without it a run that ends without the envelope is discovered only by `attempt_recorder` — after the turn is over, the context gone, and the whole run re-dispatched from scratch; refusing at `Stop` turns that into one more turn in the same context. It is SATISFIABLE, never a filter: it reuses the recorder's own `runner_result.parse_result` + `result_schema.check_evidence`, so a turn it allows is a turn the recorder accepts and the two cannot disagree about what counts as an envelope, and the agent writing the real envelope is the only thing that clears it — nothing is relaxed and no result is manufactured. It is bounded and FAILS OPEN: at most the DB-home `envelope_stop_gate_refusals` (default 2, per-overlay overridable, `0` disables — the spawn/watchdog `0 = disabled` convention) refusals per run, never a re-refusal of a turn another `Stop` hook already blocked (`stop_hook_active`), inert on the phases `result_schema.ProseSummaryPolicy.accepted` lets end on prose, and "allow" on any unreadable transcript or unexpected error, because a gate that can wedge a dispatch is worse than the failure it guards. **Per-run turn ceiling (`agent_max_turns`, [#3890](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3890)).** Cache-read cost on this lane is `turns x context_size` and every turn re-reads the run's context, so the turn count is the multiplier on a dispatch's bill — a dimension the wall-clock `watchdog_max_runtime_seconds` cannot see and `watchdog_max_turns` reads only from COMPLETED `TaskAttempt` totals, never the run in flight. `_build_options` pins the DB-home `agent_max_turns` (default 250, per-overlay overridable, `0` leaves the spawn uncapped — the ceilings' shared escape-hatch convention) onto `ClaudeAgentOptions.max_turns`, the one place the SDK accepts it. The driver names it per dispatch (`headless._turn_ceiling`) rather than letting the builder resolve it unconditionally, because `max_turns` is not lane-neutral: the neutral `HarnessOptions` adapter reads a positive value as the CALLER's explicit cap and lets it win over a backend's own per-run limit, so handing this lane's ceiling to another backend would silently displace `pydantic_ai_request_limit`. A backend that spawns no `claude` CLI child keeps its own ceiling. A cap that silently truncates would trade a runaway for a stranded phase, so reaching it is loud on both surfaces the truncation class already uses: the CLI ends the run with `ResultMessage(subtype="error_max_turns")`, which `_outcome_failure` matches BEFORE the generic error-result branch and records FAILED under a reason naming the turns taken and the setting that moves the ceiling, and `src/teatree/agents/runner_truncation.alert_owner_max_turns_truncation` DMs the owner through the audited `OWNER_ESCALATION` egress — the same shape as the `max_tokens` truncation alert, so a ceiling is raised deliberately rather than a phase quietly returning less than the work.

**Phase dispatch.** A task whose `(role, phase)` has a registered agent (`Task.loop_dispatched`) is worked by that agent; a pair with none is free-form work the generic runner takes. Either way the run goes through `core.tasks.execute_task` → the `src/teatree/agents/runner.py` executor behind the `Harness` seam. WHICH transport and credential drive it is the two-layer pair `agent_harness`/`agent_harness_provider` ([#2887](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2887), see the executor paragraph above). `Task.dispatchable_q()` is the ONE filter every dispatch consumer builds on — the `orchestrate` planner's target + admit sweep and its in-flight budget count, and the live `claim-next`/`pending-spawn` in `loop_dispatch` — so the #2104 external-delivery exclusion and the role/phase set can never diverge across them. The `agents/attempt_recorder` applies one schema+evidence gate to every recorded attempt, whichever entry point produced it. Evals split the same way: `t3 eval run` defaults to `--backend transcript` (REUSE a recorded run by grading its on-disk transcript, $0 extra); CI passes `--backend api` to RUN the model fresh in-process via the Agent SDK, on the credential `agent_harness_provider` selects — DEFAULT the subscription OAuth token, with the metered `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` selectable per run via `t3 eval run --credential api_key` — and `--trials`/`--models` force the fresh-run runner. **Credential layer ([#2707](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2707)).** `teatree.llm.credentials` is THE canonical way to authenticate any Claude SDK / bundled-CLI invocation in teatree. A `Credential` resolves one secret from injected `CredentialSource`s (`EnvSource` → `PassSource`; env wins, else `pass`, else a loud `CredentialError` naming the fix), and `child_env(base)` returns a child process env with its own `env_var` set and every `conflicting_vars` entry STRIPPED — so an invocation can never silently fall back to a different credential. The concrete `AnthropicApiKeyCredential` (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, strips `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`) and `AnthropicSubscriptionCredential` (the inverse) are the two Claude credentials. **Forbidden vars (base-URL containment).** `child_env` has a second, security-bearing rule: `CredentialSpec.forbidden_vars` are RAISED on, not stripped. A stripped conflict is a fallback to remove; a forbidden var is an operator misconfiguration to surface. The concrete case is `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` on the subscription credential — both the bundled CLI and the Anthropic SDK read it natively off the inherited env, so an ambient value silently redirects a plan-authenticated child at a third-party endpoint, where plan auth is not valid. It stays legal on the metered key (a gateway, Bedrock/Vertex, an Anthropic-compatible provider on its OWN key), which is why it is a forbidden var of one credential rather than a conflict of the pair. Seams that pin no credential get the same refusal from the shared `reject_ambient_base_url_redirect()` chokepoint, wired into every AUTONOMOUS unpinned spawn (the headless dispatch's ambient default, the clean-room one-shot turn, the maker pane) and into the eval lane's `isolated_claude_env`, whose env is a copy of the parent's. `t3 agent` on its `-p` branch is also inside it: that exec replaces the process, but the child runs headless with nobody watching its auth, which is the condition the guard exists for. The `exec` spawns that leave a human in front of the child (bare interactive `t3 agent`, `t3 loop start`, the ttyd debug session) are deliberately outside it — the operator sees the child's own auth behaviour directly. **Unattended permission mode.** `src/teatree/agents/permission_modes.py` holds the modes teatree pins: headless dispatch, the `t3 loop start` session, and `t3 agent` on its `-p` branch all pin `UNATTENDED` (`bypassPermissions`), and the #116 quarantined reader pins `READER_DEFAULT_DENY` (`dontAsk`). The pins matter because `permissions.defaultMode` in `~/.claude/settings.json` is a single global key that every lane would otherwise inherit — `t3 doctor check` advises `auto` there for the session the operator drives, and without the pins that advice would classifier-gate the autonomous loop and every headless `t3 agent` run. Bare `t3 agent` with no task argument pins nothing on purpose: it execs an interactive `claude`, and that session's mode is the operator's to choose.

**Cached-prefix stability on the dispatch lane.** The `claude_sdk` lane sets `exclude_dynamic_sections=True` on its system-prompt preset, moving cwd / git-status / auto-memory churn out of the cached system prefix and into the first user message. Those sections change between dispatches in one worktree, and any change to the prefix invalidates the whole cache entry, so excluding them is what lets consecutive dispatches share a warm prefix. Teatree's OWN append is assembled the same way: `agents.prompt.build_system_context` orders it STABLE-FIRST — the fixed framing and the skill block lead, the per-task `Task ID` / `Ticket` / `# Prior Task Result` trail — so two dispatches in one phase share a byte-identical prefix at least as long as the skill block, matching what the eval lane already does with `SKILL_BUNDLE_FRAMING`. That append is ONE argv element bounded by `MAX_APPEND_BYTES`, and every production skill bundle is 1.7-2.3x that, so `agents.context_budget` truncates on every dispatch: it drops whole `##` sections off the tail and names them (skill-qualified) in the marker rather than keeping a byte prefix, because a byte cut lands mid-sentence and this lane is denied the Skill tool that would let the agent re-read what it lost. A block with no `##` section (a JSON survey, a prose summary) falls back to the byte prefix, so the argv bound holds unconditionally. `ClaudeAgentOptions` exposes no `cache_control` surface at all, so breakpoints and a 1h TTL are unreachable from teatree and prefix stability is the only lever available — `ContextPlan`'s cache-breakpoint passthrough stays dormant for that reason rather than by oversight. NO credential (these two plus the `pydantic_ai` `OrcaRouterCredential`) carries a built-in default `pass` path: each resolves from its env var or an explicitly-configured per-account entry (`anthropic_oauth_pass_paths` / `anthropic_api_key_pass_paths` / `openai_compatible_credential_entry`) and fails loud naming that setting when unconfigured, never a dead default. The pieces are named provider-neutral (`Credential`/`CredentialSpec`/`CredentialSource`) by design, so the layer later becomes an `LLMBackend.credential` (Claude today, other providers later) with no call-site rework; DI sources keep it unit-testable without touching the real env / `pass`. **Per-account routing (experimental).** A credential's `pass_path` is not fixed: `teatree.credential_config` reads a per-overlay ORDERED LIST of candidate `pass` entries (config keys `anthropic_oauth_pass_paths` / `anthropic_api_key_pass_paths`, overlay scope then global) and its `PassPathSelector` injects the first non-exhausted account's `pass_path` into the credential. Account health is a cached probe: the FOUNDATION reader `teatree.llm.rate_limits.read_rate_limits` does one tiny `POST /v1/messages` (haiku, `max_tokens=1`) and folds the account's unified 5h/7d rate-limit response headers (a 200 AND a 429 both carry them) into a token-free snapshot (a **metered API key** is probed differently — `read_api_key_status` with `x-api-key`, no `Bearer`/oauth beta: it emits no unified windows, so a funded 200 yields its per-minute `requests`/`tokens` remaining and an out-of-credits 400 "credit balance is too low" yields a depleted credit state mapped onto the same exhaustion signal so routing refuses a dead key — the exact prepaid $ balance is not available via a standard key); the DOMAIN `AnthropicTokenUsage` cache stores each account's utilization/status/reset keyed by `pass_path`, with `is_exhausted` = 5h≥95 % ∨ 7d≥99 % ∨ 7d rejected and a `valid_until` that keeps a healthy verdict for a short TTL and an exhausted one until its window resets. The selector is STICKY (an `AnthropicActivePick` pointer per `(kind, scope)`): the HOT path reuses a fresh, non-exhausted pick from the CACHE with NO network; it only re-probes on a cache miss/expiry, and only then falls back across overlays, raising `AllTokensExhaustedError` (a loud `CredentialError`) naming the earliest reset when every configured account is spent. `runner.py` passes the task's ticket overlay as `scope`; metered/eval consumers stay GLOBAL scope. The operator-facing **`t3 tokens`** reporter (`teatree.token_report`) is the read-side diagnostic over this same state: it enumerates every configured `pass` entry (the per-overlay OAuth + API-key lists plus global), reuses a fresh `AnthropicTokenUsage` cache row or probes once through the reader, and renders each account's health per credential kind — an OAuth row shows org id + 5h/weekly utilization + weekly reset + status (healthy / warning / exhausted / missing / unreachable); an API-key row shows its credit state (healthy when funded / out-of-credits when depleted) + per-minute requests/tokens remaining (not weekly utilization, and no $ balance) — never emitting a token. An ad-hoc token with no `pass` entry can be probed through the REPEATABLE `--token` option (`t3 tokens --token A --token B`): each renders one extra row labelled `token[1]`, `token[2]`, …, its kind auto-detected from the `sk-ant-oat01` / `sk-ant-api03` prefix, probed fresh and NEVER read from or written to the `pass_path`-keyed `AnthropicTokenUsage` cache. The row/JSON identity key is `account` (not `pass_path`) with a `source` (`pass` | `token`) discriminator; a token passed as a CLI option is visible in `ps aux` and shell history, inherent to the option interface. Its FIRST applied rule is the `agent_harness_provider`-selected eval-credential policy below. **Metered-lane auth + isolation ([#2884](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2884)).** The `sdk` lane authenticates on the credential `agent_harness_provider` selects — DEFAULT the subscription `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (no per-token bill, so the lane is right-sized — a single effort tier, a smaller trial count, per-account OAuth routing — to stay inside the plan's depleting 5h/7d usage window), with the metered `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` selectable per run via `t3 eval run --credential api_key` (e.g. for a lane that needs per-token cost accounting). The in-container run recovers the same credential kind from the ONE credential var the host forwards, so no credential knob travels into the container. `make_runner(API_BACKEND, …)` is the single resolution chokepoint (`resolve_eval_credential().export()` — fail loud before the runner exists, export the selected credential for the docker `-e` pass-through); `isolated_claude_env` **strips** the OTHER credential from the child env using the same credential's `spec.conflicting_vars` (the bundled CLI prefers the selected credential only when its conflicting one is absent, so stripping it is what stops the SDK authenticating with the wrong credential). The lane runs **in a container, never on the host** (`--docker` → `dev/Dockerfile.test`, which ships the `claude` CLI; the selected credential is forwarded via docker `-e` pass-through, never argv/logs). The runner and judge run **virgin** (`isolated_claude_env` + clean-room `ClaudeAgentOptions`: `setting_sources=[]`, empty `settings`, a neutral `HOME`/cwd) so the developer's `CLAUDE.md`, auto-memory, and project config never bias a result. **Prompt caching** stays on by the bundled CLI's content-based default (5-min TTL; the API-key switch does not disable it — confirmed via the `cache_creation_input_tokens`/`cache_read_input_tokens` usage fields the harness already surfaces). The stable `SKILL_BUNDLE_FRAMING` leads the system prompt and the polluted preamble lives in the USER prompt, so the cacheable prefix is already maximal; `ClaudeAgentOptions` exposes no 1h-TTL knob, so teatree injects none (no raw `cache_control` headers). **Sub-agent-spawning isolation ([#2642](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2642)).** A spawning scenario (`scenario_exposes_subagent_spawn` — `orchestrator_delegates_*`, `full_speed_*`, `team_mate_*`, `delegates_under_load_*`) makes the SDK sub-agent do REAL, destructive git work; a neutral cwd does NOT protect the developer's clone, because the sub-agent locates it via the editable install's `.pth` (`import teatree` → real `src/teatree`) and the shared `.git`. So `ApiInProcessRunner._resolve_eval_target` runs each spawning scenario against a per-run EPHEMERAL CHECKOUT (`teatree.eval.ephemeral_checkout`): a throwaway `git worktree --detach` whose own working tree + detached `HEAD` absorb every write, with `cwd` + `add_dirs` pointed at it and `ephemeral_checkout_env` overlaying `PYTHONPATH` (so `import teatree` resolves into the throwaway) and clearing the inherited `GIT_*` pins (so `git` rediscovers from the ephemeral cwd). The checkout is torn down after the run; a non-spawning scenario keeps the existing clean-room shape unchanged. When the real root cannot be located the spawning scenario REFUSES (`EphemeralCheckoutError`) rather than fall back to the real clone. **Transient missing CLI ([#2642](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2642)).** `shutil.which("claude")` momentarily returns `None` when the bundled CLI auto-updates mid-run (the nvm symlink is swapped); `resolve_claude_path` re-probes with a bounded backoff so one transient miss never reds the rest of the batch, while a genuinely-absent binary still fails after the bounded attempts. **All-skipped guard ([#1811](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1811)).** A run that never happened (`claude` absent/unauthenticated) would SKIP as passed; `--require-executed` exits non-zero when `collected > 0` but `executed == 0`. The standalone metered workflow (`.github/workflows/eval.yml`, weekly cron + manual) passes `--require-executed` unconditionally (never key-gated) and is no-PR-guarded so a window with nothing merged skips cleanly. **Freshest-account pre-eval selection.** When the CI secret `EVAL_OAUTH_TOKENS` holds several subscription tokens (newline-separated), a pre-eval step (`scripts/eval/select_oauth.py` over the Django-free `teatree.eval.oauth_selection`) probes each token's remaining 5h/7d headroom through the same `read_rate_limits` header probe, ranks them by binding-then-weighted headroom (mirroring `teatree.ci_oauth_switch`, deterministic first on a tie), and exports the freshest as a masked `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` into `$GITHUB_ENV` for that run — so a full fan-out never lands every leg on one throttled window. The step OWNS the credential decision (it also exports `T3_AGENT_HARNESS_PROVIDER`) and is backward-safe: an unset `EVAL_OAUTH_TOKENS` passes the single `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` secret through unchanged. When every candidate is exhausted the step fails loud, unless the opt-in `EVAL_API_KEY_FALLBACK` repo variable (default off) flips the run to the metered `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Wired into `eval.yml`, `eval-pr.yml`, `eval-nightly.yml`, and `eval-weekly-reusable.yml`. This is the per-run, in-CI complement to the host-side `t3 eval ci-account switch` (`teatree.ci_oauth_switch`), which points the single `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` secret at the healthiest `pass`-stored account ahead of time.

**Structured result schema (`src/teatree/agents/result_schema.py`).** Agents return JSON: `summary`, `files_modified`, `tests_run`, `tests_passed`, `tests_failed`, `decisions`, `needs_user_input`, `user_input_reason`, `next_steps`, `commands_executed`. `additionalProperties: false`. Validated without the `jsonschema` library to keep the dep tree small.

**Skill bundle (`src/teatree/agents/skill_bundle.py`) + delegation map (`skill_support/map.py`).** A phase → companion-skills map (e.g. `coding → test-driven-development`), topo-sorted `requires:` resolution, per-overlay `companion_skills`. Reviewer-dispatch review skills come from `OverlayConfig.get_review_companion_skills()` (default `code-review`); on the `reviewing` phase `build_system_context` embeds them in full, and an orchestrator-built reviewer dispatch goes through the single shared `build_reviewer_dispatch_prompt()`, which prepends a REQUIRED Skill-tool load block (`t3:review` + overlay review skills) so conventions reach the reviewer structurally, not via orchestrator memory. The **coding phase** force-loads a symmetric contract: a REQUIRED load block (`/t3:architecture-design`+`/t3:code`), `architecture-design` embedded in full, and behavior-preservation + no-AI-signature clauses, so the discipline reaches a skill-stripped builder. It also injects an explicit stack+overlay load block computed from the resolved bundle (framework skill `/ac-django`/`/ac-python`/`fastapi` plus the active overlay skill), so a code-touching dispatch never relies on auto-detect; an unresolved stack falls back to a "load your stack's coding skill yourself" instruction ([#1368](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1368)). **Per-stage overlay skills (`OverlayConfig.stage_skills`).** A per-phase map (keyed by canonical phase, DB-overridable like `required_third_party_services`, an unknown-phase key rejected loud by `phases.canonicalize_stage_skill_keys`) of ADDITIONAL skills appended LAST in `select_for_runtime_phase` (after base/overlay/review, before the `requires:` chain; first-wins dedupe keeps the base authoritative — additive, never replacing). `active_overlay_stage_skills(phase)` sources them from the ACTIVE overlay only, so a public-repo dispatch under the base overlay never sees a team overlay's map; for a no-Skill-tool maker they embed IN FULL (`src/teatree/agents/stage_skill_prompt.py`) with an ADDITIVE precedence line, and the embed/preamble search roots include `~/.claude/skills` (`skill_injection.harness_skills_dirs`) so a team skill body resolves for headless dispatch. The SKILL.md reading + rendering primitives (`_read_skill_contents{,_scoped}`, `_find_skill_md`) live in the Django-free `src/teatree/agents/skill_injection.py`, shared by `build_system_context` and a **raw-Agent-tool spawn** preamble: an orchestrator that spawns an e2e/coder/reviewer sub-agent with a bare prompt string bypasses every skill-injection path, so `skill_injection.build_subagent_skill_preamble` concatenates the requested skills' `SKILL.md` bodies (resolved across the framework AND active-overlay skills dirs) and `t3 <overlay> skill-preamble --skills t3:rules,t3:e2e[,<overlay-skill>]` emits it for the orchestrator to prepend to every brief (fails loud, non-zero, on an unresolved skill). `skills/rules/SKILL.md` § Sub-Agent Limitations makes the carry mandatory, pinned by `evals/scenarios/orchestrator_embeds_skills_in_subagent_brief.yaml`.

**Headless and interactive resolve the SAME skills, from the agent file ([#3667](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3667)).** A sub-agent's skills are declared once, in its `agents/*.md` frontmatter `skills:` list, and both dispatch lanes read that one declaration — so a phase can no longer load a different set headless than interactive. Three pieces by layer: `skill_support/agent_declarations.py` (platform, pure) reads the frontmatter list and knows nothing about phases or roles; `src/teatree/agents/phase_agent_skills.py` (domain) maps phase → dispatched agent file → declared skills through `SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE`, role-agnostically (no phase dispatches two different `t3:` agents across roles, and that is pinned rather than assumed; a `codex:` row names a slash-command agent with no agent file and resolves to nothing); and `SkillLoadingPolicy.select_for_runtime_phase` takes `agent_declared_skills`, authoritative when present. The `_PHASE_TO_SKILL` map is KEPT as the fallback for a phase with no agent file (`ticket-intake`, `scoping`, `retrospecting`) and still resolves `lifecycle_skill`, which drives the review-companion branch. The deliverable is the equivalence test (`test_phase_agent_skills.TestResolutionPathEquivalence`), parametrised over every `t3:` row in `SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE` with two assertions per phase — the headless bundle carries every declared skill, and the fallback map never names a skill the agent file omits — so either half turns red the moment the map and the frontmatter drift.

**Deterministic phases are non-agentic on BOTH headless lanes ([#3570](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3570)).** A few headless phases are fixed data transformations scheduled as `Task` rows only so a scanner need not run an LLM inline; dispatching one through the agentic path hands it the generic "work on ticket N, check git log, code, test" brief, which contradicts its least-privilege toolset — the brief demands a shell the phase is correctly denied, so the agent parks with `needs_user_input` (or narrates a result it has no path to persist) and the scanner's dedup then suppresses the ticket. `core/deterministic_phases.py` is the one registry (`PhaseRunner`, `deterministic_phase_runner`, `run_deterministic_phase`) and BOTH entry points consult it — the `tasks work-next` CLI lane and the django-tasks worker `core.tasks.execute_task` that the post_save auto-enqueue actually fires. Registration is INVERTED, mirroring `core/agent_runner.py`: a runner that drives a model turn lives in `teatree.agents` and registers itself at app-ready (`src/teatree/agents/ticket_short_description.py` for `short_describe`, the single writer of `Ticket.short_description`), so `core` never imports the higher layer.

**The single model constant — abstract tiers.** Every concrete Claude model id in teatree's PRODUCTION dispatch lives in exactly ONE place: `src/teatree/agents/model_tiering.TIER_MODELS` (abstract tier → the current concrete id, one entry per tier). Production phase dispatch, eval scenarios, the benchmark, the eval TRANSPORT lane, and the tests reference an abstract TIER (`frontier`/`balanced`/`cheap`) or DERIVE from `TIER_MODELS`, never a concrete id: the eval lane's family aliases (`opus`/`sonnet`/`haiku` → concrete id) come from `src/teatree/agents/model_aliases.family_alias_models()`, and its judge / fallback defaults index `TIER_MODELS[DEFAULT_TIER]`, so a tier bump can never desync the eval lane from the models it measures. The remaining allowlisted concrete-id pins are NON-dispatch capability pins (`llm/rate_limits.py`'s probe model, `core/autocompact_advisory.py`'s harness 1M-native set) plus historical usage-record parsing prose (`core/cost.py`), enforced both directions — across Python string literals AND the doc/workflow surfaces — by `tests/quality/test_no_hardcoded_model_ids.py`. `resolve_tier(tier)` reads `TIER_MODELS`, overridable per tier via a `tier_models` config setting (merged OVER the default, mirroring `skill_models`); the sibling DB-home key `agent_phase_harness` pins a per-phase harness override (default = the pinned `PHASE_HARNESS` defaults). Adopting a new model is one edit to `TIER_MODELS` or one config line — zero scenario, test, or dispatch edits. This is a pure in-memory constant lookup (no I/O, no DB) so the agent-spawn hot path stays trivially safe.

**Model tiering.** `src/teatree/agents/model_tiering.resolve_phase_model(phase)` resolves phase → tier → concrete model id. `DEFAULT_PHASE_MODELS` maps each FSM phase to a TIER: the genuine-reasoning phases (`planning`/`coding`/`debugging`/`reviewing`/`retrospecting`) → `frontier`; the mechanical-but-non-trivial phases (`testing`/`shipping`) → `balanced`; the pure-handoff phase (`requesting_review`) → `cheap`. A phase NOT in the dict falls back to `DEFAULT_TIER` (`balanced`). Per-phase overrides via `[agent] phase_models` (a TIER name, a concrete id, or a sentinel that inherits the user's default → `None`).

**Model capability order.** `core/cost.tier_rank(model)` ranks a model id / tier name along a *capability* axis `cheap < balanced < frontier` (the abstract tiers). An old short-name (`opus`) or a concrete dated id ranks identically to the abstract tier it belongs to (`frontier`). An unknown full id ranks *above* every known tier (most-capable, so a lower floor never silently downgrades an unknown spawn target), the inverse of the conservative pricing fallback. This order is the comparator for the most-capable-wins floor merge below.

**Cache-multiplier SSOT ([#2401](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2401)).** The Anthropic cache-pricing multipliers — a cache *read* bills 0.1x and a 5-minute cache *write* 1.25x the model's base input rate — are a fixed property of the API, not of any model, so they live in **one** foundation-layer leaf `src/teatree/pricing.py` (`CACHE_READ_MULTIPLIER` / `CACHE_WRITE_MULTIPLIER`). Every cost path imports them from there — `core/cost` (per-attempt SDK cost), `eval/cost_fit` (the warm-equivalent benchmark fit), and `eval/models` (the billed-input regressor) — rather than each keeping a private copy that could silently drift (the single source of truth). `teatree.pricing` is a `tach` `foundation` module with no dependencies; `tests/test_pricing_ssot.py` greps `src/teatree` and fails RED if the multipliers are ever defined in more than one place.

**Per-skill model floor + spawn-model merge.** `model_tiering.resolve_spawn_model(phase, *, skills)` is what both spawn paths call (headless SDK `model=` and interactive Agent-tool dispatch). It starts from the per-phase tier (resolved to a concrete model id) and raises capability for each loaded companion skill carrying a `[agent.skill_models]` MODEL floor (most-capable-wins via `tier_rank`, order-independent; a winning tier-name floor is resolved through `resolve_tier`). The floor is MODEL only — no per-skill effort axis — and returns `None` (inherit) only for a sentinel `phase_models` override with no floor; every other phase resolves to a concrete model id.

**The single effort constant + per-tier spawn effort.** The reasoning-effort axis has its own single source of truth `src/teatree/agents/model_tiering.TIER_EFFORT` (`{"frontier": "xhigh", "balanced": "xhigh"}`), the effort parallel of `TIER_MODELS` — only the reasoning tiers carry an effort; `cheap` (Haiku, which rejects the lever) is absent. `resolve_spawn_effort(phase)` resolves phase → tier → effort exactly as `resolve_phase_model` resolves phase → tier → model (same `[agent] phase_models` override mechanism, so a phase downgraded to a cheaper tier drops model AND effort in lock-step), overridable per tier via `[agent.tier_effort]` (validated against the effort scale). The headless SDK builder pins it as `ClaudeAgentOptions.effort=` for the SAME phase the model resolved from — `xhigh` for a frontier or balanced phase, unset for the cheap/Haiku phases and a sentinel-opted-out phase. Skill floors and the honesty escalation raise only the MODEL, never the phase's effort tier. The dispatch also RECORDS what it ran with ([#3673](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3673) Tier 3): the resolved effort and the resolved skill bundle are stamped onto the `TaskAttempt` (`reasoning_effort` / `skills_loaded`, threaded as a `DispatchProvenance` through the shared attempt recorder so both harness backends record them identically) and surfaced per-attempt on the dashboard ticket-drawer provenance panel alongside model/lane/cost. Captured going forward only — a historical attempt keeps the blank/empty defaults, never backfilled.

**Session-level pins (interactive main agent).** `[agent] session_effort` (`low|medium|high|xhigh|max`) and `[agent] session_model` are the interactive main-agent pins: injected as `--effort`/`--model` into the interactive `claude` spawn argv, so the user never runs `/model`. This is a SEPARATE axis from the per-tier `TIER_EFFORT`/`resolve_spawn_effort` spawn effort above — the interactive `session_effort` pin never leaks into a headless SDK sub-agent (whose effort comes from its phase tier), and the in-session Agent tool still has no effort param. `t3 doctor` validates the values (a bad effort FAILs, an unknown model WARNs) and `_check_stale_path_t3` ([#2281](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2281)) FAILs when a `t3` binary precedes `$UV_TOOL_BIN_DIR` on `PATH`, masking `uv tool install` updates.

**Selective per-phase fan-out ([#2229](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2229)).** A declarative, default-OFF way for an opted-in phase to fan work out (adversarial-verify on review, judge-panel on planning) without the blanket session-wide `ultracode`. The `core.phases.FANOUT_BY_PHASE` registry maps `(role, phase) → PhaseFanout`, parallel to `SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE`; the opt-in is `[agent.phase_fanout]` keyed `"role:phase"` (`true` = default width, int overrides). `resolve_fanout_directive` renders `""` with no entry/opt-in (the byte-identical spine) else a **plain-text** directive (§2 SDK/API-portable, [#2236](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2236)) threaded as the `fanout_directive` dispatch field. Ships **route (b)** only (rigor inside the one in-session sub-agent); real top-level parallel fan-out is deferred.

**Situational honesty-critical escalation ([#2263](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2263)).** Routes a *verification* spawn to the most-honest configured model (`[agent] honesty_model`, default Opus) when the agent's honesty is in question — situational, NOT a phase floor. Four triggers write a session/task-scoped `HonestyEscalation` row (mostly agent-fired via the `skills/rules` directive `t3 <overlay> honesty escalate`; `shipped_incomplete` also has a deterministic backstop in the rubric done-gate). `resolve_spawn_model` raises the winner to `honesty_model` when a verification phase has an active escalation — the LAST step of resolution, so nothing downgrades it afterward. `is_active` auto-clears at read-time (`cleared_at IS NULL AND expires_at > now()`, 6h TTL backstop); the primary clear is a fully-passed `rubric-grade`.

### 5.6 Loop Topology

**Loop restructuring lives on the lead session — there are NO panes ([#1838](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1838)).** Arm the dormant `orchestrate_phase` (the DB-home `orchestrate_claim_enabled` setting, `t3 <overlay> config_setting set orchestrate_claim_enabled true`, [#2315](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2315)) and per-loop ownership ([#2318](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2318)). The original `dedicated_loops` toggle ([#2319](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2319) — split one fat `/loop` into N dedicated scoped-tick slots) is **retired**: [#2650](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2650) made the per-loop split unconditional — the singleton `t3 worker` drives one self-rescheduling `loop_timer` chain per verdict-admitted `Loop` row (below) — so the fat-`/loop`-vs-dedicated-slots toggle is gone, not a default-off setting. The work-execution fan-out is the classic in-session `Agent(run_in_background)` sub-agent path; the pane-backed teammate layer that once sat beside it is **retired** ([#3734](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3734)) — it never had a production spawn caller and was bound to the Claude SDK by construction, so `teatree.teams`, the `team:<role>` claim namespace, the `pane_reaper` loop, and the four `teams_*` settings are gone.

TeaTree drives the day from the singleton `t3 worker`, which drains one self-rescheduling `loop_timer` chain per verdict-admitted `Loop` row (#2650; the local/read-only operational core ships enabled while colleague-facing, externally-visible, destructive-capable, and token-costly loops stay opt-in, so a fresh install registers the sound operational set — migration `0043`), each firing on its own DB-configured cadence (default 12 min). The tick body `teatree.loop.tick.run_tick` is code — tested, typed, version-controlled — composing named single-responsibility phases ([#1796](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1796)): `scan_phase` (parallel world-scan), `sweep_phase` (maintenance scanners split out of the scan), `act_phase` (dispatch + mechanical + persist), and `orchestrate_phase` (the wip-driven autonomous fan-out — a no-op at `medium`; `slow` targets one live worker, `full` targets the summed per-overlay `max_concurrent_auto_starts` budget, and `boost` targets `boost_concurrency = N` live workers via a **pool-refill driver** (clamped by the PR-01 resource concurrency ceiling `provision_max_concurrency`; `boost_concurrency = 0` keeps `full`'s summed-overlay target), admitting a queued TODO/followup before a new-ticket auto-start — the admission-priority ordering lives in `teatree.loop.queue_drain`). `orchestrate_phase` is wired as a **read-only budget PLANNER** — it computes the standing live-worker *target* (and the marginal `cap = target − in_flight` it would admit this pass) but never claims a `Task` (the single claim point stays the live `claim_next` CAS, so the tick can never orphan a claim). When the opt-in, default-OFF `orchestrate_claim_enabled` toggle is ON and the wip clamps, the tick persists the **target** as an admit-budget ceiling to the `tick-meta.json` sidecar (`teatree.loop.admit_budget`); the live claimer reads it and refuses once in-flight CLAIMED WIP reaches the target, so claimed ≡ spawned — and, the pool-refill invariant, when a worker exits below the target the next tick admits the shortfall back to N. At `medium` or with the toggle OFF no budget key is written (absence = no SIDECAR clamp = byte-identical throughput as far as the sidecar goes; the admission governor below still supplies its own ceiling, [#4097](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4097)), and the reader fails open on an absent/stale/corrupt budget so a dead loop never throttles live dispatch ([#1838](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1838), [#1917](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1917)).

**Adaptive admission governor — static concurrency settings are CEILINGS, not targets ([#3644](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3644)).** Concurrency was a hand-set constant whose only feedback loop was a human watching load. `core/admission_governor.py` is the pure, model-free decision every dispatcher consults BEFORE admitting work; `loop/admission.py` assembles its signals, persists the brake state and logs the refusal; `loop_dispatch._admit_budget_exhausted` is the loop-lane admission chokepoint; and `src/teatree/agents/_runner_env.with_test_worker_cap` applies the test-worker half. **Every dispatcher means the INTERACTIVE one too ([#4107](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4107)).** The governor had exactly two callers, both factory lanes, so an orchestrator session dispatching through the harness `Agent`/`Task` tool was admitted with no ceiling and no load brake — the guards governed the headless population while the agent population on the box is the SUM of both (measured at load 58 on 8 cores with 1 GB free while `issue_implementer_max_concurrent = 3` was in force). `core/dispatch_admission.py` is the third caller, driven by `hooks/scripts/dispatch_admission_gate.py` on the `PreToolUse` `Agent`/`Task` gate — the ONE interception point a dispatch has ([#4216](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4216)). The task-LIST tools are a separate family: their `TaskCreated` event has one producer, the `TaskCreate` tool body, so no dispatch reaches it, a todo puts no agent on the box, and nothing there is seated or braked. The gate's ceiling is compared against the TOTAL live-agent population (`active_claims`, both execution targets), since a new interactive agent lands on the same box; a sub-agent's ONWARD dispatch skips the ceiling — its own lane already admitted it and its own claim is counted in that ceiling, so re-clamping would deadlock it against itself — while the brakes, which read box- and account-wide signals, still apply. **The ceiling counts the population it admits ([#4129](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4129)).** `active_claims` is durable and the right shape, but an ad-hoc interactive dispatch creates no `Task` row at all — the very scenario this lane exists for — so the ceiling could not see its own admissions and N rapid dispatches each read the same live count and every one passed, leaving only the brakes, which fire *after* the box is saturated. `core/models/interactive_dispatch.InteractiveDispatch` is this lane's `Task.admitted_at`: a seat taken AT the gate and counted by `live_agent_count` alongside the claims. As in #4125 the bound is arbitrated INSIDE the write — the seat is inserted first and its own rank among the live seats re-read, so a racer loses on its rank rather than on a count that never saw it, and a refused seat is deleted so a denial never narrows the lane it was refused by. The seat's ordinary end is `hooks/scripts/dispatch_seat_release.handle_subagent_stop_release` on `SubagentStop`, keyed on the terminating `agent_id` for idempotency (the seat predates the id, so no seat can be bound to its agent at birth); `SEAT_WINDOW` (30 minutes) is the BACKSTOP for a release that never arrives — a dispatch that never materialised, a killed agent, a harness restart — not the expected lifetime, since a seat that only ever lapsed on its window would bound a dispatch RATE rather than a population. The ceiling-EXEMPT onward dispatch still takes a seat: the exemption is from being CLAMPED, never from being COUNTED, so that path is at least visible to the arm that does clamp instead of the two gaps compounding. Fail-open is unchanged and now covers the ledger too: a seat write that raises admits. Same kill-switch, no second flag: `admission_governor_enabled` false restores the pre-gate behaviour on this path too, alongside a per-call `[admission-ok: <reason>]` escape and the shared `_fail_open_or_deny` never-lockout routing. **Token budget is the PRIMARY signal, machine load secondary** — the budget that actually runs out is the model quota on a WEEKLY window, so an idle box with a spent weekly window admits nothing. The quota signal reads the `AnthropicTokenUsage` health cache the routing selector already maintains (per-account 5h + 7d utilization, 7d reset, exhaustion) — no network probe, no model — and `decide_admission` evaluates quota brakes → machine brake → yield brake. Pacing is weekly, not a sprint to zero: `weekly_pace = headroom / runway`, and below 1 the ceiling scales down to land AT the reset, below `PACE_DENY` the governor stops admitting; an unknown reset reads as a FULL window (the conservative direction). The yield brake counts terminal `Task` outcomes over a 6h window and STOPS admitting below a 20% completion ratio with ≥5 samples, because the marginal token is buying zero; fewer than 5 samples is UNKNOWN and never brakes. Machine pressure has hysteresis (deny above `5 × cores` load, re-admit only under `3 × cores`) with the previous brake state riding in the existing `tick-meta.json` sidecar, and the ceiling floors at 1 so the factory cannot deadlock itself. Failure is safe by construction, and "safe" means BOUNDED, not unbounded ([#4097](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4097)): an `AdmissionDecision` always carries a positive `ceiling`, so an unknown budget can never buy more concurrency than a known-healthy one. A quota probe that reads successfully but reports `fresh=False` — the steady state, since healthy `AnthropicTokenUsage` rows expire in `HEALTH_TTL` minutes and are written only reactively — drops the weekly-pace scaling (the only part that needs the quota) and bounds the lane on `floor(cores × WRITE_CONCURRENCY_PER_CORE)`, which needs no quota information at all; it never invents a tighter constant, because a clamp on evidence the governor does not have is the silent-starvation failure it exists to prevent. "No opinion" is the ABSENCE of a decision, not a decision with an absent ceiling: a probe that RAISES, or the kill-switch, makes `governor_verdict` return `None` so the caller keeps its pre-governor static behaviour — never a denial. The headless lane matters here because it passes `static_ceiling=None` (no operator cap for that lane), so it is entirely dependent on the governor deriving one. Refusals are never silent: every `AdmissionDecision` carries a non-empty `reason` and `governor_verdict` logs a denial at WARNING with it, pinned by a test rather than by convention. The test-worker multiplier is the meltdown fix: `per_agent_test_workers` divides a `cores * 2` TOTAL budget by the active-agent count and exports it as `PYTEST_XDIST_AUTO_NUM_WORKERS`, which is how pytest-xdist resolves `-n auto` — the `pyproject` addopts are untouched, so a human running the suite alone still gets the whole box, while 12 agents × 8 auto-detected workers at load ~70 can no longer happen. **The bound reads MEMORY too, the resource that actually saturates ([#4163](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4163)).** `MachineSignal.ram_available_gb` was declared and populated by nothing — every live caller of `read_machine_signal` passed no argument — so a `cores * 2` total of 16 workers is 10.4 GB at the measured p50 worker RSS but **19.8 GB at the p90 against 19.7 GB usable**: safe at the median, over the line at the tail, which is why the OOMs were relentless but intermittent (1374 kills in a fortnight, ~80% pytest-shaped). The fix is two-sided. `read_machine_signal` populates the field from `teatree.utils.ram_scope.read_ram_headroom` — the ONE cgroup-aware reader (`min(host MemAvailable, cgroup headroom)`), never a second `/proc/meminfo` read, which inside a container reports the HOST figure and would be invisible to that reader's own fix ([#4118](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4118)); its `None` (unreadable) stays a different answer from `0` (nothing left). **The reading carries the SCOPE it was taken in, because the watermarks below are absolute ([#4217](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4217)).** The same code returned opposite verdicts depending only on where it ran: the 2 GiB admin sidecar — where interactive work runs and every `docker exec` lands — read 1.65 GB at the instant the worker read 15.88 GB with 22 GB free on the box, so every `Agent`/`Task` dispatch made from it was denied, permanently, against a 4 GB floor a fixed 2 GiB cap can never rise above, under a deny text advising the operator to wait for hysteresis that could never arrive. `RamHeadroom` therefore carries `cgroup_limit_mib` beside the number, and `box_watermark_mib` drops the cgroup arithmetic whenever that cap sits below the agent-workload floor `t3 doctor`'s worker-cap FAIL already defines (`agent_workload_floor_gib`, one definition, same `TEATREE_WORKER_MEMORY_FLOOR_GIB` override). **Dropping the cgroup arm is not the same as having no answer ([#4252](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4252)).** `/proc/meminfo` inside a container reports the HOST, so the sidecar holds a box-scoped reading the whole time — and `read_ram_headroom` collapsed host and cgroup into `min()` *before* the scope test, making it unrecoverable, so the abstention fired on a genuinely starved box too: at 500 MiB free the brake stayed silent, the ceiling went unreduced and `per_agent_test_workers` skipped its memory clamp. `RamHeadroom` therefore carries `host_available_mib` as its own component, and an out-of-scope reading falls back to it. `None` — UNKNOWN, which never brakes — survives only when nothing box-scoped was readable at all, never as the verdict on a scope; the remedy is deliberately NOT making callers fail closed on `None`, which would restore exactly the permanent lockout #4217 fixed. So the uncapped host and a worker-sized cgroup are judged on `min(host, cgroup)`, and a sub-floor cgroup on the host figure alone. The second half is what "available" MEANS: cgroup-v2's `memory.current` charges page cache the kernel hands straight back, so `memory.max - memory.current` counted reclaimable cache as spent — measured under-reporting by ~1.4 GiB on the worker, and trending to near-zero after a day of `-n auto` suites, at which point the brake denies everything, `adapt_concurrency` clamps intake to 1 and `resource_pressure` DMs a CRITICAL, all on a box with tens of GB genuinely free. `cgroup_headroom_mib` credits `inactive_file + slab_reclaimable` from `memory.stat` — the cgroup mirror of what `MemAvailable` already does host-side, which is why the two arms of one function disagreed — and `resource_pressure._read_cgroup_headroom_gb` DELEGATES to it rather than restating the arithmetic, so the two readers cannot drift again. Three consumers read it, and no fourth: `per_agent_test_workers` takes the smaller of the cores-derived TOTAL and `floor((ram - RAM_BRAKE_FLOOR_GB) / test_worker_ram_gb)` — applied to the TOTAL, never the per-agent share, since a per-agent memory budget would hand each of N agents the whole box; `_machine_brake` gains a RAM watermark beside the load one (`RAM_BRAKE_FLOOR_GB = 4.0`, `RAM_RESUME_FLOOR_GB = 6.0`), inheriting that brake's hysteresis and its cheap-lane exemption for free; and `resume_agent_ceiling` scales by `min(load headroom, memory headroom)`, because the recorded restore was load 58 **and** 1 GB free and a bound reading only load calls that half healthy. It is deliberately NOT a term in `_machine_ceiling` / `_adaptive_ceiling`: `adapt_concurrency` already owns "how many agents fit in memory", and two unsynchronised readers of one quantity drift ([#4125](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4125)) — a brake answers a different question. **The concurrency decision reads the WHOLE BOX, not only the factory's own count ([#4407](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4407)).** Load and memory are box-wide, but the counts every lane sizes itself against — `claimed_agent_count`, the intake in-flight budget — are lane-local by construction: an orchestrating session's harness sub-agents claim no `Task` and hold no intake marker, yet each runs a test suite on the same cores. So `adapt_concurrency` had exactly one box-wide input (memory) and sized intake off a number that excluded most of what the box was doing — measured, a fan-out took load 14 → 53 and 16 GB → 1 GB free while the factory correctly held intake at 3, every chip green and zero merges for over an hour. `box_load_headroom(load1, cores)` is the ONE whole-box occupancy reading — the fraction of the `BRAKE_LOAD_PER_CORE * cores` budget still free, `1.0` idle, `0.0` at the watermark the brake denies on, and `1.0` again for an unreadable probe, since a probe that cannot answer must never lower a ceiling. `resume_agent_ceiling` consumes it (it had computed the identical expression inline) and `adapt_concurrency` now takes it beside the memory term, the smaller of the two capacity estimates winning; `in_flight` is renamed `factory_in_flight`, because a parameter that reads as the whole box and means one lane is what made the gap easy to miss. **Foreign load BRAKES the producer and only REPORTS on the rest** — the trade the issue asks be decided rather than defaulted. Braking intake cannot deadlock the factory, because the review and ship lanes that DRAIN the pile are untouched (the same rationale that keeps `MergeSignal` out of `decide_admission`), and the term floors at `MIN_CONCURRENCY`, so a busy box tightens intake toward one slot and can never wedge it to zero. The agent lanes deliberately get no graded load-scaled ceiling: `floor(cores * WRITE_CONCURRENCY_PER_CORE)` is 2 on a 4-core box, so scaling it would leave ONE expensive slot and starve the drain — the factory-starves-itself outage that is its own incident. The reporting half is `t3 doctor check`'s `_check_box_occupancy`, which prints the factory's agent count beside the box's load on EVERY run, because the actionable thing is the difference between them and a check that speaks only when unhappy can never show one; it WARNs at the brake watermark and never FAILs, since foreign load is a fact about the machine rather than a fault in the factory. Managing non-factory processes stays out of scope — the ask is only that a decision about how much the box can take is not made from a number that excludes most of it. Failure is BOUNDED, not closed, the #4101 guard restated: an unreadable probe (or a non-positive per-worker size) yields exactly today's CPU-derived bound, the brake does not fire, and resume stays load-derived — never a denial and never a manufactured clamp to 1, because denying on an unreadable `/proc` file is a kill switch operated by a missing file. The p90 is a setting, not a magic number: `test_worker_ram_gb` (`UserSettings`, DB-home, per-overlay overridable, default **1.25**), derived from the kernel OOM log 2026-07-21..08-04 (p50 0.65 / p90 1.24 / max 23.1 GB — the p90 is the sizing point; the max is one pathological run, not a budget). The pure function reads no config: `src/teatree/agents/_runner_env.with_test_worker_cap` resolves the setting and passes it in. One trade-off stated rather than left silent: the cheap lane is exempt from the RAM brake while still consuming memory — bounded by its 2 seats plus the worker cap, which is NOT exempt. Flag and rollback lever: `admission_governor_enabled` (`UserSettings`, DB-home, per-overlay overridable, default **`True`**); `t3 <overlay> config_setting set admission_governor_enabled false` reverts both admission and the worker cap byte-for-byte to the pre-governor static behaviour. **Concurrency ceiling ([#4069](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4069)).** `WRITE_CONCURRENCY_PER_CORE = 0.5` — 8 cores → 4. It was 0.25 (→ 2), calibrated against the meltdown recorded on `TOTAL_TEST_WORKERS_PER_CORE`, which names its own cause: *the per-agent expansion is the melt driver, not the agent count* (12 agents × 8 auto-detected workers ≈ 96 workers at load ~70). That driver is now bounded independently by `per_agent_test_workers`, which divides a `cores * 2` TOTAL worker budget by the active-agent count, so total workers stay bounded however many agents run — the old value priced agent count as if it were the hazard and predated the guard. Because `decide_admission` combines the operator's static ceiling with the adaptive one using `min`, this constant — not `boost_concurrency` or `issue_implementer_max_concurrent` — is the only lever on parallelism. The raise is safe to ATTEMPT rather than safe by assertion: the load brake still denies above `BRAKE_LOAD_PER_CORE * cores` and holds to `RESUME_LOAD_PER_CORE * cores`, so an over-aggressive value throttles itself instead of melting the box. **Merge back-pressure — do not deepen a pile that cannot land ([#4044](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4044)).** The yield brake above asks whether TASKS finish, and a task that finishes by opening an unmergeable PR answers yes, so unmergeable inventory reads as success — four PRs sat stuck for a day while the implementer kept claiming more. `MergeSignal` adds the question yield cannot answer: is any of it LANDING? It pairs the live `PullRequest` count with how many of those PRs the merge sweep has refused `MERGE_STUCK_AFTER_TICKS`+ consecutive times, read off the `SweepSkipStreak` rows the sweep already maintains — two counts, no forge call, both slugs matched case-insensitively as everywhere else — and `MergeSignal.stalled` is the verdict: true only when every open PR is one of the refused ones. The consumer is `_issue_intake_scanner_for`, the single decision point for claiming NEW work: when every open PR is stuck it sets `can_claim = False`, reusing the heartbeat-only mode already there for a full budget, so in-flight claims keep their leases while nothing new is taken. It is deliberately NOT a `decide_admission` brake — ship and review are what CLEAR a backed-up pipeline, and braking them on the backlog they exist to drain would deadlock the factory against itself. Both directions are automatic: the brake applies on evidence and releases on evidence (one PR moving again suffices), with no operator step and no flag to reset; an unreadable probe reports `fresh=False` and never brakes; and a braked tick LOGS which PRs are stuck and how many, because a gate that declines quietly recreates the class of bug it exists to catch. **The verdict is per phase COST CLASS, never one answer for the whole queue ([#4098](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4098)).** The headless drain consulted the governor once and applied that single verdict to every pending row, so a 3-minute read-only `reviewing` task was refused on exactly the brake a 272-turn `coding` agent had caused — and reviewing and shipping are what RETIRE work (a merged PR frees a worktree and its agent), so refusing them alongside the class that filled the box removed the only available relief and the brake held itself on (measured 2026-08-03: 3h22m of denied admissions, zero review verdicts, 18 pending tasks, no merges). `core/modelkit/phases.CHEAP_PHASES` is the harness-owned taxonomy — the review lanes, the work-retiring `shipping`/`requesting_review` lanes, and the short assessors — and `phase_cost` classifies anything unrecognised as EXPENSIVE, so a new or misspelled phase inherits the braked class rather than the exemption. CHEAP means **does not mutate source, and converges** (a review ends at a verdict, a ship at a merged PR), NOT that the agent is free: `_TOOLS_BY_PHASE` grants these phases `shell`, the cold-review procedure provisions a detached review checkout, a reviewer is told to run the suite, and a `shipping` push fires the parity hook. Because cheap still costs, the class is paired with a small ceiling rather than a free pass — the BOUND is what makes it safe, not the price of one member. `agent_admission_verdict` probes ONCE and resolves that probe per class (a drain costs one probe however many rows it walks, because nothing changes between iterations of that loop). The exemption is from the MACHINE brake ONLY: a spent token budget is a claim about budget rather than pressure and still refuses every class. And it is BOUNDED by `cheap_phase_admission_ceiling` (`UserSettings`, DB-home, per-overlay overridable, default **2**) against the lane's OCCUPANCY — the running cheap agents plus the cheap rows already handed to the runner (`Task.admitted_at`, taken through the one `AgentAdmission.admit` seam every chokepoint books its admission through, and released after `ADMITTED_INFLIGHT_WINDOW` so a dead runner cannot wedge the lane shut). That seat is what the two headless chokepoints SHARE: the drain is a loop that could carry a bound in memory and the `post_save` auto-enqueue is one row with a fresh verdict each time, so an in-memory count would have left a burst of pending rows admitting past the ceiling one save at a time. **The seat is arbitrated INSIDE the write, not between the probe and it ([#4125](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4125)).** The occupancy read and the stamp were two unsynchronised statements, so two PROCESSES reaching a chokepoint in one window each saw room and each admitted — an effective bound of ceiling plus however many raced, which on a box that OOM-killed its worker three times in one evening is not free. `TaskQuerySet.record_admission` is therefore a conditional UPDATE whose `WHERE` re-checks the same `_cheap_lane_occupancy_q` predicate the probe counted (one predicate, two readers, so arbitration and probe cannot drift) and refuses a row that already holds a live seat; the loser matches no row and is refused rather than admitted. A conditional write rather than `select_for_update`, because the probe runs on every `post_save` of a PENDING HEADLESS row and a row lock there would sit on a hot path. `admit` takes the seat BEFORE the dispatch, so a dispatch that then fails costs at most one `ADMITTED_INFLIGHT_WINDOW` of under-admission — the safe direction, and exactly what that window already recovers. A refusal `log_denials` already announced for the pass drops to DEBUG rather than repeating per held row, and a pass that finds released seats says so, because that is the state in which the ceiling is soft and it carries no refusal of its own to name it. Sharing the classification *and* one durable count is what stops the exemption becoming the second unbounded lane #4097 cost; `t3 <overlay> config_setting set cheap_phase_admission_ceiling 0` collapses cheap onto expensive — the rollback lever. **A ceiling on the cheap class is not a reservation FOR it ([#4374](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4374)).** That ceiling bounds how much of the box the draining class may take and guarantees nothing about how much is kept for it, so the same stall returned by the opposite route: three `coding` agents held every slot for over half an hour with five `reviewing` rows queued behind them and zero reviews running — no verdicts, no merges, and every surface reading healthy because the worker was busy and the loop ticked. Coding CREATES pull requests where reviewing and shipping RETIRE them, so under purely first-come allocation the producing side can occupy 100% of the factory and the board can only grow. `drain_slot_reservation` (`UserSettings`, DB-home, per-overlay overridable, default **1**) carves slots off the TOP of the governor's ceiling that only the cheap class may occupy: the EXPENSIVE verdict gains a second arm refusing an admission once `TaskQuerySet.expensive_lane_occupancy` reaches `ceiling - reservation`, alongside — never instead of — the pre-existing live-count arm. It is measured against the EXPENSIVE lane's own occupancy, never the live population, because against the latter it inverts into the mirror-image starvation: a box full of reviews refusing the coding work those reviews exist to retire. The reservation is clamped to at most `ceiling - 2`, so however large it is written the expensive class always keeps TWO slots, and `0` restores first-come allocation byte-for-byte. Two rather than one is forced by the bullet above: the governor ceiling is `floor(cores * 0.5)`, so a 4-core box has ceiling 2 and a `ceiling - 1` clamp would leave the expensive lane the SINGLE slot this document already calls the factory-starves-itself outage (#4407). The two clamps agree whenever the reservation is at most `ceiling - 2`, which covers the shipped default of 1 from ceiling 3 up — so the 4-slots-to-3 case this change is for is unaffected. They diverge once the reservation reaches `ceiling - 1`: at ceiling 3 a reservation of 2 reserves 1 here where `ceiling - 1` reserved 2. That is the deliberate cost of guaranteeing the expensive class two slots, and it is why the wider-reservation tuning is bounded by the ceiling rather than by the operator's number alone. Like the cheap seat it is arbitrated INSIDE `record_admission`'s `WHERE` (#4125's rule, now parameterised by cost class over one lane predicate whose two arms partition the queue), because guaranteed capacity a stale probe can let two racers spend is not guaranteed. Zeroing the cheap ceiling lifts the reservation with it — holding slots for a class that no longer exists would only narrow the whole lane. The state the reservation prevents is also named rather than left invisible: `core/drain_starvation.read_drain_lane_state` reports draining work queued, none of it running, past a 15-minute wait — the stall's exact signature — and `t3 doctor check` WARNs on it (advisory, since a box deliberately run at reservation `0` should not go red). **Restore-side admission — a resume replays a staggered fleet in one step ([#4108](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4108)).** Background agents survive a harness restart: the harness restores them from their saved transcripts and resumes them, the whole set at once. The stagger the orchestrator applied was a property of the DISPATCH, not of the agents, so it is not replayed — an 8-core box went from healthy to load 58 with 1 GB free with no intervening dispatch decision by anyone. A restore is not a dispatch, so no dispatch-side ceiling can see it. `resume_agent_ceiling` is that path's own bound: `cores * HOST_AGENT_POPULATION_PER_CORE`, scaled by the load headroom still left under the same `BRAKE_LOAD_PER_CORE` watermark the dispatch lanes brake on, floored at 1 so a wedged box still gets the one agent that might unwedge it. It is a HOST population bound and deliberately NOT `WRITE_CONCURRENCY_PER_CORE` — a per-lane concurrency setting bounds that lane and says nothing about how many agents the box is carrying in total, which is exactly the conflation the incident recorded (a lane capped at 3 while the two populations summed unchecked). `resume_shed_directive` returns the empty string at or under the ceiling, so a resume on an idle host is unchanged, and over it names the restored count AND the live ceiling it was judged against — the same never-silent contract every other refusal here keeps. The consumer is `hooks/scripts/resume_admission.py`, which counts the restored set as `<session>.agents` MINUS `<session>.agents-stopped` (the append-only dispatch ledger outlives its agents, so counting it alone would warn on history) and merges the directive into the ONE `SessionStart` write. Only `source == "resume"` is gated — a compaction is the same process and restores nothing. Same kill-switch (`admission_governor_enabled`), read Django-free and only once the count is already over the ceiling, so an idle resume adds no sqlite touch to the `SessionStart` hot path. **One normalised pressure scalar, and instrument only what a decision consumes ([#4508](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4508)).** The six brakes above were six independent `if`s, each with its own watermark and its own sentence, so the only expressible answers were *admit* and *refuse*: degradation was a cliff, and a box could read healthy on load while being out of weekly runway — the state the issue names. `core/admission_pressure.py` folds them into ONE value in which **1.0 IS each dimension's own existing watermark** (`weekly_utilization / WEEKLY_WINDOW_BRAKE`, `load1 / (BRAKE_LOAD_PER_CORE * cores)`, `1 + (RAM floor - available) / span`, `(1 - weekly_pace) / (1 - PACE_DENY)`, exhaustion as a flat 1.0), so the HALT verdict reproduces the pre-#4508 brake set exactly — pinned by a grid property test against a frozen oracle of the old predicates, asserting the **reason string** as well as the verdict. `BRAKE_PRECEDENCE` preserves the old ordered-`if` precedence among HALTing components, because exhaustion drives pace to zero and a numeric `max()` would silently rename a spent fleet after its derived symptom. The signal dataclasses (`QuotaSignal`, `MachineSignal`, `MachineBrake`) live in that module so the dependency runs one way, and `box_load_headroom` / `ram_headroom` come with them since each is exactly `1 -` its own component (#4125: one quantity, one reader). **What the fold BUYS is the range below 1.0, which six separate `if`s could not read.** Documented thresholds, and what degrades at each: **FULL** (`< 0.7`) nothing degrades; **DEGRADED** (`0.7-0.9`) intake concurrency tightens on the worst dimension — a continuous term, so this band names where it starts to bite rather than a new cliff; **SHED** (`>= admission_pressure_shed_at`, default 0.9) refuses the EXPENSIVE agent class while the CHEAP review/ship lanes that RETIRE work keep draining, the #4098 asymmetry restated (shedding the drain is the self-holding brake that cost 3h22m); **HALT** (`>= 1.0`) refuses every class, and in-flight work is never killed — only new admissions are refused, which is what "halt with partial results" means here. `decide_admission` is class-BLIND so it reads HALT only; `agent_admission_verdict` — the seam that already owns the cheap/expensive split — resolves SHED. **The token dimension finally reaches INTAKE.** `adapt_concurrency`'s occupancy term was load headroom alone, so a box with idle cores and free RAM kept claiming issues against a nearly-spent weekly window; it now takes `1 - pressure` (the parameter renamed `box_load_headroom` -> `admission_headroom`, because one that reads load-only while carrying the whole scalar is the stale name that made #4407 easy to miss). Because `1 -` the load component IS that old headroom, a CPU-bound box is byte-identical and the number moves only when quota is the worse dimension. Memory is deliberately WITHHELD from the scalar at that one call site: `additional` already owns "how many agents fit in memory", and two unsynchronised readers of one quantity drift. `resume_agent_ceiling` likewise keeps its two machine terms rather than the whole scalar — its consumer `hooks/scripts/resume_admission.py` is Django-free on the `SessionStart` hot path and the quota half needs the ORM. **No telemetry was added, because the second article's warning applies here literally** — `core/cycle_time/` already exists and nothing reads it ([#4480](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4480)). The scalar is computed at the decision point and discarded: no table, no span, no emitter, no migration. Every value has a named consumer before it is produced — `decide_admission` (HALT), `agent_admission_verdict` (SHED), `adapt_concurrency` via `IntakeConcurrencyScanner` (continuous), `t3 doctor check`'s box-occupancy line (the operator read), and the `_admission_pressure_signals` health collector. **Clustering by cause, not volume:** a braked factory refuses on every admission decision — hundreds of identical observations an hour — so that collector emits AT MOST ONE `HealthSignal` fingerprinted `admission-pressure:<dominant cause>`, and `KnownIssue.objects.record_signal` (which already dedupes on fingerprint) collapses N sightings into one row with `first_seen`/`last_seen` carrying the duration, auto-resolving when the pressure falls. Nothing is emitted below SHED: a factory merely under load is not an incident. Rollback lever: `admission_pressure_shed_at` (`UserSettings`, DB-home, per-overlay overridable, default **0.9**), clamped on read into `[DEGRADE_AT, HALT_AT]` so a typo cannot wedge the expensive lane; `t3 <overlay> config_setting set admission_pressure_shed_at 1.0` collapses SHED into HALT and restores pre-#4508 admission byte-for-byte, while `admission_governor_enabled false` remains the whole-governor kill-switch. One boundary deliberately moved: pace alone denied at `< PACE_DENY` where the other five denied at `>=` their watermark, so the unified predicate now denies AT it — a measure-zero float boundary, in the conservative direction, with its own test naming the old operator.

**#786 epic — the immortal-singleton roster model is fully retired (WS1–WS5 + #54, all merged).** The original model — a coordinator spawning a fixed roster of long-lived loop sub-agents it had to keep alive and re-spawn on death/compaction — was the root cause of the recurring "loop died on compaction / re-spawned" toil and the duplicate-on-restart hazard. It is **fully retired**: no roster, no `spawn_brief`, no takeover-respawn, no resume-by-agentId. The replacement satisfies three acceptance-contract invariants, each delivered by a specific workstream and detailed in the appendix:

- **Invariant 1 — 0 sessions ⇒ nothing runs.** The loop is session-bound; zero open sessions ⇒ dormant, by design (WS3). Two in-session siblings ride `SessionStart` / `t3 doctor check` / the recovery path: `account_switch` ([#1916](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1916)) compares `oauthAccount.accountUuid` and, on a `/login`, invalidates the backend token cache so DMs do not route to the old workspace; `mcp_connectivity` ([#2282](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2282)) live-probes every enabled MCP server via `claude mcp list` and validates each against its overlay-declared provider (`get_mcp_provider_expectations()`), making a disconnected/mismatched MCP a loud finding. A third sibling, `connector_manifest` (PR-19), lets each overlay declare its required-vs-optional claude.ai connectors by NAME (`get_connector_manifest()`); `check_connector_manifest` FAILs `t3 doctor check` on a down **required** connector with mode-correct guidance (first-install vs post-account-switch, from `claudeAiMcpEverConnected`) + a `RECONNECT` line, `t3 mcp reconnect [--open]` prints those lines, and `run_connector_preflight` refuses to start the loop when a required declared connector is down. A separate in-process `scope_cache` (PR-19) records known-missing `(token_id, scope)` pairs so a backend call short-circuits pre-HTTP (`ScopeMissingError(cached=True)`, one banner per pair) instead of re-failing every call site — consulted by the Slack transport, reset per tick, cleared by `t3 doctor authorizations`.
- **Invariant 2 — at most one tick per enabled loop.** Each enabled loop ([#2650](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2650)) is driven by its OWN self-rescheduling `loop_timer` chain that the singleton `t3 worker` drains, firing `t3 loops tick --loop <name>` on that loop's cadence; there is no single machine-wide tick. The per-loop executor mutex is the WS2 `LoopLease` DB row (backend-agnostic conditional-UPDATE CAS, expiry-reapable) claimed under the disjoint `loop:<name>` slot, so two sessions never double-drive the same loop while different loops run in parallel. Atomic per-unit claim is WS1 `t3 loop claim-next` (claim == spawn boundary). A second concurrent tick of the SAME loop loses the CAS and SKIPs. **Durable per-loop control plane ([#1913](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1913)).** `LoopState` is the canonical (and only) loop enable/disable control tier (mirrors `ConfigSetting`): one row per loop name carries `enabled`/`paused`/`disabled`, an absent row resolves to `enabled` (empty table is a provable no-op), and a `paused`/`disabled` row stops even the core `dispatch` loop. `t3 loop {pause,resume,disable,enable} <name>` are atomic, idempotent transitions via the `loop_state` management command (`resume`/`enable` are the one "make it run again" verb clearing either hold), and `t3 loop loop-state <name>` is the strictly READ-ONLY status probe — it reports the current state and writes nothing, with output phrased as a read (`status: <STATUS>`) rather than the mutation verbs' "is now", so inspecting a loop can never be mistaken for a pause/enable that just ran. `enable`/`disable`/`resume` move BOTH source-of-truth planes the #2584 verdict reads in one transaction — the `LoopState` upsert AND the row-level `Loop.enabled` column the tick gates on — so the verb can never report "now enabled" while the tick keeps skipping the loop (the bug where `enable` wrote only `LoopState` and left `Loop.enabled=False`). `pause` is the reversible control-plane hold only: it leaves `Loop.enabled` untouched so `resume` returns it to running without re-enabling a row that was deliberately disabled. The hold is consulted first by the tick gate (the `LoopState` arm of `loop_state_admits`), the review-claim chokepoint (`review_claim_signals.review_loop_enabled`), AND the in-session Stop self-pump on the `dispatch` loop — the restart-surviving "pause everything" (2026-06-03 incident). It is the SINGLE loop-control plane: loop control is `/loops` (`t3 loop enable/disable/pause/resume`, emergency-only under [#3248](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3248) — presets/schedules are the normal handle and `t3 loop override` the emergency force) + the DB only — there is no env kill-switch (the former in-process `T3_LOOPS_DISABLED` is gone) and no `[loops]` toml disabled-state tier. The shared read `loop.loop_state_db.loop_held_in_db` is a `domain` leaf and every DB read fails open/safe so a hiccup never silently disables a loop or crashes the Stop hook. **Toward autonomous DB-configured loops ([#1796](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1796)).** A `Loop` row (`teatree_loop`: `name`/`prompt`/`delay_seconds`/`enabled`/`last_run_at`) is the durable definition of one autonomous loop — the DB-configured replacement for the code-cadence tick, runnable on its own `delay` (a never-run loop is due immediately). The `prompt` is the instruction to run the loop's work each tick (e.g. "run a sub-agent to call the work scan"): the domain scanners under `teatree.loops` stay as the Python code the loop invokes — they are scan units, not separate loops.
