A data migration seeds one autonomous row per loop, each with its own cadence — a fixed `delay_seconds`, or a `daily_at` wall-clock time for once-per-day loops (`news` at 08:00, `dream` at night); `t3 setup` re-runs an idempotent `get_or_create` seed (`seed_loops`) so a fresh or squashed-migration install also has the defaults. **The seed VALUES are shipped data, not code (P7):** the loops, the curated modes and the weekly schedules live in the `[loops]` / `[modes]` / `[schedules]` tables of `src/teatree/config/defaults.toml` beside the `[teatree]` settings — a shipped default an operator tunes to kickstart a box IS a setting, whatever table it lives in, so they share one hand-editable file, one stdlib reader (`config/seed_defaults.py`), and one `config_setting export`/`import` interchange (see [docs/blueprint/configuration.md](docs/blueprint/configuration.md) § 10.7). Loops are not only the registry scanners — the reactive Slack-answer loop is one too. A loop's `prompt` is a first-class reusable `Prompt` row (`teatree_prompt`, FK from `Loop.prompt`): a named instruction with declared templated `params` (`render` substitutes only declared `{name}` tokens, leaving other braces literal) and a `PromptVersion` edit history (`revise` snapshots the superseded body+params, keyed `(prompt, version)`). Prompts are triggerable on their own via `t3 prompts list|render` (the `/prompts` skill); loops are read and triggered via `t3 loops list|tick` and `t3 loop list` (the `/loops` skill). Rows are managed from the Django admin. Each default loop carries a real one-line `description` (what it does and when) — the install seed (`teatree.loops.seed.DEFAULT_LOOPS`, built from the `[loops.<name>]` tables of the shipped `src/teatree/config/defaults.toml`) is the source of truth that populates `Loop.description` (and the prompt-backed loop's `Prompt.description`), a data migration backfills it onto existing rows, and `t3 loops list` renders it as a continuation line under each loop's status row. **The `Loop` table carries cadence + the enable toggle for the live tick (#2513 cutover), gated by the unified verdict (#2584).** The live loop tick selects its scanner jobs from the `Loop` rows via `build_loop_table_jobs` — a loop runs iff its row is `enabled` AND `is_due` AND not held by the DB `LoopState` control tier (`loop_state_admits`), bumping `last_run_at` only for the loops it admits — and the statusline + `t3 loop list` read the `Loop` table too. The legacy code-cadence path (a registry fan-out gated by a `MiniLoopMarker` cadence ledger) is **deleted** (LOOP-PR-A) — `Loop.last_run_at` is the single cadence ledger and the `Loop` table the single source of truth for the live tick + live status. **Each `Loop` row carries its OWN on-disk entry point — the `script`/`prompt` column is load-bearing (#2513).** A script-backed row's `script` is its OWN module `src/teatree/loops/<name>/loop.py` (the file exposing that loop's `MINI_LOOP`), never a value shared across rows — the retired shared `run.py` runner is gone. `build_loop_table_jobs` reads each admitted row's column to decide what to dispatch (a script row resolves its `script` path UP to the loop name via the one normalization seam `teatree.loops.run.parse_script_loop_name` + the live registry; a prompt row dispatches its own loop), so which behaviour fans out is decided by the row's column, not by a name-only registry lookup. A row whose `script` does not resolve to a real registered loop module raises loudly and is logged + skipped — never a silent no-op. `arch_review` is the one prompt-backed default; its prompt instructs a sub-agent to run an architectural review with the `ac-reviewing-codebase` skill. #2584 closes the #2513 gap where `build_loop_table_jobs` gated on `Loop.enabled` alone and silently ignored `t3 loop pause`/`disable`: the loop tick now routes through the SAME unified `LoopState` verdict (`loop_state_admits`) the review-claim chokepoint's hold read shares, checked BEFORE `mark_run` so a held loop's cadence anchor is preserved. The inverse gap is closed too — the per-loop runner (`t3 loops tick --loop <name>`, #2650) scopes the SAME `build_loop_table_jobs` gate to one row, so it honours `Loop.enabled` symmetrically. The #2513 cutover shipped every loop **PAUSED**; migration `0043` reverses that for the local/read-only operational core (`inbox`, `dispatch`, `tickets`, `housekeeping`, `idle_stack_reaper`, `local_stack_queue`, `resource_pressure`) so a fresh or restored install works out of the box. The default-loops seed lands that set `enabled=True` (`LoopSeedSpec.default_enabled`) and `0043` flips the same set on existing rows — only where still at the untouched seed default (`enabled=False`, no `paused`/`disabled` `LoopState` hold), so an operator's explicit disable/pause is never clobbered. Every colleague-facing, externally-visible, destructive-capable, or token-costly loop stays `enabled=False` (opt-in). **The `review` loop is a SINGLE unified review intake ([#3569](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3569)):** it reviews the owner's OWN open PRs AND colleague PRs through the SAME `reviewing` → `t3:reviewer` gate — the author distinction lives upstream in the intake (self PRs are ALWAYS admitted; colleague PRs only when the `admit_colleague_prs_to_board` setting is on). Because self-review must keep running while the operator is away, `review` is NO LONGER `colleague_facing` (its away-gate is removed) and the `away` preset forces it ON rather than masking it — colleague admission is governed solely by the setting, not by availability. `0001_initial` still seeds all rows paused (frozen history); `0043` flips the sound set after, and `t3 setup`'s `get_or_create` seed agrees. A re-enabled — or re-disabled — row is preserved by the idempotent seed. `LoopState` (pause/disable) beats the row across the gating planes. There is no `[loops.<name>]` toml cadence tier and no `MiniLoopMarker` ledger (LOOP-PR-A deleted both) — `Loop.last_run_at` is the single cadence ledger for BOTH the live tick and the off-live-tick `dream` cron (which gates on the dream row's `is_due`/`last_run_at` + the unified enable verdict). The seeded `Loop`-table set equals `iter_loops()` (#2584: the orphan `slack_answer` row is never seeded — it has no registry `MiniLoop` and runs only via its dedicated `loop-slack-answer` `/loop` slot). **The worker owns the per-loop tick cadence ([#2650](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2650)) — one timer chain per enabled row, no master runner.** There is no single shared-tick cron and no `t3 loops run` interval loop: the singleton `t3 worker` drains one self-rescheduling `loop_timer(name)` chain per ENABLED `Loop` row (default-ON), each firing `t3 loops tick --loop <name>` on that loop's own cadence (the per-loop primitive scopes the tick to that one enabled, due row, fans it out through the shared `run_tick` pipeline gated by `build_loop_table_jobs` on the row's `is_due` and bumping `last_run_at`, and claims the disjoint per-loop `loop:<name>` lease so the loops run in parallel). Running `t3 loops tick` with no `--loop` is a hard error. The worker is the sole DB-loop cadence owner: there is no native Claude `/loop` cron mirror and no fallback cadence plane — flipping `loop_runner_enabled` OFF stops the loops entirely. Enabling/disabling a loop moves the DB planes (`t3 loop enable|disable <name>`) and the reconciler adds/prunes that loop's timer at the enable/disable chokepoint at once. The t3-master session still registers the three always-on reactive infra slots (Slack-answer, self-improve, drain-queue) at session start: `handle_enforce_loop_on_prompt` (delegating to the bare hook sibling `hooks/scripts/loop_registrations.py`) emits each as its own `/loop <duration>` directive sourced from the `teatree.loop.loop_cadences` reactive-slot seam (the same seam `t3 loop <slot> start` prints) — they have no `Loop` row and a sub-minute cadence a cron cannot express, so the owner drives the three directly; a non-owner registers nothing, and the emission is once-per-session (the `loop-pending` marker).
- **Invariant 3 — exactly one TODO-consolidation loop per agent identity, across all sessions.** The WS4 per-agent consolidation self-pump, keyed by `agent_id` in a separate consolidation-registry.

**Standing directives — the rules are re-delivered, not merely written down ([#4166](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4166)).** The golden rule (PLAN → IMPLEMENT → COLD REVIEW), the todo-consolidation pass and the PR-board drive are written in the skills and were skipped anyway: the failure is context decay, so the repetition is automated. `teatree.loop.standing_directives` is the harness-neutral model — three slots, each carrying a compiled default text overridable by a `Prompt` row named `standing-directive:<slot_id>` (an empty body switches that slot off; an over-cap one falls back to the default, so the per-session context cost stays bounded), a cadence, a `scope`, and a `wakes_session` boolean. Every resolution failure degrades to the compiled defaults, so the directives resolve with no DB at all.

**The gate follows the COST, which is what `wakes_session` declares.** `standing-golden-rule` (300s, floor 60, `T3_GOLDEN_RULE_CADENCE`, scope `attended`) does not wake the session: it is written into a turn that already exists, so it arms nothing and keeps the WIDE `_teatree_engaged` gate — reaching every engaged session from its first prompt, and it is never preset-gated because a rule that costs nothing has no reason to be rationed. `standing-todo-consolidate` (1800s, floor 600, `T3_TODO_CONSOLIDATE_CADENCE`, scope `attended`) and `standing-pr-board` (600s, floor 300, `T3_PR_BOARD_CADENCE`, scope `attended-singleton`) DO wake the session, which is arming the loop machinery, so they gate on `_loop_auto_load_active` — the [#256](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/256) contract that `.t3-engaged` engages the suggester while only `.teatree-active` arms loops — and the singleton slot additionally requires the tick-owner election, so one board is driven once per host rather than once per session. Four independent bounds hold the cost: the delivery shape (only waking slots cost a turn), the singleton scope (the 600s slot does not multiply by N sessions), the floors, and the self-pump brake — no waking directive resolves while the resolved mode masks the self-pump's own loop (`dispatch`) OFF (a self-waking loop IS a self-pump), failing OPEN to delivering if the mode cannot be resolved or holds no opinion. The brake reads the MERGED `resolve_active_mode`, never the L3/L2 preset layer ([#4196](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4196)): that layer is blind to the L0 default mode and to the live-presence upgrade, so braking on it would suppress the rule at a scheduled away slot the owner is demonstrably typing into. It is read only once a self-waking slot has survived text resolution, so a session with those slots switched off never touches the DB for it. The budget is `self_woken_turns_per_hour()`: **2 per attended session + 6 per host** at the defaults, 0 under an away mode with the golden rule still injected.

**The off switch is reachable and versioned.** `t3 loop directives disable <slot> | --all` (and `enable`) writes an empty override body through `Prompt.revise`, so the disable is snapshotted as a `PromptVersion` and `enable` restores the owner's own text; `--all` is the whole-feature kill, which is why no `standing_directives_enabled` setting exists. `Prompt.body` allows blank for exactly this, narrowed on both write surfaces — `Prompt.clean` (the admin form and every `full_clean` caller) and `Prompt.revise` (the versioned content-change path) — each refusing an empty body on a prompt a `Loop` runs. A directive override is referenced by no `Loop`, so the disable is untouched by either.

`t3 loop directives show --json` prints the `{slot_id, cadence_seconds, text, scope, wakes_session}` contract plus the turn budget, and that contract is the harness seam: teatree's Claude plugin maps it onto its own mechanisms in `hooks/scripts/loop_registrations.py` (`emit_standing_directives_once` — throttled context injection for the zero-turn shape, a recurring `/loop` registration for the waking shape, each with its own per-session marker, the Agent-SDK lane excluded, fail-open silent), while another harness gets all three behaviours by reading that JSON and writing only its own adapter. No directive text and no cadence value may appear in the adapter — pinned by a layer-purity test whose denylist is DERIVED from the layer-1 texts, so a new policy word is caught by default. **The per-session markers are never reaped by a peer**, which is what makes the budget above a per-session number: N engaged sessions is the normal operating mode and no session can tell a dead peer's marker from a running one's, so an adapter that swept foreign markers would re-register every peer's waking slots once per prompt — the `/loop` population growing with prompt count, the exact runaway the budget bounds. Reaping is left to the harness's own age-based state sweep, which cannot mistake a peer for a corpse.

**Self-owned worker — `t3 worker` + self-rescheduling timer chains ([#1796](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1796), default ON).** The singleton `t3 worker` owns the tick cadence by default: with `loop_runner_enabled` ON (the shipped default) `SessionStart` re-spawns a detached worker whenever the `worker` flock is free (the SessionStart resurrector `hooks/scripts/worker_supervisor.py`, whose Django-free cold-read default is pinned equal to the dataclass default so a fresh install actually spawns), and the worker is the sole cadence owner. `loop_runner_enabled` is the **kill-switch**: flipping it OFF is the instant runtime escape that stops the loops entirely — there is no fallback cadence plane (no native-`/loop` cron mirror), though the `default`-queue drain still runs under OFF via the reactive drain loop, so OFF halts loop ticks without stranding queued FSM/headless work. The cadence is durable, crash-surviving state — exactly one pending `loop_timer(name)` django-tasks row per verdict-admitted `Loop` row on the dedicated `loops` queue is a scheduled `run_after` timer; the DB row IS the fire, so a crash never loses a beat. When a worker executor drains a timer it runs a five-step body that re-schedules its own successor BEFORE doing the tick work (self-dedup → successor-first re-enqueue → live `admitted_loop_names` admission check → a deadlined subprocess tick in its own process group, deadline `max(300s, 3×cadence)`, whole-group kill on expiry → post-tick cadence refinement from the fresh `mark_run_if_unchanged` anchor), so a crash mid-tick always leaves a queued successor and the chain never stalls. The `mark_run_if_unchanged` CAS stays the sole idempotency guard (an at-least-once double delivery is a no-op). A deterministic zero-token reconciler `ensure_loop_timers()` keeps the "exactly one pending timer per verdict-admitted loop" invariant true — adding a missing chain head, pruning a surplus timer, repairing a chain stuck RUNNING past its deadline, deleting an un-admitted/unknown loop's timers — and runs at worker startup, on its own ~5-minute self-rescheduling chain, from the loop enable/disable chokepoint, and from the preset-transition chokepoint ([#4185](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4185)). Chain MEMBERSHIP is `teatree.loops.chain_membership.timer_chain_loop_names` — the unified enable verdict (hold > forced > mode mask > `Loop.enabled`) resolved through the ONE `teatree.loops.enable_verdict.EnablePlanes` seam the live tick's per-fire admission also asks ([#4196](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4196)), intersected with the registered live-tick set, NEVER the raw `Loop.enabled` column, which the mask overrides: reading the column alone left eight mode-admitted loops with no timer row of any status, ever, and actively pruned any they had. One object, not two call sites kept in step by convention — resolving the mask separately (membership through the L3/L2 preset layer, the tick through `resolve_active_mode`, which continues to the L0 `default_mode` row and the live-presence upgrade) made the two contradict each other at every scheduled away slot the owner typed through, and the reconciler deleted the timers of the loops the tick was about to fire. **One object, two NAMED readings, and membership is deliberately the WIDER one.** Membership is persisted as timer rows and admission is evaluated per fire, so the two questions are asked at different instants and the costs are asymmetric: a member the tick refuses costs one `skipped` no-op (the successor is queued before the gate, so it self-corrects), while an admitted loop that is not a member does not run AND has its timer DELETED. Pruning is the destructive direction, so membership is `EnablePlanes.admits_any_mask` — the **presence-invariant closure**: a member if the schedule/default mask admits it OR its `ResolvedMode.presence_alternate` does. That closes over the ONE arm that flips with no observable event (a keystroke raises the live-presence upgrade and the mere absence of one lowers it); every other arm moves on a durable write or a clock boundary and keeps its chokepoint, so a `LoopState` hold, a force-OFF and a mask both sides refuse all still prune exactly as before. `admitted_loop_names` narrows the same object further with the `is_due` and colleague-facing arms; those stay the per-FIRE admission. `EnablePlanes.admits` is what every other surface reports — reporting membership would render a masked-off loop as running — and `tests/conformance/test_enable_verdict_seam.py` is the AST guard that no fourth variant of the verdict appears. `chain_membership.starved_loop_names()` derives the admitted-but-driverless set (membership minus the live timer rows) that `t3 loop list`, `t3 loops list` and the dash live panel render as `starved` — no field, no migration, so a persistent reading is a canary that the reconcile chain itself has stopped; a daily `prune_task_results` chain caps DBTaskResult table growth, and an hourly `expire_stale_jobs` chain keeps the `default`-queue backlog swept for a long-lived worker. Worker startup ALSO expires the stale `default`-queue backlog once BEFORE spawning any executor (`expire_stale_default_jobs`, scoped to `default` so the reconciler stays the sole owner of `loops`-queue timer staleness), so a box that queued days-old provision/ship jobs while no worker ran never blind-fires them the instant the default-ON worker spawns (the flip's load-jam class). The worker runs K=4 programmatic `django_tasks_db` executor threads — 2 pinned to `loops`, 2 to `default` — so a heavy headless job can never starve a reactive timer; a supervisor thread re-reads `loop_runner_enabled` every ~5s and stops every executor on a flip-off or SIGTERM. At-most-one comes from the `worker` flock singleton; at-least-one from the worker supervisor + reconciler (NO OS scheduler — no cron/launchd/systemd) plus the `SessionStart` resurrector (`hooks/scripts/worker_supervisor.py`), which re-launches the worker whenever the flock is free; a fully-headless box starts `t3 worker` once from a login profile. This is DRIVER-ONLY: the runtime transport (the Pydantic-AI harness + OpenAI-compatible router) and cached-resume are owned by epic [#2565](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2565), and the existing Claude-SDK dispatch path is unchanged. **Availability reconciliation ([#2544](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2544)).** Both drivers of a per-loop tick — the worker's deadlined subprocess timer tick and the legacy native `/loop` cron — converge on the SAME command (`t3 loops tick --loop <name>` / `python -m teatree loops_tick --loop <name>`), so the active mode's mask is applied in ONE place for both. **A mode holds colleague-facing work off through its own table ([#2904](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2904), [#4202](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4202)).** A mode that means "the owner cannot weigh in" says so by forcing `followup` — the sole `Loop.colleague_facing` row, which fires the review-request nag — OFF in its `entries` (#3569 unmasked `review`, which now always runs so self-review keeps going when away, with colleague admission gated upstream by `admit_colleague_prs_to_board` instead of by the loop's away-gate). The mask the unified verdict `_loop_admitted` already applies (shared by `build_loop_table_jobs` AND `admitted_loop_names`, so the axis can never drift between the per-loop tick and the timer chain's admission step) is therefore the whole mechanism, with no second posture axis to drift from it; a masked loop's cadence anchor is preserved untouched, same as a `LoopState`-held row. `Loop.colleague_facing` survives as a REPORTING axis — `t3 doctor`'s stale-override finding names the colleague-facing loops a no-expiry override masks off.

**A recent anchor is not evidence of a schedule — scheduledness is its own reading ([#4140](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4140)).** `loop_staleness` asks whether `Loop.last_run_at` moved recently; `loops/schedule_liveness.py` asks the question that one cannot — is a chain still carrying the loop at all. The two come apart exactly where a worker dies mid-tick: the corpse RUNNING row wins the `_outranked_by_running` id tiebreak, the losing fire returns BEFORE the successor-first enqueue, and the chain is handed to something that will never re-chain. A manual `t3 loops tick` then bumps the anchor without restoring the chain, so every cadence surface reads healthy while the loop never fires again. `unscheduled_loops(now)` names every enabled, timer-chained loop with neither a READY timer row nor a RUNNING one inside its tick deadline, over the same `is_stranded` corpse predicate `ensure_loop_timers` reaps with, so the alarm and the reaper can never disagree about which rows are dead. `off_live_tick` loops are excluded by construction — the enumeration is `timer_chain_loop_names`, and `off_live_tick_driver` fires their own tick command, so having no timer row is their correct steady state. The `loop_runner_enabled` kill-switch is the reading's PRECONDITION, not a breach of it: `loop_timer` step 0 halts without a successor precisely to terminate every chain at its source while `Loop.enabled` stays true, so an OFF switch drains the whole fleet into the state this reading calls stopped — `unscheduled_loops` is empty there, gated the way `_check_worker_running` and `self_heal._Probe` gate theirs, and the OFF state is reported once by the surface that owns it rather than once per enabled loop. The corpse predicate bounds detection latency, disclosed: a chain dropped mid-window is named once `compute_tick_deadline + STUCK_GRACE_SECONDS` expires, not at the drop. `t3 doctor check` hard-FAILs on a stopped chain, and `tests/conformance/test_scheduling_liveness.py` is anti-vacuous by construction: it drives the REAL `loop_timer` body against a stranded row plus a higher-uuid live fire rather than hand-building the end state, with the uuid ordering flipped as its control.

**Every loop declares what kind of thing it is — reach and determinism, in code ([#3959](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3959)).** `MiniLoop.declared_reach` is a SET drawn from `ingress` (consumes a surface external to this box), `egress` (acts outward on one) and `colleague` (that outward action lands in a person's attention — a Slack post, a DM, a reaction on someone's message); `MiniLoop.determinism` is binary — `deterministic` (pure code, no model call, no spend) or `ai` (dispatches an agent or otherwise calls a model on ANY path). `colleague` is a REFINEMENT of `egress`, not a peer: `MiniLoop.reach` folds `egress` in for any loop declaring `colleague`, so filtering on `egress` catches every colleague-reaching loop while `colleague` narrows to the ones where being wrong costs a person's attention. The declaration lives on the `MiniLoop` and every surface DERIVES from it — `t3 loops list` (rendered as `[ingress egress colleague ai]`, filterable with a repeatable `--tag`) and the dashboard loop table both read `iter_loops()`, so there is no second editable copy that can disagree with what the code does. `tests/conformance/test_loop_classification.py` holds two guards: `teatree.loops.classification.unclassified_loops` names any registered loop declaring neither (an unclassified loop cannot ship), and `ai_evidence` cross-checks every `deterministic` claim against the loop's REAL dispatch behaviour — an agent-routing signal kind (`AGENT_BY_KIND`, the `("agent", …)` rows of `MECHANICAL_BY_KIND`, the payload-conditional handlers), a `phase=` argument naming an agent-dispatched phase (`SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE` + `SCANNER_DISPATCHED_PHASES`), or an import of a model-calling module — read out of the loop's own package and the scanner modules it wires. A signal routed to a MECHANICAL executor is followed through that executor's import closure as well, because a handler that reads as mechanical in the dispatch table can still spawn a headless turn several modules down (`ci_eval_heal` is exactly that shape). So a loop that reaches a model cannot carry the tag promising it costs nothing. The evidence is one-directional: finding a token proves the loop reaches an agent, finding none proves nothing, so the lane asserts `derived ⊆ declared-ai` and never the reverse. This is a distinct axis from `Loop.colleague_facing`, which is the operator-editable AWAY-GATE policy (which loops sit out while the owner is unreachable) and stays a strict subset of the `colleague` tag — `review` reaches colleagues yet deliberately keeps running when away.

**Shipped definitions are guarded by detecting INERTNESS, not by forbidding deletion ([#3842](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3842)).** Measured: 27 shipped loops, 27 present, 0 missing, 10 disabled — nothing has ever been deleted, and every failure that cost time was present-but-inert (a janitor wired to no loop, a colleague loop held dark by an override, a tick reporting 58 actions while no ticket advanced). Deletion is also self-healing, since the seed is `get_or_create` by name and `t3 setup` recreates a removed shipped row, so a deleted definition is the *recoverable* failure. `teatree.loops.seed_inertness.shipped_inertness()` therefore names every shipped loop/preset/schedule that is missing, disabled, or not ticking, and sources its EXPECTED set from the `[loops.*]` / `[modes.*]` / `[schedules.*]` seed tables of `config/defaults.toml` — never from the DB, because a check that reads the DB for both "expected" and "actual" cannot detect a missing row at all (the same self-referential defect as a golden compared against its own renderer, [#3836](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3836)). Presence alone was not enough ([#4096](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4096)): with every row present, every mask non-empty and every slot naming a real preset, a live `standard` calendar carrying an extra `Mon-Fri 19:00 → maintenance` slot against a `maintenance` mask that stopped delivery without stopping intake stalled the merge lane 13h a night and the audit still read OK. So each live VALUE is now compared against the shipped one too — `Mode.entries` and `ModeSchedule.slots`/`timezone`, through `loops.seed_drift` (whose `classification_drift` half already did this for one `Loop` field) — and reported as a NOTE naming both sides, never rewritten, because a per-box override is a legitimate operator decision and the gap was that nobody could see it. What IS a fault is the shape that stalls: `loops.mode_shape.intake_without_delivery` fails any mode — shipped or hand-written — that masks a DELIVERY loop (`ship`/`tickets`) off while an INTAKE loop (`issue_implementer`) stays admitted, either forced on or absent-and-inheriting a `Loop.enabled` that is on. The base flag is an input rather than an assumption, so a mask that legitimately stops delivery while intake is parked stays quiet. Severity keys on the shipped `default_enabled`, which is what sourcing from the seed buys: a loop that ships ON and is found off REGRESSED (a fault), while one that ships off and is off is a NOTE — without that split the box's 10 opt-in-dark loops are 10 lines nobody reads. Staleness reuses `loop_staleness.stale_loops` (the 3× cadence reading and its suppressed/unexplained split), so there is no second copy of the cadence arithmetic. Surfaced by `t3 loops audit [--json]` (non-zero exit on any fault) and a crash-proof `t3 doctor check` check that echoes faults only. Deleting a shipped definition is soft-protected rather than forbidden: `loops.shipped_guard.require_shipped_delete_confirm` demands a typed `stop-<name>` and the refusal quotes the shipped `description`, because the operator deleting a shipped loop is usually unclear on what it does — a confirm that names the consequence teaches more than a refusal, and refusal invites the hand-edited DB, which is worse than an audited delete. It is enforced at `delete_preset` plus the new `delete_loop` / `delete_schedule` seams (`t3 loops delete`, `t3 loop preset delete`, `t3 loop schedule delete`, all via the `shipped_seed` management command), and the Django admin ROUTES a shipped row to those seams while leaving an operator-created row's ordinary delete alone. The confirm never overrides an integrity refusal — a preset a slot still names, and the active calendar, are refused whatever phrase is typed.

**A gated FEATURE is guarded the same way — a declared evidence observable, not the flag's own value ([#4189](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4189)).** #3842 covers loops, presets and schedules; features had no equivalent, and that is the larger class. Measured: twelve quality gates (`require_executed_repro`, `require_rubric_verification`, `require_review_context`, `require_merge_quality_verdict`, `critic_gate_mode`, `require_anti_vacuity_attestation`, `require_integration_review`, `require_debt_delta`, `require_merge_evidence`, `require_spec_coverage`, `require_plan_adequacy`, `require_work_group_batch`) merged, reviewed, tested and default-OFF across 562 recorded `MergeClear` rows, with their evidence tables empty the whole time and every surface reporting success. `teatree.config.gate_evidence` is the declaration half: for each governed gate that SHIPS OFF it records the observable being live would generate — a model (`core.ReproEvidence`), a `Ticket.extra` key (`review_context`), or an explicit NONE for a refusal-only gate that writes no artifact and therefore can never be proven live at all. `teatree.core.factory.feature_inertness.feature_inertness()` measures against that declaration, never against the flag, because reading the flag to decide whether the flag matters is the same self-referential defect [#3836](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3836) names. Two quiet directions keep it honest: a populated observable clears a gate even while its flag is off (the evidence proves it ran, whatever the flag says), and a gate merged under `INERT_AFTER_DAYS` ago is too young to judge; a gate on in ANY `ConfigSetting` scope is doing its job, so the check reads every scope carrying a row rather than the global one alone. Severity is #3842's doctrine translated: a gate the owner deliberately staged (with a decision reference the declaration REFUSES to accept as prose) is a NOTE, a gate nobody ever decided to leave off is a FAULT. Prevention rather than reporting is `tests/conformance/test_gate_evidence_declared.py`: every default-OFF member of `FEATURE_FLAGS | DURABLE_GATE_SETTINGS` must carry a declaration, so a NEW default-OFF gate fails the PR that mints it — the walk fires only on an undeclared key, so the existing surface needed no retro-fit. Surfaced by `t3 <overlay> config_setting inert` (the full report, notes included) and a crash-proof `t3 doctor check` advisory that echoes faults only. Detection and refusal-of-new only: what to do about an inert gate stays an owner decision.

**A declaration also names what would SATISFY the gate, because "has it fired?" is not the question an arming decision asks ([#4375](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4375)).** Ten of the twelve were still UNDECIDED up to 67 days later against a report that named no next action: `_finding` assembled the never-fired line from the intent clause alone, so the producer six declarations already recorded in `rationale` prose was dropped on exactly the loudest lines. Every `GateEvidence` therefore carries a required `satisfier` — the command that writes the observable (`t3 <overlay> review record-evidence`), or, for a refusal-only gate, the state that satisfies the refusal (a `MergeAudit` row the keystone writes either way; a work group whose every open member is review-ready) — rendered on both the never-fired and the unobservable line and refused when blank by `declaration_faults`. It is a declared field rather than prose because prose drifts unseen: the first version sent operators at a `repro record` subcommand that has never existed, only `record-red` and `record-green`. `tests/conformance/test_gate_evidence_declared.py` resolves every `t3 …` citation in a satisfier against the live #550 command registry, so a satisfier naming a dead command fails CI instead of stranding the arming decision it exists to inform. Naming the satisfier still leaves the operator without the OTHER half — how a gate LEAVES `UNDECIDED` — so `render_inertness_report` closes the faults with `DECISION_TRAILER`, naming both exits: arm it (`t3 <overlay> config_setting set`), or move the declaration to `intent=STAGED` with the citation `declaration_faults` already demands. The staging exit is a source edit nothing advertised, so an operator reading the report had only the arming one — which is the friction that made "nobody decided" durable. It follows the faults only: a report of staged notes alone is a report with no decision to prompt for. Arming itself stays an owner decision, unchanged from #4189 — the report now carries every input that decision needs, and makes none of it.

**Idle usage-window auto-recovery — park, detect-reset, re-arm (Directive #3, SETTLING `limit_autorecovery_enabled`, default ON).** When a Claude usage window empties (the ~5h rolling session limit or the 7-day weekly limit) the headless plane would fold the hit into a terminal FAILED attempt (`_outcome_failure`) and go idle until a human pokes it — a measured 7.8h loss. With the flag ON (the default, graduated DARK->SETTLING by #3691), a limit hit is instead PARKED, not failed: `teatree.agents.usage_window.park_task_on_limit` records a `UsageWindowState` row per credential lane (`teatree.llm.anthropic_limits.window_horizon` fills the re-arm instant from the SDK's structured `RateLimitInfo.resets_at`, else the cause's known window length; API-credit exhaustion has no time-based recovery so it still terminal-FAILs — the operator must add credits) and returns the `Task` to the queue PENDING with `not_before` at that instant (a new `Task.not_before` claim gate, null on every never-parked task so the claim path is byte-identical). An admission guard (`maybe_park_for_active_window`) parks further LLM dispatches on the exhausted lane the same way rather than burning attempts that will 429, while non-LLM loop work — scanners, and the keystone merge of already-green PRs — keeps running (a reviewed-and-green PR still merges during the outage). The re-arm is the self-rescheduling `usage_window_recovery` loop-timer chain (`teatree.loops.usage_window_recovery`, on `LOOPS_QUEUE`, seeded by `ensure_maintenance_chains`, the same #1796 machinery — NO OS cron): once the reset instant passes it CLEARS the window, RELEASES the parked tasks (`not_before` nulled), PUMPS the loops, and posts ONE bot→user Slack line. The re-arm decision is deterministic and time-based (`resets_at` has passed), so no fragile "is the model reachable yet" inference probe is needed — a released task that re-hits the limit simply re-parks. **Harness-limited (NOT teatree-fixable, stated plainly):** this re-arms the WORKER/DB loop plane; resuming the interactive orchestrator SESSION's conversation, or auto-resuming the Claude Code harness's own session, at reset is outside teatree (no supported prompt-injection API) — the mitigation is architectural, keep moving loop ownership to this worker/DB plane so the interactive session is optional. The parked `TaskAttempt` carries the `limit_parked:` marker and is excluded from the repair-loop iteration budget (`task_repair.phase_attempts`) — a limit-park is a scheduling event, not a work iteration. Because it is a scheduling event on an UNCHANGED condition, a repeat records STATE rather than an append: `TaskAttempt.objects.create` folds a park whose reason repeats the task's most recent attempt verbatim into that row, bumping `park_repeats` and refreshing `ended_at` (a changed reason or an intervening real attempt always opens a new row, so every genuine transition still shows). Un-coalesced, the pollers that re-derive the park wrote one audit row each: 338,741 of 339,944 `TaskAttempt` rows against 1,203 real dispatches, in a 1.2 GB control DB — and, because the park rows inflated the denominator, they were what made cost coverage on real dispatches read as 0.35% when it is 1,203/1,203. The reconciliation ledger's park-spin detector (`_check_park_spin`) therefore counts park OBSERVATIONS — rows plus `park_repeats` — so coalescing cannot blind the alarm it exists to raise. Default ON after its soak (the whole point is unattended recovery); survives as a per-overlay escape hatch. While the flag is OFF the park path, the admission guard, and the recovery chain body are all inert — a limit records a terminal FAILED exactly as before.

**Loop presets + weekly-schedule layer — a read-time mask over the admission predicate ([#3159](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3159)).** Loop control is modal ("working hours", "owner away", "drain only", "low on budget", "everything off") but the two-plane `loop_state_admits` verdict was strictly per-loop. #3159 adds a read-time MASK above the base config and below the emergency brake, resolved top-down (first opinion wins per loop): **L4** a `LoopState` PAUSE/DISABLE hold (always wins, never touched by presets); **L3** a manual `LoopPresetOverride` (≤1 row — `t3 loop preset use <name>` until the next boundary / `--for` / `--until` / `--hold`, `t3 loop preset auto` clears); **L2** the active `LoopSchedule`'s governing slot (the latest slot-start ≤ now — coverage, not cron, so no gaps/overlaps; the active schedule is the `active_loop_schedule` `ConfigSetting`, `t3 loop schedule set-active <name>`); **L1** `Loop.enabled`. A `LoopPreset` (`teatree_loop_preset`) carries a **tri-state** `entries` map per loop (`true`/`false`/*absent = inherit*) and an `overlay_scope` focus allowlist. Every reference is **by name** (JSON keys, not FKs): a deleted preset/loop/schedule fails OPEN to base config with a WARNING + a `t3 doctor` finding — a broken schedule can never brick the fleet. The one behavioral code change is a REQUIRED third input to the single predicate — `loop_state_admits(*, configured_enabled, held, preset_state: bool | None)` ⇒ `not held and (preset_state if preset_state is not None else configured_enabled)` (extended to a fourth `forced` input under [#3248](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3248), below) — resolved once per tick (`teatree.core.mode_resolution.resolve_active_mode`, behind the `loops.enable_verdict.EnablePlanes` seam) and passed at every admission site (`loop_table._loop_admitted`, `loop_enabled` and its off-live-tick/connector-preflight callers) and every observability surface (`loops.live._mini_entries` → the statusline countdown + `t3 loop list`, and the shared `loops.enable_verdict.effective_verdicts` that `t3 loops list`, `t3 loop preset show` and the `dash.loop_control` read model consume), leaving the review-claim chokepoint's `LoopState`-only read untouched by documented design. `preset_state` carries NO default: the type checker structurally catches any admission/observability site that resolves a loop without the preset opinion (the drift the single predicate exists to prevent). **Empty-table no-op invariant** (the #1913/#1775 shape): with no preset, no active schedule, and no override, `preset_state` is `None` everywhere and loop admission is byte-for-byte the pre-#3159 two-plane verdict. A masked loop is skipped with its cadence anchor preserved, exactly like a held row. **A mask may not stop the BOX surviving ([#4188](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4188)).** `teatree.loops.mode_shape.LOAD_BEARING_LOOPS` (`inbox`, `housekeeping`, `idle_stack_reaper`, `local_stack_queue`, `resource_pressure`) is the tier no mask may quiet — the `low_power_preset_name` mode is the single exception, a token budget being the one reason to stop them — and no mask may admit `db_backup` while BOTH reclaim loops are quiet, the shape that can only ever consume disk and the one reached exactly when an operator grabs a halt mode mid-incident. The shared `preset_editing` write seam REFUSES both shapes, naming the loop (so the CLI and the dashboard cannot diverge), `seed_inertness` reports them as faults on any live mask including an operator-written one, and `0066_admit_load_bearing_loops` heals rows written before the guard. `off` therefore masks every WORK loop off, never the tier that can still recover the machine. The `t3 setup` seed installs 5 owner-editable presets ([#4202](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4202): `present`/`away`/`maintenance`/`low-token`/`off`, where `away` and `maintenance` differ on INTAKE — `away` keeps taking new work with the colleague-facing loop off, `maintenance` drains only, `ship`/`review` ON and `tickets`/`issue_implementer` OFF; a stored `offline` reference migrates to `off`, which shipped a byte-identical mask, and a holiday is whichever preset the owner wants while away — `off` for a hard stop, `maintenance` to drain — so no preset is "the holiday preset") + the `standard` / `always-away` schedules idempotently (never clobbering edits), and pins `active_loop_schedule` to `standard` through the provenance-aware `ConfigSetting.seed` so a fresh box runs the owner's working-hours calendar out of the box (Mon–Fri 09:00–16:00 `Europe/Vienna` → `present`, every other hour → `away`) while an operator who switched calendars or cleared the pin is never overridden on a re-seed. A small self-rescheduling `preset_transitions` chain (`LOOPS_QUEUE`, 0 tokens idle) handles side-effects ONLY — expiring a passed override and posting one Slack line per switch — so resolution never depends on it. A usage-window park auto-engages the `low-token` preset for the parked window's tenure behind the default-off `low_power_auto_engage` flag (re-pointable via `low_power_preset_name`), cleared on re-arm and never over a live user override. **No owner-intent silently rots.** A masked/idle loop is indistinguishable from "nothing to do" — the directive loop once sat masked for days while owner directives piled up at `CAPTURED`, never interpreted, producing zero signal. `t3 doctor` (`_check_intent_freshness`, `cli/doctor/checks_intent.py`) closes that gap: it HARD-FAILs (gates the exit code, like the self-heal silent-freeze detectors) when a consumable intent queue is non-empty while its consumer is not live, and WARNs (surfacing-only, like the dream/marker-jam advisories) when a live consumer lets an item age past the 24h `INTENT_FRESHNESS_THRESHOLD` (one directive-loop cadence). Each queue is scoped to exactly the rows its named consumer still has to drain, and "live" means every gate that consumer actually passes: the directive intake arc is consumed by `directive_loop` only when the loop row is unmasked AND its fail-closed INTAKE guard chain allows (`evaluate_intake_guards`, the DARK `directive_loop_enabled` flag first — the post-admission `evaluate_execution_guards` chain's extra `factory_score_enabled` gate never blocks intake, so probing it would call a draining consumer dead), so an unmask alone can never silence the finding while directives still never advance, and a `CLARIFYING` directive counts only once every clarify question of its generation is answered (`_advance_clarifying` otherwise waits on the owner, exactly like `RATIFY_PENDING`); the owner deferred-question arc is the `dispatch` poster's `unmirrored_pending()` rows only — a DELIVERED question stays pending until the human answers, so away-mode masking `dispatch` never reds the box. `_gather_intent_queues` is the seam for more queues. Each finding names the stuck items (capped, with an "and N more" tail, and age-free — the refs are the queue's identity and the WARN text already carries the threshold) and the remediation for what actually has to change.

**Presets/schedules are the handle + a tri-state emergency FORCED layer ([#3248](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3248)).** #3159's four-layer verdict could not express a manual per-loop force-ON that beats a preset force-OFF, so the emergency handle gains a tri-state FORCED plane on `LoopState` (`on`/`off`/`neutral`, TTL-bounded via `forced_until`, `forced_reason`), orthogonal to the hold `status`. `loop_state_admits(*, configured_enabled, held, preset_state, forced)` resolves **hold > forced > preset > base**: a held loop never runs, else a live `forced` opinion wins (force-on beats a preset force-off; force-off beats a preset force-on), else the #3159 preset mask, else `Loop.enabled`. `forced` is REQUIRED at every call site (the #3159/#3248 drift guard), and the empty-table no-op holds (`held=False`, `forced=None`, `preset_state=None` ⇒ `configured_enabled`). The hold + forced planes are read in ONE bulk query per tick (`LoopState.objects.control_planes`) so the #2584 N+1 guard stands. **Presets/schedules become the normal handle:** `t3 loop enable/disable/pause/resume` refuse without `--emergency` (guiding the operator to `t3 loop preset use` / `schedule set-active`), and `t3 loop override <name> on|off|clear [--for TTL] [--reason]` is the emergency force (the `loop_state` `override` verb). **The statusline loop line is single-line:** the injected preset segment composes `sched <name> · preset <name>` (⚠`manual` when a `LoopPresetOverride` governs rather than the schedule) `· ovr: <loop>±` (per-loop FORCED overrides diverging from the preset/base, via `loops.preset_status.manual_override_entries`), and the mini-loop list is filtered to a `due:` section of loops due within ~5 minutes instead of the full ~25-loop dump.

**Session-start auto-load is opt-in ([#256](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/256)).** The loop-*arming* machinery — the tick-owner claim (`handle_session_start_bootstrap`) and the `CronCreate` registration nudge (`handle_enforce_loop_on_prompt` / `_loop_registration_exempt`) — fires only when the session both opted into teatree (the `<session>.teatree-active` marker) AND the operator enabled autoload. The single arming gate is `hook_router._loop_auto_load_active` = `_teatree_active AND _autoload_enabled`. `autoload` is the ONE owner flag — env `T3_AUTOLOAD` first, then the DB store read cold via `_cold_db_bool`; defaults **OFF** and fails CLOSED on absence — and it both *engages* the session and *arms* its loops (the former separate `[loops] auto_load` / `T3_LOOPS_AUTO_LOAD` arming flag is subsumed). The loop **statusline** (`hooks/scripts/statusline.sh`) is a display-*visibility* surface, not loop-arming, so it gates on `autoload` **alone** — NOT the per-session `.teatree-active` marker. The marker is written by SessionStart-engage / a teatree-skill load, but the harness runs the loop in a background `bg-spare` daemon session (which gets the marker and owns the tick) while the owner's foreground TUI sessions frequently never get it, so ANDing the marker with autoload blanked the statusline in exactly the sessions the owner looks at; `autoload` on renders it in every one of the owner's sessions. The #256 colleague guarantee is unchanged because `autoload` defaults **OFF** and fails CLOSED: a colleague cloning the repo, even one who loads a teatree skill for dogfooding (marker present, autoload off), is never nagged to register a cron or shown the loop statusline; the loop owner opts in once and keeps the prior behaviour intact.

**Engagement is default-OFF ([#256](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/256)).** Installing the plugin must not force teatree onto every Claude session. A fresh session is *not engaged*: `handle_user_prompt_submit` early-returns after clearing `<session>.pending` so the skill suggester + the T3 CLI reminder are suppressed and the empty pending keeps the PreToolUse skill-loading gate from ever blocking (never-lockout); `handle_session_start_bootstrap` emits the one-line how-to advisory `_TEATREE_NOT_ACTIVE_ADVISORY` ("teatree is installed but not active … run /teatree … or run `t3 <overlay> config_setting set autoload true`") instead of arming the loop. The engagement seam is `hook_router._teatree_engaged` = `_autoload_enabled() OR _teatree_active(session) OR <session>.t3-engaged`. The DB-home setting `autoload` (`UserSettings.autoload`, default `False`, resolved cold by `hooks/scripts/teatree_settings.autoload_enabled` — env `T3_AUTOLOAD` first, then the DB store via `_cold_db_bool`, fail-CLOSED; a `[teatree] autoload` TOML value is ignored on read) is the owner's flip to auto-engage every session: when ON, `handle_session_start_bootstrap` touches `.teatree-active` BEFORE the `_loop_auto_load_active` check so today's loop bootstrap fires unchanged. The lighter `<session>.t3-engaged` marker (set by `handle_track_skill_usage` when the ORIGINAL Skill-tool token is `t3:`-namespaced) engages only the suggester — loop scheduling still consults exclusively `.teatree-active`, preserving downstream-overlay loop isolation. A not-engaged compact/resume skips the advisory but still runs `_merge_session_start_context`, so snapshot-recovery / hand-off context is never dropped. `autoload` is the single owner flag: it both *engages* a session and *arms* its loops — the former separate `[loops] auto_load` arming flag is subsumed, so one flip does both.

**Subsumed issues (WS5 — documented, not closed here).** [#789](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/789) (a non-owner session still arming the tick cron) is **subsumed**: under the WS1 claim/lease a non-owner tick finds nothing to claim, so the concern dissolves — #789 was closed-as-completed when WS3 landed and is **not** reopened. Board task #50 (the per-agent TODO-consolidation loop) is **subsumed by invariant 3 / WS4**; #50 is a project-board card, **not** a repository issue, so it is documented as subsumed here and tracked on the board — no repo issue to close. WS5 itself carries no GitHub closing keyword on the #786 umbrella; only an explicitly-authorized epic-completion step does.

**Deep mechanics live in [docs/blueprint/loop-topology.md](docs/blueprint/loop-topology.md).** The DB-lease singleton, the t3-master claim and `SessionStart` tick-owner record, the per-agent self-pump, the Stop-gate family, post-compaction snapshot recovery, the always-on 5-minute continuous stop-snapshotter (PR-20 — `teatree.core.stop_snapshot.prepare_stop`, called by the Stop slot / `PreCompact` / `t3 <overlay> session prepare-stop`, availability-independent so it refreshes recovery artifacts even while loops are paused, at-risk-worktree capture git-object-native under `refs/t3-resume/`), the three-stage tick (scan → dispatch → render), the full scanner set, multi-overlay/host/identity scanning, the auto-start/dispositions/completion phases, and §5.6.1 Statusline rendering / §5.6.2 Mode + training-wheel / §5.6.3 Mode posture all live there. CORE always-on or cross-referenced scanners worth naming here: `architectural_review` (cadence-and-merge-count, `architectural_review_disabled`); `scanning_news` ([#1191](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1191), with the `ask_before_creating_news_tickets` gate recording a `PendingArticleSuggestion` rather than auto-filing); `dogfood_smoke`/`eval_local` ([#1308](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1308)); `backlog_sweep` ([#2419](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2419), [#4344](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4344) — DAILY backlog GROUPING that closes nothing for real: every reduction folds the member's body into an existing host (`ticket fold`) and proves it landed (`ticket fold-check`) before the row retires, gated by `ask_before_backlog_sweep_closes`; the `Loop` row is the single switch, `backlog_sweep_disabled` ships open); `review_request_merge_react` (reacts `:merge:` once an MR merges); the closure-reverify Stop WARN (non-blocking so it cannot deadlock the loop) and its hard-blocking sibling the completion-claim Stop gate ([#2665](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2665), `handle_completion_claim_gate` — refuses turn-end on a multi-deliverable "done" lacking an on-target deliverable→evidence map; loop-driven turns only, kill-switch + `[skip-completion-gate: <reason>]` token); the `SessionHandover` hand-off (`t3 <overlay> handover`, injected on `SessionStart`; [#4194](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4194) — the DB row is the delivery surface and the XDG mirror is read ONLY when the DB is unreachable, an author holds at most one unclaimed row that later hand-offs are absorbed into behind a fence, and no row the DB accepts is claimable by nobody: a partial unique index plus two check constraints on `SessionHandover` refuse a self-addressed target and the `loop-runner` slot alias; the sub-agent wrap-up is ROW STATE — the `SessionHandover.subagent_wrapup` union of every agent its barriers ever enumerated, plus `last_barrier_at` and `barrier_ran_at_latest_handoff` — RENDERED onto the delivery surface (mirror + claimed payload) at delivery and never spliced into the payload, so an authored body can never be edited by the barrier, an agent whose worktree has since vanished is still named, and a hand-off that ran no barrier says so instead of inheriting an earlier barrier's freshness; and a payload that resolves EMPTY writes NO row at all, since a hand-off that transfers nothing is not durable state — ONE resolution, taken after the sub-agent barrier and immediately before the write, decides both the refusal and what is persisted, because state settling across that slow barrier otherwise let the gate pass on one payload while a second resolve wrote another); the open-PR statusline anchor ([#271](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/271)) read from a per-tick `open-prs.json` cache so the display costs no API call; the manual-MR reconciler ([#1912](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1912), `loop/manual_pr_reconcile.py`, invoked from `render_phase` alongside the open-PR snapshot writer) that upserts `PullRequest` rows from the same per-tick `my_pr.*` signals for manually-opened MRs resolving to a ticket (close-keyword footer + `Ticket.extra['prs']` fallbacks, unique on URL, footerless MRs stay statusline-only, a live-merged PR transitions via `mark_merged`) so a PR opened outside the ship pipeline still enters review-request tracking and the FSM; and the typed `jobs_for_domain` seam ([#1482](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1482)) partitioning the per-overlay scanner fan-out.

The [#1554](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1554) `issue_implementer` mini-loop closes the auto-implement-intake gate: each tick its per-overlay scanner claims open issues via the TOCTOU-safe `ImplementedIssueMarker.claim`. It is **default-OFF behind a triple gate** — the master `issue_implementer_enabled` flag, an `in_flight_count < limit` budget, and per-issue `claim()` idempotency. That `limit` is not a constant: `resolve_intake_concurrency` ([#3992](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3992)) hands back the resource loop's headroom-derived number, falling back to `issue_implementer_max_concurrent` verbatim whenever it is missing, stale, or switched off. Each claim emits `issue_intake.admitted` routing to `t3:orchestrator` as a **maker-side kickoff** — it starts the normal pipeline, issues no `MergeClear`, gains no merge authority (the §17.4 maker≠checker boundary holds).

**A held budget slot is released on evidence, and a jammed budget is visible ([#3978](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3978)).** The budget gate above is only as good as what frees a slot: with a small budget and a 24h stall grace, two claims that stop progressing close intake for a day, and nothing surfaces it — a full budget makes the scanner factory return `None`, so the tick legitimately does nothing and reports success. `ImplementedIssueMarker.find_stale` therefore releases a slot on the FACT that ends the attempt rather than on a grace the ticket will never reach: a MERGED PR completes the marker immediately (SHIPPED is deliberately not a `marker_release_states()` member, so a ticket frozen there after its PR landed satisfied no release condition at all), and a ticket with nothing queued and no open PR is held to a short dead-claim grace rather than the mid-flight one. Visibility is one reading — `core.intake.budget.read_intake_budget` — shared by the tick, which logs `IntakeBudget.report()` naming every held slot instead of declining silently, and by `t3 doctor check`, which HARD-FAILs (`_check_intake_budget_deadlock`) when a full budget is held entirely by claims with no active task and no open PR.

**A cancellation is a DECISION, and a decision survives the next tick ([#4105](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4105)).** The release rule above knew four ways an attempt ends, all of them accidents — terminal, landed, gone, stalled — so an operator cancelling a claim landed in the one bucket that undoes itself: a cancelled attempt has nothing queued and no PR, so the dead-claim grace released it as ABANDONED, the single state `claim()` re-claims from, and intake handed the same issue back within two hours (observed on [#4048](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4048), twice). `Ticket.newest_task_was_cancelled()` is the ONE reading of "an operator said stop" — a `FailureKind.CANCELLED` newest task — and `find_stale` releases such a marker as `DECLINED`: terminal, so the budget still self-heals, and outside the re-claim path, so the decline holds. It needs no grace, because a grace buys time for an attempt that might still be alive and a human has already said this one is over; `SUPERSEDED` is excluded, being rework the factory initiated. Something changing is what puts the issue back in play: a PENDING/CLAIMED task on the ticket holds the slot ahead of both branches, whatever the last outcome was. The `stuck_ticket_redispatch` drain reads the SAME predicate and skips such a ticket, because one actor honouring the decision while the other undoes it is no decision at all — a cancel leaves exactly the shape both of that sweep's admission predicates match, so it re-queued the phase on the next tick and two cancels were needed to stop it, by exhausting a repair budget that then pages a human about a doomed phase rather than an honoured one. `State.relinquished()` (ABANDONED + DECLINED) names the states in which the attempt was LET GO rather than finished — the fleet-claim heartbeat skips them, so no refreshed ref keeps a sibling instance out of work nobody is doing, and the completion receiver leaves them alone rather than erasing why the attempt ended.

**A landed merge is recorded where merges LAND, not where one route records them ([#3984](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3984)).** Both readings above are drawn from `PullRequest.state`, so the release rule and its alarm are only as good as what writes that field. It was written solely by the keystone post hook, and the sweep — the path most merges actually take — reaches the forge through `merge_pr_squash_bound` instead: its merges left the row reading `open` forever (measured: 32 of 33 rows), which held the slot *and* silenced the alarm, because a stale `open` reads to the alarm as proof of a live attempt. The write therefore sits at `core/merge/execution.execute_bound_merge` — the same single chokepoint every merge route already crosses for the gates above — so no route can land a merge without recording it, and record-and-proceed there (log, never raise) because the forge merge is already irreversible. `PullRequestQuerySet.reconcile_forge_states` is the convergence pass for rows merged out of band entirely: the intake tick runs it over the budget HOLDERS' rows before every budget read (bounded by the slot count, not the ledger size), and `t3 loop reclaim-markers` runs it over the whole overlay, so the operator lever no longer needs a forced-zero grace to free a slot whose PR demonstrably merged.

**ONE intake scanner, ONE decision function ([#3634](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3634)).** The two former issue-intake scanners (`assigned_issues`, `issue_implementer`) are folded into a single `issue_intake` scanner so the factory cannot hold two divergent opinions about which issue becomes work. Every candidate runs through `core.intake.factory_admission.decide_intake`, evaluated top-down, first match wins: (1) `needs-triage` present → IGNORE (maintainer hold); (2) one of the overlay's `exclude_labels` present → IGNORE (operator hold, [#4134](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4134)); (3) the row is an umbrella/epic tracking parent → IGNORE ([#4105](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4105)); (4) an active ticket / claim / forge read-back already exists → IGNORE; (5) author trusted → ACT immediately, with no admit label, no assignment, and no grace window; (6) author untrusted **and** the owner-applied admit label present → ACT; (7) author untrusted, no label → IGNORE (fail-closed). The admit label is the `issue_implementer_label` setting, defaulting to the shipped `t3-auto` convention (`factory_admission.DEFAULT_ADMIT_LABEL`) — a forge-only convention with no other code coupling. This retires the per-overlay `admission_policy` tiers and the `issue_implementer_require_label` narrowing gate, both superseded by the table.

**The exclude rule is the operator's reservation surface ([#4134](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4134)).** It reads `OverlayConfig.exclude_labels` — already plumbed to the scanner and, until now, never consulted there — and shares ONE predicate (`core.intake.label_admission.excluded`) with the GitLab sync path, so the two intakes cannot drift on what "excluded" means. It defaults to EMPTY, which excludes nothing, so an overlay that configured no denylist keeps its pre-#4134 verdicts. It sits directly under `needs-triage` because the two are the same kind of thing — a label the human applied to withhold an issue — and ABOVE the umbrella rule because an explicit per-issue operator act outranks a structural classification: a row that is both an epic and excluded logs the exclusion its operator wrote, not the shape the detector inferred. The two label rules keep separate verdicts because `needs-triage` is a shipped convention and the denylist is per-overlay data, and the log line has to say which one held the issue.

**An umbrella row is not an implementable unit ([#4105](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4105)).** The umbrella rule sits above "work exists" because umbrella-ness is a property of the ROW, not of what has happened to it. An epic carries no single acceptance criterion and no bounded diff, so an agent handed one holds a bounded `issue_implementer_max_concurrent` slot for an unbounded scope and displaces a real, implementable ticket for the whole run. `core.intake.umbrella.umbrella_reason` recognises one by three INDEPENDENT signals, any one sufficient: a LABEL in the operator-maintained `umbrella_issue_labels` set (the explicit exclusion list, per-overlay overridable, and an EMPTY set is the honest "none configured" rather than a silent fallback to the shipped one); a DECLARATION that the row is never closed ([#2663](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2663)); or the STRUCTURAL shape of a tracking parent — a checklist whose items are bare child issue references, and no acceptance / definition-of-done heading anywhere. The `Epic:` title prefix is deliberately NOT a signal: a prefix convention stops working the first time someone titles an epic differently, and admitting it as a disjunct would make the weakest signal sufficient on its own. An emphasised `DO NOT CLOSE` is admitted on the opposite grounds — it is not a way of naming a category but an explicit instruction that no state of the world ends the claim, which IS the unbounded-scope property the umbrella rule refuses. [#2663](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2663), the standing dream-pass ledger, carries no label and lists prose checklist items, so it defeated both other signals and a coding agent was dispatched at a row with nothing to implement. A false positive STARVES a genuinely implementable issue, so all three branches are narrow and the decline logs which signal fired: the declaration counts only where the directive both LEADS an emphasised line (a heading, or bold-opened text), sits outside a fenced code block (a quoted example documents the detector rather than invoking it), and ENDS there at line's end, sentence-final punctuation, or a dash introducing an aside — a colon, comma, or attached hyphen instead means the phrase runs on to describe some other object, so a heading naming another object is prose and an unemphasised "please don't close this yet" is a temporary hold on work that stays implementable. The two EXPLICIT signals are tested ahead of the inferred structural one, so a row carrying several reports the reason its author actually wrote; an acceptance heading defeats only the structural branch, because it withdraws an inference and there is none to withdraw once the row has stated the answer itself.

Discovery is scoped so the factory never fetches work it may not do: one author-scoped query per handle in the trusted union (`CodeHostBackend.list_authored_issues`) and one label-scoped query for the admit label (`CodeHostBackend.list_labeled_issues`), both bound to the overlay's own repo slugs. Selection narrows; the decision function decides — every candidate is RE-CHECKED at claim time through the shared author-trust decision, so an over-returning forge query cannot launder an untrusted author past the fail-closed last rule, and the classifier's private-repo bypass cannot hand an unlisted collaborator the keys to the factory. An unresolvable author, an unparsable issue URL, and an unset admit label all resolve to REFUSE.

**ONE author-trust decision function, two gates ([#3577](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3577)).** `core/review/author_trust.decide_author_trust(subject, *, gate, extra_trusted)` is the single home for "may the factory act on this author's work?", over the existing `classify_author` / `classify_pr_provenance` resolvers. Its types are `AuthorSubject` (slug / author / host kind / same-repo), `AutonomyGate` (`INTAKE` | `MERGE`) and `TrustVerdict` (`AUTONOMOUS` | `HUMAN_REVIEW`) — `TrustVerdict` rather than `Autonomy`, because `teatree.config.Autonomy` already names the operator's babysit/notify/full autonomy TIER, a different axis. All four call sites consume it, so intake and merge cannot disagree about who is trusted: `loop/scanners/issue_intake.author_is_trusted` and `core/review/stranger_pr.pr_is_admitted` at the `INTAKE` gate, `loop/scanners/pr_sweep_decision.untrusted_merge_provenance` and the §17.4.3 keystone `core/merge/authorization` at the `MERGE` gate. The two gates' asymmetry now lives INSIDE the decision rather than as a hand-rolled conjunction at each site: `INTAKE` additionally requires EXPLICIT trusted-set membership (closing the private-repo bypass), while `MERGE` applies the strict fork model. `classify_pr_provenance` takes `extra_trusted` so both gates draw on the same trust union.

**Intake is by TRUSTED AUTHOR, not by a hand-applied label ([#3235](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3235)).** The owner does not tag tickets, so the issue's author *is* the authority. The trusted set is an explicit **UNION** of three named sources — the owner's `user_identity_aliases`, the `trusted_issue_authors` allowlist (other humans whose issues the factory may act on), and the canonical `TrustedIdentity` rows; `config.effective_trusted_issue_authors` resolves the two config tiers and `core.review.author_trust` unions in the DB tier. This governs **intake only**: merge authority is untouched, and a substrate PR still needs a recorded human approver whoever filed the issue that spawned it.

**Stranger-PR policy — ignore until admitted ([#3634](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3634) §4).** An untrusted author's PR is invisible to the factory until the owner applies the admit label: `core.review.stranger_pr.pr_is_admitted` runs the SAME `decide_intake` table over the PR payload, and `ReviewerPrsScanner` skips an unadmitted PR before any review dispatch. That closes the prompt-injection surface an unattended reviewer would otherwise expose to arbitrary PR text and conserves review cycles. Once admitted the PR IS reviewed — and it is still never auto-merged, because merge authority is a separate axis the label does not touch. The gate is disarmed when no trusted set is configured (no admission context), so an ad-hoc caller keeps the pre-#3634 behaviour.

**MR triage is a pure ladder, and it has no silent no-op.** `core/review/mr_triage.triage(facts, *, thresholds)` is the single decision core for "what does this open MR need next?", over frozen `MrFacts` (draft flag, `CiState`, tri-state `ReviewRequestState`, repo owner, ages, stacked-base and escalation inputs) and returning one `TriageVerdict` (`TriageAction` + `TriageReason` + a rung-owned `detail`). The module does NO I/O: the caller reads the forge and does the arithmetic, so the policy is a table under test rather than a scanner's control flow. `_LADDER` is first-match-wins and its order is load-bearing — `FIX_CI` outranks EVERY other rung, a draft and a review-exempt repo included, because every MR owes green CI whatever else is true of it, so a red draft asks for the fix rather than sitting broken in silence. Below red CI a draft leaves triage entirely, review exemption answers for everything social, and the stacked-on-unreviewed-base flag, the repo-fit `ESCALATE_MODEL` rung and the `WORK_GROUP_NOT_READY` hold (`WAIT`, while any sibling in the same unit of work is not review-ready) outrank both the review-request and the nag rungs, so an MR whose disposition needs a judgement call — or whose group would be reviewed as a fragment — is never auto-pinged. Anything the ladder does not match falls to `ASK_OWNER`: a gap in the policy surfaces as a question, never as a dropped MR. `TriageThresholds` carries the four durations — `engineering_nag` is DERIVED (the same 2 days `ReviewNagScanner` re-pings on FIRST), `devops_nag` and `stale_no_review` are INFERRED, so both are named, overridable settings rather than constants, and `nag_backoff_cap` is CHOSEN (the operator's `review_nag_max_interval_days`, resolved by the transport and passed in, since nothing in the data fixes where re-asking should stop widening); `RepoOwner` picks between the two nag intervals and every unknown-owner path resolves to the PATIENT (DevOps) one.

**A work group is connected components over shared signals, never one group key.** `core/review/work_group.group_members(items, *, generic_scopes)` answers "which open merge requests are ONE unit of work?" over `(url, title)` pairs, returning each url's whole group, so the batch gate can hold an individually-ready merge request until every sibling in its group is ready too. Like `mr_triage` it is pure — stdlib only, no forge read, no Django, no clock — so the policy is a table under test and the caller owns every read. Sharing a signal is TRANSITIVE, so the pass is union-find rather than a single group key: A and B on a ticket reference plus B and C on a feature flag is one group of three, and a single-key grouper would split that trio and release A while C is still unfinished. `signals_for` extracts three `GroupSignal` kinds — a `.../-/(issues|work_items)/<n>` url or a `<repo>#<n>` reference (`ticket`), a bracketed tag (`flag`), and the conventional-commit `(scope)` — as forge-agnostic supersets of the shapes a release-notes validator enforces, so core carries no forge- or project-specific prefix. Extraction is deliberately NARROW because the two errors are not symmetric: an over-grouped merge request waits on a sibling it has nothing to do with, silently and forever, while an under-grouped one is merely released on its own. Hence the `generic_scopes` filter (a scope every housekeeping change shares says nothing about being one unit of work), hence the placeholder and security-scanner bracket literals that are not feature flags, and hence a signal-less merge request being a group of exactly itself — which satisfies the batch gate trivially. `core/review/work_group_settings.generic_scopes_from_settings` is the settings seam, kept OUT of the grouper so it stays pure, and is the ONE place `work_group_generic_scopes` becomes the frozenset the grouper takes.

**R1 — a merge request is broadcast for review with its whole work group or not at all.** `core/gates/review_request_batch_gate.work_group_ready(mr_url=…, overlay_name=…)` answers whether every open member of the subject's group is review-ready, over the operator's GLOBAL cross-project open-merge-request listing rather than a url-prefixed subset: a filtered list makes a cross-repo group look smaller than it is, and a group that looks complete fires early — the exact premature broadcast the gate exists to prevent. A member is ready when its draft state is confirmed NOT_DRAFT, its `CiState` is GREEN, and it is not held by a live pause reaction (`resumed_at` lifts that permanently). **Every axis fails CLOSED** — `DraftState.UNKNOWN`, a `CiState` the allowlist cannot name, `PauseState.UNKNOWN`, an unresolvable host, an erroring listing, and a merge request absent from the listing all answer not-ready — because `review_request_draft_gate` already shipped the opposite shape once and a probe that answered the benign value on a failed read made the owner's hold mechanism inert exactly when it was needed. The one axis that does NOT hold silently is an oversize group: past `work_group_max_members` the shared signal is likelier a coincidence than one unit of work, so `ask_mr_state` surfaces the merge request to the owner instead of holding it where nobody can see. A group of one satisfies the gate trivially, so R9's standalone merge request is never stalled. `review_exempt_repos_count_toward_group_readiness` decides whether an exempt member still gates its group (default `true`, the conservative reading — an unready infrastructure change keeps holding the batch it supports); an exempt member is always LISTED and never offered a broadcast line either way. Both review-request chokepoints (`review_request_post`, `review_request_check`) consult `work_group_batch_refusal` AFTER the draft gate and BEFORE `should_post_review_request`, so a held batch leaves no orphan `ReviewRequestPost` claim to wedge every later post; it is inert until `require_work_group_batch` is on. `t3 review-request group-status [--mr-url …]` (`review_request_groups`) is the read-only half — every group, every member's blockers, and the exact ordered `t3 review-request post` lines for a ready one — posting nothing and raising no question, and exiting non-zero on an unreadable forge so a failed listing can never render as a reassuringly empty queue.

**One payload→`CiState` reading, shared by every review surface.** `core/review/mr_ci_state` holds `PIPELINE_FIELDS`, `GREEN_STATUSES` and the `CI_BY_STATUS` table, so the batch gate, `MrTriageScanner` and `MyPrsScanner` cannot drift on what "green" means. The table is an ALLOWLIST: a cancelled, skipped or manual pipeline — and any status nobody has seen — resolves to `UNKNOWN`, which never satisfies a green and never names a failure, because a status this code does not understand is exactly the one it must not guess about. An unenriched payload (none of `PIPELINE_FIELDS` present) is likewise `UNKNOWN` rather than red.

**A cross-project MR list carries no CI, so the red-MR lane reads it live.** GitLab's `/merge_requests?scope=created_by_me` payload has no `head_pipeline`, no `status_check_rollup` and no `mergeable_state`, so every MR in it resolved to the empty status — which `MyPrsScanner` classifies as legitimately in-progress. `my_pr.failed` was structurally unreachable for those MRs however red they were. `loop/scanners/my_prs_ci.BoundedCiEnricher` fills the gap per MR through `CodeHostQuery.required_checks_status` — never `classify_required_rollup` directly, because on GitLab the rollup fetch returns PIPELINES rather than check-runs and an empty required set classifies `green`, manufacturing a false green for an MR nobody read. It inherits that query's fail-CLOSED bias (an errored rollup surfaces as `failed`, routing the MR to the debug agent rather than letting a red MR read as benign). Bounded on four axes: URL-allowed PRs only, payload-carried-nothing only, a per-tick cap, and a memo keyed on the head SHA so an MR nobody pushed to is read once and a new push is a new key.

**The re-ping interval belongs to whoever reviews the repo.** `ReviewNagScanner` resolved one 2-day window for every MR, so an infrastructure repo reviewed by a separate rota was re-pinged on the engineering cadence. The interval comes from `TriageThresholds.nag_interval(owner)` — the base the per-attempt backoff below multiplies — with the owner resolved per MR through the `OverlayReview.repo_owner_for_slug` hook. Core's default answers ENGINEERING for every repo — deliberately NOT the ladder's fail-safe, because `MrFacts` defaults to the PATIENT owner so a verdict built from missing facts does not act, whereas an overlay that declares no ownership model must keep the cadence it shipped with. An overlay with a model owns its own unknown-repo answer. The mention and the text are unchanged: the interval is the only thing ownership varies.
