**Each unanswered re-ask waits longer, and the nag train ends in a question rather than forever.** A flat interval re-pings the same group at the same rate whether it is the first ask or the twentieth, which is how a reminder becomes noise people learn to ignore. `TriageThresholds.nag_interval_for_attempt(owner, attempt)` returns `min(nag_interval(owner) * fibonacci_step(attempt), nag_backoff_cap)`, so the Fibonacci step MULTIPLIES the per-owner base rather than replacing it and the engineering/DevOps patience distinction survives the backoff: engineering widens 2, 2, 4, 6, 10, 16, 26 days, DevOps 5, 5, 10, 15, 25. `core/modelkit/fibonacci.fibonacci_step` is the shared unitless sequence — the local-stack queue multiplies it by a minute, the nag by a day — so the two schedules cannot drift apart. `ReviewNagScanner` reads the attempt from `ReviewRequestPost.nag_count` and claims it in the SAME atomic conditional `UPDATE` as `last_nag_at`, restoring both on the gated and the failed-transport path, because a re-ask that never reached Slack must not advance the backoff — otherwise a permanently-blocked row walks itself to the ceiling having delivered nothing. `nag_backoff_at_cap` compares the UNCLIPPED product (the `min` has already erased the overshoot), and at the ceiling the scanner records an owner question through `ask_mr_state` instead of nagging again: the group has been asked enough, so the remaining question is for the person who owns the merge request.

**The nag fails CLOSED on every axis, because its harmful direction is POSTING.** A re-ping is a colleague-visible `@engineers` mention, so an unverifiable merge request must not produce one. `_is_approved` returned a bare `bool` and answered "not approved" when the probe raised — the same shape as the draft probe that once made the user's own hold mechanism inert, and the last surviving instance of it here. `ApprovalReadState` (`APPROVED` / `NOT_APPROVED` / `UNKNOWN`, the sibling of `DraftState` over the `ApprovalState` payload) makes the unreadable case its own answer, and every unreadable answer now SKIPS: no code-host backend, a failed open-state lookup, an `UNKNOWN` open state, an unparsable URL, `DraftState.UNKNOWN`, `ApprovalReadState.UNKNOWN`, and `PauseState.UNKNOWN`. A merge request whose review request the owner PAUSED (`review_pause.read_pause_state`) is likewise never pinged — escalating past the owner's own hold would @-mention a group about work its author has said is not ready. None of these close the row, so a later merge-react still fires and the next tick retries once the read works.

**Review exemption is a declared axis, never a reading of repo ownership.** On some repos the owner asks for review in person, so a posted review request is noise a colleague has to dismiss. `core/review/repo_exemption` answers "is this repo exempt?" from two explicit declarations — the `review_exempt_repos` setting and the `OverlayReview.review_exempt_repo_slugs` hook — matched on the shared host-stripped leading-segment-prefix grammar `private_repos` uses, so a repo pattern means one thing everywhere. The two are a TIER and a PIN, not a union: the hook is the table an overlay ships, and the setting is the operator's opinion over it, winning in BOTH directions because a `!`-prefixed entry SUBTRACTS an exemption rather than only adding one — a union could only widen, so an overlay-declared exemption outlived every operator opinion and a changed policy needed a code change and a merge. Both feed one flat list where the DEEPEST match decides (`slug_segment_depth`, the same host-stripped segment axis the match runs on), so `!org/one-repo` carves a single repo out of an `org` namespace exemption and the source that wrote a pattern carries no weight of its own — a pin beats the overlay by being written at its target's own depth, never by being read second. A TIE at equal depth resolves to NOT exempt, the same direction every unresolvable input already fails toward, so a contradiction is never read as an exemption. It is deliberately NOT derived from `repo_owner_for_slug`, whose fail-safe answers DEVOPS for every repo it does not recognise: an exemption read off that would silently cover every unrecognised repo, and a review request that never goes out is the one failure nobody sees. Both `review_request_post` and `review_request_check` refuse an exempt repo with `reason=review_exempt_repo` and exit 2, BEFORE the dedup claim (an orphan `ReviewRequestPost` would wedge every later post on `already_claimed`) — the same position, and the same rationale, as the draft gate. The ladder carries the matching rung `MrFacts.review_exempt` → `TriageAction.NONE`, placed directly BELOW the failed-CI rung because an exempt merge request owes no review request but still owes green CI. Both sources default empty, so the whole path ships inert.

**The MR-triage surveyor decides and surfaces; it never acts.** `loop/scanners/mr_triage_scan.MrTriageScanner` is the other half of the pure ladder — it reads the operator's own open MRs, does the arithmetic `triage()` refuses to do, and emits one `mr_triage.verdict` signal per MR into the `action_needed` statusline zone. It has NO post path and NO dispatch: every action the ladder can name is colleague-visible or a delegation the owner has not made, so surfacing is the whole behaviour and turning the gate on cannot produce a colleague-visible action. It lives in `Domain.SHIP` (the ship loop is enabled and ticks; `followup` is not) behind the default-OFF `mr_triage_enabled`, gated exactly like `_triage_assessor_scanner_for` — with the default config no scanner is built, so the fan-out is byte-for-byte unchanged. Facts it cannot read honestly stay UNKNOWN rather than assumed: absence of a `ReviewRequestPost` row is NOT proof nobody was asked, and an unreadable approval probe takes the MR out of the requested lane, so both surface as `ASK_OWNER` instead of a confident wrong verdict. The scan is TWO-PASS: `group_members` runs first over the UNFILTERED global listing to resolve every work group and each member's readiness (not-draft AND `CiState.GREEN`, both failing CLOSED), and only then is each MR triaged with `work_group` / `work_group_unready_members` populated — grouping a url-prefixed subset would make a cross-repo group look smaller than it is, and a group that looks settled surfaces a fragment as review-ready. Readiness is resolved only for MRs that HAVE siblings, and each MR's `CiState` is read at most once per tick, so the pass costs no enrichment round trip a group of one would not need. The nag-patience inputs come from the same `OverlayReview.repo_owner_for_slug` hook the review nag uses, so the two can never disagree about how long a repo waits.

**The lease covers raw branch/PR work, not only the ticket ([#3561](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3561)).** The ticket lease excludes two lifecycle actors from one ticket but has no visibility into work done at the raw git/PR level — a hand-opened PR, an ad-hoc `git worktree add`, a direct branch push — so work that never entered the lifecycle was unlocked by construction. `core/work_lease.py` registers a claim at the two seams where branch/PR work becomes real (`loop/manual_pr_reconcile.py` and `core/provision/worktree_adopt.py`) under every identity the work is known by: the branch, the PR, and the ISSUE it serves. The issue identity is the join that makes raw work visible to the lifecycle — `foreign_work_holder` reads it, and `IssueIntakeScanner._claim` (covering both the plain marker claim and the fleet-ref claim) DEFERS to a live foreign holder rather than blocking, the least invasive resolution and the one that never stands in a human's way. The rows are `LoopLease` records under a `work:` prefix, so this reuses the backend-agnostic conditional-`UPDATE` CAS with no new table, and the TTL (`DEFAULT_WORK_TTL_SECONDS`, 4h — long enough that a human mid-PR is not preempted between pushes, short enough that an abandoned claim frees the loop within a working period) is the fallback release.

**WRITE parallel, MERGE serial ([#3634](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3634) §1).** The `wip` fan-out is split into two independently-budgeted lanes by phase. Implementation (coding / testing / reviewing) runs `write_wip` wide; the merge lane (shipping) is single-flight, so the next PR always rebases against what just landed — that serialization IS the conflict-safety guarantee, and `merge_wip` is clamped to 1 rather than honoured above it. Each lane counts its own in-flight rows, so a saturated merge lane never starves implementation. Within a lane, candidates are listed in admission-priority order and then spread round-robin across a CHEAP area key (`loop.phases.conflict_area.area_key` — the ticket's declared repos, else the issue URL's repo slug), so a fan-out prefers tickets in different repos. It is deliberately a heuristic, not conflict prediction: no diff fetch, no merge-base probe, and it only re-orders — nothing is dropped, so an unknowable area can never starve. Set with `t3 <overlay> wip split N`.

**Fleet-safety Stage 1 — idempotent read-back as the safety net.** Running two teatree instances (a laptop + a headless box) against one GitHub repo double-claims work, because coordination state (`ImplementedIssueMarker`, the outward-post ledgers) is per-instance SQLite while the work domain (issues, branches, PRs) is global on the forge. Stage 1 does NOT introduce shared state — the durable claim-ref mutex is Stage 2. It makes the local coordination safe against double-claims via idempotency, on three fronts. **(1) Stable instance identity:** `teatree.instance_id.instance_id()` returns a durable UUID persisted once in the machine data dir (`instance_id.machine_data_dir`, the non-isolated dir a worktree and the main clone both resolve), stable across restarts and distinct per machine; it is stamped into `ImplementedIssueMarker.claimed_by_instance` at claim time (the identity Stage 2's claim refs will fence on) and surfaced by `t3 info`. **(2) Pre-dispatch forge read-back:** before `ImplementedIssueMarker.claim`, `teatree.loop.scanners.forge_readback` reads the forge's open and merged PRs (reusing `list_my_prs`, asking for the UN-enriched hits since the read-back never looks at CI state) for an existing `<ticket_number>-*` branch or a referencing PR, scoped to the issue's repo, and skips the claim when found. The corpus is bucketed once per tick into a `ReadbackIndex` keyed by the numbers each PR cites, so a candidate reads only the PRs that could name it: re-deciding an already-decided issue was a scan of every PR, which cost the product of two growing sets and consumed the whole scan budget before the newest issues were reached ([#4466](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4466)). The scanner also stops itself at `issue_intake_pass_budget_seconds` (shipped 45.0, below the scan phase's 60s pool deadline, past which a scanner is ABANDONED rather than stopped) and records where it stopped in `IntakeScanCursor`. A resume point is left only by an INCOMPLETE pass, so the next tick continues at the frontier instead of dropping the same tail forever, while a finished pass still starts at the oldest candidate and the first freed slot goes to the longest-waiting issue. Three consecutive incomplete passes are a `t3 doctor check` FAILURE (`_check_intake_pass_incomplete`), not just a worker-log WARN — a **net, not a mutex**: it closes most of the cross-instance window the local ledger cannot see, but two instances can still both read clean in the same tick before either opens a PR. **(3) Outward idempotency keys from work-domain identity:** an outward-post idempotency key derived from a local autoincrement pk shifts between instances, defeating any future cross-instance dedup; the colleague-facing ledgers (`ScannedBroadcast` on `(channel, slack_ts)`, `ReviewRequestPost` on `mr_url`, `MRReviewLock` on `(slug, pr_id)`, `OutboundClaim`/`BotPing` on caller keys built from commit SHA / repo-slug) are already work-domain-derived; the two genuinely pk-derived keys — `DeferredQuestion`'s bot→user resurface/mirror keys — now derive from the stable `tool_use_id` (`DeferredQuestion.stable_notify_ref`, falling back to an instance-qualified pk).

**Fleet-safety Stage 2 — the durable claim-ref mutex.** Stage 1's read-back is a net; Stage 2 is the actual MUTEX, so N instances can never double-claim one work item. The primitive is a **git ref update as a server-side compare-and-swap**, enforced by the same forge that owns the work domain — no new database. A claim lives at `refs/teatree/claims/<slug>` (`teatree.core.fleet.claim`, Django-free): `acquire` creates it with a plain push — the receive-pack ref transaction rejects a create when the ref already exists, and a random nonce per claim commit makes an existing ref never a fast-forward of a rival's fresh commit, so exactly one create wins; `heartbeat`/`steal_if_expired` re-point it with `git push --force-with-lease=<ref>:<observed-sha>` (CAS against the observed value), so exactly one steal wins and a stealer that lost the race is rejected server-side. The commit sha the ref points at IS the fencing token: `is_held_by_me` re-reads the ref, and a stolen-from instance sees a sha that is no longer its own. `FleetClaimUnavailableError` distinguishes an unreachable forge from a normal contended loss (a local `mktree`/`commit-tree` failure is classified the same way, per the module contract). It ships behind the **DEFAULT-OFF kill-switch `fleet_claim_enabled`** (per-overlay, DB-home), wired at the issue-implementer dispatch (`IssueIntakeScanner._claim`), NOT in the model manager — so `teatree.core.models` keeps no dependency on the higher `teatree.core` coordination modules (the tach boundary). When ON the mutex governs the whole in-flight lifecycle, so exactly-once holds end to end (not just at claim time):

- **Claim.** The scanner wins the ref FIRST and the local `ImplementedIssueMarker` becomes a CACHE of it (`cache_from_fleet_claim` stamps the fencing sha onto `claim_ref_sha`). `resolve_claim_repo` pushes the claim only to a clone whose `origin` slug MATCHES the issue's `owner/repo` — a name-only/`T3_REPO` fallback that resolves the wrong remote fails SAFE (does not claim) rather than split the mutex across remotes.
- **Heartbeat.** A per-tick sweep (`heartbeat_inflight_claims`, on the issue-intake scanner — emitted even at full budget so it never stalls) re-affirms every in-flight claim with a `--force-with-lease` re-point at a cadence comfortably shorter than the TTL (`DEFAULT_TTL_SECONDS` = 4h vs the ~1h tick), so a claim can never expire and be stolen mid-dispatch. A `ClaimLost` (the CAS failed → someone stole it) ABANDONS the marker so the in-flight work aborts rather than push under a lost claim.
- **Fence.** Every outward write is re-verified fail-closed against `is_held_by_me` via one shared `ticket_claim_is_lost` helper: the sync pre-ship gate (`run_fleet_claim_fence_gate`), the async `execute_ship` (immediately before BOTH the branch push AND the PR-open — the sync gate ran back at `pr create`, and the claim can be stolen in the async gap), and the orphan-branch PR-create (`_ensure_pr`). Unreachable ref infra while ON fails SAFE at each fence (does not push under an unconfirmable claim, logs loudly).

- **Zero object footprint.** Every object a claim op writes — the throwaway claim commit, and the rival commit an expiry probe fetches to read its metadata — is routed into an EPHEMERAL object directory (`claim._ephemeral_odb`: `GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY` at a temp dir, with the clone's real object DB attached as the READ-ONLY alternate push/fetch negotiation needs) that is deleted when the op ends; the expiry probe fetches with no destination refspec and `--no-write-fetch-head`, so it leaves the ref store untouched too. The remote ref is the claim's only durable home, so nothing local is needed once the push lands. The bug class this forecloses: an op writing into the shared clone leaves an unreachable loose object, nothing on the claim path triggers git's auto-gc, and at the defaults the footprint then grows by one object per in-flight claim per ~1h heartbeat tick, without bound, for as long as the switch is ON. Isolation rather than pruning is deliberate: there is no pass that could ever drop a live claim. Pinned by `tests/teatree_core/fleet/test_claim_objects.py` (footprint probe with a plant-the-old-write control, plus the fail-closed half proving a live claim is still enforceable and its metadata still readable after the cycle).

Flipping the switch OFF restores Stage 1's local-only `get_or_create` behaviour exactly. Two limitations bound what the mutex covers while it is ON:

- **The §17.4 merge keystone is NOT fenced.** `ticket_claim_is_lost` guards three writes — the sync pre-ship gate, `execute_ship`'s branch push + PR-open, and the orphan-branch `_ensure_pr` — and `merge_ticket_pr` is not one of them (`chokepoint_registry.MERGE_KEYSTONE` declares no claim gate). So losing a claim stops nothing once the PR exists: `heartbeat_inflight_claims` marks the marker ABANDONED, but abandonment bites only at a fenced write, and the merge is reached through the `MergeClear`, which carries no claim reference. An instance whose claim was stolen still squash-merges its own PR, and `MergeAudit` records no claim ownership, so an operator cannot tell afterwards which instance held the ref at merge time. What stands between that and a cross-instance double-merge is not the mutex — a rival would need its own `MergeClear` + `merge_safe` `ReviewVerdict` (per-instance SQLite) on a competing PR, which the read-back net and Stage 3's forge-authoritative PR budget both work against — and both of those are nets, so the residual window is unmeasured rather than closed. Wiring the existing fence in is not a drop-in either: its fail-CLOSED posture is cheap at push time (refuse, retry next tick) but at the terminal step it would refuse a reviewed, green, SHA-bound merge whenever the claim-ref infra is unreachable, and which posture the keystone should take is undecided.
- **Claim-repo resolution is single-root and forge-host-blind.** `resolve_claim_repo` finds the clone by BARE repo name under one `clone_root()` (`find_clone_path` warns and takes the alphabetically-first match on a basename collision), then checks that clone's `origin` — but both sides of that check drop the forge host (`git.remote_slug` and `owner_repo_from_issue_url` both yield `owner/repo`). For an overlay spanning two forges: a same-basename, different-slug collision makes the guard refuse and the acquire fail safe, so that issue is **never claimed** while the switch is ON and the operator sees `resolved clone … does not host … (origin mismatch)` repeat every tick with the issue never picked up; and the same `owner/repo` slug on two forges **passes** the guard against the wrong-forge clone, pushing the claim ref to the wrong remote and splitting the mutex across forges — the exact outcome the guard's contract claims to prevent. A strict host match is not the drop-in fix: an SSH host alias, a mirror hostname, or a `file://` origin each read as a different host than the issue URL's, turning working single-forge claims into fail-safe skips.

**Fleet-safety Stage 3 — forge-authoritative PR budget.** The same per-instance-SQLite blind spot that Stage 1's read-back nets on the CLAIM path applies on the PR-CREATE path: the per-(repo, ticket) budget gate (north-star PR-2, above) counted only local `PullRequest` rows + `extra["pr_url_by_branch"]`, so a sibling instance's just-opened PR was invisible until the local reconciler imported it — a one-tick window in which two instances could each open a PR for one ticket, breaking invariant I2 (at most one open PR per `(ticket, repo)`). Deterministic branch names + GitHub's one-PR-per-head-branch rule foreclose the identical-branch case, not two different branches for one ticket. Stage 3 makes `check_pr_budget` forge-authoritative by unioning a live `host.list_my_prs` read (`core/gates/pr_budget_forge`) with the local set at all three create seams (`_ensure_pr`, the ship fail-fast gate, and the ship chokepoint the loop's task-driven ship converges on) — repo-scoped, ticket-attributed by the shared `<slug>#<n>` close-keyword join, deduped by URL, and fail-open on any forge error (see the north-star PR-2 paragraph for the full mechanism). Like Stage 1's read-back it is a **net, not a mutex** — the durable claim-ref mutex remains Stage 2 — but it closes the PR-create half of the double-open window the local ledger cannot see. The shared close-keyword parser lives in `teatree.utils.close_keywords` because `teatree.core` (domain) may not import `teatree.loop` (orchestration).

**A disabled loop is never overdue ([#4066](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4066)).** The statusline's `overdue:` section judged lateness from `next_fire_at` alone, so a loop with no next-fire instant read as late — and a DISABLED loop has none BECAUSE nothing scheduled it, not because it slipped. `snapshot_warmer` and `triage_assessor` were therefore reported overdue permanently, with no state that could ever clear the alarm; an alarm that cannot clear is indistinguishable from no alarm, and it had already spent the banner a genuinely-late loop needs. The schedule row is now `MiniLoopSchedule(name, next_fire_at, cadence_seconds, enabled)` — a typed record rather than a tuple that could not express the difference — and `enabled` (the row's own `Loop.enabled`, what `t3 loop list` prints) gates the overdue predicate. It is deliberately NOT the same question as `admitted`: a base-disabled loop a preset forces on stays admitted and keeps its due-soon countdown, because the tick may fire it, but it is still nothing anyone is waiting for and so can never be reported late. "Enabled but never fired" remains genuinely overdue.

### 5.7 Self-Improving Monitor

A detector swarm that rides the same tick the regular `/loop` runs. It watches for smells the rest of the loop cannot self-report — dispatcher silently skipping a phase, a `MergeClear` issued but never reconciled, a statusline entry whose evidence has gone stale — and converts each into a `SelfImproveFiring` row plus a graduated action (`log → statusline → slack → ticket → auto_fix`, monotonic ladder). It is the legibility substrate §§17.4–17.8 relies on. Auto-fix is whitelisted: today only `StaleStatuslineEntryDetector` carries `auto_fix = True`, and its self-heal is wired for real — the prior `_default_rerender` no-op is retired (#2625) in favour of the idle render seam `teatree.loop.phases.render.rerender_statusline`, which re-renders the zones file so a merged-PR / terminal-ticket URL drops out. Because the heal is idempotent and side-effect-free, the detector declares `max_rung = auto_fix` and the action ladder sends an `auto_fix` report straight to its ceiling on the FIRST observation — a graduated climb would be pointless (surfacing a stale statusline on the statusline it is about to re-render) and would in fact never reach `auto_fix`, since a persistent smell keeps the same `state_hash` and the cool-down would pin it at rung 1 forever (#2625 Part B). The detector stays in the domain layer and never reaches up into the orchestration render seam (tach-enforced): instead the orchestration entry point that drives the cheap self-improve tier — the dedicated `loop_self_improve` slot — injects `teatree.loop.phases.render.self_improve_rerender` as the ladder's `auto_fix_callable`; the schedule's `_detector_auto_fix` adapter (routing a detector's own `rerender`) is the fallback for a directly-constructed detector with no injected seam. This covers stale *entries*; whole-render *age* (a dead loop, when no tick runs at all) is caught instead by the read-path freshness banner since the detector cannot fire when the loop is not ticking. The shipped detector set (`detectors/registry.py`) is `DispatchGapDetector`, `ForgottenMergeDetector`, `StaleStatuslineEntryDetector`; additional `auto_fix` slots land with their own structural whitelist test. **The cheap tier is the only tier with detectors.** `--tier medium|expensive` (the sets sketched but never built in the § 5.7 plan) and any unknown tier name are REFUSED: `detectors_for_tier` raises `UnimplementedTierError`, and both surfaces — the Typer `t3 loop self-improve run` and the `loop_self_improve` management command it delegates to — exit 2 with that message before the `loop-self-improve` lease is taken. An empty detector list would let the cycle report a clean success having scanned nothing; the loud refusal is the honest contract. `--tier all` is the union of implemented tiers (today: cheap).

Sibling loop scanners (under `loop/scanners/`, not `SelfImprove` detectors) close the gaps the detectors only surface:

| Scanner | Closes | Contract |
|---|---|---|
| `PrSweepScanner` ([#1248](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1248), wired [#1257](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1257)) | forgotten / conflicted / un-reviewed merges | Invokes the §17.4 keystone merge for any open PR whose `MergeClear` is actionable, head SHA matches, and required checks are green (`--fallback-uv-audit` escalation when the only red check is `uv-audit` and `main` is red too). A conflicted PR emits `pr_sweep.flag_conflict` ([#78](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/78)), flag only — never an auto-rebase; a solo bypass lacking a recorded cold-review emits `pr_sweep.flag_no_review` ([#68](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/68)) (below). A solo bypass whose live head carries an UNRECONCILED HOLD emits `pr_sweep.flag_held` and arms no further reviewer ([#4380](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4380)): `ReviewVerdict.objects.unreconciled_holds_at` asks whether anyone said no and never took it back, which — unlike the newest-wins `effective_state_at` the merge keystone shares — a later `merge_safe` from a DIFFERENT reviewer does not answer. Two reviewers running concurrently on one unchanged tree disagreed, and the autonomous no-CLEAR merge resolved the contradiction by timestamp in the direction of merging. `pr_sweep_decision.head_review_state` names the two shapes apart (on `HeadReview.hold_reason`) because they are different events to the owner they escalate to, selected by whether `ReviewVerdict.objects.standing_merge_safe_at` finds a non-stale `merge_safe` standing beside the hold: with one, `reason=contested_hold_at_head` — a real two-reviewer disagreement, necessarily two DISTINCT identities since a re-record by the same identity updates its own row; without one, `reason=hold_at_head` — the ordinary outcome of every cold review that holds, where reporting a disagreement would name a second reviewer who does not exist. That selector is deliberately NOT the newest-wins `authorizing_verdict_at`: recording ORDER decides what a merge may rest on, not whether two reviewers disagree, and gating the wording on it reported a third of contested heads — the ones whose HOLD landed second — as ordinary lone holds. Both refuse, both escalate, and the flag carries the `(verdict id, reviewer)` refs so the DM names who stands where. A MERGED attempt names the newest-wins `ReviewVerdict.objects.authorizing_verdict_at` instead — what the merge actually rested on — so a no-CLEAR merge records WHICH review authorised it rather than only that no CLEAR existed. Fail-closed HERE ONLY: the guard sits on the no-CLEAR bypass, where no human authorised anything; the CLEAR path keeps newest-wins because a `MergeClear` IS the human reconciliation. The holding reviewer lifting their own hold (one `update_or_create` row per normalised identity per head), a CLEAR at that SHA, or a new commit all clear it. A PR with any failing REQUIRED check whose branch is BEHIND `main` judged a base it has since fallen behind, so its verdict is UNKNOWN rather than red — a `gh run rerun` re-tests the run's pinned OLD base, so a fix that landed on `main` is unreachable to it. BEHIND-ness is a property of two commits and is read as one — `loop/scanners/pr_behind_base` aliases the whole open-PR set into ONE GraphQL `Ref.compare(headRef:).behindBy` call per base ref ([#4526](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4526)), where the REST `compare` endpoint costs a ~75 KB response per PR. It is NOT read off `mergeStateStatus`, which carries a single highest-precedence blocker rather than a set of flags: a PR that is behind AND red reports `BLOCKED`, behind AND conflicted reports `DIRTY`, and — absent the "require branches to be up to date" protection — a behind PR reports `CLEAN`, so `BEHIND` appears only when nothing else is wrong, which is mutually exclusive with the red this remedy repairs. That predicate was structurally unreachable and `BranchUpdateAttempt` had never recorded a single row. A read that fails degrades to the old `mergeStateStatus` signal with a WARNING naming the PRs — a strict subset of the truth, never a silent "nothing is behind" — and a CONFLICTED branch is refused by `red_required_at_stale_base` itself, since a conflict needs human resolution rather than a merge-update. The sweep APPLIES the remedy ([#4063](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4063), generalising [#2045](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2045)'s repo-state-only rule): the SHA-bound `PUT .../update-branch` merges the base in, emitting `pr_sweep.branch_updated`. Three bounds keep a broken PR from update-looping — one attempt per head (the `BranchUpdateAttempt` ledger, claimed BEFORE the call), a per-tick cap, and the operator's own PRs only. Every refusal degrades to the flag-only `pr_sweep.needs_branch_update`. On a COLLABORATIVE overlay a self-authored PR that is green, not-draft, not-conflicted, and not-behind but has no actionable `MergeClear` emits `pr_sweep.flag_mergeable` (`reason=mergeable_awaiting_review`): the sweep cannot auto-merge (colleague review is the gate, and auto review-request is already disabled by the `notify` tier's resolved `review_request_post_disabled`, [#2579](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2579)), so it DMs the user the MR link + "mergeable, ready to request review" — exactly once per head via the `MergeableNotified` ledger, re-firing only on a new commit. Notify-only: the sweep never requests review and never merges. **One question about the SET, not about any one PR ([#4090](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4090)).** Every rung above decides per PR, so a mutually-blocking red set — each PR red only because another's unmerged fix is missing — is invisible by construction: no re-run, no waiting and no merge ordering clears it, and the sweep correctly merges nothing while logging a sound per-PR skip forever. The sweep therefore carries the CI facts it already computed (`failing_required`, `base_current`) out to every emitted signal, and `loop/red_set_surface` folds them in the tick's `act` phase — one PAGINATED `main` check-runs read per repo (`--paginate --slurp`, `per_page=100`; the unpaginated read saw only the first 30 of main's 40+ runs, so which required contexts it reported depended on API ordering), no second PR listing and no second classifier — into the pure `loop/red_set_report` verdict: `main-red` (main fails a check the set fails, so the whole board inherits it), `shared-cause` (the failing sets overlap — one cause, one fix), `disjoint-reds` (no failing check in common, every run judged the CURRENT base), else `independent`. `disjoint-reds` asserts NOTHING about dependency: a mutual block and wholly unrelated failures reach that rung through identical records, so the note states the observation and names both readings without picking one — telling them apart needs evidence (a "this PR fixes check X" signal, failing-job-log content, or the merge-and-re-run experiment) no surface records, which is also why `independent` gains no new rung. Both indeterminate rungs refuse to read an inherited red as a property of the set: a red judged at a stale base is UNKNOWN, and an unreadable `main` is never assumed green. `main-red` over two or more PRs is the one verdict the data EARNS, so it alone is DM'd — keyed on the set's own signature, whose main term is the INHERITED set (a fixed red set took 4 distinct signatures across 8 main commits on the unscoped set, 1 on this one), so a permanently stalled board is announced once and an incidental main flip is not a new claim. Every other verdict, and a lone PR inheriting main's red, is log-only. Report only: no mutation, no merge decision. |
| `SlackBroadcastsScanner` ([#1131](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1131), wired [#1255](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1255)) | inbound review-request | Polls the review channel for MR-link broadcasts → `slack.review_intent` dispatch without an explicit reaction. |
| `SelfUpdateScanner` ([#1249](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1249)) | editable-install drift | Ff-only updates the editable teatree clone (`T3_REPO`) + every overlay clone on `self_update_cadence_hours` (1h); `SelfUpdateMarker` carries the cadence. A clone that ADVANCES then reconciles the schema (`loop/scanners/self_update_schema.reconcile_schema_after_pull`, [#3901](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3901)): pending control-DB migrations are applied in place, and a migrate that fails — or a schema the probe cannot verify — pages the owner and emits `self_update.schema_behind` (exempted from the `self_update.` statusline drop, so the outage renders in `action_needed`) rather than resuming. A tick on which nothing advanced re-attempts an unresolved reconcile, so the park self-heals instead of waiting on a human. |
| `PullMainCloneScanner` | stale work-repo main clones | Same ff-only contract for the `$T3_WORKSPACE_DIR` main clones a worktree is created from, so a clone parked behind never poisons `git show` / `grep`; `pull_main_clone_cadence_hours` (1h), `PullMainCloneMarker`. |
| `ClaudeSelfPrReviewScanner` ([#1254](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1254), [#3569](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3569)) | self-review vigilance gap | Reviews the owner's OWN open PRs through the SAME `reviewing` → `t3:reviewer` (Claude) gate colleague PRs get — emits `self_pr_review.dispatch` → `_handle_reviewer`'s self-PR branch, which claims one per-SHA `CodexReviewMarker` (keyed `(slug, pr_id, head_sha)`, "since last review") before creating the `reviewing` Task. That claim expires ([#3921](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3921)), so a self-PR review whose task died is re-dispatched on a later tick instead of leaving the head un-reviewable until a force-push. Codex is RETIRED as the self-review mechanism (#3569): `t3:reviewer` is the sole gate for both self and colleague PRs; the codex agent/CLI/marker stay in the tree but are no longer wired into the review loop. Lives in the single `review` intake (`Domain.REVIEW`, alongside `ReviewerPrsScanner`), NOT a separate loop — self PRs are ALWAYS admitted, colleague PRs gated upstream by `admit_colleague_prs_to_board`. So a codex-less box reviews its own PRs out of the box instead of failing every dispatch. |
| `ResourcePressureScanner` ([#128](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/128)) | host OOM / full disk | Measures **absolute** free disk + reclaimable RAM (never percent-of-nominal, so APFS/macOS reporting cannot mis-fire); L0 OBSERVE / L1 WARN / L2 CRITICAL (`free_resources` allow-list cache purge + safe Docker disk reclaim via `reclaim_disk` — build cache + dangling images + unreferenced volumes, fixed argv that can never use `-a`/`system prune` so a running container's images survive — + dormant-venv eviction + the proven-done worktree sweep + idle-docker stop + the agent-scratch retention sweep, which runs in BOTH ladders because on a tmpfs-backed `/tmp` that scratch is memory rather than idle disk ([#4165](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4165)): `scratch_retention_days` bounds the age and **defaults to `0`, so the lane ships OFF** like every other destructive lever, and the autonomous sweep is additionally gated on `allow_destructive_disk`; an entry is reclaimed only when it is also owned by this uid, held open by no live process, not a registered worktree, and not protected by name — every guard that cannot be answered KEEPS the entry, so an unreadable process table reclaims nothing rather than guessing. The open-file guard's witness is RESOLUTION, never listability: a source that lists entries but resolves none is BLIND, and one BLIND source anywhere blinds the whole probe. `scratch_sweep_root` resolves the HOST view (`/host-tmp` paired with the host process table at `/host-proc`) when the deployment mounts it, because a container's own `/tmp` is not the one that fills the box; the operator surface is `t3 <overlay> retention scratch`, dry-run unless `--apply`) / L3 DESTRUCTIVE (flag-gated heuristic worktree GC + renderer SIGTERM). Every destructive lever defaults OFF; dry-run-first, best-effort, `resource_pressure_disabled` kill-switch; `ResourcePressureMarker`. Worktrees are enumerated through `checkout_registry.linked_worktree_paths` and an unreadable enumeration is an ERROR line, never an empty candidate list ([#4244](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4244)); the reclaim guards read one host-aware `process_table`, so a container's own PID namespace can never pass as evidence that nothing is running inside a checkout. Every destructive consumer of that table refuses the pass when it is unusable, matches a checkout under both its written and its resolved spelling, and re-establishes the guard immediately before each deletion rather than trusting the plan-time snapshot — reporting what it stopped, since a silent skip is the invisibility #4244 is about. |
| `IntakeConcurrencyScanner` ([#3992](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3992)) | intake concurrency chosen once, in advance | The resource loop's second job — sets the issue-intake in-flight limit from cgroup-aware observed headroom instead of a hand-set constant, so it neither wastes an idle box nor over-admits when several agents run full suites. Target is the SMALLER of two capacity estimates — memory's `factory_in_flight + floor((available_gb - intake_ram_reserve_gb) / intake_ram_per_agent_gb)` and the whole-box `floor(hard_cap * box_load_headroom)` ([#4407](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4407), so load the factory did not start still lowers the number) — clamped to a core-derived hard cap: the reserve makes the number fall BEFORE the box reaches its memory ceiling, and the ramp is asymmetric (a drop adopted whole, a rise one step per measurement window) so it errs toward the cheaper mistake. Persisted on `ResourcePressureMarker` and read by `resolve_intake_concurrency`, which falls back to the static setting on an absent, stale, or unreadable answer; `adaptive_intake_concurrency_enabled` kill-switch. |
| `IdleStackReaperScanner` ([#2190](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2190), [#2227](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2227)) | idle stack holding a slot + RAM | Emits `local_stack.reap_idle` for each idle running worktree (no live session/task, `last_used_at` past `idle_stack_idle_minutes`, not the CWD, no live external-delivery lease, no E2E run within `idle_stack_e2e_recent_minutes`, not `reaper_pinned`; fail-safe ⇒ KEEP). Reads `classify_running_worktrees`/`preserve_reason` and LOGS preserved-vs-reaped so a reap is never silent (#2227). The `reap_idle_stack` handler re-verifies live then `stop_services` (reversible demote to `provisioned`, whole compose project down). Cadence `idle_stack_reaper_cadence_minutes` via `LocalStackReaperMarker`; `idle_stack_reaper_disabled` kill-switch; mechanical-only, never an agent. |
| `LocalStackQueueDrainerScanner` ([#2190](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2190), [#44](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/44)) | queued stack acquisition | Emits `local_stack.queue_acquire` for each due `LocalStackQueueItem`; the `drain_stack_queue_item` handler re-checks the cap and re-fires `start` (slot freed) or reschedules a Fibonacci-minute backoff (`fibonacci_minutes`, capped `local_stack_queue_max_attempts`). Never tears down another ticket's stack; per-item backoff IS the cadence (no marker); `local_stack_queue_disabled` kill-switch; mechanical-only. |
| `TaskSweepScanner` ([#129](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/129)) | open teatree `Task` rows (not the harness TODO list) | Verifies each open `Task`'s artifact via `is_issue_done`; terminal → `task.completion_detected` re-checks live (fail-CLOSED) before `Task.complete`; unverifiable → `task.orphaned` (fail-OPEN). Per-item, idempotent via `Task.last_sweep_check_ts`; `task_sweep_disabled` (a legacy `todo_sweep_*` row still resolves via the backward-compat alias). The TODO-vs-task glossary it follows is canonical in `skills/checking/SKILL.md`. |
| `IssueDispositionScanner` ([#2122](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2122)) | `needs-triage` dead noise | Lists `needs-triage` open issues and auto-closes ONLY high-confidence DEAD ones via three deterministic buckets — `already_shipped` (a delivered ticket exists, falsified by a live in-flight ticket), `exact_duplicate` (title fingerprint matches another open issue, falsified by a unique fingerprint), `obsolete` (every body-referenced path is gone, falsified by one still-existing path). Conservative bar: ANY uncertainty emits nothing. Emits `issue_disposition.close_candidate` → the idempotent `close_dead_issue` mechanical handler closes with an audit-trail comment; bounded `auto_disposition_max_closes_per_tick`. **Default-OFF** behind `auto_disposition_enabled` (per-overlay overridable). It can CLOSE noise but is physically unable to enqueue work — no `Task`, no claim, never an agent route — so the burn-down doctrine (done-signal = open-bug count, never an empty queue) is structurally preserved. |
| `SnapshotWarmerScanner` ([#2949](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2949)) | reference-DB DSLR snapshot rot | Checks every overlay-declared `DjangoDbImportConfig` (`overlay.provisioning.snapshot_warmer_configs`, default empty) for staleness — a DSLR snapshot's own `YYYYMMDD_<tenant>`-embedded date doubles as its "last refreshed" timestamp, no separate marker. A stale/missing snapshot emits `snapshot_warmer.refresh_needed`; the `refresh_snapshot` mechanical handler restores the newest snapshot, migrates, and re-snapshots only when migrations were applied (`teatree.utils.django_db.refresh_reference_snapshot`) — absorbing the slow restore+migrate path out-of-band so a ticket-critical-path provision never pays it. Daily cadence (its own mini-loop, `snapshot_warmer_max_age_days`); `snapshot_warmer_disabled` kill-switch; mechanical-only. |
| `DbBackupScanner` (directive #2) | teatree's own control DB is unbacked-up | Cadence-gates on the newest artifact's OWN embedded `db-YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS.sqlite3` timestamp — no new model/marker (`teatree.utils.django_db.backup.hours_since_last_backup`); no prior backup ⇒ a `bootstrap` trigger. A due backup emits `db_backup.due`; the `run_db_backup` mechanical handler drives the shared engine (`teatree.utils.django_db.backup.run_backup`) — a consistent `sqlite_snapshot` of the LIVE control DB into `<data_dir>/backups/` plus a keep-last-N-days prune, off the tick. Backs up the control DB (resolved from the live Django connection), so unlike the snapshot warmer it carries no overlay anchor. Daily cadence (its own mini-loop, `db_backup_cadence_hours`, `db_backup_retention_days`); `db_backup_disabled` kill-switch; mechanical-only. The `db_backup` management command drives the same engine for a manual run. |
| `RatchetStalenessScanner` ([#4451](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4451)) | a reference ratchet left loose on the core clone | Reads the editable teatree clone's own `known_unresolved_refs.yaml` against its own source and reports pins the tree no longer resolves, naming `t3 tool ratchet-prune --write`. Detection is from the TREE, not from CI, so the answer needs no forge round-trip and arrives without waiting for a pipeline. READ-ONLY by construction: writing to the clone would leave it dirty and make `PullMainCloneScanner` skip its fast-forward, trading one stale artifact for another. Observe-only like `CiEvalHealScanner` — opening the repair PR is the sibling half that loop keeps behind its own dark flag, and is deliberately not built. 30m cadence (its own mini-loop); ships `enabled=False` and masked off by the `low-token`/`off` presets. |
| `MemorySkimScanner` (directive 32) | factory knowledge accumulating in personal Claude memory | Runs `teatree.memory_audit.scan_all` weekly — the classifier behind `t3 tool audit-memory`, which nothing called on a cadence — and records ONE `DeferredQuestion` per ISO week naming every memory that reads as factory behaviour, asking which to promote into the repo and which to drop. The promote step is the owner's call by the directive's own terms, so the scanner asks rather than editing memories or skills; the question rides the ordinary ask-gate, so the backlog nag resurfaces it until answered. Dedupe is the ISO-week marker over EVERY row, not just pending ones, so an answered week is never re-asked. Weekly cadence (its own mini-loop, `cadence_is_floor`); ships `enabled=False` and masked off by the `low-token`/`off` presets. |

`PrSweepScanner`'s auto-merge dispatch is gated on the fleet doctrine (`mode = "auto"` + `require_human_approval_to_merge = false`); every other overlay stays manual. Self-PR REVIEW is not fleet-gated — it runs through the ordinary `review` intake like colleague review. All scanner knobs are per-overlay overridable.

The monitor never auto-merges substrate unless a standing owner opt-in authorizes it (the default-off `substrate_self_signoff` at `autonomy = full`, or the `substrate_auto_merge_authorized_by` delegation). ONE policy decision — `merge.authorization.substrate_standing_authorization` — governs BOTH merge paths, the keystone CLEAR path and the solo-overlay sweep bypass ([#3648](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3648)), so the two can never disagree about the same PR under the same config; the substrate hold is a blast-radius sign-off, so lifting it never touches the cold-review floor below. The monitor never auto-edits memory / skills / `BLUEPRINT.md`, and never bypasses the §17.4 `MergeClear` reviewer-attestation **except** on a solo overlay the user declared end-to-end-trusted (`mode = "auto"` + `require_human_approval_to_merge = false`) — where the per-diff CLEAR cannot be issued because maker and reviewer are the same human identity, so an unrelaxed gate would silently no-op every green PR ([#1309](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1309)). The carve-out is *minimal*: every precondition gate (draft, changes-requested, CI, uv-audit escalation) stays in force, the per-diff CLEAR is replaced by the SHA-bound `merge_pr_squash_bound` fallback (#1985, re-checking live SHA-bind/draft/CI), and the **cold-review floor still holds** — the bypass requires a recorded INDEPENDENT `merge_safe` `ReviewVerdict` at the live head (`reviewer != maker`) ([#68](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/68)). Non-opted-in overlays keep the CLEAR requirement.

**Auto-review dispatch closes the no-review loop ([#68](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/68)).** On `flag_no_review` for a green+clean own PR the scanner enqueues ONE claimable `Task(phase=reviewing)` (gated on `auto_review_dispatch = solo_overlay and not require_human_approval_to_merge`); the reviewer's recorded `merge_safe` verdict is the artifact the sweep merges on. Because a reviewing-phase headless dispatch is denied the shell (PR-11), the reviewer cannot run `review record` — it **RETURNS a typed `review_verdict` in its result envelope**, and the orchestrator (`attempt_recorder.record_result_envelope` — a DIFFERENT actor, so maker≠checker holds by construction) records the `ReviewVerdict` server-side from it, resolving the per-MR `MRReviewLock` ([#1405](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1405)) so the chain never stalls with the lock held; the interactive/CLI path still uses `review record`, whose `slug` positional must be the repo slug `owner/repo` — the command refuses a branch-shaped slug at the CLI boundary (`_looks_like_owner_repo`), because `ReviewVerdict.objects.latest_for_pr` and the sweep trigger key by the PR-URL-derived repo slug, so a verdict recorded under a branch name is unfindable by every consumer. The merge is **event-driven, not cadence-bound** ([#2026](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2026)): the CLI `review record` calls `trigger_sweep_for_verdict` the moment a `merge_safe` verdict lands, rebuilding the overlay sweep scanner and running its single-PR `evaluate_one` — the same decision ladder `scan` runs, so the two paths cannot drift; the periodic sweep is the backstop that merges the server-recorded verdict on the headless path. `AutoReviewDispatch`, its `CriticDispatch` twin and the `CodexReviewMarker` that dedups the codex / self-PR path are expiry-acquirable claims, not permanent ones ([#3920](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3920), [#3921](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3921)): each carries a `state`, a `deadline` and a bounded `attempts` count, and shares `MRReviewLock`'s compare-and-set shape through the one `core/modelkit/expiring_claim.acquirable_q` predicate — so "when may a review be dispatched?" has one answer across the four claim tables rather than four. A dispatched review that ends without recording a verdict expires and the next sweep re-arms that head, instead of the claim dedup'ing the PR out of ever being reviewed again (the pre-#3920 behaviour, where only a force-push escaped). A recorded `ReviewVerdict` is the terminal for both per-head claims — whichever path armed the review, the other's `mark_resolved` is a no-op; exhausting `MAX_DISPATCH_ATTEMPTS` leaves the claim saturated, which `t3 doctor check` surfaces through the reconciliation ledger rather than failing silently. A spent retry budget gets a NAMED cause rather than a bare count ([#4522](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4522), [#4530](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4530)). When a reviewer returns `merge_safe` over required checks it reports RED, `ReviewVerdict.record` refuses it (§17.8 clause 3) and that refusal is right, but nothing lands — so the head keeps no verdict and the claim is re-acquired once `DEFAULT_DISPATCH_TTL` lapses. That burn was always **bounded** by the `MAX_DISPATCH_ATTEMPTS` above, and the retries are **not** waste: `gh_verify_result` is the reviewer's own self-report and `record` makes no live CI read, so the contradiction is between two fields of ONE envelope and describes that reviewer, not the tree. Measured on this deploy, 6 of the 9 heads that ever hit the refusal recorded a verdict at that SAME head afterwards, three of them a `hold` over checks that genuinely were red — the recordable outcome `VERDICT_CHECKS_RULE` asks for. A red head does not make a verdict impossible; it makes `merge_safe` impossible. So `mark_refused` fires ONLY at `MAX_DISPATCH_ATTEMPTS`, where the budget is gone either way and the latch costs nothing: it renames a claim that would otherwise stop `saturated` — "three attempts ran out", indistinguishable from three crashed reviewers — to `refused`, which says the last reviewer contradicted its own checks report, and records ONE `DeferredQuestion` deduped per reviewed head. The row leaves the doctor's saturation ledger as it gains that page, so exactly one surface owns it. Two scopes govern which claims a terminal may touch, and they are not the same: a recorded `ReviewVerdict` is a fact about the **tree**, so `resolve_head_claims` retires every per-head claim on it (`review_verdict._PER_HEAD_REVIEW_CLAIMS`); a refusal is a fact about one **run**, so it retires only the claim that armed that run (`ReviewTarget.armed_by`) and frees no `MRReviewLock` on either path. Reaching across was not a corner case — 328 of 444 `AutoReviewDispatch` rows share a head with a `CodexReviewMarker` row — and it let a codex-path refusal consume a #68 claim whose reviewer had not run and release the review lock it held, which is the #1405 race the lock exists to prevent. Both tables still share the one `expiring_claim.retire_head_claim` write, the write half of the `acquirable_q` predicate they already share. The safety valve is the per-head key: a push mints a new `head_sha`, which has no claim, so it arms a fresh review normally and the latch binds one tree rather than the pull request. The discrimination is the EXCEPTION TYPE (`ChecksContradictionError`), never the message text: every other `ReviewVerdictError` — an unknown choice value, a maker identity, an out-of-scope finding — names something the next reviewer could get right, so it keeps the ordinary retry. Reviewer briefs carry the recordable shape (`modelkit.review_contract.VERDICT_CHECKS_RULE`: red checks are a `hold` carrying the CI finding) so the contradiction is cut where it is written, not only caught after. Dedup/contract mechanics live in `AutoReviewDispatch`'s docstring.

**Delivery-ownership predicate stops double-scheduling externally-delivered units ([#2104](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2104), [#2217](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2217)).** A unit delivered out-of-band by a hand-dispatched delivery agent (per `/t3:wip`) is implemented directly with no planning phase and no loop-armed reviewer, so the loop must not re-derive that work each tick. `external_delivery.under_external_delivery(ticket)` is true while a TTL'd `external_delivery` lease on `Ticket.extra` is live; it is claimed at the `workspace ticket` entry the delivery agent runs (never by the loop's own FSM) and refreshed on each external-owner seam (`ticket plan`/`transition`), so a hand delivery cannot lapse mid-flight while a crashed owner still self-reaps on the 1h TTL. The lease is consulted at **three** chokepoints: `execute_provision` skips `schedule_planning`; the `pr_sweep` review-arm no-ops (resolving the PR back to its author/delivery ticket via `resolve_author_ticket`, never by `issue_url == pr_url`); and the global dispatch chokepoint `orchestrate._dispatchable_filter` excludes live-lease tickets at the DB layer via `not_under_external_delivery_q` (held in lockstep with the Python predicate by a parity test). Without the third, hand-advancing STARTED → PLANNED made any non-planning phase dispatchable and a second coder was claimed (the #2217 incident).

**Filesystem-evidence double-dispatch guard ([#2217](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2217)).** The lease above only protects a unit the DB knows about; the #2217 incident produced a worktree on disk with **no `Ticket` row**. `worktree_collision.find_foreign_issue_worktrees` keys on the issue number instead — the dir for issue `N` is deterministically `<N>-<slug>`, so globbing `<N>-*` finds any existing worktree regardless of DB state. The single chokepoint is `workspace ticket`: inside the same `atomic()` that creates the ticket, a foreign `<N>-*` dir raises `ForeignIssueWorktreeRefusedError`, rolling the ticket back so a refusal leaves zero DB trace; the CLI prints the colliding path with a `--take-over` opt-out (the ticket's own dir is excluded, re-provision is idempotent). It is the filesystem-keyed complement to the DB lease.

**Adopt outside-originated work ([#2275](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2275)).** `workspace ticket --adopt` / `--adopt-branch <branch>` registers a Ticket + Worktree against an EXISTING branch instead of deriving `<number>-<slug>` — for work that originated outside the derive-a-branch flow. Run from inside the checkout, the branch auto-detects (`resolve_adopt_context` reads the branch, the worktree root, and the repo slug from git; `--adopt-branch` names it explicitly, a detached HEAD is refused). The provisioner records the existing `worktree_path` verbatim (`Ticket.extra['adopt']` maps repo → path, the backing clone derived from the checkout's own shared git dir when it lives outside the clone root) instead of `git worktree add`, so no second worktree dir is created; the `create_branch=False` fallback still covers checking a pre-existing branch out into a fresh dir. The foreign-dir guard is skipped like `--take-over` (adopt is an explicit "use this checkout" intent).

**Lightweight trivial-work plan-skip carve-out.** Some AUTHOR tickets are trivial mechanical edits (a typo, a one-line constant) where planning is pure overhead. `trivial_plan_skip.mark_trivial_plan_skip(ticket, reason=…)` stamps a durable marker (no TTL) carrying a MANDATORY reason (a malformed/empty marker is treated as absent, fail-safe to "planning required"). Consumed at three seams — `execute_provision` skips `schedule_planning`, `check_plan_artifact` accepts the marker so the ticket advances STARTED → PLANNED with no `PlanArtifact`, and `plan_dispatch_gate.unplanned_dispatch_refusal` accepts it so the implementing dispatch proceeds ([#4409](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4409)). Recorded via `t3 <overlay> ticket skip-planning <id> --reason`. The lightweight sibling of the heavyweight `plan-bypass` (`--human-authorize`, which fabricates a `PlanArtifact`); both stay intact and scoped to a ticket that explicitly carries the marker.

### 5.8 Reactive Slack-Answer Loop

A token-cheap reactive `/loop` slot that answers user DMs out-of-band so a quick ack / status question gets a reply in seconds, not at the next slower per-loop tick. Coalesces consecutive same-user messages into one logical turn, classifies (pure Python) into `ACK_ONLY` / `SIMPLE` / `NEEDS_WORK`, and either reacts, posts a threaded reply, or delegates to the `t3:answerer` sub-agent.

**Event-driven wake is the primary drain; the timer is a fallback (default 5m).** The Socket Mode receiver (`t3 slack listen`) enqueues a one-shot `wake_slack_answer` task the moment it appends an inbound event to the durable JSONL queue, so the answer cycle runs on the worker's next ~1s loops-queue poll (~1s latency) instead of lagging up to a full cadence behind the arrival. Because the wake carries the primary path, the `run_slack_answer` timer chain is only a missed-wake / retry / startup catch-up safety net — its `T3_SLACK_ANSWER_CADENCE` default is therefore **300s (5m)**, not a tight spin (a deliberate low override is still honoured down to the 15s floor). The wake shares the cadence chain's lease-guarded body (`_run_slack_answer_cycle_under_lease` under the `loop-slack-answer` `LoopLease`) so it can never double-post with the chain or an owner session, self-dedups so an event burst collapses to one cycle, and does not re-arm. The receiver's `backends` layer cannot reach the orchestration layer, so the enqueue is injected as an `on_event` callback wired at the CLI composition root (best-effort — a failed signal only costs latency; the JSONL write is already durable).

---

## 6. Overlay System

An overlay is a downstream Django project that customizes teatree for a specific project/organization.

**§6.0 Overlay Thinness Principle (Non-Negotiable).** Generic workflow logic belongs in core, not in overlays. Before adding logic to an overlay, ask: "Would a different project using the same framework need the same logic?" If yes, it belongs in core — parameterized and configurable. Overlays provide only: (1) configuration values, (2) project-specific glue, (3) truly unique workflows. Everything else — DB provisioning strategies, migration runners, symlink management, service orchestration — is a configurable engine in core; the overlay configures it, never reimplements it. An overlay method exceeding ~30 lines of non-configuration code likely contains generic logic to extract.

**OverlayBase ABC (`teatree.core.overlay`).** Composition over inheritance (PR-27b). `OverlayBase` itself carries only the **two required abstract hooks** plus a thin **identity/reference** surface (~10 methods total); every optional extension hook regroups by concern onto a **composed facet** exposed as an attribute — mirroring the pre-existing `overlay.config` / `overlay.metadata` split. An overlay overrides a concern by subclassing the one facet and assigning it (e.g. `provisioning = MyProvisioning()`), so the hooks stay lazy instance methods and behaviour is preserved exactly (no eager spec computation).

| Composed facet | Attribute | Concern (hooks) |
|---|---|---|
| `OverlayConfig` | `config` | credentials, URLs, labels, scope — now a **Pydantic model**, fail-closed (typed fields validate on assignment; a wrong-typed setting-module/TOML override raises instead of silently corrupting) |
| `OverlayMetadata` | `metadata` | PR title/validation, required description sections, CI/E2E config, skill metadata |
| `OverlayProvisioning` | `provisioning` | `env_extra`, `symlinks`, `services_config`, `compose_file`, `base_images`, `docker_services`, `db_import_strategy`, `db_import`, `post_db_steps`, `reset_passwords_command`, `envrc_lines`, `cleanup_steps`, `health_checks`, `snapshot_warmer_configs`, `reap_external_resources`, `resolve_variant`, `declared_env_keys`/`declared_secret_env_keys` |
| `OverlayRuntime` | `runtime` | `run_commands`, `pre_run_steps`, `test_command` (→ `run tests`), `lint_command` (→ `run lint`), `verify_endpoints`, `readiness_probes` |
| `OverlayE2E` | `e2e` | `env_extras`, `run_provenance`, `playwright_args`, `scenarios`, `preflight` |
| `OverlayReview` | `review` | `visual_qa_targets`, `classify_customer_display_impact`, `can_auto_merge` (→ `MergeGuard`), `merge_candidate_repo_slugs` (§17.4 cross-repo merge probe, [#2323](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2323)), `repo_owner_for_slug`, `review_exempt_repo_slugs` |
| `OverlayConnectors` | `connectors` | `preflight`, `mcp_provider_expectations`, `manifest` |

| `OverlayBase` direct | Returns | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `get_repos()` *(abstract)* | `list[str]` | Repositories to provision |
| `get_provision_steps(worktree)` *(abstract)* | `list[ProvisionStep]` | Ordered setup steps |
| `get_workspace_repos()` | `list[str]` | Repos for the multi-repo workspace |
| `get_issue_title(url)` / `is_issue_done(data)` / `resolve_mr_token(iid)` / `resolve_issue_token(iid)` | — | Forge issue/reference resolution |
| `get_timeouts()` / `get_health_signals()` / `get_checking_sources()` / `get_eval_scenarios_dir()` | — | Loop / factory operational hooks |

Every facet hook defaults to empty / permissive so existing overlays keep working — core enforcement only activates for overlays that opt in (the one exception is `review.classify_customer_display_impact`, which defaults **fail-closed** to `True` so the mandatory-E2E gate is never silently skipped).

**Load-time signature conformance ([#3526](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3526)).** The framework calls each facet hook with the parameters the base declares, so an override that cannot receive one — a dropped keyword-only parameter, or a dropped positional slot with no `*args`/`**kwargs` — is call-incompatible and crashes only on the path that forwards the missing argument. `teatree.core.overlay_conformance.overlay_signature_violations` compares every override against its base signature at discovery (`_discover_overlays`): an entry-point overlay fails loud with `ImproperlyConfigured`, a registry overlay is skipped with a warning, both naming the hook and the missing parameter. A renamed positional parameter stays legal (leading arguments forward positionally); only a dropped slot or dropped keyword-only name is a break. This retires the conditional-forward idiom (`kwargs = {} if … else {…}`) that hid such a mismatch until the non-default path ran.

**Typed provisioning substrate — `ProvisionStep` DAG + `Variant` (PR-27, [#787](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/787)/[#1385](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1385)).** `ProvisionStep` is a typed DAG node: `requires`/`produces` tokens order the steps (a `requires` token no step `produces` fails the run loud, fail-closed) and independent steps run concurrently, while `post_condition` is a truth check evaluated after the callable so a step that "ran" without producing its resource is recorded FAILED. The aggregate of every step's `post_condition` plus the core invariants (worktree dir, env cache, DB present) is `teatree.core.provision.provision_postconditions.aggregate_provision_post_conditions` — the truth test `worktree status` runs on a `provisioned`/`services_up`/`ready` worktree, so deleting `.t3-cache/.t3-env.cache` or the DB out from under it makes `status` refuse green with a non-zero exit. **Variant** is a first-class frozen dataclass (`teatree.core.provision.variant.Variant`: `name`, `canonical_tenant`, `default_language`, `dslr_snapshot_name`, `e2e_credentials_key`) resolved through `overlay.provisioning.resolve_variant(name) -> Variant` — no overlay hook takes a raw `variant: str`, so a tenant/snapshot/credentials name is never reconstructed from a bare string at a call site.

**Repo SCOPE axis (`owned_repos`).** `OverlayConfig.owned_repos` is a forge-host-keyed dict of the namespace patterns the overlay legitimately works on — the **scope** axis, orthogonal to **visibility** (`private_repos`/leak-prevention, fails open) and **collaboration** (`author_is_self`). Classification (`teatree.core.intake.repo_scope`) gates host equality before the namespace half, so a `gitlab.com` repo can never match a `github.com` scope. The unknown-repo gate (`owned_repo_guard`) holds a push or keystone merge to a repo no opted-in overlay owns — fails **CLOSED** on a clean unknown verdict (opposite the visibility gate) and **OPEN** on a resolver exception (never-lockout). It ships **INERT** (`require_owned_repo_approval` default `false`); enabling it requires first declaring the FULL owned host/namespace list in the private DB `ConfigSetting` store, since a partial list would hold the operator's own private merges as unknown. A path-only overlay cannot opt itself in (its repos are declared under an instantiable overlay). The PreToolUse hook bootstraps Django before resolving overlays so the gate actually fires in production. Owned does NOT imply auto-merge: a shared product repo is in scope yet still needs colleague review — and the declaration never reaches merge authorization, which resolves an overlay from the repo ENUMERATION alone. Two narrow readers sit outside the gate: `merge.host_kind` recovers the forge a bare `owner/repo` dropped, and `cli.review.forge_target` attributes a review target no overlay's repo table lists (GitLab-hosted declarations only, since `t3 review` posts nowhere else).

**Overlay API version pin + `teatree.overlay_sdk` (PR-27 / PR-27b).** `teatree.__overlay_api_version__` is held at `"1"` through the pre-stable window — pre-1.0 the overlay-facing API is explicitly unstable and every registered overlay migrates IN LOCKSTEP with each breaking core change (the PR-27b facet regrouping + Pydantic `OverlayConfig` is one such change, and every overlay loads and passes conformance against the reshaped base at `"1"`), so bumping the counter would manufacture a teatree-vs-overlay mismatch with nothing to detect. From the first stable release it becomes a genuine compatibility counter, bumped on any breaking change to the overlay-facing API. Overlays assert it at import to fail loudly when teatree diverges from what they were built against. `teatree.overlay_sdk` is the surface-frozen namespace an overlay imports from — a single documented facade (`OverlayBase`, `OverlayConfig`, the facet base classes `OverlayProvisioning`/`OverlayRuntime`/`OverlayE2E`/`OverlayReview`/`OverlayConnectors`, `ProvisionStep`, `Probe`, `Variant`, the config/util helpers) whose `__all__` and whose extension-hook signatures — on `OverlayBase` **and** each facet — are snapshotted by `tests/teatree_overlay_sdk/test_overlay_sdk_surface.py` in **core's** CI, so an internal rename or a signature change fails core *locally* instead of breaking an overlay's own CI asynchronously.

**Docker base-image sharing (§6.2a).** Teatree builds each `BaseImageConfig` once from the master clone's lockfile and tags it with the single master tag `{image_name}:base` (NOT per-lockfile) — shared across every worktree. `ensure_base_image` (re)builds only when the image is **absent** (`docker image inspect` rc≠0) or **broken** (the tag resolves but its config Id does not — a corrupt/interrupted build → `rmi -f` then rebuild); a worktree provision otherwise never triggers a build. Code isolation is volume-mount level (`.:/app:rw`); dependency drift between the branch's lockfile and the baked master deps is reconciled at container start by the overlay's entrypoint (`uv sync`), so no per-lockfile image tag is needed.

**`t3 startoverlay <name> <dest>`** scaffolds a lightweight overlay package: `src/<name>/{__init__,overlay,apps}.py`, `skills/t3:<base>/SKILL.md` (where `<base>` is the overlay app name with the `t3_` prefix and `_overlay` suffix stripped **and underscores mapped to dashes** — `t3_my_thing_overlay` → `skills/t3:my-thing/` — else `overlay`), `pyproject.toml`. No `manage.py`/`settings.py`/`urls.py` — teatree is the Django project.

**Discovery:** the DB `overlays` registry row (its `[overlays.<name>]` entries) wins over `teatree.overlays` entry points on name conflicts. `discover_active_overlay()` returns the unique overlay if one exists, else the one whose `manage.py` is in cwd ancestors.

---

## 7. Backend Protocols and ABCs

Every external API concern is a `@runtime_checkable Protocol` in `teatree.core.backend_protocols` (the domain owns the abstractions it consumes; `backends` implements them). PR is the canonical term in core; GitLab MRs are translated at the API edge.

| Protocol | Implementations |
|---|---|
| `CodeHostBackend` — PR/issue/comment (incl. `list`/`update_issue_comment`)/upload/review-state + the §17.4.3 merge-RPC surface (`fetch_live_head_sha`, `fetch_pr_merge_state`, `fetch_pr_draft_state`, `fetch_required_checks_rollup`, `merge_pr_squash_bound` — raw forge I/O resolved via `core.backend_registry`; `merge.execution`/`merge.ci_rollup` keep the verdict/error classification) | `GitHubCodeHost`, `GitLabCodeHost` |
| `CIService` — pipeline cancel/trigger/quality-check | `GitLabCIService` |
| `MessagingBackend` — mentions/DMs/post/reply/react | `SlackBotBackend`, `NoopMessagingBackend` |

Multi-field request parameters are grouped into frozen `slots=True` dataclasses (`PullRequestSpec`). `repo + pr_iid` is the natural unit on both code hosts — protocol methods never accept free-form PR URLs.

**Selection.** Per-overlay configuration (the DB `overlays` registry row) declares `code_host = "github" | "gitlab"` and `messaging_backend = "slack" | "noop"`. The loader (`backends/loader.py`) resolves the overlay's selected backend with no platform branches in caller code, cached `lru_cache(maxsize=1)` per overlay identity.

**Per-remote GitLab credential.** `get_code_host_for_repo` already resolves the repo's `origin` remote to pick the forge; it hands that same remote to `OverlayConfig.get_gitlab_token_for_remote(remote)`, which defaults to `get_gitlab_token()` so an overlay that does not override it is unchanged everywhere. An overlay overrides it where ONE repo must be written under a different identity than the rest — the case being a repo whose PRs the sole human reviewer has to stay eligible to approve, which a forge refuses for a PR's own author. The scope belongs to the overlay and is expected to be an exact slug match: a prefix match would hand the second identity to every repo whose name merely contains the first one's.

**Inbound events.** `t3 slack listen` runs a Socket Mode receiver that writes events to append-only JSONL queues (`slack-events.jsonl`, `slack-reactions.jsonl`) so scanners can drain atomically without racing.

**Reaction surface (#1281).** FSM `reactions.add` failures (`missing_scope`, `not_in_channel`, `mcp_externally_shared_channel_restricted`, …) raise `SlackReactionError` from `backends/slack/react_errors.py` — never silently return `False` — so callers cannot fall back to a `chat.postMessage(text=":emoji:")` thread reply. `SlackBotBackend.post_message` / `post_reply` reject bodies matching `^:[a-z0-9_+\-]+:$` with `SingleEmojiBodyRefusedError`, foreclosing the failure-mode shape at the backend boundary. FSM-side wrappers (`add_reactions_for_transition`, `add_approval_reaction`) catch the raise locally so Slack auth gaps cannot roll back FSM transitions.

**Destination-routed post/react ([#1750](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1750)).** Token picked by *destination* via `SlackBotBackend._route_token`: user's own DM → bot; colleague/channel → personal `xoxp`. Every colleague-surface post/react under the user's identity routes through the one gate+route+audit chokepoint `core/on_behalf_egress.OnBehalfSlackEgress` (self-DM acks ungated); the call-site authorization is pinned by the `on-behalf-routed-egress` / `on-behalf-colleague-primitives` entries in the chokepoint registry (`quality/chokepoints.yaml`, enforced by `scripts/hooks/check_chokepoints.py`); see [`skills/rules/SKILL.md`](skills/rules/SKILL.md).

**Deterministic reference linkifier.** The clickable-references rule (a bare `#N` / `!N` on a user-facing surface must become a clickable link) is enforced *in code* by `core/reference_linkifier.py`, not by asking the model. `ReferenceResolver` resolves DB-first (`PullRequest`, `Ticket.issue_url`) then constructs from the active repo context; `linkify` (idempotent; skips linked refs, inline code, fenced blocks) runs at the Slack chokepoint `SlackReplier._deliver`. Forge posts are NOT linkified (forge auto-links bare ids). It is the sole mechanism for the rule — the former bare-reference *blocking* gate was removed (it over-blocked and asked the model to rewrite refs non-deterministically); the linkifier rewrites in code with no block and no model round-trip.

**Sync ABC (`teatree.types`).** `SyncBackend` is an ABC — defined alongside `SyncResult` in `teatree/types.py` (the foundation-layer contract) with `is_configured(overlay)` and `sync(overlay) → SyncResult`. `core/sync.py` wires and drives the implementations `GitHubSyncBackend`, `GitLabSyncBackend`. Both consume `CodeHostBackend` — platform-specific code lives only in the Protocol implementation, not in sync logic.

---

## 8. Command Tiers

| Tier | Tool | Needs Django? | Examples |
|---|---|---|---|
| Runtime | django-typer management commands | Yes | `worktree provision`, `tasks work-next`, `followup sync` |
| Bootstrap | Typer CLI (`t3`) | No | `t3 startoverlay`, `t3 info`, `t3 ci cancel` |
| Overlay | Typer CLI delegating to `manage.py` via subprocess | Indirectly | `t3 <overlay> workspace ticket`, `t3 <overlay> worktree start` |

Internal utilities (`utils/`) are Python modules, not a CLI tier.

**Runtime commands** (`core/management/commands/`): `lifecycle`, `tasks`, `followup`, `workspace`, `worktree`, `db`, `env`, `run`, `pr`, `ticket`, `tool`, `e2e`, `overlay`, `standup`, `signals`, `mr_reminder`, `checking`, `retro`, `loop_tick`, `generate_*_docs`. Each is a django-typer command group with subcommands. `db query` and `db shell` enforce read-only at two layers (leading-keyword filter + transaction `READ ONLY` / `query_only=ON`).

**Retro enforcement tooling** (`t3 <overlay> retro review-findings`, [#1573](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1573)): the scaffold behind invariant 6 / §17.6. Fingerprints a PR's review comments, records the **supplied** A/B/C verdict (never auto-guessed), and files one deduped enforcement issue per class-C finding. The untrusted comment text is a leak vector, so its bare refs are neutralized and the rendered body banned-term scanned before filing — the `gh api` stdin path bypasses the PreToolUse publish gate, so this is the only guard.

**Gate-failure feedback loop** (`t3 <overlay> retro gate-failures`, [#2024](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2024)): the review-findings sibling whose input is a **gate firing on the agent's own output**. A teatree gate BLOCK lands in the session transcript as a `hook_blocking_error` attachment whose text leads with a `TEATREE GATE — <phrase>` marker. `eval.gate_failures.extract_gate_failures` reads the single transcript chokepoint, keys on the attachment type + marker (never `exitCode`), and reduces the gate identity to a bounded slug; `classify_gate_failure` routes each as `environmental` (a `hook_non_blocking_error` — missing plugin dir, hook-runner traceback) or `preventable` (anything agent-output-shaped, and any unknown BLOCK — fail toward an eval), and `--escalate` files one deduped enforcement issue per *recurring preventable* failure. Privacy by construction: `GateFailure` stores only the slug + session id, never the message/`stderr`/`command`. It lives in `teatree.eval` (a `core → eval` import would be a backwards tach edge; `retro` calls in on a forward edge). Auto-invoking it from the loop tick is orchestrator-only ([#837](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/837)), a separate change.

**Intake landscape survey** (`t3 <overlay> workspace landscape`, [#2541](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2541)): the read-only, gather-once half of ticket intake. Before the planner designs a plan, `teatree.core.intake.landscape.survey_landscape` composes three deterministic signals through narrow consumer-defined code-host Protocols (`PrLister`, `MergedPrLister`) — the local worktree state (dirty / unpushed checkouts, both failing *open* so in-flight work is never silently hidden), the operator's open PRs, and a per-issue `done|partial|superseded|open` disposition with a `close|merge|supersede|keep` action. The classification is a mechanical floor (a *merged* PR naming the issue ⇒ done/close — the resolved-but-open path; an *open* PR ⇒ partial/merge; otherwise open/keep); the planner agent refines the verdict from the prose. The `run_landscape` command renders a JSON `LandscapeReport`; every forge probe failure degrades to a `warnings` entry rather than aborting intake. Distinct from §9's checking report (a mid-loop catch-up) — this is the pre-plan landscape the `/t3:ticket` step surfaces.

**Checking report** (`t3 <overlay> checking show`, #1529): a terse, read-only "what did I miss" catch-up for when the user checks in mid-loop. By default aggregates ALL configured overlays into one `AllOverlaysReport`; `--this-overlay` restores single-overlay scope. Each overlay has its own `checking_checkpoint_<overlay>.json` marker (atomic `tmp.replace` write, tolerant read); each marker advances independently AFTER gathering so a second run sees an empty window. Three groups: Merged / In-flight / Needs you, every reference clickable, capped at 5. `DeferredQuestion` is queried ONCE for the whole report; overlay-scoped items carry an `[overlay]` inline tag. A window start at/after `now` falls back to the 24h lookback; advance is monotonic. `--since` and `--no-advance` inspect without advancing. The needs-you group is overlay-extensible via `OverlayBase.get_checking_sources()` (default `[]`); core makes no live forge calls.
