**Global CLI** (`cli/`): `t3 startoverlay`, `t3 agent`, `t3 capabilities`, `t3 info {,artifacts}`, `t3 sessions`, `t3 cost`, `t3 docs`, `t3 ui`, `t3 admin`, `t3 peer {list,up,down,status,open}`, `t3 ci ...`, `t3 review ...`, `t3 review-request ...`, `t3 tool ...`, `t3 config ...`, `t3 doctor ...`, `t3 update`, `t3 setup ...`, `t3 assess`, `t3 loop {start,stop,status,tick,slack-answer,claim-next,pause,resume,disable,enable,loop-state}`, `t3 mcp serve`, `t3 recover`, `t3 push`, `t3 fast-push`, `t3 overlay {install,uninstall,status}`. `t3 push` ([#3927](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3927), `core/forge_push.py`) is the ONE supported push path from the worker container — without a named seam every agent improvises, and the two forms it reaches for first are the damaging ones: a bare `git push` from a `docker exec` shell (which bypasses the entrypoint's `GH_TOKEN` export and inherits only `TEATREE_GH_TOKEN`) BLOCKS on git's interactive credential prompt until something kills it, and the "fix" for that writes the token into `remote.origin.url`, persisting the credential in the `.git/config` of a host-bind-mounted worktree where it outlives the session. It resolves the forge-write token through the same chain the loop scanners use (`GH_TOKEN` → `TEATREE_GH_TOKEN` → the overlay's `pass` store → the ambient helper) and hands it to git as env ONLY — never on argv, never written into a remote URL — runs under `utils/git_run.py`'s `git_env_non_interactive()` so a missing credential fails in milliseconds with a hint that distinguishes "no token resolved" from "token supplied but the helper is unwired" (`gh auth setup-git`), REFUSES a remote whose URL already embeds a credential, never passes `--no-verify` (the pre-push hooks still gate the push) and offers `--force-with-lease` but no bare `--force`, and verifies the post-condition against the REMOTE ([#4088](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4088)) — an rc=0 `git push` only ends the attempt, so the branch counts as pushed once `ObservedRemoteRef.observe` reads it back with `git ls-remote` and finds it at the local tip; `git rev-parse <remote>/<branch>` cannot play that role because the remote-tracking ref is what this clone last HEARD, not what the remote holds, and it answers the empty string when it resolves nothing. Every ref the module reads or writes is FULLY QUALIFIED (`core/forge_push_refs.py`'s `BranchRef.qualified`), the bare name surviving only in the operator-facing report ([#4117](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4117)): a tag sharing a branch's name makes each bare spelling answer something else — `rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` (and `symbolic-ref --short`) answer `heads/<name>`, which the config check then refuses as a typo; `rev-parse <name>` answers the TAG's sha; and `push <remote> <name>` refuses the refspec as ambiguous BEFORE any hook runs, so the pre-push gate is blamed for git's own refusal. So the checked-out branch comes from plain `symbolic-ref HEAD` (whose non-zero exit is the detached-HEAD case the caller already wanted), and the refspec pushed is the same string the read-back verifies. A ref absent from the remote, a ref at another sha, and a remote that could not be read are three distinct refusals (`not-on-remote` / `remote-sha-mismatch` / `unverifiable`) — an unreadable remote is an unknown and never a success. The endpoint read back is the PUSH url, not the fetch url: `remote.<name>.pushurl` divorces the two, so `RemoteUrls` resolves both — verification targets the push url (the only endpoint whose answer proves anything) while the embedded-credential refusal covers EITHER, since a secret in either persists in `.git/config`. Every refusal carries a `PushFailure` kind and its own exit status ([#4076](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4076), `PUSH_EXIT_CODES`: 1 transport, 2 repo config, 3 credential, 4 pre-push gate refused, 5 non-fast-forward, 6 not delivered, 7 unverifiable, 8 the remote's own policy declined) so a caller branches on the fix rather than parsing prose, and a refusing pre-push gate is reported with the GATE's own stderr — `GitPushError` strips git's outer `failed to push some refs` commentary, which is true of every push failure and therefore evidence of none, and names the hook when an OOM-killed gate leaves no output at all. That gate verdict rests on POSITIVE evidence (`refused_by_a_gate`: an executable hook AND git's rc=1 AND its aborted-push summary AND no remote contact) — never on the absence of remote-contact lines, which an unreachable network produces too and which would blame the hook every teatree checkout carries for every transport outage. `PushOutcome.exit_code` fails closed: it is never 0 while `ok` is false. `--json` emits the `PushReport` an orchestrator routes on, carrying that kind and code. It is also the ONLY push in the tree: `runners/ship.py`'s `execute_ship` (the `pr create --sync` path) and `cleanup/cleanup_salvage.py`'s salvage hook both call `push_branch`, and the raw `utils/git_sync.push` helper they used to reach for is GONE ([#4103](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4103)) — it ran under the non-interactive env but supplied no credential, so a ship from a `TEATREE_GH_TOKEN`-only venue died on git's own `could not read Username` while `t3 push` beside it worked. A ship push that does not land is a `RunnerResult(ok=False)` carrying that `PushFailure`, never a raised `CommandFailedError`. `t3 fast-push` (directive #8, `core/push/fast_push.py`) is the leak-gated escape hatch for session hand-offs: it stages everything, runs ONLY the four leak gates in-process and fail-closed (banned-terms, the `scripts/privacy_scan.py` secret scan, overlay-leak terms + opaque IDs, and the #730 public-repo commit-author-identity check — the same canonical matchers and DB-home sources as the hook chain, so bypassing the hook chain never bypasses leak protection), then commits, pushes with the hook chain bypassed, and idempotently creates-or-updates the branch PR (`--remaining` records unfinished work as a `REMAINING:` PR-body section). Any finding refuses the push and prints the offending path/term; every non-leak gate (ci-parity, coverage, lint, module-health, tests) is skipped by design. `t3 ui` is a trogon-backed terminal browser of the command tree (optional `ui` dependency group). `t3 admin` runs the Django admin for the teatree project under a local gunicorn server (`teatree.wsgi:application`, a production WSGI server — not Django's dev `runserver`; #3134) — it migrates, collects static into `STATIC_ROOT` (so WhiteNoise serves the admin and dashboard assets with DEBUG off; #3162), ensures a superuser (creating one non-interactively from `T3_ADMIN_USER`/`T3_ADMIN_PASSWORD` when absent), and opens `/admin/`. The same process also serves the first-party `teatree.dash` admin dashboard at `/dash/` ([#3162](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3162)) — a ticket-FSM kanban (`build_kanban_columns` groups tickets by `Ticket.State`, tasks are card badges), an "is-everything-OK?" health view (over `read_health` / `loops.live.build_report` / parked-lane / utilization / `t3 cost` / queue readers), a UNIFIED loop table ([#3559](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3559) — one row per loop carrying its effective verdict + deciding layer, cadence, last-run/next-due and held state, with the infra lease slots kept as a small distinct section) whose emergency tier is per-loop pause/resume/disable/enable through the SAME paired `LoopState`/`LoopManager` verbs plus the `danger_gate_fail_open` toggle behind a typed confirm and the global `loop_runner_enabled` kill-switch as a real control (`dash.loop_control.RUNNER_CONFIRM_PHRASE`, `views.loops.runner_toggle`), modelled on that same toggle so the page teaches one doctrine for both master switches — the asymmetry IS the design: OFF is the dangerous direction here (stopping the fleet produces no error and no visible failure, work simply stops arriving) so it requires typing `stop-the-fleet`, ON needs no phrase because restarting the fleet is recoverable and a confirm step there is friction on exactly the path an operator takes under pressure, and both directions are audited through the same `dash.audit.record` seam every other mutating dash POST uses, a per-loop **cadence editor** on that table (interval XOR wall-clock, validated by `loops.loop_cadence_editing` against an absolute floor and the registry cadence floor a self-gating loop declares via `MiniLoop.cadence_is_floor`), a **schedule + preset editor** at `/dash/presets/` (switch the active schedule and the active preset override, edit each preset's tri-state per-loop entries — `on` / `off` / *absent = the base decides*, surfaced as an explicit no-opinion state with the loops it leaves undecided named — create/rename/delete a preset, edit its description, and add/remove schedule slots), every write routed through the `loops.preset_editing` / `preset_admin` / `schedule_editing` seams `t3 loop preset use` / `preset edit` / `schedule set-active` also call; a rename re-points every by-name referrer (override row, schedule slots, preset-selecting settings) in one transaction and a delete is refused while one still names it, and the mode switch refuses a name no `Mode` row carries rather than writing an override that would silently fall open to base config, ONE **settings page** at `/dash/settings/` ([#3664](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3664), nav item *Settings*) — the merge of the former read-only config page into the editable one, so there is a single surface for what this box is configured to do. Its **live readouts** (`dash.settings_readouts`, on their own 15s htmx poll at `/dash/settings/readouts/`) are the values that are RESOLVED rather than typed, composed from the existing resolvers with no second source of truth: the model & reasoning-effort pins (`resolve_agent_config()` — `session_model`, `session_effort`, `honesty_model`, per-tier model + effort), the credential coordinates (`get_effective_settings()`, matching a credential REFERENCE by suffix so a new one needs no second registration), and the self-repairs (the non-paging surface the #3665 stamp renders on); **no secret value is ever rendered** there — a credential row carries the credential-store entry NAME plus a resolves yes/no (the probe's return value is discarded, never stored or shown) and an entry name that is itself private renders as `<private>` while still reporting whether it resolves, and the readouts fail open like the health page, degrading to a visible error rather than a 500. Every OTHER dial the retired page showed read-only (kill switches, concurrency & memory caps, `agent_harness` / `agent_harness_provider` / `mode` / `wip` / `autonomy`) is an EDITABLE row on the same page instead — and `/dash/config/` now redirects here, its nav entry gone. The editable half walks the pydantic schema (`dash.settings_editor.build_settings_editor`) so every config key is listable with no hand-kept list, GROUPED by `config.setting_groups.group_tree` into a NESTED hierarchy several levels deep (`Gates / Quality / Merge & done`) — membership AND shape DERIVED, never listed: each `UserSettings` declaration base declares its own `GROUP_PATH`, and the base set plus their render order come off `UserSettings.__mro__`, so adding a field to a base places it with zero edits anywhere (a key no base declares is grouped by the registry that registers it, each registry declaring its path beside its own keys); `tests/config/test_settings_group_partition.py` pins the bases pairwise-disjoint and exhaustive over the TREE as well as the flat field set (no two bases claim one path, no path is a strict prefix of another, the leaves partition every field exactly once), and the tree's LEAVES are the page's sections, so a key whose group is unknown — or whose path names a level nothing else declares — lands in a VISIBLE leftovers section rather than vanishing; the dashboard renders ONE leaf per request (sections on the left, the selected section's rows `hx-get` into the right pane — [#3825](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3825)) while `setting_groups.group_outline` streams the SAME tree as headings + rows for the three text surfaces — `config_setting export`'s `[teatree]` table, the shipped `config/defaults.toml`'s `[teatree]` table (both emitted through the ONE `setting_groups.grouped_settings_table` renderer as real NESTED sub-tables, so the owner-approved `snapshot_settings_defaults --apply` rewrite cannot re-flatten the hand-edited file) and `config_setting list` (indented headings) — the TOML KEYS staying FLAT because that namespace is the persisted contract, `cold_defaults.flatten_settings_table` collapsing the group wrappers back on read, so a nested dump re-imports byte-identically to a flat one: the retired page's name-shaped `classify_setting_band` returned `""` for 130 of 184 `UserSettings` fields and `continue`d each one out, leaving 185 of the 236 schema keys off the page entirely. It writes each edit through `ConfigSetting.set_value` (the same seam `config_setting set` uses, so the #258 coercion + #3688 cross-key checks fire), restores-to-default by DELETING the row (the zero-row / `restore = delete row` semantics), gates a safety-posture key behind an extra confirm phrase, and and hands the whole-store transfer to a page of its OWN at `/dash/import-export/` ([#4340](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4340), nav item *Import / export*) — hosting it here under-stated it, because a dump reaches past the settings store into the `[loops.*]` / `[modes.*]` / `[schedules.*]` seed families, so an import from the settings page could change which loops run and when while reading as "restore my settings". That page STATES its scope up front from `core/config_interchange/scope.py`'s `EXPORT_SECTIONS` — one entry per top-level table, declared off the same constants `document_layout` and `seed_defaults.SEED_TABLES` give the writers, so the copy and the file cannot drift (`tests/teatree_core/config_interchange/test_scope.py` exports a box tuned in every family and asserts the emitted tables against the statement, with the `[backup]` format marker the one named exception) — offers export (secrets withheld, personal included, with the two independent filters whose both-ticked shape is a byte-identical drop-in replacement for `config/defaults.toml`) + import of an UPLOADED file with an `import_toml_to_db(dry_run=True)` preview whose result is ALSO counted per section (`dash/interchange.py`'s `changed_sections`, over the one `section_for_row` canonicaliser), so "12 rows" can never hide the one that changes a loop; the settings page keeps a link to it and `/dash/settings/export/` redirects there with its filters intact; a SECRET value AND its shipped default are masked to `***` in the surface builder BEFORE the row enters the view context (reusing the export withhold taxonomy — `Category.SECRET` / `SECRET_SETTINGS` / credential coordinate / personal identifier), pinned by a Django-test-client test asserting a configured secret never appears in the response bytes; each row ALSO shows the shipped default `config/defaults.toml` ships for that key beside a verdict — `✓ same as default` / `● differs from default` — so an operator sees at a glance which dials this box has moved, with the tier of the resolution chain that actually supplied the effective value (`config.provenance`, walking the resolver's own layer readers — it replaced a near-constant `category` column showing the setting's KIND) carried as the CELL's tooltip rather than a column of its own, with colour never the only signal (a text icon AND words, and both palette tokens computed against every surface in both themes) and a Secret/Personal key (absent from the shipped file by construction) showing no default and no verdict; each cell posts ITSELF on change and answers the edited `<tr>` ALONE to an htmx request (`dash/partials/_settings_row.html`, `hx-target="closest tr"`) so an edit never re-renders the document and the scroll position never moves — the whole row comes back rather than the one cell, because a global write changes what every overlay column inherits — and a refused write answers 400 carrying that same row RE-READ plus its reason, so a failed edit can never leave text that looks saved; its per-overlay SCOPES are the grid's COLUMNS (global plus every registered overlay and every scope already holding rows) rather than a picker the operator switches between, and an import's own tables (`[teatree]` vs `[overlays.<name>]`) decide where each ROW lands. Beside it sits the read-only **comparison page** at `/dash/settings/compare/` (`dash.settings_compare` / `settings_compat` / `settings_peers` / `settings_files`) — this box in one grid with every other one, showing what differs and what an import WOULD do with each row, never doing it. A column arrives by one of TWO routes producing the same `PeerSnapshot`, so there is one set of rules rather than two: FETCHED over httpx from a peer's own loopback-bound `/dash/settings/snapshot.json` through an `ssh -L` tunnel (the `peer_instances` registry; no instance publishes a port, no `ALLOWED_HOSTS` entry, the same `require_loopback_or_staff` gate, and the path derived from the URLconf so the two ends cannot drift) — resolved for the venue by `settings_peers.fetch_target`, because under the deploy stack the fetch runs in a container whose own `127.0.0.1` is not the machine the forward was opened on, and every peer would otherwise read as refused while its forward is up, or LOADED from a saved snapshot file the operator hands to the page's one POST as picked files or pasted JSON — the only route to the three columns a live fetch can never produce (a box whose tunnel is down, a box that is gone, and THIS box as it stood weeks ago). A loaded column is labelled a RECORD and carries the snapshot's own `captured_at` so it can never read as live; the POST writes NOTHING (in-memory snapshots for that one response) and a document that is not a `teatree-settings-snapshot`, carries an unread `format_version`, is not UTF-8 JSON or exceeds the size limit is refused BY NAME with what was wrong, one bad document never costing the good ones. `t3 peer` is the EXECUTOR for that registry ([#86](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/86)): the page computed each peer's tunnel command and printed it for an operator to paste, which is the workaround — the verb takes a peer LABEL, reads that peer's entry and runs the command it renders, knowing no host of its own. Resolution happens in the CLI (only it reads the control DB) and OPENING is a host act (`deploy/peer-forward.sh`, reached through the same `deploy/t3` handoff `t3 admin` uses), because the transport binaries and their credentials are the operator's and the near end must bind the loopback the fetch means. A port held by something that opens no forwards is REFUSED rather than read through, a live forward is REUSED by the pid teatree recorded rather than duplicated, only a forward teatree opened may be closed, and one that never answers FAILS after a bounded poll. `snapshot.json?download=1` is the SAME read-only payload with a filename built from the instance label + capture date (one `core.settings_snapshot` builder, no second copy; `build_snapshot` takes no `include_private` parameter, so the route is structurally incapable of serving a raw secret). An older record stays comparable because the no-opinion rule treats "no row", "no rows in this scope" and "this code never declared the key" as one fact that compares EQUAL, and because comparability is decided across the LIVE columns alone — a record's differing fingerprint is reported at INFO as its AGE, never as drift and never as a blocker. Every page shares one shell: an `<h1>` naming the page, an off-screen-until-focused skip link to `#dash-main`, a labelled nav landmark, and a header chip naming THIS box (`dashboard_instance_label`, falling back to the hostname, so an operator running teatree on several machines can tell two dashboards apart). The shell also guards a focused text field's value through Idiomorph's `beforeAttributeUpdated` callback, without which a polled morph swap assigns the DOM `value` PROPERTY of the focused element — bypassing the dirty-value flag — and resets a cadence field mid-edit; the blunter `ignoreActiveValue` flag is NOT used, because the bundle also consults it to skip morphing the active element's CHILDREN, which freezes the label of the button just clicked so `pause` never becomes `resume` after its own swap. `/dash/transcript/<id>/` answers the bare fragment only to an `HX-Request` and the full page to an ordinary navigation, since the drawer links it with a real `href`. The credential readout's `pass` resolve-probe is cached per entry name, so an open Settings tab no longer GPG-decrypts every configured credential on each 15s poll, and the preset editor builds the full card only for the OPEN tab, so its query plan no longer scales with the preset count. Every mutating POST on the loops and preset pages answers the page BODY to an htmx request (`dash/partials/_loops_body.html` / `_presets_body.html`, `hx-target="#loops-body"` / `#presets-body`, `morph:innerHTML`) instead of redirecting to a full re-render, and `ticket_transition` answers the refreshed DRAWER — redirecting to the board closed the very panel the action lives in, and the board re-renders on its own 4s poll anyway. Each keeps `method`/`action`, so the pre-htmx redirect still works with JavaScript off. A refused write is no longer a plain-text dead end: an htmx client gets the same surface back at 400 carrying its reason (the `htmx:beforeSwap` opt-in that #3760 scoped to the settings page now lives once in the shell), and a no-JS client gets `dash/error.html` — the reason plus the nav and a link back. There is a ticket drawer (a right side panel — slides in, `Esc`/close dismiss, board stays visible) with the per-ticket lifecycle Mermaid, the legal-FSM-transition action menu and an **enqueue-a-task row** ([#4085](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4085)) — *Scope / Code / Test / Review / Ship now*, each a POST to `dash:task_action` that creates a phase task through `core/models/task_enqueue.py`, the SAME seam `t3 <overlay> tasks create` calls, so prioritising a PR is a click rather than the raw `db query` (PR number → ticket PK) plus CLI call it used to take. The seam's two entry points are the whole design: `enqueue_phase_task` is the CLI's unconditional create (the loop's phase handoff must stay free to re-enqueue), `enqueue_phase_task_once` is the dashboard's, refusing inside one locked transaction while an UNSTARTED task for that `(ticket, phase)` exists — so a double-click reports the queued task instead of adding a second, and the button for a queued phase renders disabled naming it. The drawer offers only the five canonical phases, and the POST re-validates that allowlist, so the endpoint cannot be driven to enqueue an arbitrary phase string. Every BOARD CARD carries the same row cut to the two phases the owner prioritises from the board (*Review now* / *Ship now*), so the common case needs no drawer at all; their enabled state is ONE bounded read of the whole board's unstarted tasks bulk-fetched alongside every other per-card signal, never one read per card, because the board is polled every 4s and an N+1 there is paid at the poll rate (pinned by `tests/teatree_dash/views/test_page_query_pins.py` at two populations). It enqueues WORK and can never record an OUTCOME: no dashboard control creates, alters or satisfies a `ReviewVerdict` or a `MergeClear` — the maker≠checker gate is the one thing standing between the owner and self-merged unreviewed code, so `tests/teatree_dash/test_no_review_outcome_writes.py` walks the WHOLE `teatree.dash` package (an AST pass over imports, lazy `get_model` resolutions and write calls, itself proven anti-vacuous against each shape) rather than pinning the one view that added the buttons. There is also a debug tier (a loopback `ttyd --writable --once` "Debug session" button + an allowlisted-`t3`-command surface). The visual layer is a tea-derived design-token system (`dash/css/tokens.css` — steeped-dark default + porcelain-light via `prefers-color-scheme`/a header toggle, four FSM state hues, a 12px type floor, a 4px spacing scale) that `dash/css/dash.css` and the `/admin/` re-skin (`templates/admin/base_site.html` + `dash/css/admin-theme.css` mapping Django's admin CSS vars) both build on, so `/dash/` and `/admin/` read as one product; the signature is the **FSM rail** — the board's state hues form a connected pipeline (Backlog → Building → Reviewing → Landed, Ignored detached) reused for the column tops, the drawer's transition-history chips, and the token-themed Mermaid. Card and drawer references are clickable and code tokens are monospace ([#3624](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3624)) — the card's id IS the forge link (`#N` / `!N`, with `stopPropagation` so it opens the forge rather than the drawer) and the drawer heading id likewise, its meta chip carrying the REF rather than the bare word "issue" (a link-only label the reader cannot identify is the anti-pattern `/t3:rules` § "Render the Title Inline, Never a Bare/Link-Only Id" names); `TicketDetail.issue_href`/`issue_ref` come from the same `issue_link` helper the card uses, so there is one derivation, and a synthetic loop key (`scanning-news://…`) yields `("", "")` and renders as plain text, making a dead anchor impossible. `core/code_tokens.py` is the ONE detector for path / dotted-symbol patterns and protected-span stashing, shared by two surfaces that differ only in wrapping — `news_digest.format_code_symbols` backticks for Slack, the `code_spans` template filter emits `<code>` for the dashboard — so the two cannot disagree about what a code token is; URLs are always protected (a URL is both a `/`-path and dotted, so an unprotected rewrite would shred every link) and the filter escapes FIRST so the only markup it introduces is its own `<code>` pair. Server-rendered Django templates + vendored IBM Plex Sans/Mono (mono for ids/numbers) + vendored htmx with idiomorph morph-swapped polling (no innerHTML flash, scroll- and drawer-stable), the loops table showing only the state-valid verb of each pair, loopback-only behind the same `LocalAdminAutoLoginMiddleware` (its prefix gate widened from `/admin/` to also cover `/dash/`); it owns no models and reads the existing selectors as the only source of truth (`docs/debug-runbook.md` documents the host-SSH+tmux break-glass tier). Behavioral coverage the test client cannot reach — htmx swap stability, drawer flows, Mermaid render, loop round-trips, theme toggle — lives in the pytest-playwright `e2e/dash/` lane (a dedicated CI job against `live_server` with `e2e.dash.settings`, outside `testpaths` and the coverage floor; behavioral only, no pixel snapshots). `t3 cost` reports cycle-to-date SDK-equivalent spend vs the monthly Agent-SDK credit from each `TaskAttempt`'s captured cost, alongside the GitHub agentic-workflow ET (effective-tokens) metric ([#657](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/657), `ET = m*(1.0*input + 0.1*cache_read + 4.0*output)`, `core/cost.py`'s `ET_MODEL_MULTIPLIER`) and both totals split per Layer-2 lane (`subscription`/`metered`) so the two-lane cost strategy ([#2565](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2565)) is observable. An unrecognised model id with no `cost_model_prices` override is billed in a separate `unpriced` bucket (at the opus rate, annotated) instead of being SILENTLY priced as opus; the DB-home `cost_model_prices` key supplies per-model (id-substring → in/out MTok rate) overrides. `CostBreakdown` splits the same dollars three ways — per tier, per Layer-2 lane, and per PHASE (`per_phase_usd`) — and `core/cost_baseline/` holds the committed pre-cutover per-phase distribution (`pre_opus5.yaml`: attempts, median/p95 output tokens, billed input tokens, turns and cost, per phase and per `(phase, model)`) plus `compare_phases()`, which diffs a live per-phase aggregate against it. The phase is the comparison axis because a model cutover changes which model serves a phase, so a per-`(phase, model)` comparison has no counterpart across it. `TaskAttempt` is dominated by park-audit rows carrying no model and no tokens, so every figure is measured over REAL dispatches only; the artifact's `coverage` block records all three candidate real-dispatch predicates separately so the filter choice is evidenced in the data. `scripts/freeze_phase_cost_baseline.py --check` re-derives it read-only (`mode=ro` + `PRAGMA query_only`) and is reproducible because rows are filtered to `started_at < --cutover-at`. `t3 info artifacts <ticket>` is the read-only "find our eggs" report — where a ticket's worktrees/stacks live, its `PlanArtifact` rows, each `Task.result_artifact_path`, and its `E2eMandatoryRun` evidence (aggregated by `Ticket.artifacts()`, no new storage). `t3 overlay install <name>` editable-installs a sibling overlay checkout into a teatree feature worktree — refuses to run in the main clone.

**Attachment ingestion** (`t3 tool to-markdown <file>`, #1479): converts binary spec attachments (PDF, XLSX, DOCX, PPTX) to Markdown so the agent can read them as structured text. Wraps `markitdown` (`teatree.backends.markdown_conversion.MarkdownConverter`) behind the **optional** `markdown` extra (`markitdown[pdf,docx,xlsx,pptx]` — never `[all]`); absent the extra the command exits non-zero with an install hint rather than crashing. Plugins are disabled and no LLM client is wired — converted output is treated as untrusted data and emitted verbatim.

**Child work items** (`t3 <overlay> ticket create-sub --parent <url> --title <…> [--type Task|Incident|Issue]`): the sibling of `ticket comment`, resolving the code host per-URL across overlays and delegating to `CodeHostBackend.create_sub_issue`. On GitLab this folds the three error-prone hops into one — REST create, `workItemConvert` to the child type (an Issue→Issue parent link is forbidden, so the default `Task` is the natural sub-item), then `workItemUpdate hierarchyWidget.parentId` to nest it — returning the child IID + URL for chaining. GitHub child work items use a different API and are unsupported.

**Structured-search MCP server** (`t3 mcp serve`, [#1023](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1023)): teatree *exposes* an MCP server (it already *consumes* several — Slack, GitLab via glab, Notion). A stdio server built on the official `mcp` SDK's `MCPServer` (`mcp.server.mcpserver`), so an agent reads teatree's internal model as typed tool calls instead of shelling out to `t3 … list` and parsing text. The `teatree.mcp` package separates the three concerns: `serializers.py` (pure model→JSON-dict), `search.py` (synchronous ORM queries that **reuse the model managers** — `Ticket.objects.for_overlay`/`in_flight`/`resolve`, `Worktree.objects.active`, `IncomingEvent.objects.unprocessed` — never re-deriving query logic), and `server.py` (`build_server()` registering each query as an async tool that crosses the ORM boundary through `sync_to_async`). Thirteen always-registered **read-only** tools (`ToolAnnotations(read_only_hint=True)`, the `_READ_TOOLS` table in `server.py`): `command_search` (which `t3` leaf to run), `ticket_get`, `ticket_list`, `ticket_search` (overlay/state/kind/role/text/in-flight), `worktree_status` (by ticket ref or active overlay set), `pr_for_ticket`, `task_list`, `question_list` (the pending `DeferredQuestion` backlog, the read counterpart to the `question_answer` write tool), `loop_stats` (task counts by status + dead-letter total), `incoming_event_recent`, `config_setting_get`, `gate_status`, and `factory_signals` (the derived-on-read factory quality/velocity report, §Factory signals below) — plus `factory_score` when the DARK `factory_score_enabled` flag is on, the per-service read groups (below), and the write suite. The read-only tools were the first version; the write tools below ([#3076](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3076)) add mutation without weakening the §17.4 guarantee, because each write tool routes through the exact gate-carrying seam the CLI calls rather than the transport directly.

**Per-service declared tool groups** ([#3076](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3076)): the MCP server also wraps *third-party services* — but only the ones a registered overlay declares. `Service(StrEnum)` (`backends/types.py`: github/gitlab/slack/notion/sentry/sharepoint) is the closed vocabulary; each overlay declares its needs on `OverlayConfig.required_third_party_services` (a `frozenset[Service]` Pydantic field — code default in the overlay's `overlay_settings.py`, DB-overridable via the `overlays` registry row, validated fail-loud against the enum). `build_server()` computes the UNION across all registered overlays (`_required_services()`) and registers a service's tool group iff its `Service` is in the union — no declaration ⇒ no tools (fail-closed), and the server instructions advertise only registered groups. Resolved once at server build; an overlay or override change needs an MCP restart (same staleness contract as the flag-gated `factory_score`). Six service groups ship (reads, plus the gated writes below on the forge + slack groups): **github**/**gitlab** (`mcp/services_forge.py`, one parametrized registrar since both satisfy `CodeHostBackend` — `current_user`/`my_prs`/`review_requested`/`pr_author`/`pr_comments`/`issue`/`issue_comments`, plus the wave-1 reads `issue_search`/`issue_list_assigned`/`my_merged_prs`/`pr_get` (a one-read PR snapshot composing open/merge/draft state, author, and approvals), all riding existing `CodeHostBackend` methods — no protocol change; the wave-2 reads `pr_list`/`pr_diff`/`pr_commits`/`repo_get`, which DO add four `CodeHostBackend` methods (`list_prs`/`get_pr_diff`/`list_pr_commits`/`get_repo`) implemented on both concrete hosts, an unknown repo/PR degrading to a caught structured error rather than crashing the server; and the wave-2 **issue WRITES** `issue_create`/`issue_comment`/`issue_close`/`issue_update` on the pre-existing `create_issue`/`post_issue_comment`/`close_issue`/`update_issue` `CodeHostBackend` methods, each routing the outbound title/body through the public-repo leak scrub (`gates/privacy_gate.scan_outbound_text` — a customer codename bound for a public forge is REFUSED before the backend call) + the #117 send-proxy chokepoint, so no leaking or non-allowlisted write reaches the forge), **slack** (`mcp/services_slack.py` — `mentions`/`channel_history`/`thread_replies`/`permalink`, plus the gated `react` reaction write routed through `OnBehalfSlackEgress` — a self-DM reaction is ungated, a colleague/channel reaction runs the #117 send-proxy + on-behalf approval gate + notify receipt and is refused without a recorded approval), **notion** (`mcp/services_notion.py` — `page_status`), the **sentry** pilot (`mcp/services_sentry.py`), and the read-only **sharepoint** document-library connector (`mcp/services_sharepoint.py` — `sharepoint_list`/`sharepoint_cat`/`sharepoint_verify_link`/`sharepoint_verify_read_only`; an rclone-backed OneDrive/SharePoint `documentLibrary` remote whose encrypted `rclone.conf` is unlocked non-interactively from `pass`, with read-only enforced at the OAuth-scope level — `access_scopes` limited to `Files.Read*`/`Sites.Read.All` so a write returns HTTP 403 — and `verify_read_only()` asserting that contract; calendar/mail integration is deferred pending the owner's scope/consent decision per #3084). Every group resolves its client through a `core/backend_factory` seam (`code_host_from_overlay`/`messaging_from_overlay`/`notion_client_from_overlay`/`sentry_client_from_overlay`/`sharepoint_client_from_overlay`), never a direct `backends.{github,gitlab,slack,sentry,sharepoint}` import — the transport-boundary fitness test (`tests/teatree_mcp/test_transport_boundary.py`) enforces that.

**Teatree-own write tools** ([#3076](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3076)): `mcp/write_tools.py` registers gated mutation tools (`pr_create`, `pr_merge`, `ticket_visit_phase`, `record_e2e_run`, `config_setting_set`, `task_create`/`task_complete`/`task_fail`, `notify_user`, `question_answer`, `worktree_teardown`, `review_post_draft_note`/`review_post_comment`, and the review-request seam `review_request_check` (decision-only peek, read-only) / `review_request_post` (the #1094 dedup + #960 on-behalf + review-state gate chain)). `task_create` rides the `tasks create` command seam (dispatch-quote gate wiring preserved); `notify_user` routes through the audited `core/notify.py` egress (send-proxy + BotPing idempotency + own-DM never-lockout carve-out). The invariant: a write handler **never touches a transport** (forge/messaging argv, raw httpx, `subprocess`) — it calls the same core seam the corresponding `t3` command calls (`django.core.management.call_command` for command-shaped writes; the inverted `mcp/review_seam.py` factory for review posts), so every gate — shipping-phase FSM, sanctioned-merge keystone (`pr_merge` re-verifies CI-green/SHA/maker≠checker/substrate-hold), on-behalf + live-post approval, banned-terms/leak scrub, close-trailer strip — fires identically on the MCP surface. `config_setting_set` additionally refuses safety-gate keys (`*_gate_enabled`, `require_*`, feature flags, cold-hook wires, registry rows, and the cold-read `COLD_SETTINGS` group — leak-scrub lists, the `danger_gate_fail_open` switch, agent-routing tables), a tightening over the CLI. Gate-satisfier commands (`approve-on-behalf`, `approve-live-post`, `e2e-bypass`, `recipe approve`) are deliberately **never** exposed — exposing them would let the agent self-approve (maker≠checker). The `review_seam` dependency is inverted because `teatree.cli` sits above `teatree.mcp`: `cli/review/mcp_seam.py` registers the gated `ReviewService` factory at import time. Two fitness tests pin the architecture: no `teatree.mcp` module imports a forge/messaging transport (AST walk, since tach can't pin lower-layer imports), and every registered non-read-only tool declares its seam in `server.declared_write_tool_seams()` — `write_tools.TOOL_SEAMS` plus each admitted overlay group's own, since core's half alone cannot see an overlay's tools at all.

**Overlay-contributed tool groups**: a registered overlay adds its own tools through `overlay.connectors.mcp_tool_group()`, which returns an `McpToolGroup` (`core/mcp_tool_group.py`) — the `McpTool`s themselves, not a registrar callback, so the server can judge each one before it reaches an agent. `build_server()` refuses a group whose `requires` names a `Service` no overlay declared (the per-service fail-closed contract, applied to the overlay path) and a group carrying a non-read-only tool with no declared `seam` (an ungated mutation is the one thing this surface must never carry); both refusals are whole-group, so the instructions never advertise a tool that did not register. A `mcp_tool_group()` that RAISES costs only that overlay its tools — one contributor's missing credential does not take the server down for the others. Registration itself runs through `_NoShadowServer`, whose `add_tool` refuses a name already given out rather than letting mcp's `ToolManager` keep the incumbent and drop the newcomer with a log line.

**Registration + consumption** ([#2863](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2863)): the server is registered as a **plugin-bundled** MCP server — a `.mcp.json` at the repo root (sibling of `.claude-plugin/`), the convention official Claude Code plugins use, so it starts automatically once `t3 setup` enables the plugin, with no separate `claude mcp add` step. `teatree.core.mcp_registration.verify_teatree_mcp_registration` is the single chokepoint that confirms `.mcp.json` still declares the server correctly — `t3 setup` runs it as a read-only, idempotent confirmation step, and `t3 doctor check` (`_check_teatree_mcp_registration`) re-verifies it and, when `claude` is on PATH, live-probes visibility via `claude mcp list` too (layered on top of, but distinct from, the general #2282 enabled-MCP connectivity check, which never sees a plugin-bundled server since it only reads `~/.claude.json`). `/t3:ship` § 4b step 5 is the first skill call-site consuming a tool (`ticket_search`) instead of shelling `t3 <overlay> ticket list` and parsing text.

**Factory signals** (`core/factory/`, `t3 <overlay> signals`, MCP `factory_signals`): five derived-on-read quality/velocity signals over the merge/review/CI/repair ledgers already recorded — mirrors `core/standup.py`'s read-only-aggregator doctrine (no new models, no migrations, no LLM calls). Each signal is a **provider-shaped** function returning `SignalReading(value, sample_size, window_days, status)` with `status ∈ {ok, insufficient_data, instrumentation_gap}` — fail-loud, never fabricating a green (sample `< MIN_SAMPLE=5` → `insufficient_data`; a provably-silent upstream recorder → `instrumentation_gap`, never 100%). The five: **S1 first_try_green** (`MergeAudit` merges with ZERO `RedMrFixAttempt` rows, joined via `core/merge/pr_slug_resolution.normalize_repo_slug` + `pr_ref_from_url`; a dead `my_prs` recorder → `instrumentation_gap`, plus the `re_ci_count` companion); **S2 defect_escape** (fix-kind tickets + `RedCardSignal` rows in the window over the PRECEDING window's merges — window-level, not per-PR); **S3 review_catch** (fraction of merged PRs whose `ReviewVerdict`/`for_pr` history holds or surfaces a blocker/major `Finding`; ≥5 merges at catch-rate 0 → hard RED "vacuous review lane" — the rubber-stamp detector); **S4 merge_latency** (median `MergeClear.issued_at`→`MergeAudit.merged_at` exact FK join + the stale-actionable-CLEAR age, RED above 48h even in a zero-merge window); **S5 repair_burn** (mean terminal `TaskAttempt.iteration` per succeeded `(ticket, phase)` + the failed-attempt fraction). `compute_factory_signals(window_days=28)` composes the readings against the immediately-preceding window as a rolling baseline into a `FactorySignalsReport` (per-signal red-floor `tripped` + rolling-baseline `regressing` verdicts, one top-level verdict ∈ {ok, regressing, red}); starving a signal can only lower the score — an `instrumentation_gap` is RED, never a free pass. The five providers are a star-shaped `SIGNALS` registry the recipe (`evals/recipe.yaml`, §Factory score below) names verbatim; the module produces signal only — no gate, no merge lockout.

**External outcomes** (`core/factory/external_outcomes.py`, `ExternalOutcomeSnapshot`, the `external_output_vs_internal_success` + `merged_without_verdict` reconciliation checks — [#4506](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4506)): the counterweight to every signal above. §Factory signals and §Factory score are both derived from teatree's OWN ledgers, so all five can read healthy through a window in which nothing reached `main` — tasks reporting `completed`, phases visited and tickets advanced are bookkeeping, not evidence that anything shipped. `read_external_outcomes(Forge)` reads the one number the factory cannot write for itself: the merged-PR count the FORGE reports for the overlay's own repos (`core/overlay_repos.owned_repo_slugs` — the same scope issue intake claims from) over a trailing 7d window, through the `CodeHostBackend.list_merged_prs_since` Protocol method both hosts implement. Three states are kept apart on purpose: a completed read is a number; `NO_FORGE` (no host, no declared repos) is UNMEASURED; and a read that FAILS raises `ExternalOutcomeReadError` — downstream an empty list IS the alarm condition, so a rate-limited read degrading to "zero merges" would manufacture the very finding the measure exists to raise honestly. `refresh_if_stale` records each completed read as one `ExternalOutcomeSnapshot` and serves any snapshot younger than `EXTERNAL_OUTCOME_TTL` (6h) without touching the network, so the watchdog's many daily `t3 doctor` runs cost at most four forge reads. The two disagreement checks live on the §Reconciliation ledger — unflagged, running on every `t3 doctor`, surfacing-only, DM'd once per day — and each names BOTH numbers: `external_output_vs_internal_success` alarms on ≥`MIN_INTERNAL_SUCCESSES_FOR_OUTCOME` internal SUCCESS attempts against zero forge merges, and `merged_without_verdict` takes its DENOMINATOR from the forge (which PRs actually merged) against teatree's own numerator (which carry a `ReviewVerdict`), so no amount of internal success can satisfy it. An unmeasured or failed read is `degraded` — surfaced, never DM'd, and never scored healthy.

**Factory score** (`core/factory/`, `t3 <overlay> recipe score|approve`, MCP `factory_score`, flag-gated behind the DARK `factory_score_enabled` — SIG-PR-2): the recipe-weighted aggregate the T4 outer loop optimises against, composed over the §Factory signals `SIGNALS` seam READ-ONLY (`compute_factory_signals` + the `SignalReading`/`SignalRow` dataclasses — never re-deriving a query). The committed `evals/recipe.yaml` names each SIGNALS id with a `weight` (the five sum to 1.0), an optional `red_when` floor, and a normalisation `cap` for the two magnitude signals (`merge_latency` hours, `repair_burn` iterations); `load_recipe()` fails loud (`RecipeError`) on drift — a missing file, an unknown/missing id, weights off 1.0, or a missing/forbidden cap — so the file can never diverge from the registry (git is the audit log for weight/floor changes). `score()` folds worst-wins honesty structurally: any `instrumentation_gap` or RED row → verdict RED; a covered reading below its `red_when` floor → RED; an uncapped rate outside `[0,1]` → RED (never clamped); coverage below `coverage_floor` → `aggregate=None` + RED — an untrustworthy score is never a number, and a RED verdict always carries a `None` aggregate. Each scored read persists one `FactoryScoreSnapshot` (`recipe_sha` + `tree_sha` provenance, per-signal evidence) whose `delta_vs_previous` and `delta_vs_last_different_recipe_sha` give the outer loop its time-series — the cross-recipe-sha delta is what stops a re-weighting from hiding a regression behind its own sha. Recipe evolution is doubly human-gated: the committed-file PR diff, plus `approved_recipe_sha` written ONLY by the human-run `t3 <overlay> recipe approve`; an unapproved sha stamps `recipe_approved=false` on every payload and snapshot and queues exactly one deduped `DeferredQuestion` per new sha. Ships flag-gated OFF: `recipe score` COMPUTES read-only (to calibrate weights against real ledger data pre-enable), but `--record` refuses, zero snapshot rows are written, zero questions are queued, and `build_server()` does not register the MCP `factory_score` tool — the empty migrated table is the only footprint (the `ConfigSetting` empty-table doctrine). Per the repo doc-alignment rule, this section moves with the code.

**Autoresearch outer loop** (`core/models/outer_loop_experiment.py` + `loops/outer_loop/`, `t3 outer status|propose|history|tick` — T4-PR-3): the self-improving runtime that turns the factory score into an optimisation target. It is a two-loop frame — the INNER maker pipeline builds one ticket into a merged PR; the OUTER loop advances at most one `OuterLoopExperiment` one FSM step per tick through **propose → ratify → implement → measure → keep-only-if-better**, consuming `factory_score.score()` (the T4-PR-2 metric-to-beat) as its measure. Implement rides the exact `schedule_gap_fix` shape — a synthetic-issue `Ticket` + `Ticket.schedule_coding()` — so an experiment's PR merges through the SAME critic-gated keystone every ticket uses; the outer loop gains ZERO new merge authority. The keep rule is pure and anti-Goodhart (`loops/outer_loop/decide.py`): KEEP iff the target signal improved beyond its own `regress_band` AND nothing else regressed vs the admission baseline — a non-improving or collateral-damaging experiment is never kept, it moves to `REVERT_PENDING`. The human is embedded STRUCTURALLY at BOTH ends: `admit()` is the only writer of the `ADMITTED` state and RAISES unless a consumed (answered) ratify `DeferredQuestion` exists — there is no auto-admit code path. KEEP is human-gated too (H1-KEEP): a keep decision no longer auto-writes `KEPT` — `measure_and_decide` routes it to `request_keep()` → `KEEP_PENDING`, a tick records a keep `DeferredQuestion` (`loops/outer_loop/keep.py:ask_keep`), and the operator runs `t3 outer resolve-keep <id>`, which consumes it and calls `record_kept()` → `KEPT` (the guard RAISES without a consumed keep question, the same idiom as `admit()`). `ask_keep` consults the taint-floored `approval_policy` seam via the #119 `policy_dial` (action class `outer_loop_keep`, owner-taint), so a graduated keep is auto-answered by policy (`resolved_via='policy'` + audit) WITHOUT relaxing that guard. Revert is likewise human-ratified and drives to a TERMINAL state (no dead-end, no permanent slot hold): a `REVERT_PENDING` tick records a revert `DeferredQuestion` (`loops/outer_loop/revert.py:ask_revert`), the operator performs the git revert and runs `t3 outer resolve-revert <id>`, which consumes that question and calls `record_reverted()` → `REVERTED`, freeing the max-concurrent slot for the next experiment (full AUTO-revert is a later PR). **Ships QUADRUPLE-OFF** — a full tick is a proven no-op at defaults: (1) the DARK `outer_loop_enabled` feature flag is off (G1); (2) the seeded `outer_loop` `Loop` row lands disabled (`t3 loop enable outer_loop` required); (3) `off_live_tick=True` keeps it off the live fan-out — the worker's `drive_off_live_tick_loops` maintenance chain fires its `off_tick_command` instead, like `dream`; (4) the guards that matter are CODE not config — G2 (`critic-live`) refuses every tick until a `CriticVerdict`-backed critic gate is registered and has `>= MIN_CRITIC_SAMPLE` verdicts (a defensive lazy lookup that fails CLOSED when it cannot confirm a live critic), and G3 (`signal-trust`) refuses while ANY factory signal reports `instrumentation_gap` (never optimise an untrustworthy score). G1b additionally refuses while `factory_score_enabled` is off — the loop has no metric and must not write a `FactoryScoreSnapshot` (T4-PR-2's empty-table-when-off doctrine). G4 bounds the run (`precheck_budget` + max-concurrent=1 + weekly cap + a convergence brake that parks after N consecutive non-kept decisions). The ledger row binds hypothesis → shas → both score snapshots → the human decisions; kept = a merged sha, rejected = a closed PR, reverted = a revert commit — git is the audit log; the operator-facing liveness surface is `t3 outer status`/`history` (the typed tick result names the refusal reason). **Known weakest link:** MEASURE is a time+merge-count window, NOT causal attribution — a horizon-window delta is confounded by unrelated merges, so a KEPT decision is "correlated better", not "proven caused"; the no-regression-anywhere rule and the human-ratified revert bound the risk. Per the repo doc-alignment rule, this section moves with the code.

**Overlay contract check** (`t3 overlay contract-check --compose <paths>`) reads every `${VAR}` reference in compose files and fails if any is neither defaulted nor declared by core (`_declared_core_keys()`) or the active overlay (`overlay.provisioning.declared_env_keys()`).

**Teatree source resolution in overlays.** `[tool.uv.sources] teatree = { path = "../../souliane/teatree", editable = true }` is the committed default — no SHA pinning, no mode switching. CI clones teatree at the same relative path before `uv sync`. Local dev uses whatever is checked out locally.

**Machine interface — `--json` / stable exit codes ([PR-30](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2565)).** `t3` is a machine interface a front-end (Pi, a CI runner, another agent) drives by shelling to `t3 … --json` and parsing stdout. The contract: under `--json` stdout is a **pure JSON channel** (valid `json.loads`, zero human bytes); the human view (tables, banners, diagnostics) always goes to **stderr**. `teatree.core.machine_output.emit(payload, *, json_output, out, err, human)` is the single seam every record/query command routes through — it serializes the typed return via `json.dumps` on stdout under `--json` and renders the human view on stderr otherwise. A converted command sets `print_result = False` so django-typer does not additionally repr the return onto stdout, while `call_command` consumers still receive the typed object (the same typed return PR-18's `print_table` renderer consumes — one seam, not two conversions). **Exit codes:** subcommands raise `SystemExit(N)` (never `typer.Exit`, swallowed under `call_command`); the overlay bridge (`cli/overlay.py` `managepy`/`managepy_core`) catches `CommandFailedError` and re-raises `SystemExit(returncode)`, so a bridged child's exit code propagates faithfully with no traceback instead of collapsing to a stack-trace exit 1. `t3 capabilities --json` publishes the machine-readable capability registry — each command's `--json` support and exit-code contract — so a front-end discovers the interface instead of scraping `--help` (the registry, not BLUEPRINT prose, is the SSOT for the converted-leaf set: `queue status`, `tasks list`/`create`, `followup sync`, `workspace emit`, `worktree status`/`diagnose`, `questions list`, plus the pre-existing JSON commands). **Every** command that declares `--json` routes through the `emit` seam — the earlier "returns a human string, both channels on stdout" precedent is retired, so a `--json` command's stdout is JSON or nothing at all and its human view is always stderr. `MachineOutputCommand` is the base a converted command subclasses: `BaseCommand.execute` swaps in Django's own `OutputWrapper` whenever a stream is passed as `call_command(…, stdout=buf)`, and that wrapper has no `disable` flag, so `print_result = False` would silently stop working and the typed return would land on stdout *after* `emit` wrote the JSON there; the base installs django-typer's disable-capable wrapper so the pin holds on every call path. The rollout is incomplete and ratcheted: `teatree.quality.machine_output_seam` statically detects the two bypass shapes (a `--json` flag not routed through `emit`; a structured return with no `print_result = False`) and `tests/quality/test_machine_output_seam.py` pins the remaining unconverted handlers in a shrink-only ledger, so a NEW bypass turns CI red.

**Golden-path lifecycle wrapper — `t3 <overlay> do <ticket-ref>` ([PR-31](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2565)).** One command walks a ticket through the lifecycle (intake → provision → plan → code → test → review → ship) so a front-end (or a human) never has to remember the sequence. It is a **thin** front-end-seam wrapper over the EXISTING chokepoints — it SEQUENCES and REPORTS, it adds no gate and never touches the FSM directly. The step order and the `Ticket.state`→status mapping live in `teatree.core.lifecycle_pipeline` (`PIPELINE` + `drive`, kept pure and side-effect-free against injected `DriveSeams`). Two step kinds split the walk: `auto` steps (intake=`workspace ticket`, provision=`workspace provision`, ship=`pr create`) are invoked in-process via `call_command` (child streams redirected to stderr so `do`'s stdout stays a pure JSON channel); `agent` steps (plan/code/test/review) are reported `pending` and the walk stops — dispatching the phase agent is the loop's job (§5.2), not this wrapper's. **Resumable + idempotent:** it re-reads `Ticket.state` each step and resumes at the live phase, never redoing a completed one; a gate-refused auto step surfaces the blocker and stops. `--json` emits the per-step typed status through the `emit` seam (steps array + `stopped_at`/`stopped_reason`); `--plan` prints the sequence with no side effects. **Exit codes:** `0` = progress/pending/done, `1` = a gate blocked an auto step (or the ticket is `ignored`).

---

## 9. Code Host Sync

`teatree.core.sync.sync_followup() → SyncResult` is platform-agnostic. Per-overlay it resolves the overlay's `CodeHostBackend`, fetches open PRs authored by the current user (incremental via cached `updated_after`), upserts tickets by `issue_url` (or PR URL if no issue linked), enriches non-draft PRs with pipeline + approvals + review threads, infers ticket state from PR data (`infer_state_from_prs()` advances forward only, never regresses — and a post-ship inferred state is routed through the shared DoD decision per § "Sync writers honor the same gate"), and detects merged PRs. It also re-checks the user's OWN open authored PRs for merge conflicts every sweep — a non-incremental fetch (always `updated_after=None`, so a re-conflict that did not bump the PR's timestamp as `main` advances is still seen) whose conflicted PRs land in `SyncResult.conflicted_mrs` (the overlay-agnostic `ConflictedMR` shape: iid, repo, web_url, title). The GitLab backend reads `has_conflicts` / `merge_status == cannot_be_merged` / `detailed_merge_status == conflict`; `followup sync` surfaces them in a loud WARNING block ([#78](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/78)). Detection only — never an auto-rebase or auto-push.

| PR data | Inferred state |
|---|---|
| Draft | `started` |
| Non-draft | `shipped` |
| Non-draft + review-requested or approvals > 0 | `in_review` |

Review threads are classified `waiting_reviewer` / `needs_reply` / `addressed`. Draft notes (GitLab) / pending reviews (GitHub) surface as a statusline `review_draft` prompt to publish.

Posting discipline (#1207): `t3 review post-comment` defaults to creating a DRAFT and DMs the user the link; the colleague-visible `--live` path is gated on a single-use, MR-URL-scoped `LivePostApproval` minted by `t3 review approve-live-post <mr-url> --slack-ts <ts>` after the Slack DM at that timestamp is verified (from the user, recent within 15 min, contains an explicit approval phrase). The historical immediate-post default is retired; CLI enforces draft-by-default rather than relying on prose discipline.

One-step authorization (#126): `t3 review authorize <repo>!<mr> --approver <id>` collapses the former two-command on-behalf dance — it records the durable `OnBehalfApproval` AND mints the matching single-use `LivePostApproval` in one call. The `post-comment --live` path consults one consolidated read-only decision (`review_authorize.resolve_live_authorization`); the two original commands remain for the Slack-ts verification path.

Published-note removal: `t3 review delete-discussion <repo> <mr> <note_id>` removes a published MR note; `t3 review delete-issue-note <repo> <issue_iid> <note_id>` is the issue/work-item twin (`DELETE /issues/{iid}/notes/{id}`). Both are colleague-visible on-behalf mutations routed through the recorded-approval gate (#960) — the issue path scopes the approval to `<repo>#<issue>` (distinct from the MR `<repo>!<mr>` scope, via `issue_gate_target`) and verifies the delete took (#2081, `verify_issue_note_deleted`). These CLIs are the only sanctioned path: the `block-raw-review-post` hook (#1164) hard-denies a raw `glab api`/`gh api` DELETE to `…/issues/<iid>/notes/<id>`.

Note CREATION splits by surface, and the hook's refusal names the CLI that can address the one the caller hit. A merge-request note is `t3 review post-comment` (draft-default, dedup, on-behalf approval). An issue/work-item note is `t3 <overlay> ticket comment <issue-url>`: the `t3 review` create seam is merge-request-shaped — `post-comment` is keyed on an integer MR IID, and its draft-default rests on a draft-notes API the forge exposes only under `merge_requests`, so there is no `issues/<iid>/draft_notes` to mirror. `ticket comment` reaches the same `POST /issues/{iid}/notes` endpoint through the shared scanned forge-write seam (`route_forge_write` — the public-repo leak gate + the #117 send-proxy audit/allowlist/redaction that `chokepoints.yaml`'s `forge-comment-write-seam` mandates for every `post_issue_comment`), which is what the hook's `issues` arm exists to keep a raw REST write from skipping.

Verified-delivery notify wrapper ([#1181](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1181)): `teatree.messaging.notify_with_fallback` tries `notify_user` and, on a transport `FAILED`, falls back to a round-trip-verified backend send (`BotPing.transport` records the path). Loop/CLI sites route through the wrapper; `teatree.core` callers stay on `notify_user` (no `core → messaging` edge). A `NOOP`/`FAILED` INFO `BotPing` is durably parked, and the always-on `undelivered_notify` scanner re-attempts parked rows each tick under their `idempotency_key`; the drain is bounded ([#2064](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2064)) — a row past `MAX_REDELIVERY_ATTEMPTS` or 72h is `EXPIRED`, so the backlog never grinds and stale DMs never surface late.

Named non-delivery + ambient egress resolution: `notify_user_outcome` is the egress and returns a `NotifyOutcome` carrying a `NotifyReason` (`no_messaging_backend`, `no_user_id`, `feature_disabled`, `delivery_failed`, `claimed_by_concurrent_tick`, `ledger_unavailable`, `already_sent`); `notify_user` is its boolean face. A bare `False` is banned by construction — nobody watches the CLI turn a notification fires in, so an unexplained non-delivery reaches no one, and the two halves of the old conflated "no messaging backend or user_id" reason have different fixes. Every drop is logged at ERROR and recorded on its `BotPing` row. Resolution is ambient: the headless worker, the loop, and the MCP server export no `T3_OVERLAY_NAME`, so `messaging_from_overlay()` falls back to the SOLE registered overlay carrying a real messaging transport (`_sole_credentialed_messaging`, the credential-keyed peer of `notify_targets._sole_overlay_field`) instead of collapsing `Multiple overlays found` into "no backend". Two credentialed overlays stay ambiguous and resolve to `None` — the box must never guess which workspace the owner reads. The Slack round-trip doctor gate probes that ambient seam directly and reads the `BotPing` ledger back, so a per-overlay config check can no longer read as proof that notifications deliver.

**Push vs pull — the owner DM carries decisions only** ([#4524](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4524)): a DM interrupts a person, so it is the right vehicle for one class of message — something needs the owner's decision and nothing proceeds without it. `NotifyAudience` (§ above) answers WHO a notification is for; `teatree.core.modelkit.dm_channel_policy` answers whether that person should be INTERRUPTED for it, and it is deny-by-default: an `OWNER_DELIVERY`/`OWNER_ESCALATION` notification reaches Slack only if one of its idempotency key's colon-delimited prefixes is in the closed `PUSH_SIGNALS` allowlist, so `watchdog:compose-up-failed` pages while `watchdog:red` does not. `OWNER_QUESTION`/`COLLEAGUE_ACTION` push on their audience alone. A new alarm therefore cannot reach the DM by being *written* — only by being *registered*, which is the conversation the gate exists to force. Enforced at the `notify_user_outcome` chokepoint beside the `INTERNAL` short-circuit, so every caller inherits it, including the shell ones arriving through `t3 <overlay> notify send`; `notify_with_fallback` stands down on the OUTCOME's `ROUTED_TO_PULL` reason rather than on the audit row, which would fall open exactly when the ledger write was lost. Nothing is discarded: the signal lands as a terminal `BotPing.Status.PULLED` row keeping its text, key and audience verbatim (excluded from `_RECOVERABLE`, so the drain never resurrects it onto the DM channel), and `t3 <overlay> notify digest` is the pulled surface — grouped by signal with counts. `notify send` exits 0 on a withheld signal, because a non-zero exit reads as a transport failure to `deploy/watchdog.sh`, which parks such a page and re-sends it every pass.

Tabular DMs ([PR-18](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1777)): `teatree.backends.slack.table_format` is the pure formatter turning headers + rows + per-column alignment into a native Block Kit `table` block (`rich_text` cells, alignment via `column_settings`, capped 100 rows total incl. the header / 20 cols) plus a space-aligned monospace fence fallback (`slack_table_fence`; over-budget cells ellipsis-truncated widest-column-first, never wrapped, rows capped to match the block with an `… and N more` trailer, empty → `(no rows)`). `notify_user` takes an opaque `blocks` argument threaded to `post_message` so a bot→user DM posts the block with the fence as the `text` degradation; the blocks stay opaque JSON so `teatree.core` never imports the Slack backend (no `core → backends.slack` edge). The CLI twin is `teatree.core.table_output.print_table` (§ "Machine interface"), so both surfaces render tabular data through one seam each; the `slack-formatting` reference skill (an overlay companion) documents the formatter and the mrkdwn gotchas.

Review-shape audit (#1206): `t3 review run <MR_URL>` is the read-only entry point reviewer sub-agents call before scanning a diff. It fetches MR metadata, classifies complexity, counts existing-review state (open discussions + draft notes + approvals), and emits a structured JSON summary so every reviewer starts from the same shape rather than improvising. The command never publishes — it stays outside the on-behalf surface. A merged/closed MR (live `state` field from `/changes`) short-circuits to a `skipped_merged` / `skipped_closed` verdict (#2081) — a note can never land on one, so a mid-flight close aborts the review. Both forges are served: GitLab through `teatree.cli.review.run`, GitHub PRs through `teatree.cli.review.run_github` over the existing `GitHubCodeHost` (same payload shape, same classifier, same skip verdicts — an `UNKNOWN` live state is a failed read that surfaces `api_unavailable`, never an audit reported as open). A URL naming neither forge exits 2 with `bad_url`.

Structured-evidence gate (#1280): `t3 review post-comment` and `post-draft-note` refuse a finding whose body matches an "X is missing/wrong/broken/stale" pattern unless an accompanying `FindingEvidence` record (`--evidence-json '{...}'`) carries verified receipts — it passes only when `confidence='verified'` AND at least one verified-path field is non-empty (full schema in code). Implemented in `teatree.cli.review.evidence_gate`; runs alongside the on-behalf (#960), colleague-MR shape (#1114), comment-bloat (#2663), and TODO-anchor (#1186) sibling gates inside `ReviewService._run_pre_publish_gates`.

Guarded reads (#3509): every external read on the review CLI goes through `teatree.cli.review.guarded_read`, which comes in two shapes because the right answer differs per site. `guarded_read(what, read, *, neutral) -> ReadOutcome[T]` still degrades to the documented neutral but LOGS the failure and carries `failed`, so "the read failed" and "there is nothing there" stop being the same answer — used by `shape_gate.fetch_mr_author`, `todo_gate._fetch_file_diff` and `inline_shape_gate.count_inline_drafts`, each of which keeps its fail-open DIRECTION deliberately (failing closed would refuse every review post whenever the forge is unreachable). `read_or_refuse(what, read) -> T` raises `ReadRefusedError` instead, for a read whose neutral would be a guess with outbound consequences: `forge_target.resolve_base_url` REFUSES when no forge base URL is configured rather than falling back to `gitlab.com`, while an explicitly-set `$GITLAB_URL` is still honoured because that is an operator's stated choice, not a guess. This is `/t3:rules` § "External Read Failure Must Fail Loud" applied at the reader: the fail-open belongs to the caller as a conscious orchestration choice, and what is banned is the reader hiding the failure.

URL-derived review target (#3793/#3794): both coordinates a review post needs — the forge base URL and the API token — are resolved in `teatree.cli.review.forge_target` from the `owner/name` slug the command was given, via `get_overlay_for_url`. Resolving them from the AMBIENT overlay instead made the whole surface conditional on how many overlays are registered, because `get_overlay()` raises `Multiple overlays found` with more than one and no explicit pin; deriving them from the invocation makes resolution total, and routing BOTH through the same owning overlay stops a post being addressed to one forge with another's credential. `ReviewService` carries the target repo, and the MCP review seam (`review_post_seam(repo)`) is built per repo rather than once per process. `forge_target.read_token` returns a `ReadOutcome` so a FAILED read stays distinguishable from an absent one — `commands._require_token` reports the underlying cause for the first and keeps the login hint only for the second.

Comment-bloat gate (#2663): the same publishing path refuses a review note that drags in *project chatter* — a stakeholder `@handle`, a Slack thread timestamp, or a tracker id (`#1234`/`!42`) paired with a coordinate-with-people directive (`ping the author`, `sync with the team`, `in standup`). A review comment is about the diff, not the tracker. The note-LENGTH dimension of bloat stays owned by the colleague-MR shape gate (#1114, 3-paragraph/200-word cap), so this gate adds no parallel length cap; a *bare* `tracked at #1234` non-blocker pointer (the TODO-gate remediation form) is NOT refused. `--allow-bloat` is the #126 per-call escape for a genuinely load-bearing reference. Implemented in `teatree.cli.review.bloat_gate`.

Close-trailer scanner (#1398): `ban_close_trailers_on_namespaces` lists fnmatch patterns over `namespace/repo`. DB-home (#1775); set via `t3 <overlay> config_setting set ban_close_trailers_on_namespaces`; the TOML value is ignored on read. When the target PR/MR's repo matches and the body carries a `Closes|Fixes|Resolves` trailer (`part of` and full-URL variants too), `ShipExecutor._build_pr_spec` silently strips those lines before opening the PR (`teatree.core.intake.close_trailer_scanner`). Distinct from the overlay-scoped `forbid_close_keywords` gate (#1012), which refuses the publish; this scanner cleans the body and proceeds.

Open-questions warn (#1933): any open question (solved or not) and any non-explicit assumption must be listed in BOTH the commit body AND the PR description under an `Open questions & assumptions` section (per-item status `decided-by-user` / `assumed` / `open`). `teatree.core.gates.open_questions_gate` runs at both PR-creation chokepoints (`ShipExecutor._build_pr_spec`, orphan-branch `create_or_defer_pr`): a body lacking the heading warns and proceeds. Warn-only per the "a gate without a reliable heuristic warns" rule — the heading wording is not separable enough to block on. Canonical doctrine in `skills/ship/SKILL.md` § 5.

Required description sections (#312): the MR-description generator emits a standard body — `## What`, `## Why`, plus any section an overlay declares mandatory via `OverlayMetadata.get_required_description_sections()` (e.g. `["Configuration"]`). `teatree.core.review.mr_metadata.ensure_standard_body` appends every missing section (with the overlay's `get_description_section_defaults()` body when supplied, so a thin commit still ships a meaningful default) at every PR-creation chokepoint (`ShipExecutor._build_pr_spec`, `ship_preview`, orphan-branch `create_or_defer_pr`); never duplicating a present section and preserving the first line. The gate side — `OverlayMetadata.validate_pr` → `validate_mr_metadata(required_sections=…)` — flags a description missing a declared section, so an explicitly-authored body that bypasses the generator is still rejected. Core is overlay-agnostic: it renders only what the overlay declares, hard-coding no section names or wording.

The no-orphan pre-push hook (`t3 <overlay> pr ensure-pr`) is the seam that has no author-written body at all: it opens the PR from the branch's own commit alone, so every PR it created was born failing this same gate and had to be patched by hand afterwards. `mr_metadata.auto_created_description` renders that body — first line the conventional-commit subject, `## What` the commit message, and `## Why` a statement of PROVENANCE, never an invented rationale, since the hook has no source for the why. The gate itself is unchanged: it recognises no auto-creation marker and still rejects a raw commit-message body.

**That `## Why` states what the PR IS; it does not instruct an author who is not there ([#4424](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4424)).** It read `TODO — … Replace this line with the rationale before requesting review.`, addressed to the author whose absence is the whole reason the hook opened the PR — so nothing ever filled it in, and three cold reviews on one day each filed it separately. The `## Why` now carries the hook's provenance (`AUTO_CREATED_MARKER`, the stable phrase `lacks_rationale` keys on) and the two facts the hook genuinely holds: the branch it rescued, and the `issue_url` of the ticket owning that branch's `Worktree` row. The explicit "no author-written rationale exists" line is CONDITIONAL, not blanket: the hook nests the whole rescued commit message under a generated `## What`, so it is emitted only when that nested body has no `## Why` of its own — found live and self-fixed on the PR this change shipped through ([#4542](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/pull/4542)), where the unconditional version asserted "no rationale" directly beneath one this very commit message carries. The tracked issue carries no closing keyword: a rescue PR is not necessarily the whole fix, so auto-closing its ticket on merge would claim more than the hook knows.

**The ship step CLEARS that body when it adopts the hook's PR ([#3991](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3991)).** `create_pr` adopting an already-open PR took the URL only, so the hook's own body was what merged — a `## Why` a reviewer cannot distinguish from "the author had no rationale". `backends/github/pr_create._adopt_body` now writes the ship's own description onto the adopted PR, gated on the pure core predicate `mr_metadata.lacks_rationale` (blank body / the `AUTO_CREATED_MARKER` phrase / a `## Why` header with nothing under it) so the shapes the hook produces are replaced while an authored body — a redelivered ship re-finding its OWN PR, a human-opened one on the same branch — is never clobbered. A body that cannot be READ is left as-is with a warning, for the same reason. The write is confirmed under §17.1 invariant 13 (`verify_by_reread`), and what it confirms is that the write LANDED (the body is no longer the one that was read) — not that the replacement reads well, since `ensure_standard_body` appends a bare `## Why` to a ship description whose commit has none, and failing on that would red a ship whose write worked. A write that did not land fails the ship rather than reporting success on a PR still carrying the placeholder.

Egress-leak gate family — one doctrine, several entry points, each named here with its load-bearing invariant; detector lists, exit codes, thresholds, and exempt sets live in the code:

- **The ONE leak-policy decision function** ([#3532](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3532), `teatree.hooks.leak_policy`): `decide(term_class, visibility, surface) -> BLOCK | WARN | ALLOW`, TOTAL over (class × visibility × surface). The subsystem's enforcement points each used to compose their own tuple of term source × matcher × visibility resolver × carve-out set × fail direction, so the same decision was re-derived and drifted per site. This module owns the decision, the term TAXONOMY (`LEAK` / `PROSE_COLLIDER` / `TONE` / `ALLOW`) and the three-value `Visibility` enum; it carries no term data, no config reads and no I/O — the registry (`banned_term_registry`), the matcher (`term_match`) and the visibility resolver (`_repo_visibility`) stay where they are, and being a pure leaf keeps it importable from the cold PreToolUse hook and from the Django-side gates alike. **Polarity:** `UNKNOWN` visibility folds onto `PUBLIC` — a destination the gate cannot PROVE non-public is scanned and blocked, so detection failure never weakens the gate. **Surfaces:** `LOCAL_COMMIT` is the one surface that downgrades a blocking class to `WARN` (a commit reaches no remote until `git push`, and the #703 pre-push gate re-scans commit messages before it does); every other surface is an egress with no backstop behind it, so it BLOCKS. Class routing is derived here too — `banned_term_registry.GATE_CLASSES` is built from `classes_for_surface`, so "which classes does this gate scan" also has exactly one answer.
- **An unresolvable publish destination is SCANNED, not skipped** ([#3477](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3477)): `hooks.public_visibility.destination_visibility` classifies an EMPTY slug and one carrying an unexpanded `$VAR` as `UNKNOWN` → scan. `$OWNER` expands at run time and can expand to a public repo, and the sibling classifier `publish_destination.is_public_destination` has always treated both as PUBLIC — the two disagreed, and the disagreement was the fail-open half. The cost of this direction is a scan on a command whose target cannot be read; the cost of the other is an unscanned public egress. The offline `private_repos` allowlist remains the network-free way to keep an own-private post skip-eligible.

- **Public-repo diff privacy-scan** (#685, #730): pre-push hook `refuse-public-push-with-leak.sh` runs `t3 tool privacy-scan` over the pushed diff + commit messages when `origin` is PUBLIC, blocking emails / home paths / private IPs / keys / internal hostnames / banned terms. It fails CLOSED on a genuine finding (a dedicated exit code) but fails OPEN on any other non-zero so a scanner crash cannot wedge every push (#126). Visibility is resolved by `teatree.hooks.repo_visibility_cli`, a thin seam onto the host-routed probe (`gh` for GitHub, `glab` for GitLab) with its day-cached per-slug verdict; the FULL remote URL is passed, never a host-stripped slug, because the host segment is what selects the forge tool. Shelling `gh repo view` here hard-coded ONE forge, so a `gitlab.com` remote was permanently undetermined and the gate re-scanned a PRIVATE repo on every push. `_repo_visibility.slug_for_remote_url` is the normalizer both that CLI and the checkout-based `slug_for_cwd` share.
- **Proactive public-repo identity gate** (#762, #785, #2655): the proactive sibling of the reactive backstop above. `teatree.core.public_identity.is_public_github_remote` drives the clone-local stamping of the canonical GitHub `users.noreply.github.com` author identity onto any worktree off a PUBLIC GitHub clone (the worktree provisioner and `workspace stamp-identity`), so a public-repo commit never carries the inherited real-domain email the leak backstop would later block. It is HOST-AWARE (#2655): the gate short-circuits any non-github host to `False` BEFORE the `gh repo view` visibility probe (`is_github_host`, recognising `github.com` and `github.com-<ssh-alias>`), so a non-github remote (e.g. a `gitlab.com` SSH URL) whose host-stripped `owner/repo` slug collides with a real public github.com repo is never mis-stamped with the github identity. Callers pass the full remote URL — host intact — never the bare slug.
- **Foreign-open-MR push guard** (#2211): pre-push hook `refuse-push-to-foreign-mr.sh` — a collaboration-safety sibling (not a leak gate). For each pushed ref it resolves the branch's backing OPEN PR via `gh pr list --head <branch> --state open` and BLOCKS, naming the author + PR number, when the PR's author login != our `gh api user` login — a teammate's open MR our changes would silently modify (our work belongs on our own branch; a worktree opened to INSPECT a colleague's MR is read-only).
