Our own open MR, a branch with no open MR, and a foreign CLOSED/merged MR (which `--state open` excludes) all pass; so does an MR authored by a login the operator declared for that host in the DB-home host-keyed `self_forge_identities` (a declared `COLD_SETTINGS` key, so `t3 <overlay> config_setting set self_forge_identities '{"gitlab.com": ["my-factory-bot"]}'` validates the table, stores it, and republishes the host projection this hook reads — the write seam a raw sqlite INSERT bypasses; it is a personal identifier, so it is withheld from a shared `config_setting export`) — a bot that authors our MRs so the owner stays eligible to approve them would otherwise read exactly like a teammate, since a forge CLI answers with ONE login. Declaring nothing leaves every verdict unchanged, and a login declared for another host never applies; an explicit `[push-to-foreign-mr-ok: <reason>]` commit-message token allows a genuine co-authoring push. Fails OPEN on a `gh` failure / gh-unavailable / unresolved login so a transient forge-API error never bricks a legitimate push.
- **Banned-terms posting gate** (#1415, `teatree.hooks.banned_terms_scanner`): the `PreToolUse` non-commit sibling. It and the #1213 quote-scanner share `extract_bash_payload` and a per-segment override (#2031/#2034): the `--allow-banned-term`/`--quote-ok` flag token OR a leading `ALLOW_BANNED_TERM=1`/`QUOTE_OK=1` env-prefix clears only on the publish segment, so a chained-segment decoy cannot vouch. The deny message names the **env-prefix** form (`ALLOW_BANNED_TERM=1 <command>` / `QUOTE_OK=1 <command>`), not the `--…` flag: the flag is consumed by the gate's parser, never the posting command, so a `t3 review post-comment` subcommand would reject it as an unknown option — the env prefix is the spelling that actually works on every command. A `> path <<EOF` heredoc is scanned only when its path is posted via a body-file flag; a stdin `--body-file -` heredoc always is. A `t3 review post-comment`/`post-draft-note` segment carries the published body in any of three sources — the positional `NOTE`, `-m`/`--body`, or `--body-file <path>` (#32) — all of which are scanned, while the `--file` argument (a diff ANCHOR, a SOURCE path, not a body-file) is deliberately skipped (`teatree.hooks._t3_review_post`). An inline `--body`/`-m`/positional-NOTE body whose `$(...)` command-substitution sits inside a SINGLE-quoted span is inert literal text bash passes verbatim, so `resolve_inline_body_value` reads the value token's source span (`raw`) and SCANS such a body rather than fail-closing on "publish body could not be read" — a body bash WOULD expand (unquoted / double-quoted `$(...)`) still fails closed toward a PUBLIC or UNKNOWN-visibility target since its real content is unknown before the command runs (#1415), while toward a LITERAL provably-private destination it skips (nothing to leak; only a substitution that DETECTABLY publishes, `$(gh … create …)`, keeps the scan — `public_visibility._construct_bearing_segment_verdict`) — though for a LOCAL `git commit` that unreadable-body sentinel then DOWNGRADES to a warn regardless of landing-repo visibility (`command_targets_local_commit`: a commit is local and the pre-push gate, `refuse-public-push-with-leak.sh` #703, re-scans every commit message before a public push), while a `gh`/`glab` POST keeps hard-blocking; a `git commit -F -` reads its message from STDIN, so a heredoc / `printf|git commit -F -` writer body is RESOLVED and scanned (a real term in a readable commit body still blocks) rather than fail-closed as an unreadable file named `-`. The raw-aware `command_has_opaque_forge_transport` mirrors this so a non-forge segment that merely mentions a single-quoted `$(...)` is not treated as a hidden forge transport. A forge post hidden behind an opaque, wrapper, or path-form transport — `sh -c "gh …"`, `eval "gh …"`, `xargs gh`, `env GH_PAGER= gh`, a `foo=1 gh …` env-prefix, or an absolute-path `/usr/bin/gh` / `./glab` — is now DETECTED as a publish: the leader is canonicalized by basename and wrapper-stripped (`_canonical_leader` / `_WRAPPER_PROGRAMS`, mirroring `raw_merge_detect`), so publish DETECTION and payload EXTRACTION agree and the segment fails CLOSED at BOTH the banned-terms gate and the #1213 quote gate (the opaque-transport fail-closed sentinel is appended outside the body-file guard so the quote gate, which passes `fail_closed_body_file=False`, fails closed too) instead of passing unscanned. An unresolved `gh`/`glab` WRITE destination — e.g. `gh pr review <n> --body …`, whose target repo the hook cannot resolve — is now SCANNED, not skipped (`public_visibility` scans unless the destination is provably non-public). The gate delegates matching to `check-banned-terms.sh`, whose exit-code contract is 0=clean / 1=banned-term-found / 2=scanner-could-not-run; when the shell fallback resolves to an old system `python3` (below the >= 3.13 floor) the matcher import crashes, so the fallback probe-imports the module first and exits 2 (fail CLOSED, loud) rather than letting the crash exit 1 (which collides with "found") become ALLOW. `scan_text` mirrors this: a crash, unexpected exit, or exit-1-with-no-report returns `SCANNER_UNAVAILABLE_MARKER`, while a scan KILLED at its budget returns the separate `SCANNER_TIMEOUT_MARKER`. Both BLOCK on every surface — a security gate that fails open on a crash is the bug class (#1954) — but they are distinct markers because they call for opposite remedies: a timeout is a LOAD signal answered by the `T3_BANNED_TERMS_SCAN_TIMEOUT_S` budget seam (default 10s, kept under the harness's 30s hook ceiling so a killed hook can still report), not by repairing a healthy interpreter. The per-scan `uv run` is `--frozen` so unbounded dependency resolution is not charged against that budget; `--no-sync` is deliberately not used, since the hook's own `UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT` is provisioned by nothing else and skipping the sync would fail closed permanently rather than intermittently. **Company-identifier carve-out** (#1415 over-block): an optional `[teatree].banned_terms_allowlist` array names the org's OWN compound identifiers (`<org>-engineering`, `<org>-product`, internal-URL namespaces) that are never customer PII; `term_match._strip_allowlisted_tokens` blanks each allow-listed identifier's whole-token run BEFORE matching (mirroring the email carve-out), so a SHORTER single-token banned term (a bare org slug) can no longer surface inside a longer company-owned identifier or an internal-URL path — while a genuine customer codename NOT on the allow-list is still blocked, and a bare standalone org-slug token still fires. The carve-out flows through the SINGLE shared matcher, so the shell hook (`banned_terms_cli`), the posting gate (`banned_terms_scanner`, incl. report attribution), and the diff-only commit scan all honour it identically; empty (default) is a no-op preserving prior behaviour.
- **Pre-dispatch quote-scanner** (#1401, `handle_dispatch_prompt_quote_scanner`): the dispatch-boundary companion to #1213 — denies only a HIGH user-voice/PII match in an `Agent`/`Task` prompt, with a `[quote-ok: <reason>]` opt-out, so a verbatim quote cannot leak downstream.
- **Diff comment detectors** (added-lines-only): `code_comment_self_reference` (#1465) is a **blocking** `privacy_scan.py` diff detector (**fail-open per detector**, #1536) for bookkeeping self-references. `code_comment_density` (#1538) is the commit-side half of the comments-as-code rule (#1532; names + types are the docs, a long comment is a code smell → refactor); it is **content-aware** — beyond the line-count ratio / consecutive-run passes it flags a comment whose words merely **restate** the next code line and a docstring opening that merely **echoes the signature** (a single such line suffices), while leaving a genuine non-obvious-why comment and a justified multi-line docstring ALLOWED. It is **advisory** — the standalone check (#1369, `t3 tool comment-density`), the pre-push hook, and the `comment-density-warning` CI job warn and **exit 0**, and it is NOT a `privacy_scan.py` blocking detector (#1844 — no content-blind "overly long prose" heuristic that would flag legit long comments). A golden corpus (`tests/test_comment_density_gate.py`) + an eval pin must-DENY (restatement + signature-echo) symmetric with must-ALLOW (a docstring carrying a real why). The push-stage gates (`doc-update-gate`, `ensure-pr`, the leak gate) are `stages: [push]` — skipped by a bare `prek run --all-files` but re-run by CI — so `t3 tool verify-gates` runs both stages and returns the combined exit code, so local green == CI green.
- **Full-tree banned-brand backstop** (#1570/#1591, `core.banned_terms_tree`, CLI `t3 banned-terms scan-tree`, CI job `banned-terms-tree`): catches what the diff/payload gates structurally cannot — a brand ALREADY committed, invisible to any post-landing diff — by scanning every tracked file's **committed blob** (`git show HEAD:<path>`, working-tree fallback only for an uncommitted file) with the shared whole-token matcher (`teatree.hooks.term_match`, pinned by the parity test). The brand list (`[teatree].banned_brands`) is a curated high-confidence subset distinct from the egress `banned_terms`; unpopulated, the scan is INERT and says so loudly rather than returning a silent clean 0. **The same fail-loud rule holds on the ENUMERATION axis** ([#4354](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4354)): `git_tracked_files` RAISES `TreeEnumerationError` when git could not answer (a non-repo `--repo-root`, an absent `git`, a `safe.directory` refusal, a loaded-box `ls-files` timeout) or answered with no tracked files at all, so the CLI exits MISCONFIGURED (2) instead of reporting `clean (0 findings)` on a tree it never read — zero files scanned yields zero findings whatever the tree contains. `--allow-unset` governs the brand-LIST axis only and never downgrades an unreadable tree. The CI step passes `--require-brands`, upgrading that inert state to a hard MISCONFIGURED failure (exit 2, distinct from exit 1 = findings) so a missing secret reds the job loudly instead of a fake-green no-op. Local dev omits the flag.
- **Consolidated banned-term registry** (banned-terms-registry PR 1, `hooks.banned_term_registry`, core coordinator `core.banned_terms_tree.migrate_registry`, CLI `t3 banned-terms migrate-registry`): the three separate DB-home sources — the egress `banned_terms` (→ `prose_collider`), the high-confidence `banned_brands` (→ `leak`), and the `banned_terms_allowlist` (→ `allow`) — collapse into ONE class-tagged `banned_term_registry` row (or the `TEATREE_TERM_REGISTRY` secret) that `terms_for_gate(gate)` routes by class (`leak` → diff+tree+core, `prose_collider` → diff+core, `tone` → diff, `allow` → the allowlist). **PR 1 is ADDITIVE + dual-read only:** the resolvers (`resolve_banned_terms` / `load_brand_terms` / the allowlist loaders) read the registry when present ELSE fall back to the legacy source, so with the registry unset (today — the row and the secret both absent) resolution is byte-identical, and BOTH-unset still RAISES `BannedTermsUnsetError` (fail-closed). `migrate-registry` reads the three sources, class-tags them, and SELF-VERIFIES the built registry reproduces every effective term (no drop, allowlist round-trips, no per-gate drop) before the operator cutover (PR 2) sets the one row and drops the three secrets — it never writes the store. An explicit `--allow-unset` on `scan-tree` is the fork-PR affordance — fail-closed by default (a genuinely-unset brand list still exits 2), and the fork-PR CI step opts in to the terminology-only pass; the `banned-terms-tree` + lint CI jobs thread the `TEATREE_TERM_REGISTRY` secret alongside the legacy secrets (dual-env, empty until cutover).
- **Programmatic on-behalf egress privacy-scan** (#1295 capability J): the autonomous-events reply transport (`core.reply_transport`) wires the publication privacy gate (`core.gates.privacy_gate.scan_outbound_text`, the egress wrapper of the pure `scan_for_publication`) into its single on-behalf chokepoint — `_BaseReplier._send` and `redeliver`, via `_enforce_privacy`, **scoped to code-host events** (`_FORGE_BY_SOURCE`: GitHub/GitLab only — a Slack channel ref is not a repo target and is out of scope here). A GitHub PR comment / GitLab MR note posted under the user's identity is body-scanned for the overlay's `privacy_redact_terms`/`privacy_block_patterns` plus the built-in quote anchors BEFORE the wire call — closing the gap the Bash-hook banned-terms/quote gates structurally miss (those scan raw `gh`/`glab` command text, not a programmatic API post). **Public-ness is derived from the SAME visibility axis the Bash gates use** (`hooks.publish_destination.is_public_destination` → `_target_is_public`), NOT a per-overlay `public_repos` list (which is empty by default and would make the gate inert): a repo is scanned unless provably private (`private_repos` / `internal_publish_namespaces` allowlists or a cached `gh`/`glab` probe), so it actually protects a real public repo like `souliane/teatree`. It fails CLOSED on a finding (`PublicationPrivacyBlockedError` → FAILED `ReplyDispatch`, never delivered; on `redeliver` it propagates to the retry sweep) and on a classification error (treats the target as public → scanned); a provably-private repo is a clean pass and a bot→user `post_dm` is never scanned. Overlay redact-rule resolution is a best-effort ADD on top of the always-on built-in quote anchors — when no overlay resolves, the built-in detectors are the floor, so the gate never goes inert.
- **Outbound send-proxy — one chokepoint + audit** ([#117](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/117)): `teatree.core.send_proxy` is the single seam EVERY outbound artifact routes through — Slack (`notify_user`, `OnBehalfSlackEgress.post/react`, `reply_transport`) and forge PR/MR/issue comments (`cli/review.ReviewService`). It owns three concerns. **(i) The only posting-credential reader:** `read_posting_credential` is the single point-of-use reader for a Slack bot/app/user token or a GitHub/GitLab forge token, so the two send-path backend constructors (`core.backend_factory`, `backends.loader`) read tokens through it instead of scattered `read_pass` calls (pinned by the single-credential-reader grep-gate; the `t3 slack …` credential-setup/rotation CLI is a documented separate surface). **(ii) A per-overlay destination allowlist** (`send_proxy_allowlist`, DB-home, `fnmatch` over the raw destination and the `<channel>:<destination>` form; the user's own DM is always allowed — never-lockout). **(iii) Redaction/banned-terms on every payload** (whole-token, reusing the overlay's `privacy_redact_terms`). Enforcement posture is the DARK feature flag `send_proxy_mode` (`SendProxyMode`, default `warn`): **`warn` is audit-only** — every send writes a `SendAudit` row (channel, destination, allowlist verdict, redaction matches, and the delegation provenance #119 consumes) but is NEVER blocked and the live payload is NEVER mutated, so the ship-safe rollout accumulates the destination soak an operator seeds the allowlist from before flipping to **`enforce`** (a non-allowlisted destination is refused — `SendBlockedError` before the wire call — and the payload is redacted). The audit row is the `SendAudit` M-C model (its schema folded into the squashed `0001_initial`). The proxy is fail-open in `warn` and never-raise in the audit path, so it can sit on the hot outbound path without ever breaking a send. NOT in scope: credential-broker MITM, network default-deny.

---

## 10. Configuration

**Boot-time credentials from `pass` — no plaintext secrets on disk ([#3454](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3454), [#3433](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3433)).** The headless deploy keeps the box's runtime secrets in its gpg-encrypted `pass` store, not as plaintext in the compose `env_file` (`deploy/teatree.env`). `deploy/entrypoint.sh` sources `TEATREE_GH_TOKEN` and `T3_ADMIN_PASSWORD` from `pass` at boot (paths overridable via `TEATREE_GH_TOKEN_PASS_PATH` / `T3_ADMIN_PASSWORD_PASS_PATH`, defaulting to the box's provisioned layout) — an existing env value always wins, the store is the fallback, so a rotated secret is picked up on the next boot without rewriting `teatree.env`. The deploy workflow no longer writes those two secrets to the env file. This folds credential provisioning into the boot path (the same plane as the Anthropic OAuth token's `anthropic_oauth_pass_paths` routing), so token rotation is a secrets-only `pass` update plus a redeploy rather than an SSH edit of the on-disk env file; `deploy/README.md` documents the provisioning and rotation flow. <!-- skill-symbol-ref: teatree.env is gitignored — the deploy workflow writes it on the box, so it is absent from the tree by design -->

**Honest GitHub-token permission preflight ([#3436](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3436), expanded [#3477](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3477)).** `teatree.core.gates.gh_token_preflight` (mirrored in `deploy/entrypoint.sh`, pinned equal by a test) probes whether `TEATREE_GH_TOKEN` carries the permissions the loop uses, split into two tiers under a never-lockout invariant: a REQUIRED tier (`metadata: read`, `issues: write`, `pull_requests: write`, `contents: write` — unchanged from #3405) that hard-FAILs (`exit 1` / a doctor FAIL) when missing, and a RECOMMENDED tier (`workflows: write`, `actions: write`, `actions: read`, `checks: read`, `statuses: read`, `projects: read`, `secrets: write`, `variables: write`) that only WARNs with remediation — a running deployment missing a recommended permission still boots, with the gated feature (CI trigger/status, auto-merge's required-checks rollup, board sync, the `t3 eval ci-account` OAuth-account switch) simply degraded. It is token-class aware: a **fine-grained** PAT is judged by the per-route `403 Resource not accessible` probe (both tiers, with `workflows: write` deliberately left unprobed — the route's 403-vs-404 ordering for a denied token could not be empirically confirmed, so it is always reported as unprobeable rather than risking a false "missing"), but a **classic** PAT does NOT return that 403 (the probe would fail OPEN), so a classic token is judged entirely by scope membership from the `X-OAuth-Scopes` response header (`repo` required; `workflow`/`read:project` recommended). `projects: read` is probed only when the overlay configures a Projects v2 board. The bash mirror distinguishes a genuine REQUIRED-tier DENIAL (block the deploy loudly) from a TRANSIENT/indeterminate probe failure (retry with backoff, then warn and continue) so a GitHub/network blip can never crash-loop `init`; GitHub exposes no API to widen an existing token, so `format_remediation` always proposes a recreate, never an auto-add.

**Stranded pre-commit patches.** `prek` / `pre-commit` stash every unstaged change in the checkout before running hooks and restore them afterwards; the restore can FAIL (the tree moved on in between, so `git apply` reports `patch does not apply`) and the working tree is then left holding NONE of those changes — the edits are gone from disk, `git status` reads clean, and the only notice is one line in a hook log. The patch file is written on every commit with unstaged changes and never deleted, so its EXISTENCE is the normal steady state and carries no signal; `checks_stranded_prek_patches` therefore keys on CONTENT — a file-hunk whose EVERY added line is absent from the working-tree copy of that same file — bounded to a 24-hour window, since an abandoned edit and a lost one are indistinguishable once they age. Advisory (never gates the exit code), and it names the patch that still holds the work. The root cause is two lanes committing in ONE checkout, which one checkout per lane removes and nothing inside a shared checkout can.

**Git-hook install completeness.** `teatree.core.gates.git_hooks_preflight` probes whether a checkout's `pre-commit` and `pre-push` hooks are actually installed — a `.git/hooks` holding only `*.sample` files is a silently-broken install of the same class as the token preflight above: nothing errors, but every push runs with the whole local gate layer absent (`scripts/hooks/refuse-public-push-with-leak.sh`, the banned-terms gate, and `dev/push-gate.sh` via the push-stage `ci-critical-parity` hook). The failure is not that hooks are never installed but that they land in a DIFFERENT clone than the one work happens in: a containerized `t3 setup` installs into the container clone while every commit and push comes from the host checkout, so a verdict drawn from the installed clone alone reads green while another clone pushes ungated. `teatree.core.gates.git_checkouts.discover_checkouts` therefore enumerates the checkouts no other mechanism covers — the installed clone, every checkout under the auto-isolated worktrees root (including ad-hoc ones created by a bare `git worktree add`), and the clone owning each — and `probe_checkouts` returns one verdict per git hooks dir, so a worktree family collapses onto its clone. It is Django-free, so setup and doctor can call it before `ensure_django`; a teatree-provisioned worktree already gets its hooks from `worktree provision`. Both `t3 setup` (`teatree.cli.setup.git_hooks_installer.GitHooksInstaller`, delegating to the existing `teatree.core.prek_hook.install`, which is idempotent by overwrite) and the doctor check `checks_bootstrap._check_git_hooks_installed` consume that one discovery, so the repair and the verification can never disagree; the doctor hard-FAILs per unprotected checkout, naming its path, the missing hook names, the gates each carries, and the `t3 setup` remediation. An explicitly set `core.hooksPath` pointing anywhere other than the default hooks dir is the operator's deliberate intent — reported and left untouched, never stomped and never counted as a gap.

**Generated-docs merge driver ([#3582](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3582), [#4259](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4259)).** The generated docs — `docs/generated/cli-reference.md`, the antipattern catalog, the management-commands list — collide on nearly every CLI-touching PR: two PRs each adding a `t3` subcommand touch adjacent lines of the same generated list, and a merge-queue rebase re-triggers the same conflict, after which `check_cli_reference_sync` fails until regenerated. The right resolution is a re-run of the file's generator, and **a merge driver cannot be that re-run**: git decides every content merge before it writes any of the merge result to the working tree, so the tree a driver would regenerate from is still the local (ours) side — measured with a control on a merge whose source files did not even conflict. A generator run there reproduces ours byte-for-byte, exits 0, and git records the doc as a CLEAN merge holding ours, dropping the other side with no marker; the outcome then also depends on whether this clone happened to run provisioning, so one merge yields two results. `.gitattributes` still marks these paths `merge=generated`, but `scripts/hooks/git_merge_generated.py` now resolves them by git's own textual 3-way merge (`git merge-file` into the `%A` slot — byte-identical to an unregistered clone, conflicted when the text genuinely conflicts) and writes a stderr advisory naming the generator to re-run once the merge completes. The CI sync gates (`check_cli_reference_sync`, `tests/eval_replay/test_readme_sync.py`) stay the backstop; the advisory is what makes a stale doc loud at merge time rather than red in CI. The driver's *command* lives in per-clone `.git/config` and is never committed, so `teatree.core.git_merge_driver.install_merge_driver` registers `merge.generated.driver` wherever teatree commits from: `t3 setup` walks the same `discover_checkouts` set as the git-hooks installer above (via `teatree.cli.setup.merge_driver_installer.GitMergeDriverInstaller`), and worktree provisioning registers it per worktree. Django-free and idempotent — a clone without the driver merges the same bytes, it just gets no advisory.

The **DB/TOML hard partition** ([#1775](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1775)) — every `UserSettings` field has exactly ONE home (the `ConfigSetting` DB store for the ~100 DB-home fields, including the per-overlay `workspace_dir` whose unset default regroups worktrees under `~/workspace/t3-workspaces/<overlay>/` — resolved by `config.worktree_root()`, distinct from the CLONE root `config.clone_root()` at `~/workspace` where main repo clones live; the `UserSettings` partition is now **FULLY DB-home** (config-unify is complete for settings — the carve-out is EMPTY, so NO `UserSettings` field reads a config file; the last NON-settings config — the definition registries `REGISTRY_SETTINGS` (`[overlays]`, `[e2e_repos]`, `[peer_instances]`) — is DB-home too: each is one JSON-dict `ConfigSetting` row (`config/registries.py`) that `loader._inject_db_registries` overrides into `config.raw` via the Django-free `cold_reader`, so `discover_overlays` / `load_e2e_repos` / `load_peer_instances` are untouched and teatree boots fully DB-configured with no config file present) — config-unify has moved `check_updates` (its pre-Django reader `check_for_updates` now reads the store via the Django-free `cold_reader`), `orchestrator_bash_gate_enabled` (the last per-overlay-TOML-overridable field — per-overlay override now lives in a `ConfigSetting` overlay-scope row, `TOML_OVERLAY_OVERRIDABLE_SETTINGS` is empty; the gate reader `teatree_gate._gate_key_is_enabled` is DB-first via `cold_reader` with a TOML self-rescue fallback; its fellow-travellers `timezone` and `privacy` made the same move and were later retired reader-less by [#4203](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4203)), `handover_mirror_path` (its pre-Django SessionStart reader reads the store via `cold_reader`, which fails open to the same default bootstrap path `write_mirror` uses when unset, so it needs no TOML even when Django is down), `statusline_chain` (the bash statusline hook reads it from the canonical sqlite via the `sqlite3` CLI + `json_each`, so it needs no importable teatree python and no TOML parse), and `autoload` (the #256 engagement flag; its cold pre-Django readers — `hooks/scripts/teatree_settings.autoload_enabled` via `_cold_db_bool` and `statusline.sh` via the `sqlite3` CLI — read the store DB-ONLY, so a `[teatree] autoload` TOML value is ignored on read and the SessionStart how-to advisory points at `config_setting set autoload true`), and finally the last two — the nested structured tables `speak` / `mr_reminder` (stored as JSON-dict `ConfigSetting` rows via `parse_speak_setting` / `parse_mr_reminder_setting`, rebuilt bespoke by the resolver since a dict cannot flat-replace the dataclass field — `resolution._BESPOKE_STRUCTURED_FIELDS`, with `speak` keeping its per-overlay MERGE; the cold Stop-hook `speak` reader uses `cold_reader.read_setting`, a dict) to the DB, env on top of either — Django settings, `OverlayConfig` methods, logging, data storage, and the state-placement rule (cache vs intent, #628) live in [docs/blueprint/configuration.md](docs/blueprint/configuration.md). A DB-home value is set with `t3 <overlay> config_setting set <key> <json> [--overlay <name>]` (migrate a pre-partition file once with `config_setting import`); a value left in `[teatree]` / `[overlays.<name>]` for a DB-home key is ignored on read. Its override table also documents the DB-home knobs: `mr_title_regex` (#1540, the Conventional-Commits title pattern the `pr create` gate enforces, no `--force` bypass), the `autonomy` switch (#1668, collapsing those gates into one value, set via `t3 <overlay> autonomy set`), and the `wip` throughput dial (`slow < medium < full < boost`, orthogonal to `mode`/`autonomy`). The `### 10.1 Configuration store` subsection cited from `commands/followup.py` is preserved there. **Toward DB-only runtime (config-unify PR1).** `teatree.config.cold_reader` is a Django-free, stdlib-only reader of the same `teatree_config_setting` store for the cold bash/statusline path that cannot afford a Django import — the deliberate inverse of `teatree.paths.resolve_data_dir`: it always targets the PRIMARY `~/.local/share/teatree/db.sqlite3` (a worktree checkout reads the installed `t3`'s config, not an isolated per-worktree copy), with a test pinning its duplicated path computation equal to `teatree.paths.TRUE_CANONICAL_DB`. Every read fails open to the caller's default. Beyond the `teatree_config_setting` store it also exposes `loop_status(name)` — a Django-free read of the `teatree_loop_state` control plane (the cold twin of `LoopState.objects.status_of`: an absent row / unreadable DB → the runnable `enabled` default), sharing one WAL-aware sqlite path (`cold_db.fetch_one`; the Django-free sqlite plumbing — path resolution, read-only open, single-row fetch, `loop_status`, `row_exists` — lives in `teatree.config.cold_db`, re-exported through `cold_reader`). Its cold callers are the `teatree_settings` gate readers AND the fast Stop/PreToolUse hooks: `loop_state_self_pump_gate` (the durable loop pause/disable via `loop_status`) replaced a per-hook `t3` subprocess that cold-booted Django in a child process on the synchronous hot path — the ~5s the Stop hook paid every turn/tick end, i.e. the recurring Stop-hook TIMEOUT. There is no posture read left to make: [#3826](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3826) deleted the mirror file the probe used to obey (a second source of truth that drifted a week out of date and silently muted the owner), and [#4202](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4202) deleted the posture booleans themselves along with the Django-free resolver that read them — a mode is a per-loop on/off table, so no hook asks the control DB what it MEANS. The same treatment covers the **UserPromptSubmit** hot path (#22): `cold_reader.row_exists` is a Django-free existence probe (fail-open on an unconfirmable read) backing the `hooks/scripts/ups_fastpath.py` sibling — `has_pending_question_work` / `has_pending_chat_work` let `handle_inject_pending_questions` / `handle_inject_pending_chat` return BEFORE `django.setup()` when the `DeferredQuestion` / `PendingChatInjection` backlog is empty (the common turn), and `record_presence` writes the live-presence heartbeat with no Django at all, by calling the shared foundation leaf `teatree.live_presence.PresenceHeartbeat.record` itself (one implementation, not a byte-compatible copy), so `handle_record_presence` no longer boots Django on every prompt. `django.setup()` is idempotent, so removing all three in-process boots is what drops the cold UserPromptSubmit hook from ~8s to stdlib-only. **Lossless cold-hook import (config-unify PR2).** `teatree.config.cold_hook_settings.COLD_HOOK_SETTINGS` registers the pre-Django, hook-only global `[teatree]` keys that have no `UserSettings` field — the `teatree_settings` gate kill-switches (`deny_circuit_breaker_enabled`, `self_dm_gate_enabled`, …) plus the bespoke `hook_router` integer budgets (`deny_circuit_breaker_threshold`, `orchestrator_turn_budget`, `orchestrator_turn_wall_clock_seconds`). `import_toml_to_db` now unions it with `OVERLAY_OVERRIDABLE_SETTINGS` for the GLOBAL `[teatree]` table only, so a non-default gate or threshold survives the TOML→DB import instead of resetting to its in-code default when a later PR flips the cold readers onto the store. `config_setting` get/set/clear now handle these keys as part of the unified known-key set (each is folded into `ALL_KNOWN_CONFIG_SETTINGS` by its parser), and the cold readers are DB-only: `hooks/scripts/teatree_settings` resolves each flag from the `ConfigSetting` store via `cold_reader` and falls back to the compiled-in `ColdHookSetting.default` when no row exists, so the never-lockout fallback reads no file. A no-silent-drop fitness test mechanically enumerates the live cold-read sites, so a new hook gate flag added without a registry entry turns the suite red. **TOML round-trip export (config-unify PR6).** `teatree.core.config_interchange.migration.export_db_to_toml` is the precise inverse of `import_toml_to_db`: it serialises the `ConfigSetting` store back to TOML — GLOBAL rows under `[teatree]`, each overlay scope under `[overlays.<name>]`, each JSON-stored value rendered as its native TOML scalar — so `export → import → export` is a fixed point and TOML is a full round-trip interchange format (no longer import-only). The load side is `import_toml_to_db(text, dry_run)` + `t3 <overlay> config_setting import [--input <path>] [--dry-run]`: it folds retired aliases onto their live key, REJECTS unknown and secret/personal-identifier keys (reusing the export withhold rule and refusing the WHOLE import so a bad key never leaves a partial store), validates every value through the same registry parser the resolver applies on read (`ALL_KNOWN_CONFIG_SETTINGS`), and writes NO row for a value equal to the shipped default — so a dump of `defaults.toml` imports to zero rows and `restore = delete row` holds. Export key-sorts every table/scope so the dump is a deterministic function of the store's CONTENT, which is what makes the round-trip byte-stable. The `t3 <overlay> config_setting export [--overlay <name>] [--output <path>]` subcommand dumps to stdout by default; `--overlay` scopes the dump to one overlay. Only `ConfigSetting` rows are read, so bootstrap-file-only and secret-ref keys never materialise in the dump. **Byte-identical `defaults.toml` round trip + nested file shape ([#3825](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3825)).** The store holds only DELTAS from the shipped floor, so the dump and `defaults.toml` were not inverses — importing the shipped file writes zero rows and exporting them emits no `[teatree]` table. Two INDEPENDENT filters make the inverse expressible: `default_keys_only` restricts what is ELIGIBLE to the `Category.DEFAULT` key set (dropping registries, secrets, personal identifiers and every overlay scope) and `include_defaults` widens WHICH eligible keys are emitted from divergent-only to all of them, filling a key with no DB row from `teatree.config.provenance.resolve_settings` restricted to the PERSISTED tiers (`env` and the active overlay's code defaults are machine/overlay state a shipped file must not bake in). Both default off, so an unfiltered dump emits exactly the rows it always has (the nesting below re-shapes how every surface RENDERS them, but adds and drops nothing); both on is the defaults shape, whose emitted key set is asserted EQUAL to the `Category.DEFAULT` set (equality, not a subset — a subset check still lets a key vanish) and which renders through `defaults_snapshot.render_toml`, the SAME emitter the owner-approved `snapshot_settings_defaults` writes with. `export(defaults-shape)` after `import(defaults.toml)` therefore reproduces the file byte for byte, header and seed tables included. The file renders the declaration hierarchy as real NESTED TOML tables while the KEY NAMESPACE stays flat — that namespace is the persisted contract every env override, `ConfigSetting` row and cold sqlite3 read depends on — and `config/cold_defaults.flatten_settings_table`, the single Django-free reader, flattens the group wrappers back on read (a sub-table named after a declared setting, `speak` / `mr_reminder`, is that setting's value; any other is a group). Import applies the same flattening, so a nested file and a flat one resolve identically. The seed-table half of both directions lives in `teatree.core.config_interchange.seed_tables`. **Export-leak guard — secrets MAY live in the DB (config-unify, PR1).** The earlier rule that banned-terms/brands carry **NO DB tier** is **superseded**: the DB store is private to the operator, so a customer/brand secret LIVING in the DB is fine — the leak surface is the **EXPORT** (the future "auto-configure a fresh teatree for a new user" path), not the storage. `export_db_to_toml` therefore applies a **secret guard** that withholds a row by DEFAULT under two complementary defenses (neither sufficient alone): (1) a per-setting `private` flag expressed as membership in `teatree.config.secret_settings.SECRET_SETTINGS` (the `banned_*` lists, `private_repos`/`private_tests`, `internal_publish_namespaces`, `overlay_leak_terms`, the token refs) — a static denylist; and (2) an active **banned-term content scan** over each exported `key + json(value)` (terms resolved from the live config via `resolve_banned_terms` + `load_brand_terms`, fail-safe to empty), which catches a NON-listed key whose value happens to embed a customer/brand term — the case a keylist can never enumerate. `export_db_to_toml` returns a `ConfigExport(toml, redacted)` carrying the withheld `RedactedRow(scope, key, reason)` set; `t3 <overlay> config_setting export` warns each withheld row + a summary on stderr, and `--include-private` exports everything for a PERSONAL, never-shared backup. **A private backup is restorable, and says so ([#4156](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4156)).** `--include-private` emits exactly the rows the import refuses, so the file it wrote was one nothing could read back and nothing said so — the flag whose purpose is a COMPLETE backup produced an unrestorable one. The fix keeps the fail-closed row rule and adds the file-level half of the same guard in `config_interchange.secret_guard`: such a dump stamps itself with a reserved `[backup] include_private = true` table (`mark_private_backup` / `is_private_backup`), the export names on stderr what it wrote and how to restore it, and `t3 <overlay> config_setting import --restore-private` accepts the private rows of THAT file only — `restore_private` grants nothing unless the document also declares itself a backup, so an ordinary shared dump's secret rows are refused under the flag exactly as without it, and every other caller (the dashboard included, which points at the CLI instead) keeps the default-False posture. The marker is an intent declaration, not a security boundary; what the guard rests on is the DEFAULT import refusing every private row whatever the file claims. The defaults shape is exempt from the stamp so `export(import(defaults.toml))` stays byte-identical. This guard is the prerequisite that lets a later slice move the secret lists themselves into the DB (closing the last TOML carve-out) without a shared export ever leaking them. The pre-push scanner alignment still holds: `scripts/privacy_scan.py` resolves via the shared `teatree.hooks.banned_terms_cli.resolve_banned_terms` (env override → `[teatree].banned_terms` TOML, fail-CLOSED — a present-but-unset/unreadable config reports the detector INERT on stderr rather than degrading to a silent empty ban list), and the banned-terms GATE flag (`banned_terms_gate_enabled`) is already DB-first via the cold reader (PR3).

**The settings dashboard renders one section per request, and names value PROVENANCE ([#3825](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3825)).** `/dash/settings/` rendered every schema key at once — 272 `<form>`, 1,060 `<input>` (812 hidden), 271 CSRF tokens, 260KB, 14,212px tall and wider than the viewport. Sections now sit on the LEFT and the selected section's rows on the RIGHT: `dash.settings_editor.build_settings_sections` derives the nav from the group tree over the key NAMES (no value resolution), `build_settings_group` resolves one section's rows, and the nav `hx-get`s `dash:settings_group` into the detail pane. The per-row `hx-target="closest tr"` swap is unchanged (an edit never re-renders the document), `key`/`scope` moved into the `hx-post` URL instead of hidden fields, and the body's `hx-headers` carries ONE CSRF token per page. The never-drop guarantee moved with the layout: the partition is total, so every schema key is reachable through exactly one section — asserted over the whole section list rather than within one page. The near-constant `category` column (the setting's KIND, `default` for hundreds of rows running, misread as "this value came from the default") is replaced by `teatree.config.provenance`, which walks the resolver's OWN layer readers (`read_setting_layers` / `env_setting_overrides` / `overlay_code_defaults`) instead of folding them, so a row's value and the tier that supplied it — env / DB overlay scope / DB global scope / overlay code default / shipped file / code default — can never disagree. Import is a file upload rather than a textarea; the dry-run preview and the safety-posture confirm phrase are unchanged, and both live on the `/dash/import-export/` page rather than here.

**The settings dashboard is a comparison GRID, and its help text is authored once ([#3880](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3880)).** A row is one SETTING with its shipped default and then one cell per scope — `global` first, then each overlay — so a value that diverges between scopes is one line rather than a per-scope hunt (the class of divergence that muted the owner for a week: a stale mirror holding one posture while the DB held another). A cell is green while it equals the default and brown once someone has changed it, with the icon and words kept beside the colour so the verdict survives greyscale; each section in the nav carries a brown count of the settings in it that DIFFER, counting a setting ONCE however many scopes override it. Provenance moved from a column to the cell's tooltip and *restore default* is gone — with click-to-edit, emptying a cell IS the restore, and the cell posts itself on change with no save button, swapping the whole `<tr>` back because a global write changes what every overlay column inherits. Anything the schema constrains to a listable set (`bool`, an enum, a `Literal`) renders as a SELECT derived from the field's own annotation (`schema.setting_choices`), so an invalid value is impossible to ENTER rather than rejected afterwards. Help text lives once in `config/setting_help.py` — Django- and pydantic-free, because the shipped file is written from the cold path — and renders both as the tooltip here and as the tail of the trailing comment beside that key in `config/defaults.toml`, with the byte-identical round trip still green and `tests/config/test_setting_help.py` pinning the table TOTAL over the schema. That comment LEADS with what the key ACCEPTS ([#4053](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4053)) — `config/setting_annotation.py` names the stored type off the field annotation and pairs it with the alternatives from the SAME `schema.setting_choices` the select above is built from, written out as one line there and nowhere else — so a dump reviewed away from this page answers "may this take any string?" without the reader opening the schema, and the two surfaces cannot come to disagree about what a key admits. The type naming sits beside the rendering rather than in `schema` for the reason `setting_choices` gives about its own values: how a stored form READS is a surface decision (a `dict` is a `table` in the file), and `tests/teatree_config/test_setting_annotation.py` pins it TOTAL over the schema so a shape it cannot name never ships silently. The page's query cost is now the number of SCOPES and still constant in the number of settings.

**Every dashboard read is bounded by the page, never by table size ([#3873](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3873)).** `/dash/health/` took 21.3s and `/dash/tickets/<id>/` answered 5,005,822 bytes, so a card read as one that would not open. Neither surface had changed — `TicketTransition` reached 2.96M rows and `TaskAttempt` 340k, and code that reads a whole table crosses the threshold silently. Four reads are bounded at the source. The cycle-to-date spend chip aggregates in SQL (`TaskAttemptQuerySet.usage_groups` groups on `(model, lane, phase, estimated, reported?)`, `CostBreakdown.from_groups` totals the buckets, and `from_usages` funnels through the SAME accumulator so the per-row and aggregate spellings cannot compute different numbers) behind the `taskattempt_cost_cover` covering index, so the scan reads an index rather than a table whose rows carry the error/result payloads. The drawer keeps the newest `TRANSITION_ROWS` / `TASK_ROWS` / `ATTEMPT_ROWS` of each history — the attempt cap a SQL window, since a prefetch that fetches thousands to render ten is the same unbounded read one level down — and each panel STATES the total it truncated from, because a silent truncation is the same defect wearing a different hat. `build_ticket_lifecycle_mermaid` draws DISTINCT edges: a state diagram is a set, so the worst ticket's 22,965 rows are 10 arrows. And a polled panel carries `hx-sync` while a board card carries a stable `id`, so a slow render cannot start a second poll over itself or re-morph the element under the cursor. `RequestScopedReadCacheMiddleware` + `teatree.request_cache` collapse the repeated settings / overlay-discovery / preset / loop reads one render issues into one each, memoized only inside an explicitly entered scope (the CLI and the loop tick are untouched) and dropped by ANY non-SELECT statement — keying invalidation on the SQL rather than on model signals is what keeps read-your-writes true for a bulk `update()`. Django 6.0 has no automatic prefetching (verified: ten rows still cost eleven queries), so the explicit tools do the work. Every page's plan is pinned by `assertNumQueries` at two populated sizes, so an absolute count AND its flatness both turn red.

**Cycle time is measured continuously, not by hand ([#3847](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3847)).** [#3994](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3994) produced the per-transition median table once, from a hand-written query, and it named `planned -> coded` and `reviewed -> shipped` as 71% of a 210-minute cycle — a bottleneck visible only by hand is one nobody looks at until after something has gone wrong. `core/cycle_time/` answers it from recorded rows: `spans.py` walks each ticket's `TicketTransition` chain over `state_edges()` and measures every `from_state -> to_state` edge against its predecessor's timestamp; `distribution.py` reduces a window to median AND p90 per edge (a median alone hides the tail, and the tail is what blocks a delivery day) plus the same edge bucketed over time; `timeline.py` splits each span into the stretch an agent HELD the phase and the remainder it merely waited, since a phase starved of dispatches and a phase an agent ground on for an hour are different problems. **No duration subtracts `TaskAttempt.started_at`.** That column is `auto_now_add`, and every attempt row is INSERTED at agent completion, so `ended_at - started_at` is ~0 for every row the factory has ever written — a work-time built on it reports that no agent ever worked. Work time comes from `Task.admitted_at` (the runner-handoff stamp) paired with the attempt's completion stamp, merged across overlapping tasks so parallel dispatches into one phase are one elapsed stretch rather than double-counted; no new column was added, because every figure was already recorded for another purpose. `admitted_at` is stamped going forward only, so a span where an attempt demonstrably ENDED yet no task carries the stamp reports its split as UNMEASURED rather than as zero work — calling an unmeasurable stretch "queue wait" would be the same fiction reached by a different route, and it is the shape most historical rows have. Spend rides alongside through the shared `AttemptUsage` seam, carrying the estimated share separately so a price-table guess is never rendered as a reported figure. `/dash/cycle-time/` (nav item *Cycle time*) draws it: the aggregate table, a trend line per slowest edge, and one stacked bar per recently-active ticket whose segments carry the queue/work split, with that ticket's spend beside them marked estimated or reported. The charts are inline SVG whose geometry `dash/charts.py` computes — no charting library is introduced, every mark inherits the FSM rail's `--col-hue` through the same `[data-group]` attribute the board columns use, and every coordinate is assertable from a plain test rather than locked inside a canvas. All bars share ONE scale and all trend series share one vertical scale, because per-bar normalisation would draw a 3-minute edge and an 85-minute one identically and erase the comparison the page exists for. Its query plan is pinned flat like every other page.

**Agent transcripts have a home in the nav ([#3873](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3873)).** `/dash/transcript/<session_id>/` always answered 200 but was linked only from a ticket's drawer, so reading a transcript meant already knowing which ticket to open. `/dash/sessions/` (nav item *Sessions*) lists the most recent `SESSION_ROWS` agent sessions — one row per session, de-duplicated by a SQL window over `agent_session_id` — each linking to its redacted tail, and the transcript page's nav lands back on it. The index renders dispatch facts only and never an attempt's `error` body; the tail routes every line through `redact_for_local_display`, pinned by a Django-test-client test asserting a configured banned term never appears in either page's response bytes. Each row also carries the skill bundle its dispatch recorded, on the shared `dash/partials/_skills.html` rule.

**The dashboard answers "what is running right now" ([#3856](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3856), [#3886](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3886)).** The board renders ticket FSM state, which is a LAGGING view — a refused loop and a loop that swept and found nothing both leave it unchanged, so the single question an operator asks most often about an autonomous system had no surface. `/dash/live/` (nav item *Live*, read model `dash.live.build_live_view`) is that surface, four panels over state that is ALREADY recorded rather than re-derived at render time: **running** — `TaskAttempt` rows with no `ended_at`, carrying phase, execution target, lane, model, elapsed and the transcript link; **queue** — the PENDING/CLAIMED `Task` counts in ONE grouped read (two `count()` calls would report an instant that never existed) plus what is next; **loops** — every REGISTERED mini-loop with its cadence anchors and, when the live tick would refuse it, the tick's OWN reason, because `loops.loop_table.loop_block_reasons` is the read-only projection of the same `_admission_block` the fan-out gates on and can therefore never drift into a second vocabulary; and **outcomes** — a short tail of ENDED attempts with usage-window parks marked, so a park storm is visible while it happens. Polled with the board's `hx-sync` + `morph:innerHTML` pair and pinned by `assertNumQueries` at two populations like every other page. It renders dispatch facts ONLY and never an attempt's `error` body — the transcript click-through remains the one surface showing agent output — pinned by a test asserting a configured banned term never reaches the response bytes. Every surface listing a task also renders the skill bundle the dispatch RECORDED (`TaskAttempt.skills_loaded`), through one shared rule (`dash.skills.skill_bundle` + `dash/partials/_skills.html`): the bundle is assembled from sources that can each silently contribute nothing — phase frontmatter, cwd detection, the transitive `requires:` chain against a cached index, the overlay's companions — so an EMPTY bundle on a headless dispatch renders as a fault chip rather than as blank, while an interactive attempt (which resolves no bundle by construction) is exempt. Re-derivation at render time is deliberately not done: it would report today's answer for yesterday's dispatch, the exact bug class the panel exists to expose. A ticket's PR chips ([#3909](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3909)) render the row's human state label and carry that state as a class, so a merged, a review-waiting and an abandoned PR do not read as three identical chips; `PullRequest.State` gained the terminal `CLOSED` member that made the third of those expressible, and the teardown gate — which already re-reads every non-terminal row's live forge state — now RECORDS that verdict (`PullRequest.objects.settle_forge_state`, terminal verdicts only, never on the fail-open `UNKNOWN`) instead of discarding it, so an abandoned PR stops advertising live work and a settled row is not re-probed on every later pass.

**The shipped posture is autonomous by default, and every raise is a named authorization ([#3895](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3895)).** `config/defaults.toml` is the DEFAULTS tier the resolver reads (`resolution._toml_default_rows`), so a dataclass-default edit alone is INERT — the shipped file wins. On the owner's explicit authorization the file ships `autonomy = full`, `mode = auto`, `wip = full`, and the `issue_implementer_enabled` / `triage_assessor_enabled` / `directive_loop_enabled` master gates ON: a fresh install intakes and delivers without an operator opt-in. Both tiers move together, so `defaults_approvals.audit_shipped_defaults` sees no divergence — that gate compares the shipped file against the in-code default and reports a `SAFETY_POSTURE_KEYS` / dark-flag divergence as FORBIDDEN, unapprovable by any path. `autonomy` is therefore raised by moving BOTH tiers, and the raise is visible in exactly one place: `tests/config/test_defaults_file.py::TestSafetyAndDarkFlagsPinned` carries `_FAIL_CLOSED` (what safe MEANS per safety-posture key) beside `_PINNED` (what ships), and refuses any key whose shipped literal leaves its fail-closed value without an `_OWNER_RAISED` entry naming the authorization. The completeness assertion is untouched — `_PINNED` still covers `SAFETY_POSTURE_KEYS | dark_flags()` exactly, and every dark flag's literal must equal its own `off_value`. The raise is DECOUPLED from colleague egress: `on_behalf_post_mode` leaves `_AUTONOMY_COLLAPSED_GATE_VALUES` on the #3630 argument one gate over — "carry the work end to end" is a different decision from speaking to a colleague under the owner's own identity with no per-action approval. Its shipped `draft_or_ask` was INERT while the collapse held (a shipped TOML default is `defaults_base`, not a pin, so `full` rewrote it to `immediate` at read time); with the key out of the set, the shipped default is what resolves and the gate stays closed at every tier. Opening it is its own named opt-in — an explicit `on_behalf_post_mode = "immediate"`. What did NOT move: `require_human_approval_to_merge` (#3630 — no tier collapses it, and neither does the raised default), `on_behalf_post_mode` (#3895, above), the DARK safety gates, and every always-on floor (independent cold review, CI-green-before-merge, the leak gate, the recorded-approver keystone).

**Feature flags vs. settings (T4-PR-1).** A *setting* is a durable knob the operator tunes and keeps; a *feature flag* is a TEMPORARY switch that gates in-flight code and is meant to DIE — born with the code it guards, removed once that code is trusted (or abandoned). Both are `UserSettings` bool fields and resolve their VALUE identically through the untouched `env → ConfigSetting(overlay) → ConfigSetting(global) → dataclass-default` chain; the code-level registry `teatree.config.feature_flags.FEATURE_FLAGS` only DISCRIMINATES a flag from a setting, GOVERNS its lifecycle, and AUDITS dead toggles — it never becomes a second value tier (the value lives in the `ConfigSetting` store; the registry lives in code, because a flag is born and dies with the code it gates). Each `FeatureFlag` carries a `stage` (`FlagStage`: `DARK` → `SETTLING` → `REMOVE`), a `tracking_issue`, a `summary`, and an `off_value` (`False` for a positive-sense `*_enabled` flag, `True` for an inverted-sense `*_disabled` one). The **lifecycle** runs `DARK` (gated code in flight, ships OFF, enabled per-install for a trial) → `SETTLING` (trusted, default-enable imminent, flag survives as an escape hatch) → `REMOVE` (gated code permanent, the toggle is dead weight to delete). This is the typed-registry-plus-fitness-test idiom of `SETTING_HOMES` / `COLD_HOOK_SETTINGS`: the conformance suite `tests/config/test_feature_flags.py` keeps every entry honest — each names a real `bool` `UserSettings` field registered in `OVERLAY_OVERRIDABLE_SETTINGS` (registration-drift closed like `cold_hook_settings`), carries a non-empty `tracking_issue` and a valid `stage`, and — the load-bearing Goodhart guard — a `DARK` flag's dataclass default MUST equal its `off_value`, so a dark feature can never ship default-ON without a code-reviewed stage demotion. Surfaces: `t3 config show` partitions flags OUT of the user-facing `intent` dump into a stage-labelled `flags` section (a temporary switch never reads as a knob to keep); `t3 <overlay> config_setting set|get <flag>` appends a `[feature flag, stage=…, tracking …]` trailer; and `t3 <overlay> config_setting flags` is the read-only dead-toggle audit report that surfaces a `REMOVE`-stage flag LOUD. Seeded with `outer_loop_enabled` (DARK — the OFF switch the T4 autoresearch outer loop ships behind; behaviorally inert, nothing reads it yet), among others. `loop_runner_enabled` is a durable operational kill-switch, not a feature flag — the worker owns the cadence by default, so the *setting* survives as an escape hatch (it is not registered in `FEATURE_FLAGS`), the DELETE the lifecycle prescribes rather than a `REMOVE` stage. The live registry is mostly `DARK` plus three `SETTLING` (`incremental_push_gate` #122, `limit_autorecovery_enabled` #3691, `directive_loop_enabled` #3895) and no `REMOVE`, so the stage-discrimination machinery (`dark_flags`, `render_flags_audit`) is proven non-vacuously over a mixed fixture. A graduation is a REAL code change reviewed as one — stage moved, summary rewritten, and a dedicated conformance class pinning the new stage, the default-ON, the exit from `dark_flags()`, and that the neighbouring safety gates did NOT graduate along with it. `dark_flags()` is the query hook a later self-catching critic uses to tie a dark flag to the done-means-merged status of the code it gates. **Classification is mandatory, not optional:** every `require_*` `UserSettings` toggle must appear exactly once in `FEATURE_FLAGS` or in `DURABLE_GATE_SETTINGS` (the sibling frozenset naming the gate toggles that are durable operator policy — each gates a complete capability whose satisfying artifact has a producer command, or is a standing doctrine knob). An unclassified toggle turns the conformance suite RED; that hole is how `require_spec_coverage` shipped gated, default-off and reviewed by nothing while its ON state blocked every delivery.

Local text-to-speech (#2060): `teatree.core.speak.deliver_user_dm()` is the one bot→user-DM chokepoint, gated on macOS `say`, driven by the per-overlay `[teatree.speak]` sub-table. `local` controls speaker playback (`off`/`dm`/`all`) and the independent `slack` bool attaches audio to the DM; local playback is silenced at the playback call site when the resolved mode defers questions (`away` or `away` — the user is not at the keyboard, #2544), preserving `slack` and the configured `local` value (exception-safe). Playback is mutually exclusive via a bounded-wait cross-process `flock` so fan-out reads never overlap or backlog (#2152/#2156), and `clean_for_speech()` strips markdown/URLs/emoji and drops status/log lines so `say` reads prose, not "Info" preamble (#277). **Meeting-aware mute + question-mirror parity (#2171):** the same playback call site also silences local `say` while a configured presence backend (`[teatree.speak] presence_backend`, `teatree.core.presence` + `teatree.backends.msteams.presence` over MS Graph `/me/presence`) reports the user in a meeting — Slack arm and configured `local` untouched, unknown/unreachable never suppresses; and the AskUserQuestion Slack mirror now carries audio to the phone too (the `hook_router` injects an enricher into the `slack_mirror` leaf that spawns `t3 speak-dm` detached after the text question lands). Config: `SpeakConfig`/`LocalPlayback`, appendix §10.1.1.

**Every empty renders as its own kind ([#4078](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4078)).** `core.config_display.render_value` is the ONE value-to-display rule behind the settings editor, the read-only config surface and the Django admin. `None` reads as the em-dash — it is the one empty that means "no value here" — while `""`, `[]` and `{}` each name their own shape, so an empty list can never read as an unset value. A key the shipped file carries no entry for renders `NO_SHIPPED_DEFAULT` rather than a bare `none` that reads as a value. A free-text cell shows `ScopeCell.editable`, which is the JSON literal except that an unset value renders as an empty box rather than the four letters `null`; `ScopeCell.selected` keeps the literal so a `<select>` still matches its own `null` option, and the emptied-cell restore gesture is unchanged.

**Cadence is a derived grid, not an invented floor ([#4079](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4079)).** `loops.loop_cadence_editing.CADENCE_STEP_SECONDS` IS `timer_chains.IDLE_POLL_FLOOR_SECONDS`, not a second constant restating it: every loop rides a self-rescheduling `loop_timer` chain whose successor is held at that floor on every path that does not complete a clean tick (a held/not-due loop, a faulted tick whose anchor never moved, a cadence-less loop), so a sub-floor or off-grid cadence is honoured only while nothing goes wrong. Intervals must be positive multiples of the step, and an off-grid write is **refused** with a message naming the admissible values either side — never silently rounded, because storing a number the operator did not type is the same untruth the derivation removes. Rows written before the grid are REPORTED (`off_grid_cadences`, surfaced on the loops table) and left exactly as they are: only the operator knows what a 45s row meant. The bounds explanation is the table's legend once; a per-row note survives only where that row's bounds genuinely differ — a `cadence_is_floor` loop's registry maximum.

**A stored row's DIVERGENCE from the shipped default is reported, not just its value ([#4074](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4074)).** `ConfigSetting` holds deltas from `config/defaults.toml`, and nothing said when the file's floor moved UNDER a row: `config_setting get` answers `[source: db, global]`, accurate and silent about the default having changed since the row was written. `teatree.cli.doctor.checks_config_drift` closes that — a `t3 doctor` listing of every stored row whose value differs from its shipped default, naming key, scope, stored value and shipped value. It is **advisory and never gates the exit code**: most divergent rows are deliberate operator intent, and the finding is precisely that a drifted row and an intended one are otherwise indistinguishable. Values pass through the shared `core.config_display` masking taxonomy, so a drifted secret is still NAMED without either value reaching the output. A key the shipped file carries no entry for is skipped — there is no shipped value to diverge from, and the Personal/Secret keys absent by construction would otherwise bury the real findings.

---

## 11. Skills & Plugin Architecture

Skills live in `skills/*/` — one `SKILL.md` + optional `references/` per skill. When installed as a plugin, skills are namespaced under `t3:` (e.g. `/t3:code`). The lifecycle skill set is `code`, `debug`, `test`, `review`, `review-request`, `ship`, `ticket`, `workspace`, `handover`, `next`, `retro`, `contribute`, `setup`, `platforms`, `rules`. The read-only auxiliary skill `checking` sits alongside it — the what-did-I-miss report, the pending deferred questions, the daily follow-up routine, and the session's task list (via `tasks list --session`) plus the in-session harness-TODO maintenance discipline — the agent reconciles its live `TaskList`/`TaskUpdate`/`TaskCreate` list against the conversation each turn, aided by the read-only `tasks reconcile-checklist` emitter; teatree cannot write the live harness list from a hook/subprocess, so this maintenance is an in-session discipline, not a background job).

Skills declare dependencies via a single YAML frontmatter `requires:` field (transitive, topo-sorted, cycle-checked; a required skill with no `SKILL.md` — an external methodology skill — warns and passes through so the `Skill` tool still loads it). Third-party skill frameworks (e.g. superpowers) are absorbed into the `rules` skill rather than delegated, to avoid context duplication.

**Sub-agents (`agents/`).** Phase agents wrap skill bundles (`orchestrator`, `coder`, `tester`, `e2e`, `e2e-review`, `reviewer`, `review-request`, `shipper`, `debugger`, `followup`, `planner`, `answerer`, `scanning-news`, `bughunter`, `triage-assessor`) — each a YAML+description wrapper referencing skills via `skills:` frontmatter, no content duplication. `e2e-review` ([#2298](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2298)) is the reviewer leg of the EXTERNAL review loop (dispatched for the `e2e_reviewing` phase), making the skill↔agent pairing 1:1 for `e2e`/`e2e-review`. `review-request` (`(author, requesting_review) → t3:review-request` in `SUBAGENT_BY_PHASE`) is the spawnable agent for the review-request phase — read-only on the codebase, it discovers the ready PR and posts the request under the on-behalf policy. They are invoked by lifecycle skills, by the headless executor (§5.2) on a claimed phase task, and by the loop tick (§5.6) when a scanner signal calls for agent judgment. Interactive-only skills (no agent): `retro`, `next`, `contribute`, `setup`.

**Worktree-scoped dispatch resolution ([PR-12](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2564)).** A dispatched task runs in its OWN worktree, so the overlay and framework-skill detection root are resolved from the task's ticket — its `overlay` field (via `get_overlay_for_ticket`) and `dispatch_worktree_path(ticket)` (the first materialised worktree whose recorded path still exists on disk) — never the orchestrator's ambient cwd, which is the loop's clone rather than the ticket's checkout. `resolve_skill_bundle(..., worktree_path=…)` threads that path into `SkillLoadingPolicy.select_for_runtime_phase`'s detection `cwd` at every dispatch consumer (headless SDK run, `loop_dispatch pending-spawn`, and the interactive `claude` command); a missing/pre-provision path falls back to the ambient cwd. The same seam (`agents.dispatch_preflight`) injects a **target head-state** block into a coding/maker brief — the worktree's HEAD commit (sha, subject, committed-at) classified against the task's triggering timestamp — so the maker builds on any commit already on the branch instead of restarting; robust by construction (any missing worktree / non-git path / git failure yields no block, never a crash on the dispatch path). Every dispatched spawn carries a type-prefixed **display name** `t3-<type>-<id>` (`dispatch_gates.spawn_display_name`) the `pending-spawn` payload hands the `/loop` slot as the Agent tool `description`, so every spawn is attributable at a glance; an untyped task has no registered sub-agent and is already excluded upstream by the dispatchable claim filter. Long-running maker briefs auto-inject a heartbeat-DM cue; verification (review) briefs carry the anti-rubber-stamp block (reproduce the proof first, grade every quality dimension).

**Distribution.** Two install paths, one source of truth:

- **APM**: `apm install souliane/teatree`
- **CLI-first**: `git clone … && uv tool install --editable . --overrides uv-overrides.txt && t3 setup` (the flag is required — `uv tool install` does not read `[tool.uv] override-dependencies`, so the SDK's `mcp` bound goes uncorrected without it) — also registers the plugin in `~/.claude/plugins/installed_plugins.json` with `installPath` pointing at the main clone (no `~/.claude/plugins/t3` symlink; always live)

On every `t3 setup` run, `dep_drift` checks `[project].dependencies` against the editable install and reinstalls + `execv`-restarts if a declared dep is missing. The same run re-syncs runtime skill links and **prunes stale ones** — a teatree-managed link whose skill was removed or renamed upstream is removed so the dropped skill stops resolving, while contribute-mode workspace links and a user's own real skill directories are left untouched. Because `t3 update` re-runs `t3 setup`, updating teatree auto-cleans skills dropped upstream.

**§11.4 Bash Permissions.** The plugin's `settings.json` ships a **broad allow, narrow deny** `permissions` list — every tool family the workflow touches is allowed, with load-bearing denies (push to default branches, `--force`, `--no-verify`, root `rm -rf`, `curl|bash`, `gh repo delete`) taking precedence; the `t3` CLI is the workflow's safety wrapper, so blocking inside the CLI is the wrong layer. Plugin config is **not self-modifiable** — edits to the allow-list are rejected by Claude Code's autonomy guardrail, so a mid-workflow classifier denial means the agent stops and asks via `AskUserQuestion` (`skills/rules/SKILL.md` § "Classifier Denial Protocol"). `t3 doctor authorizations` is read-only — it reports which generic recommended auto-mode authorizations are absent from `~/.claude/settings.json`; teatree ships **no** classifier whitelist of its own.

**§11.5 Explicit skill loading.** Skill loading is fully explicit — slash commands (`/t3:code`), phase mapping (`t3 agent --phase coding`), ticket status, the transitive `requires:` chain, and cwd/overlay context. There is no free-text keyword scan of the prompt or a task description: `SkillLoadingPolicy` resolves from those explicit signals only, and the `UserPromptSubmit` hook surfaces only the framework / overlay / companion skills a prompt's cwd context implies (`PreToolUse` blocks Python code edits until those load). Session-end retro fires from the `SessionEnd` hook keyed on the lifecycle skills loaded during the session; ticket-URL intake resolves through `t3 <overlay> workspace ticket <url>` (overlay-resolved, provider-agnostic), not a frontmatter URL pattern. The `SKILL.md` schema (`skill_support/schema.py`) rejects a stale `triggers:` / `search_hints:` / `companions:` key loud, so a skill file never silently carries a removed mechanism.

**§11.6 Progressive disclosure in skills.** A long SKILL.md carries its **decision-relevant spine** — the trigger, the do-X-never-Y verdict, and any always-gated safety list — and moves the mechanics behind each rule (step-by-step procedure, config precedence chain, per-flag rationale, worked recipes) into `skills/<skill>/references/*.md`, split largest-section-first. A spine entry names its reference by repo-relative path, because the headless dispatch denies only the external-contact built-ins (`src/teatree/agents/_runner_options.py` `_EXTERNAL_CONTACT_BUILTINS`) and keeps `Read`, so a path is loadable where a bare "see the reference" is not. Content **moves**; removing a rule is a separate reviewed decision. Progressive disclosure composes with — and does not replace — the two bundle-level levers: **phase-scoped loading** (only the skills a phase needs are embedded) and **summary demotion** (a non-primary skill in a dispatched bundle gets a one-line "available — load if needed" summary rather than its full body). This is the design rule the `/t3:rules` § "Split Long Skills With Progressive Disclosure" prose enforces on authors.

**§11.7 Configuration review cadence.** `CLAUDE.md`, `skills/*/SKILL.md`, and the hook/permission config (`hooks/hooks.json`, plugin `settings.json`) get a deliberate, deletion-first review every 3–6 months — the accumulation of gates, rules, and skill prose is pruned back to what still earns its place, rather than only ever growing. The dreaming pipeline (`/t3:dreaming`, `t3 dream run`) is the executor: its memory-consolidation + core-gap pass is the standing cadence that surfaces stale config and drives each removal to a merged change.

**§11.8 A skill's worked examples resolve against the tree.** An agent skimming a skill cannot tell an illustration from a work item, so an example naming a plausible-but-absent teatree symbol reads as a bug to go fix — and unlike a stale import or a stale patch target, nothing mechanical catches it. `quality/skill_symbol_refs` resolves every teatree-shaped reference in `skills/**/*.md` — a `src/teatree/...` path, a repo path under any other top-level directory, a dotted `teatree.<...>` name, a bare module-local `<module>.<SYMBOL>` name, a `from teatree.<mod> import …` clause — against the live tree, and `tests/teatree_quality/test_skill_symbol_refs.py` turns red on the rest. Findings are per-reference, so a resolving citation never vouches for a broken one beside it on the same line. A dotted name resolves only through source, never through a bare directory: Python reads any leftover directory as an implicit namespace package, so a retired module's `__pycache__` remains would otherwise vouch for the citation that outlived it — silently, in the permissive direction, and differently in every clone. The bare shape resolves through an index of the modules the tree ships (`src/teatree/**`, `hooks/**`) and is bounded at both ends, because an extractor keyed on shape alone cannot tell a module-local symbol from ordinary attribute access: the head must name one of those modules and the tail must be module-symbol-shaped (`UPPER_SNAKE` or `_private`), which is what keeps `typer.Exit`, `permissions.allow` and `overlay.config` out. Outside `src/teatree/` a path is a citation only when it names a file under a directory that exists — the tree's own top-level names recur in prose (`dev/staging`) and in stand-in namespaces, so that precondition is what lets the guard reach a stale `tests/…` citation without making every illustration red. An illustration that needs a stand-in target uses a namespace the tree has no claim on (`src/acme/...`, `src/<pkg>/...`); a token that is genuinely not an importable name — an entry-point group, a config-section header, a legacy artifact named so it is never re-created — carries a `skill-symbol-ref: <reason>` marker, scoped to its own line or to the fenced block it introduces.

The same resolver reaches two surfaces the markdown walk cannot read. The charter documents an agent loads before any skill (`BLUEPRINT.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/blueprint/*.md`) are scanned by `scan_file`; Python docstrings and `#:` attribute comments under `src/teatree/**` and `hooks/**` are scanned by `quality/python_prose_refs`, which decides which lines are prose and masks the rest before handing the source to the same `scan_source`. That second surface is where a `:func:` citation of a function that MOVED modules shipped to `main` and survived a cold review: `--doctest-modules` collects nothing from a module carrying no `>>>`, the comment-density gate exempts docstrings outright, and a reader following the citation finds nothing and cannot tell renamed from moved from deleted. An ordinary `#` comment and a non-docstring string literal stay unscanned — a prompt template names symbols it does not cite. Both surfaces carry a seeded known-set so the walk lands without a tree-wide cleanup first, and each is asserted in BOTH directions: a NEW unresolved reference reds, and a pinned one the scanner no longer reports reds until its entry is deleted, so a stale allowance cannot outlive the citation it covered. Both known-sets live in `quality/known_unresolved_refs.yaml`, read by `quality/ref_baseline` — data rather than a test literal, because the repair has to be mechanical: a pin goes stale for reasons OUTSIDE its own PR. [#4451](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4451) is the shape — two PRs off a shared base, one seeding a pin while the other rewrote the citation it named; neither `refs/pull/N/merge` contained the other, so both were green alone and `main` was red on six required contexts the moment the second landed, with every open PR inheriting it. `t3 tool ratchet-prune` reports both halves and repairs exactly one of them. The asymmetry is the guard: deleting a pin the scanner no longer reports makes the ratchet strictly TIGHTER, so it is safe to automate, while banking an unresolved reference LOOSENS it — that half is reported and never written, so no repair path can widen the guard it is repairing. `load_baseline` fails loud on a missing or malformed file rather than degrading to an empty set, which would read as "nothing pinned" and satisfy the staleness half vacuously.

---

## 12. Testing

**>90% branch coverage, non-negotiable** (`fail_under = 93, branch = true`). Omits only migrations.

- In-memory SQLite (`:memory:`) for isolation and speed; `django_tasks.backends.immediate` for synchronous task execution
- `conftest.py` monkeypatches `HOME`/`XDG_*` to `tmp_path`, strips `GIT_*`, isolates overlay env, resets backend + overlay caches between tests
- Tests mirror `src/` paths under `tests/teatree_core/`, `tests/teatree_agents/`, `tests/teatree_backends/`, `tests/teatree_loop/`, plus top-level cross-cutting suites
- New tests lean integration / E2E / functional (Django test client, `call_command`, real `git` under `tmp_path`); unit tests are reserved for pure logic, and only unstoppable externals are mocked
- Core has no Playwright suite (no UI). Overlays declare their own via `get_e2e_config()`; `t3 <overlay> e2e {run,external,project,in-tree}` runs them. The three sources are disjoint: `project` is the repo's own in-tree pytest suite, `external` a cloned specs repo run against a live stack, and `in-tree` a Playwright config that lives in the INVOKING checkout — resolved from the invocation cwd plus the overlay's declared `e2e_dir`, with the ambient environment inherited and nothing injected, so a browserless CI lane (a unit or static-analysis lane needing no stack, tenant or credential) reproduces byte-for-byte in a contributor's own worktree rather than a cache-repo clone. Its `-c <config>` comes from the same `overlay.e2e.playwright_args(spec_path)` hook the `external` runner uses, or an explicit `--config`; resolving neither REFUSES (exit 2) rather than let Playwright fall back to a default config whose global setup logs in. Every specs source is a declared repo cloned at a ref, so the `external` runner resolves its Playwright working dir in two arms ([#2620](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2620)): an explicit `--repo` (a `[e2e_repos.<name>]` clone) wins; else, when `get_e2e_config()` supplies BOTH a `url` and a `ref` (`overlay_e2e_repo` lifts them into an `E2ERepo`, `e2e_dir` default `e2e`), the overlay's own repo is cloned at that ref (a `--branch`/`--ref` override wins) — so a migrated suite on an unmerged branch runs without a separate checkout. Neither declared is `E2eSpecsResolutionError.no_specs_source` (exit 1). A green/red `e2e run` records run provenance on the durable recipe (`Ticket.extra['e2e_recipe'].last_run`, `core/e2e_workitem.record_run`): result + per-repo SHAs + env, plus (for vanilla-spec suites whose specs live in another repo, [#272](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/272)) the exact `spec_path` and the overlay-resolved `manifest_entry` id (its CI lane) via the `overlay.e2e.run_provenance(spec_path)` overlay hook — so a run is reproducible from the DB alone. Core stays overlay-agnostic (default hook returns `""`; empty provenance fields are omitted).
