For a multi-config Playwright suite (one config per lane — api-flow vs contrib vs portal), the overlay maps a spec to its `-c <config>` (and any other per-spec Playwright flags) via the `overlay.e2e.playwright_args(spec_path)` overlay hook; the `external` runner threads those into the `npx playwright test` command ahead of any explicit `--playwright-args` (default `[]` — no flags, prior command shape preserved). The per-feature **acceptance scenarios** a templated test plan renders from come through the sibling `overlay.e2e.scenarios(spec_path)` overlay hook (default `()`): each element is an overlay-defined frozen `Scenario` (`surface`/`title`/`preconditions`/`steps`/`expected`/`modality` `ui|api`/`captures` of `Capture{slot,caption}`), so the overlay owns the scenario manifest and core just threads the tuple to the renderer. `t3 <overlay> e2e write-test-plan --manifest` ([#1409](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1409)) maintains ONE structured test-plan note per **ticket** (never the MR), idempotent on a hidden marker carrying a JSON state blob (the merge SOT); each run merges its manifest's env(s) over the prior state, freezing the side it omits. The note renders a test plan — a multi-repo header with clickable commit-provenance and a `Dev ± Local` reconciliation, then bodies in one of four templates ([#2304](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2304)): the three workflow-shaped ones (`capture-matrix` default side-by-side table, `browser-click-first` screenshots, `link-api` links + code) and `scenario-plan`, which renders ordered acceptance scenarios — each a `### Scenario N — <surface>` block with Preconditions / numbered Steps / Expected / Actual (a ✅ pass marker or a blocked-or-omitted note) and either captioned inline screenshots (`ui` modality) or a contract-check marker (`api` modality) — separated by `---` rules with an `**Environment:**` footer. `--body-file` posts a pre-authored body verbatim (mutually exclusive with `--manifest`). Pure render/merge lives in `_test_plan_render`; the `scenario-plan` shape in `_test_plan_scenario`; the two non-default workflow renderers in `_test_plan_workflow_templates`; host orchestration in `_test_plan`.
  - **Upload integrity.** Artifacts embed the relative `/uploads/<secret>/<file>` GitLab claims on save and upload to the project that owns the note's issue URL; a post-upload `verify_upload` gate re-fetches each via the token-authed API and magic-byte-checks it, so "posted" means "verified renderable" (`TestPlanMediaError` refuses otherwise).
  - **Image preflight** (`core/test_plan_validation`, before any upload): every image must carry a `highlightAndShoot` red box, none may be byte-identical (md5 dedup), and a manifest with no media is refused (`TestPlanValidationError`); `--skip-validation` is the user-only bypass.
  - **Video preflight** (`core/video_evidence`, the video counterpart to the image preflight): an evidence video is refused when its opening frames are blank (the dead-pre-roll defect that shipped a 40s-blank walkthrough) or the clip is blank throughout — `scripts/analyze_video.py` yields the verdict and the `e2e write-test-plan` path plus the `/t3:e2e` / `/t3:e2e-review` skills reject on it. Pinned by the `e2e_review` eval scenario (HOLD on a blank-pre-roll video).
  - **Honesty gates.** A colleague-repo body gate (`core/test_plan_blocked_gate`) refuses a `--body-file` post whose free text signals incomplete verification when the issue URL matches `colleague_repo_url_pattern` (`BlockedTestPlanPostError`); the structured `--manifest` render is exempt because its `**Blocked:** <reason>` disclosure is the honest channel. The plan is a FILE in the e2e repo (`test-plans/<repo>-<ticket>.md`, `core/management/commands/_test_plan/file_store`), so nothing leaves the machine and there is no note to withdraw — the on-behalf gate applies only to the MR/PR-comment poster. A sibling field-context evidence probe (`core/doc_evidence`, [#2296](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2296)) asserts a generated-doc term in its expected structured field/column, never a page-wide substring — an available primitive, not yet a globally-wired gate.

---

## 13. Quality Gates

| Tool | What it checks |
|---|---|
| `pytest` + `pytest-cov` | >90% branch coverage |
| `ruff` | All rules enabled, specific ignores justified (`# noqa` requires approval) |
| `ty` | Static type checker with `error-on-warning = true` |
| `import-linter` | Wildcard sibling-independence tach cannot express (substrate → `contrib`; mini-loops independent). Acyclicity + backend/agent-independence are tach-enforced (`forbid_circular_dependencies`, #1922). Config: `pyproject.toml` |
| `ast-grep` | Deterministic AST anti-pattern rules under `.ast-grep/blocking/` (run by the push gate's Engine B `TestBlockingSetIsGreen` scan + the CI `test-shard`) — e.g. `except-swallow-to-empty`, `cas-stamp-before-side-effect`, and `lenient-git-run-dead-except` (an `except CommandFailedError` around the lenient `git.run`, which never raises, is a dead handler — use `git.run_strict`) |
| `codespell` | Spell check |
| `prek` | Runs all above on commit |

Key ruff decisions: ALL rules selected then specific ignores; D1xx disabled (no docstrings — self-documenting code); `from __future__ import annotations` banned (use native 3.13 syntax).

---

## 14. Django Project Workflows

Reference DB architecture, the import fallback chain (`DjangoDbImportConfig` strategy + selective fake-migration retry + renumber-record reconcile + post-import steps), DSLR integration, the worktree provisioning workflow (`worktree provision`), the server startup workflow (`worktree start`), and the state reconciler (`t3 <overlay> workspace doctor`) are implementation details — they live in code under `teatree.core.runners`, `teatree.utils.db`, `teatree.utils.django_db`, `teatree.utils.django_db.reconcile`, `teatree.core.worktree.reconcile`, and the `lifecycle` / `worktree` / `workspace` management commands. The architectural contract:

- **One reference DB per overlay** (canonical control DB at `teatree.paths.CANONICAL_DB`; worktree-aware code auto-isolates onto a per-worktree DB under `teatree.paths.auto_isolated_worktrees_dir()` = `~/.local/share/teatree-worktrees/<slug>/`, the `<slug>` deterministic from the checkout path via `teatree.paths.isolated_slug`). `t3 <overlay> workspace clean-all` reaps the orphan dirs under that root — those no LIVE CHECKOUT owns — while keeping any dir holding a `.git` checkout (the #706/#835 data-loss guard). Ownership is one answer fed by three evidence sources (#3852), with the **filesystem** as the primary one: every directory carrying a `.git` under the scanned roots, UNION every checkout `git worktree list` reports across the discoverable clones, UNION the `Worktree` rows — plus each dir's own `owner-checkout.path` stamp, which inverts the one-way slug hash so liveness is proven rather than inferred. The slug is `sha256(checkout_path)`, so "does this path exist on disk" is the question itself and a registry-derived answer is only a proxy for it: asking registries first made coverage depend on which clones happened to be discoverable, and a clone that is never a candidate produces no gap, so its checkouts' absence was indistinguishable from death. That only holds while the scan reports everything it did NOT cover, so anything it skips is a gap ([#3872](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3872)): it walks THROUGH symlinked dirs (deduplicating recursion on the resolved path, so a loop terminates) and reports rather than truncates at its runaway depth cap — skipping both silently left `complete` true while live checkouts were missing from the keep-set, which is exactly the evidence a deletion rests on. Every source contributes gaps and any gap fails CLOSED — every dir is kept and the gap reported, never a deletion on partial evidence. A dir modified at or after the instant the keep-set was computed is likewise kept: the box provisions continuously, so a snapshot-then-delete loop would otherwise reap a dir minted mid-pass. `retention prune --apply` finishes with a `VACUUM`, since deleting rows on SQLite reclaims no disk and this DB is the seed each env dir is copied from
- **Remote-state freshness is a precondition of the #706 guard.** `commits_absent_from_all_remotes` is a purely local graph query over `refs/remotes/*`, and a tracking ref goes stale when its branch is deleted upstream by anything other than this clone (a forge's auto-delete-on-merge, or a sibling clone) — against a stale ref it reports unpushed work as pushed, authorising the reap of the last copy. Every destructive consumer therefore refreshes the clone's remote-tracking refs (`git.fetch_all_prune`) before classifying anything, and **fails closed to KEEP** when that refresh fails: unknown remote state never authorises a deletion
- **Analyze-then-wipe done-worktree reaper** (`teatree.core.worktree.worktree_done`, surfaced by `workspace clean-all` / `clean-merged`) — a worktree is auto-wiped ONLY when its ticket is FSM-done (`MERGED`/`DELIVERED`/`IGNORED`, or a forge squash-merge) AND every unpushed commit and uncommitted change is PROVEN content-redundant against `origin/main` (`teatree.core.worktree.branch_classification.branch_redundancy`: patch-id `git cherry` zero, synthetic-squash `commit-tree` tree-delta capture, or `branch --merged`). A forge merged-PR signal alone never authorizes deletion (post-merge work is kept). There is **no recovery snapshot** (#1770 removed): unproven work is kept, never destroyed. Two fail-safe pre-gates run ahead of done-detection — a **liveness guard** (`cleanup_liveness`: live `Session` / active `Task` / process CWD / git `index.lock` / recent HEAD commit ⇒ skip-and-report) and an **ownership guard** (`cleanup_ownership`: data-driven via `colleague_repo_url_pattern` + `user_identity_aliases`, a colleague-authored branch on a shared product repo is excluded up front; a no-op for the owner's solo repos). Everything NOT auto-deleted is surfaced as a machine-readable **structured EMIT** record (`workspace emit` → JSON array, schema in `cleanup_emit`: path/branch/kind/`unique_commit_shas`/`uncommitted_paths`/`merged_with_post_merge_work`/`content_verified`/`verdict_source`/`banned_terms_status`/liveness/`last_commit_date`/owner) for a separate judgment skill to route. The record carries the verdict's PROVENANCE, not just its answer: an empty `unique_commit_shas` used to mean both "proven to hold nothing unique" and "no probe could run", so an unresolvable clone emitted the same bytes as a shipped branch and the skill routed both to DELETE. `content_verified` is `false` for every verdict nothing proved and `verdict_source` names the deciding layer (`cherry-zero-unique`/`synthetic-squash`/`branch-merged`/`not-redundant`) or the reason none could (`inconclusive`/`clone-unresolvable`); the skill routes an unverified record to KEEP. The record also carries the WORKING TREE's delta (`uncommitted_paths`, from `working_tree_dirt`), because a checkout whose entire work is staged-but-uncommitted has no commits for any probe to speak for while holding content on no ref: `CleanupEmitRecord.to_dict` forces such a record `content_verified: false` with `verdict_source: uncommitted-work`, so the KEEP branch that produced it and the record it hands the skill can no longer disagree. Its upstream cause is closed too — `clone_paths.resolve_clone_path` returns a stored `extra['clone_path']` only while `probe_checkout` confirms it is still a checkout, otherwise falling through to a fresh `find_clone_path` scan, and `workspace doctor` reports the `StaleClonePath` drift that `--fix` repairs. The **salvage primitive** (`workspace salvage`, `cleanup_salvage`) captures an emitted item's banned-terms-clean unique content onto a fresh branch + PR, verifies the forge has it, then deletes the source ONLY after a verified landing — a failed push/open/verify leaves the source intact. Every decision-bearing git probe in the reaper/salvage/stash paths runs `git.run_strict` (raises on failure) and routes an inconclusive probe to the conservative KEEP outcome: the orphaned-stash reaper (`drop_orphaned_stashes`) keeps a stash whenever the upstream-capture probe (`branch_classification._branch_captured_upstream`) fails or its `git cherry` produced no comparison lines (a stash ref is a merge commit `git cherry` omits — an empty result is "nothing compared", never "captured"), and `cleanup_salvage._unique_content_texts` refuses to salvage a `require_banned_clean` item whose content is unreadable, so an unscanned blob is never pushed to a public PR
- **One canonical answer to "is this branch landed?"** ([#4070](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4070)) — `t3 <overlay> workspace branch-verdict <branch>…` (`teatree.core.worktree.branch_verdict.branch_verdict_report`) is the read-only serialization of `branch_redundancy` for an ARBITRARY local branch, PR-backed or not. It closes the gap that `landscape` cannot fill — its `has_unpushed` comes from `commits_absent_from_all_remotes`, which is sha-based and, per its own docstring, not empty for a squash-merge (deliberately fail-OPEN, because intake asks "might something be in flight?", not "is it landed?") — and that `emit` fills only by ABSENCE. The payload carries `redundant` + `source` alongside `forge_merged`, `merged_with_post_merge_work` and `unique_shas` TOGETHER, so the forge signal is never readable on its own as "safe to delete post-merge work". A fourth field, `content_present_on_target`, is the one layer the module adds of its own: `git cherry` reads a patch's PRIOR appearance on the target and a REVERT there does not erase it, so `git merge-tree --write-tree` asks the present-tense question instead. `ShipExecutor`'s [#776](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/776) duplicate-PR refusal consumes the AND of both (`branch_is_landed`) instead of the ancestor-only `git.branch_merged`, which a squash-merge defeats — so a squash-merged branch is refused, a reverted one ships, and presence runs first as a pure-git short-circuit so the ordinary unlanded branch pays no forge probe. Two enforcement halves keep the primitives from being re-derived: the WARN-only PreToolUse advisory `handle_warn_merged_detection_probe` (a hand-rolled `git cherry` / `branch --merged` / `merge-base --is-ancestor` / `log --not` against a DEFAULT-branch-shaped target earns one stderr line naming this command; never a deny, because the same command is a legitimate mid-rebase read — kill-switch `merged_detection_gate_enabled`, per-call `[merge-detect-ok: <reason>]`), and the AST ratchet `tests/conformance/test_merged_detection_single_home.py`, which pins every `src/` call site to the canonical home or an allow-listed non-landed-ness use.
- **Configurable import strategy per overlay** (`overlay.provisioning.db_import_strategy(worktree) → DbImportStrategy | None`) — overlays declare *what* to import; core runs the engine
- **Migration retry with selective faking** for known-stuck migrations, declared per overlay
- **Renumber-record reconcile** ([#1038](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1038)) — when a snapshot DB carries OLD migration numbers and the base branch has since renumbered (a migration inserted earlier bumped later numbers), `django_db.reconcile` detects Django's `InconsistentMigrationHistory` and renames the stale `django_migrations` record to the new name so `check_consistent_history` passes and the forward migrate proceeds. It fires ONLY on a *provable* pure renumber (identical descriptive suffix, exactly one lower-numbered stale record, unapplied readable on-disk dependency); a genuine divergence is left untouched so real schema drift surfaces as a failure, never masked
- **Post-DB steps** run after import (password reset, fixtures, …) — declared per overlay
- **State reconciler** (`t3 <overlay> workspace doctor`) reconciles DB ↔ disk ↔ docker drift on demand. Alongside the infra stores it surfaces the **work-tracking-truth** drift classes (SELFCATCH-1) — `UnpushedWork` (commits absent from all remotes), `DoneButUnmerged` (a done-claiming ticket with no `MergeAudit` and a non-upstream branch), `DuplicateScope` (>1 worktree dir per issue) — each built from the existing `commits_absent_from_all_remotes` / `branch_redundancy` / `find_foreign_issue_worktrees` probes with fail-closed semantics (an inconclusive probe is a finding, never a silent pass). The global `work_state` loop scanner runs the same probes each tick and emits `workstate.drift` into the action-needed statusline zone, so committed-but-unpushed / done-but-unmerged / duplicate-scope work surfaces autonomously instead of when a human asks. Read-only — it surfaces drift; destructive remediation stays gated
- **Redis is a normal per-stack compose service** — there is no teatree-owned shared Redis container or DB-index pool. An overlay whose app needs Redis runs it as an ordinary `docker-compose` service in the worktree's own stack (its own container, isolated network), reached by the app at the in-stack hostname. One-stack-at-a-time provisioning makes a shared pool pointless; the per-stack container IS the isolation. `worktree start` heals a `provisioned` worktree whose DB was never created (interrupted provision), and `ServiceLauncher` is single-flight per `(worktree, service)` so concurrent frontend rebuilds never race the output dir

---

## 15. Dependencies

`pyproject.toml` is the SSOT for exact versions; this section names the architecturally load-bearing pins so a reader knows WHY each is there. Runtime:

```toml
asgiref>=3.8
claude-agent-sdk==0.2.139
coverage>=7
croniter>=6.2.2
django>=6,<6.1
django-fsm-2>=4
django-linear-migrations>=2.19
django-rich>=2.2
django-tasks>=0.9
django-tasks-db>=0.12
django-typer>=3.3
gunicorn>=23
httpx>=0.27
mcp>=2,<3
pillow>=12.3
pydantic-ai-slim[anthropic,openai]>=2.3
pyyaml>=6
tomlkit>=0.13
whitenoise>=6.6
```

Load-bearing pins: `claude-agent-sdk` is an EXACT pin (not a floor) because `tests/test_claude_cli_pin.py` derives the eval/test image's `claude` CLI generation from that exact version — the generation the wheel BUNDLES and actually executes — and reds on a bump until it is re-derived from the new wheel. It resolves against `mcp>=2,<3` only behind a `[tool.uv] override-dependencies` entry: the SDK declares `mcp<2.0.0`, a bound broader than its usage — it reaches only the low-level `mcp.server.Server` / `mcp.types` surface, never the `mcp.server.mcpserver.MCPServer` surface `teatree.mcp.server` uses. `tests/test_claude_agent_sdk_pin.py::TestTheOverrideIsHonest` re-derives that import set from the installed wheel and reds if any of it stops resolving, so the override cannot quietly outlive its justification. It carries NO Dependabot `ignore` entry: the quarantine that once froze it (a bundled CLI at/after 2.1.204 renders an `AskUserQuestion` call as a markdown chip rather than a `tool_use` block, reddening every scenario matching that tool call) was replaced by the `surface` axis below, which makes those scenarios advisory ([#3855](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3855)); `pydantic-ai-slim[anthropic,openai]` is a RUNTIME dep (not dev-only) because `agent_harness=pydantic_ai` is a live headless-dispatch path (the `[anthropic]` extra backs the CLI-free Anthropic Messages-API binding, the `[openai]` extra the OrcaRouter client); `croniter` parses the `[teatree.availability].windows` cron expressions (§17.1 invariant 9); `tomlkit` renders the `config_setting export` DB→TOML interchange dump; `django-linear-migrations` records the per-app `max_migration.txt` fork sentinel (§4 migration-fork probe); `gunicorn`/`whitenoise` serve the dashboard; `mcp` backs the MCP server; `pillow` handles image attachments; `coverage`/`asgiref`/`pyyaml` are transitive-critical direct pins.

Optional extras (installed on demand):

```toml
markdown = ["markitdown[docx,pdf,pptx,xlsx]>=0.1.6"]
notion   = ["browser-cookie3>=0.20"]
slack    = ["slack-sdk>=3.35"]
```

Dev (`[dependency-groups] dev`): `ruff`, `pytest`, `pytest-cov`, `pytest-django`, `pytest-xdist`, `pytest-timeout`, `pytest-playwright`, `ty`, `import-linter`, `tach`, `prek`, `typer`, `django-types`, `factory-boy`, `inline-snapshot`, `cyclonedx-bom` — plus the `docs` / `mutation` / `shard` / `shuffle` / `ui` groups. Security auditing is `uv-audit` in CI (there is no `safety` dependency).

**Keeping dependencies current, and knowing when that failed ([#4346](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4346)).** Three surfaces, each covering a gap the others cannot see. Dependabot's `uv` ecosystem watches `pyproject.toml` **and** `uv.lock`, so a security release the declared constraint already permits — needing only a lockfile move — is still proposed; the `pip` ecosystem could not propose one, which is how Django 6.0.8 sat unoffered for a week under `django>=6,<6.1`. The weekly `uv-lock-upgrade` workflow re-resolves the whole graph, which per-package PRs never do. And the `uv-audit` job opens with a **canary** (`scripts/ci/audit_canary.py`): a green audit is indistinguishable from a blind one, because nothing reds when the tool reports *nothing* — an empty OSV response, a parser regression, an over-broad allowlist and a renamed flag all look exactly like "no findings". The canary feeds the same tool a pin with permanent advisories and demands one back (`SEEING` / `BLIND` / `UNREADABLE`, exit 0 only on `SEEING`), so the gate's green means the gate ran. It bounds that green to *"as current as the advisory databases"*, and no further: measured on 2026-08-15, neither the `osv` nor the `pypi` backend reports any advisory for `django==6.0.7`, eleven days after 6.0.8 shipped four CVEs — so no audit backend could have caught that miss, and the canary would have been green throughout. Catching a release the databases have published no range for is version-currency's job, which is why the dependabot half above is the one that closes it. When the gate does find something, a failure-only step runs `t3 tool dependency-audit` (`teatree.quality.dependency_audit`), which annotates each advisory with whether the affected component is reachable from `src/` — 6.0.8's High-severity raster CVE could not be reached here while its Moderate admin one could, so severity alone ranked them the wrong way round. The verdict is three-valued: `NOT_IMPORTED` is the reassuring answer, so it is reported only when the distribution→import mapping came from installed metadata; a guessed mapping that finds nothing reports `UNKNOWN`. Reachability is a static lower bound over `import` statements, never a proof of deadness.

**What that currency may move on its own ([#4437](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4437)).** The weekly refresh above opens its PR with a PAT so CI fires and then calls `gh pr merge --auto --squash`, so whatever it resolved lands on green with no human step. The gap that made dangerous was a ceiling nothing asserted: widening `django>=6,<6.1` to `<6.2` resolves to the SAME version in isolation — `uv` preserves locked versions — so the diff reads as a no-op, and what it actually authorises is the *next* automated resolve (measured: 6.0.8, the in-series patch, under `<6.1`; 6.1, a new feature series, under `<6.2`). Two surfaces close it. `tests/test_django_version_ceiling.py` pins the floor and the ceiling as constants and additionally refuses a window spanning more than one feature series, naming [#4404](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4404) — which owns the deliberate Python 3.14 + Django 6.1 move and its branch-protection ordering — as the only place the pin is decided. And `scripts/ci/lock_delta.py` classifies the resolve itself: it diffs the pre-upgrade lockfile against the produced one, ranks each move `major` / `minor` / `unknown` / `patch`, renders the PR body from what it measured (a body written once at creation described a resolve later reruns replaced), and returns the auto-merge verdict. That verdict is patch-only and fail-closed — a minor, a major, and a version string it cannot parse all withhold auto-merge and route the PR to a human, an unreadable lockfile reds the job rather than opening a PR of unknown provenance, and an empty verdict (a skipped or failed classify step) reads as unsafe by shape. Added and removed transitive packages are reported and never block: the whole-graph resolve churns those routinely and neither is a version boundary. Because a withheld PR does not merge and the same boundary is still there next Sunday, the refresh reuses an already-open refresh branch instead of stacking one more identical PR every week.

---

## 16. Key Conventions

- Python 3.13+. Use `X | Y` union syntax. Never `Optional`.
- `from __future__ import annotations` is banned.
- No docstrings on classes/methods by policy. Self-documenting code (names + types are the documentation).
- Management commands use `django-typer`, not `BaseCommand`.
- `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE` is stripped from env when running `_managepy()` so the overlay's own settings win.
- **Port allocation is ephemeral (Non-Negotiable).** Host ports are auto-mapped by Docker at `worktree start`; never written to `.t3-cache/.t3-env.cache`, the DB, or any other persistent store. Inter-service traffic uses compose service DNS.
- **GitHub access is HTTPS + `gh auth git-credential` (Non-Negotiable).** Every GitHub remote is `https://github.com/<slug>.git` and git delegates auth to the `gh` credential helper `deploy/entrypoint.sh` installs via `gh auth setup-git`; auth is token-based, so there is no key to distribute, mount or rotate. An ssh-form GitHub remote, a `url` … `insteadOf` rewrite naming github.com, and a `~/.ssh` made load-bearing by either are all forbidden — while the GitLab credential helper and the ssh-tolerant remote *parsers* (`utils/git_remote.py`, `core/public_identity.py`) stay separate, legitimate paths. `tests/conformance/test_github_access_is_https.py` asserts it over the tracked tree; `t3 doctor`'s `_check_github_remotes_are_https` asserts it over the live gitconfig of every discovered checkout, the untracked half a static scan cannot see ([#4447](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4447)).
- Coverage omits only migrations.
- The headless executor's SDK run is non-persistent (exits with the task). The user's interactive `/loop` session is the only persistent Claude Code session.
- Statusline state is rendered to a file (`${XDG_DATA_HOME:-$HOME/.local/share}/teatree/statusline.txt`, override `TEATREE_STATUSLINE_FILE`) by the loop and `cat`-ed by the hook, which does no DB or network I/O.
- **Overlay-specific names must not appear in the scanned roots.** The gate (`scripts/hooks/check_no_overlay_leak.py`) enforces this — `BLUEPRINT § 1` — across `src/teatree`, `docs`, `skills`, `agents`, `tests`, `scripts` and top-level `README.md`/`BLUEPRINT.md`/`AGENTS.md`, run full-tree by the `overlay-leak-tree` CI job. Forbidden terms are loaded at runtime from `$TEATREE_OVERLAY_LEAK_TERMS` or the `overlay_leak_terms` setting in the DB `ConfigSetting` store so the public repo never holds tenant names; the always-on opaque-ID pass needs no config and the CI job's `--require-terms` reds the job if the secret is missing.
- E2E tests use file-based SQLite (not `:memory:`) because Playwright spawns a separate server process.

---

## 17. The Self-Improving Factory Architecture

Teatree is a durable self-healing **and** self-improving development factory. This section is the lasting architectural reference — the umbrella under [#836](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/836); each component below is a separately tracked ticket implemented as deterministic teatree code, not skill prose.

Why this architecture exists, observed repeatedly: durability comes from **enforcement encoded in code/structure**, not prose that decays. A rule kept in memory/skills and relied on by vigilance recurs anyway; the same rule encoded as a gate/test/hook does not. The invariants below are the structural form of that lesson — load-bearing, binding every change to teatree itself.

### 17.1 Invariants

1. **Two layers, never conflated.** *Self-healing* (independent review, draft-locks, recovery, gates) is the substrate. *Self-improvement* runs on top: each caught failure-class becomes the smallest enforcement artifact that makes the class structurally impossible. Self-improvement is **gated by** self-healing — the system is never changed in a way the healing layer cannot catch or roll back.

2. **The flywheel.** A defect (from diff review, OR the code-health loop on un-changed code, OR an orchestrator-noticed near-miss) → the orchestrator synthesises → output is the *smallest enforcement* (gate/test/hook), never a prose rule → the failure-class is extinct. A repeat failure whose only output is memory/prose is a flywheel failure.

3. **Topology.** The orchestrator is the synthesis brain (retro synthesis, code-health triage, enforcement escalation, merge/clear decisions). Sub-agents are sensors/hands emitting structured signal into durable state, never self-judging. Skills carry judgment/methodology; teatree code carries the deterministic loops, gates, and intake. Corollary: mechanics → code, judgment → skill.

4. **Blast-radius rule.** Substrate changes are draft-locked and need a recorded human sign-off, satisfied by EITHER a per-PR `MergeClear.human_authorizer` OR the overlay carrying a STANDING merge-approval grant — `autonomy = full`, OR an explicit `require_human_approval_to_merge = false` on a non-collaborative tier ([#2666](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2666): `mode = auto` + `require_human_approval_to_merge = false` is the same owner statement as `autonomy = full`; the collaborative `notify` tier is excluded since it merges only after a colleague approval). Either standing grant lets the overlay merge substrate without a per-PR sign-off. Either way the agent runs the `t3 <overlay> ticket merge` keystone (§17.4.3); the human never performs the merge. The carve-out removes ONLY the per-PR sign-off — the floor (cold-review `reviewer != maker`, SHA-bind, CI-green, not-draft, never-lockout, privacy/leak scan) holds on every substrate merge; with the merge-approval gate ON on a below-full tier the `human_authorizer` stays mandatory. The path detector `diff_paths_are_substrate` is what holds this when `blast_class` is left at its `logic` default, and it matches each changed path BOTH as the forge reported it and with the `vendor/teatree/` prefix (`paths.VENDORED_CORE_PREFIX`) stripped — a forge reports paths relative to the WORKING-TREE root, so in a fork that vendors core every `src/teatree/…` literal would otherwise match nothing and the whole detector would be dead code, while such a fork's own root-level `hooks/` and `docs/blueprint/` must keep matching.

5. **Durability discipline is load-bearing.** Durable task/state plus pre-compaction snapshots let the orchestrator brain survive compaction/restart; keep them.

6. **Enforcement over prose, as a standing audit.** Invariant 2 makes the flywheel's *output* a gate; this invariant makes the *standing posture* explicit. Every user behavioural directive MUST be (a) codified in teatree and (b) enforced by deterministic code/gates wherever mechanizable, so skill prose shrinks to the judgment that genuinely cannot be mechanized and skills get **lighter** over time. This is a recurring retro/review responsibility: the enforcement gate (§17.6 / [#850](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/850)) turns a mechanizable rule into a gate, and the recurring audit keeps reclassifying prose rules → code gates.

7. **Consolidation over drift.** Behavior encoded outside the teatree framework — personal `settings.json` hooks, dotfiles automation, overlay-local ad-hoc config, personal memory guardrails — must be considered for promotion into teatree on every retro/review pass. Genuine per-instance variance must be modelled as a documented teatree setting or config knob; undocumented divergence silently drifts and violates invariant 2.

8. **All FSM state transitions go through the `t3` CLI.** The pre-condition and pre/post transition hooks are the coherence mechanism (ledger update, attestation-binding to the HEAD/workstream the phase was earned against, privacy/AI-signature scan, `mark_merged()`). Out-of-band state mutation — raw `gh pr merge` / `glab mr merge`, or hand-editing the phase ledger / FSM state — is prohibited and **mechanically guarded** (`hook_router._BLOCKED_COMMANDS`, the same hook layer as the draft-lock and structured-question gates). The keystone IN_REVIEW → MERGED transition is §17.4; the non-bypassable gate placement is §17.6.3. The two sanctioned escapes for stale state — `lifecycle clear-ledger` and the hardened `lifecycle visit-phase … reviewing --agent-id` — are themselves `t3` commands, never manual edits.

9. **Every user-directed question is captured — sync or durable — and reaches Slack.** A user-directed question must either (a) call `AskUserQuestion` with the user reachable this turn, or (b) be recorded as a `DeferredQuestion` row and posted to the owner's Slack DM. Mode resolution is a single deterministic precedence (exposed by `t3 loop preset show`): unexpired manual `ModeOverride` → live presence (a recent `UserPromptSubmit`, upgrade-only) → the active schedule's governing slot → the configured `default_mode` (`present`). A `Mode` row is a pure per-loop on/off table ([#61](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/61) merged availability into the mode; [#3826](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3826) deleted its last serialization, the fast-hook mirror file; [#4202](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4202) deleted the three posture booleans that survived), so no mode buffers a question: questions are asked immediately ([#4045](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4045)) and only a LOOP-DRIVEN call is denied, because a suspended autonomous session has no path to receive a Slack reply. One reader walks that one chain over the one control DB — `mode_resolution.resolve_active_mode` — and every unreadable input fails toward ASKING, never toward silence. The deferral path is a *sanctioned destination* for the same `AskUserQuestion` call (converted at the `PreToolUse` layer, never bypassing the §807 structured-question gate); since the user reads Slack, every question is mirrored to their DM. A live user turn (a same-session `UserPromptSubmit` within a 90 s freshness window) renders in-client so `/checking` works without a mode flip, and every capture unifies on one generation-stamped, mirror-linked `DeferredQuestion` whose *answer* is delivered back — a Slack reply binds the live generation and the next prompt injects it once. The presence/window-slide mechanics live in `docs/blueprint/loop-topology.md`. Component: §17.3 C3.

10. **Orchestrator never executes work directly — every implementation, review, test, debug, and ship action is dispatched to a sub-agent.** The orchestrator's role is synthesis, classification, dispatch, and CLEAR issuance (invariant 3, §17.4.1, §17.8 clause 3); the *hands* are sub-agents (`t3:code`, `t3:review`, `t3:test`, `t3:debug`, `t3:shipper`, `/t3:wip`'s delivery sub-agent) and the durable loop. Inlining implementation work — even a "trivial" typo Edit, a Bash run redoing a sub-agent's job, a local test cycle dodging `t3:test` — conflates judgment with execution, denies maker≠checker independence, and concentrates the compaction risk the topology spreads across durable handoffs. **Narrow exceptions:** read-only orientation in the orchestrator's own session (`Read`/`Grep`, `gh pr view`, `git status`, `AskUserQuestion`, sanctioned messaging), the `t3 …` invocations it owns (`MergeClear`, attestation, next-dispatch), and conversational replies with no repo mutation. Mechanized as gate 2 in §17.6.4 (`handle_enforce_orchestrator_boundary`): a `PreToolUse` deny on a main-agent LONG/HEAVY foreground `Bash` command, with `run_in_background: true` the escape hatch and a payload `agent_id` distinguishing sub-agent from main ([#115](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/115)). It shares one `_fail_open_or_deny` chokepoint (with always-available `t3 <overlay> gate … disable` kill-switches and the `self_rescue` allowlist) with the sibling over-deny gates; the PUBLIC-egress leak gate is excluded and stays fail-CLOSED. Detail in §17.6.4.

11. **Any interactive Claude Code session that mounts this teatree install MAY drain the `PendingChatInjection` queue.** The inbound-Slack bridge (#1014) records each user DM as a `PendingChatInjection` row; the `handle_inject_pending_chat` `UserPromptSubmit` hook drains unconsumed rows into the next prompt's `additionalContext`. Drain eligibility is **decoupled from loop ownership**: the autonomous `t3 loop start` session holding `_OWNER_LOOP` never receives `UserPromptSubmit` events, so a t3-master gate here would block *every* user reply from reaching *any* interactive session. At-most-once delivery rides primitives orthogonal to ownership — the single-use `PendingChatInjection.consume()` (`UPDATE … WHERE consumed_at IS NULL`) and the `(overlay, slack_ts)` `UniqueConstraint` — so the scanner can over-poll safely. The t3-master gate stays on the §5.6 self-pump (singleton), not on inbound message drains.

12. **An outcome-claiming completion carries a resolvable artifact pointer, fail-closed.** The out-of-band completion surface (`tasks complete --note`) records externally-landed work. `teatree.core.review.completion_evidence` makes two separate judgments: a note *asserts an outcome* when an `OUTCOME_CLAIM_KINDS` verb (`merged`/`posted`/`shipped`/`deployed` + synonyms) co-occurs with a context cue (internal idioms like `merge conflict` are stripped first), and an asserting note must carry a *resolvable pointer* an auditor can follow — a URL, a cued git SHA, an MR/PR/issue ref, a note id, a Slack ts, or a real/dotted-module path — else `check_completion_evidence` refuses (`CompletionEvidenceError`). A completion with no note, or asserting no outcome, is never gated (the structural form of invariant 6's "done claims require evidence"); the exact matchers live in `completion_evidence.py`. A `failed → completed` resolution requires a mandatory `--note` ([#1949](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1949)); `claimed → completed` stays note-optional.

13. **Post-write side effects are confirmed by re-reading, not assumed from the write's own response** ([#1192](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1192)). A write's own success response (`ok: true`, a harness tool call returning without error) is not proof its effect is actually visible — eventual consistency, a stale replica, or a race can make a claimed write unobservable moments later. `teatree.core.verify_by_reread.verify_by_reread` is the small core contract: given a `reread` callable that independently re-observes the target system, it returns a `RereadOutcome` (`confirmed` / `not_confirmed(reason)`) and never raises — a verification failure is a signal to log or retry, never a reason to crash the write path. Call sites: Slack `reactions.add` (`teatree.backends.slack.reactions.add_reaction_verified`, wired into `add_reactions_for_transition` / `add_approval_reaction`) re-reads via `reactions.get` before trusting the write; and `create_pr` ([#1194](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1194), `teatree.core.merge.pr_create_verify.verify_pr_exists`) re-reads a just-created PR's open-state on the ship / `ensure-pr` path before recording the URL, so a phantom PR (a 404 re-read) is reported failed and never advances the FSM. Inbound-event ingestion applies the sibling shape — a failed drain records the error and retries with backoff, then dead-letters (`IncomingEvent.record_failure`, [#673](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/673)) so a poison event never silently drops nor blocks the queue. Direct MCP Slack writes are refused at the `PreToolUse` boundary (`handle_block_mcp_slack_write`, [#1196](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1196), kill-switch `[teatree] mcp_slack_write_gate_enabled`) so every Slack write routes through the `t3` CLI egress chokepoint under this contract.

14. **teatree resolves all required state from its own stores; assistant memory is never a functional input** ([#3277](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3277)). Every piece of state teatree needs to *function* — config, gate enablement, publishing/merge doctrine, factory settings, loop state, trusted identities, credential routing — resolves from teatree's OWN stores: the DB-home `ConfigSetting` / `LoopState` tables (via the Django-free `teatree.config.cold_reader` / `cold_db`, or the ORM), `pass`, and repo config. An assistant's `MEMORY.md` / `~/.claude/**/memory` / per-project memory file is a convenience for the *assistant*, NEVER a source of truth for the factory — otherwise the factory would behave differently on another machine, another agent, or a fresh session, violating the portability requirement (§17 clause 5). The only teatree paths that read the memory dir treat it as *product data teatree maintains or surfaces*, each degrading to a clean no-op when the dir is absent: the nightly `teatree.loops.dream` consolidation pass (cross-link / merge / reindex / decay) that MAINTAINS the corpus, and the cold-tier recall injector (`hooks/scripts/memory_recall` + `teatree.loops.dream.recall`) that surfaces an archived rule as *advisory `additionalContext`* for one turn — its own enablement gate (`memory_recall_enabled`) resolves from the DB, not from memory, and a missing dir/index injects nothing. Pinned behaviorally by `tests/test_no_agent_memory_dependency.py`: a representative slice of the runtime state resolvers is byte-identical whether the assistant-memory dir is absent or populated with contradicting config-shaped bait, and a poisoned `Path.home()` proves no state resolver reaches the home tree.

### 17.2 The flywheel — 17.8 Orchestrator-as-keystone contract

The flywheel diagram, components (C1 Retro / C2 Code-health loop / C3 Availability), §17.4 Orchestrator-decides / loop-executes topology, §17.5 TODO-consolidation triage, §17.6 Enforcement gate (anti-relaxation, sound tach boundaries, the shipped gate family), §17.7 Enforcement-over-prose, and §17.8 Orchestrator-as-keystone contract — all live in [docs/blueprint/factory-architecture.md](docs/blueprint/factory-architecture.md), where the section headings (`### 17.2`–`### 17.8`) are preserved for cross-references.

**Anti-pattern catalog ([#166](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/166)).** `src/teatree/quality/antipatterns.yaml` is the SSOT for recurring architectural anti-patterns; `teatree.quality.catalog` loads it and a hook renders [docs/generated/antipattern-catalog.md](docs/generated/antipattern-catalog.md). The `docs-drift` CI job regenerates the catalog (with `ANTIPATTERN_CATALOG_NO_STAGE=1` so it cannot self-stage and hide drift) before the `git diff --exit-code docs/generated` gate, so a yaml→catalog skew reds CI unconditionally. Each entry's `detection` tier (`greppable` vs `judgement`) feeds the three review tiers: design-time (`architecture-design`), per-PR deterministic (`check_antipatterns.py`), periodic holistic (`ac-reviewing-codebase`). The periodic tier's own output is gated the way its subject is ([#4215](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4215)): a finding ships only when a **distinct** reader positively confirms it — default refuted, severity set by the verifier — and every subsystem verdict states what was read, so a skipped subsystem cannot render as a clean one. Its judgement-entry list is a derived cache of this catalog, kept honest by `tests/teatree_skill_support/test_ac_reviewing_codebase_method.py` rather than by a second store. The sibling `t3 tool test-shape` flags near-identical unparametrized tests and test:source ratio regressions past a baseline — advisory `warn` by default, opt-in `block`.

**Worktree-FSM diagram generator ([#12](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/12)).** `src/teatree/core/diagrams.py` renders the `Worktree` django-fsm transition graph to a byte-stable Mermaid `stateDiagram-v2` (pure introspection, no I/O); `scripts/hooks/generate_fsm_diagrams.py` writes the canonical [docs/generated/diagrams/worktree-lifecycle.md](docs/generated/diagrams/worktree-lifecycle.md) and splices the identical block between worktree-fsm markers in `README.md` + `skills/workspace/SKILL.md`, with `check_fsm_diagrams_sync.py` as the `docs-drift` gate — the same generate→drift-gate shape as the cli-reference pair, so the lifecycle diagram can never drift from the model.

**Admin-dashboard HTML snapshot ([#12](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/12)).** `src/teatree/core/factory/dashboard_snapshot.py` renders the teatree Django admin index — frozen to a dedicated `AdminSite` carrying only `teatree.core`'s registered model admins, with output-affecting settings (`ROOT_URLCONF`, `STATIC_URL`, `LANGUAGE_CODE`, `TIME_ZONE`, `DEBUG`) pinned and the CSRF token stripped — to a byte-stable HTML string via Django's test client; `scripts/hooks/generate_dashboard_snapshot.py` stands up a throwaway sqlite test DB and writes the canonical [docs/generated/dashboard/admin-index.html](docs/generated/dashboard/admin-index.html) (which carries the two token `<link>`s the `templates/admin/base_site.html` re-skin injects; the hook's `files:` glob re-fires on that override + `admin-theme.css`). The `docs-drift` job regenerates it (NO_STAGE) before the same `git diff --exit-code docs/generated` gate the diagrams use, and `tests/teatree_core/factory/test_dashboard_snapshot.py` is the loud in-suite gate (it owns a DB, so it plays the `check_fsm_diagrams_sync.py` role) — so the dashboard "screenshot" can never drift from the registered models.

**Representative CLI-output snapshot ([#12](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/12)).** `src/teatree/cli/cli_output_snapshot.py` renders the front-door `t3` command output (`t3 --help`, `t3 loop --help`) to byte-stable markdown via `teatree.cli.command_tree.render_help_blocks` — the same #2599 determinism seam (pinned width, no env-derived sizing, home-rooted defaults folded to `~`, overlay surface pinned to `t3-teatree`) the CLI reference uses, so no timestamp/duration/PID/path can leak; `scripts/hooks/generate_cli_output_snapshot.py` writes the canonical [docs/generated/cli/representative-output.md](docs/generated/cli/representative-output.md). The `docs-drift` job regenerates it (NO_STAGE) before the same `git diff --exit-code docs/generated` gate, and `tests/teatree_cli/test_cli_output_snapshot.py` is the loud in-suite gate — the curated, always-fresh complement to the exhaustive CLI reference (#67).

**Scoped mutation testing ([#131](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/131)).** `t3 mutation run` runs mutmut over only diff-touched modules in the NARROW `[tool.teatree.mutation]` safety registry; `tests/quality/test_mutation*.py` carry the ledger + kill-proof. `BaselineRatchet` ([#1926](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1926)) makes the recorded `baseline_surviving` count a ratchet — a run above it fails in BOTH `warn` and `block` mode (count may only shrink), the prerequisite to flipping `mode` to `block`.

**A verdict is a claim about a set that was ENUMERATED ([#4135](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4135)).** `t3 tool triage-issues` printed a clear backlog while both of its `gh` calls had failed unauthenticated, and a hand triage of the same repo at the same moment found three resolved-but-open issues. Each enumerator was individually correct — it wrote the failure to stderr — and each verdict was individually correct given its input; the defect is the seam, because `return []` on a failed read makes "the forge could not be reached" and "the backlog is clear" the same bytes. Every `gh` read in `teatree/triage.py` now goes through one chokepoint that raises `ForgeEnumerationError`, and the three `t3 tool` verdicts report UNKNOWN and exit non-zero rather than a verdict they never established — so `--close-resolved` cannot act on a scan that did not run. `tests/conformance/test_failed_enumeration_reports_unknown.py` holds the family as an AST walk plus behavioural lanes, and it is SCOPED to the triage tool surface on purpose: `src/teatree` has ~34 sites returning a neutral value on a non-zero `returncode` and most are legitimate probes whose empty answer honestly means absent, so a tree-wide property would need an allowlist longer than itself — a change detector wearing an invariant's clothes. The general doctrine it instantiates is `skills/rules/SKILL.md` § "External Read Failure Must Fail Loud, Never Silent-Empty".

**Test-path-mirror forward-guard.** `t3 tool test-path-mirror` enforces *tests mirror production code*: a test file's first-party imports (parsed by AST, never executed) must place it under the mirror dir `tests/teatree_<pkg>/...` of at least one imported module; a top-level src *module* maps to the `tests` root as an EXACT-only expectation. The verdict is a per-path **ledger**, not a scalar count: `[tool.teatree.test_path_mirror] baseline_file` points at a committed sorted grandfathered-path list (`tests/test_path_mirror_grandfathered.txt`). The gate is RED when any LIVE violation is NOT in the ledger (a NEW mis-pathed file, named) AND RED when any ledger entry no longer violates (forced banking — closing the old count-baseline's headroom hole where unbanked reductions let new mis-pathed files ride in green). `--update-baseline` rewrites the ledger to the exact live set but refuses to ADD an entry without `--allow-regression`. A path list merges as a git **set-union**, so two disjoint PRs never collide — the semantic non-mergeability of a single integer (git cannot union two +1s) is gone. A small reviewed exemption set (`tests/integration/`, `tests/e2e*`, `conftest.py`, a `# test-path: cross-cutting` pragma, …) keeps legitimate multi-package tests out of the count.

**Skill-CLI-migration forward-guard ([#3076](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3076)).** `teatree.quality.skill_cli_ratchet` enforces the MCP-serves-everything migration on the *skill* side: skills route their forge/monitoring reach through the teatree MCP tools, not raw `gh`/`glab`/`sentry-cli` shell-outs. It shares the test-path-mirror ledger shape — `[tool.teatree.skill_cli_ratchet] baseline_file` points at a committed `<skill file>::<command signature>` list (`tests/skill_cli_ratchet_grandfathered.txt`) grandfathering the current raw-call surface (the documented `skills/platforms/references/` CLI fallback lane, bootstrap exceptions, and call sites with no MCP tool yet). RED on a NEW raw call (a skill regressing to a shell-out, named) and RED on a stale ledger key (forced banking). **Prohibition examples** ("never `gh pr merge`", detected by surrounding never/FORBIDDEN/mechanically-refused markers) and per-line `mcp-ratchet: allow` pragmas are excluded, so rewriting a prohibition into an MCP call — which would invert its meaning — is never demanded. Detection is textual and code-context-aware (fenced blocks + inline backticks only, so prose mentions of a tool are not flagged). The gate runs as a pytest fitness test in the CI `test (3.13)` lane (`tests/teatree_quality/test_skill_cli_ratchet.py`); regenerate the ledger with `python -m teatree.quality.skill_cli_ratchet --update-baseline`. Scoped to the 3rd-party CLIs (`gh`/`glab`/`sentry-cli`); a `t3`-direct ratchet awaits the ratified long-running-CLI exception class. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Merge-safe ratchet ledgers + CI parity.** The two intra-package deferred-import ratchets follow the same per-item ledger shape: `tests/quality/deferred_import_pegs.toml` (`[intra_core]` / `[intra_loop]`) pegs each source file's function-scoped `teatree.core` / `teatree.loop` imports (unlisted files peg at 0), counted by the shared `tests/quality/_deferred_imports.py` walker. Over-peg blocks (names the file), under-peg banks (lower the entry); disjoint per-file bumps merge as set-union, same-file contention surfaces as a git textual conflict. The local/CI predicate gap is closed two ways without violating the no-full-suite-on-push invariant (`tests/test_no_full_suite_on_pre_push.py`): a push-stage `ci-critical-parity` prek hook runs `dev/push-gate.sh` (the fast early signal — the never-lockout contract + the incremental push gate; the broad `tests/quality` dir is CI-only, ~420s locally, covered whole-tree by the `test (3.13)` shard), and `dev/ci-parity.sh` chains the EXACT full CI predicate (prek all-files, `makemigrations --check`, `t3 tool test-path-mirror`, `check_module_health.py --from-ref`, `dev/test-cov.sh`, `t3 ci coverage`) as the one documented pre-push command for src-touching PRs — opt-in by workflow, never a push hook, because the 93% whole-tree coverage floor is a whole-tree property no diff-scoped subset can prove.

**Incompleteness-marker ratchet.** `teatree.quality.incompleteness_markers` scans every shipped `.py` under `src/teatree`, `hooks/`, and `scripts/`, plus `BLUEPRINT.md` and `docs/blueprint/`, for prose in which the code declares itself unfinished — an author marker, a component described as disconnected from what would make it take effect, a carve-out kept with nothing in it, a field held against work that has not happened. Scope is code plus the architecture spec because those are the surfaces where a false claim actively misleads; dated one-shot design docs are pinned OUT (`ONE_SHOT_DESIGN_DOCS`), so widening the doc scope cannot pull one in unnoticed. Only comments and docstrings are read (`teatree.quality.prose`) — a phrase inside a string literal is a runtime value, not the module describing itself. Matching is case-insensitive and spans one line wrap, because the tree emphasises with capitals and docstrings break sentences mid-phrase. Phrase families are SHAPES in `incompleteness_markers.yaml`, data rather than literals in the scanner, which is what lets the detector scan itself; each carries trigger literals that decide what is worth parsing, with a test proving no file the pre-stage rejected could have matched. The ledger `tests/quality/incompleteness_marker_pegs.toml` takes the same per-file shape as the deferred-import pegs above: over-peg blocks naming file, line, phrase and remedy; under-peg blocks too, so resolving a statement is banked instead of leaving budget for the next one. Raising an entry means shipping an admission that the implementation is incomplete, which is inadmissible by default — finish it, or do not ship the phase. A deterministic sub-gate additionally blocks a marker whose adjacent issue reference resolves to a CLOSED issue (done-but-uncleaned, or a promise nobody tracks), reading the committed `tests/quality/closed_issues.toml` snapshot and never the network: a gate that flakes on an expired token is worse than no gate, and an issue absent from the snapshot reads as open, so staleness costs recall and never a wrong block. Refresh it with `scripts/refresh_closed_issue_manifest.py`. What the ratchet structurally CANNOT catch is the inverse — prose claiming shipped-ness it lacks, which by construction contains no marker. The same registry serves a second, stricter gate over the verification trees `tests/`, `evals/` and `e2e/` (`banned_markers`, stated in `tests/quality/test_deferred_marker_ban.py`): a marker there records work a test did not do, so it is banned outright with no ledger to bank a count in. Two narrowings make a zero-tolerance gate safe where a guard's own tests must name the strings the guard detects. Only the families the registry marks `form: marker` apply, so adding a family with that form extends the ban and there is no second list; and the marker must OPEN a comment, in the registry's own marker form (the tag carrying a colon, a paren, or an issue ref — a bare word is the harness's domain noun). A docstring is the object documenting itself, a string literal is a runtime value, a YAML block scalar is verbatim carried text and markdown's fenced or indented code is quoted rather than said, JSON and JSONL have no comment syntax at all, and the same word mid-sentence is the file talking ABOUT markers. Ruff's `FIX`/`TD` families are the commit-time half over `.py`, drawing the same comment-versus-mention line; `tests/quality/test_ruff_antislop_caps.py` pins them out of `per-file-ignores` too, since ruff resolves an exemption by path. *Flagged for owner review per the BLUEPRINT change policy.*

**Safety-biased incremental push gate ([#122](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/122)).** `dev/push-gate.sh` scopes the two whole-tree costs that dominated every push — the `--doctest-modules src/teatree` sweep (Engine A) and the ast-grep `TestBlockingSetIsGreen` scan (Engine B, relocated to `push_heavy` and CI's `test-shard` like #3032's jscpd/mutmut) — to the changed files. `teatree.quality.changed_set` is the single changed-files + FULL-trigger classifier both the push gate and `scripts/ci/changed_lanes.py` consume (one SSOT, no drift — the `CONFIG_*` sets are hoisted here and imported back). `teatree.quality.push_gate.plan_push_gate` decides scoped vs whole-tree; `scan_findings(paths=…)` scopes Engine B (`paths=None` = the CI-identical whole-tree path). The doctrine is fail-safe-to-FULL: every uncertainty (a `.ast-grep`/manifest/config/conftest/migration change, any non-`.py` under a code dir, any delete/rename, an unclassifiable path, a missing merge-base) runs the whole sweep; a missing ast-grep engine DEFERS Engine B to CI loudly, never wedging the push. The DB-home flag `incremental_push_gate` (SETTLING, default TRUE) gates it — ON ⇒ scoped to the diff (the default, after the CI `selection-audit` soak — ground-truth: any whole-tree finding/failure the scoped gate would have skipped fails loud — came clean); OFF ⇒ whole-tree both sweeps (the pre-#122 run), the per-overlay escape hatch. A fresh/restored install resolves the code default, so the scoped gate is live without a seed row; a read failure fails safe to whole-tree FULL. The CI whole-tree backstop (`TestBlockingSetIsGreen` + whole-tree `--doctest-modules` in the required `test (3.13)` check) is NEVER removed; the push gate is a fast early signal, CI is the hard gate. `dev/ci-parity-fast.sh` is the inner-loop parity (scoped prek + `makemigrations --check` + the scoped gate, NO coverage floor — run the full `dev/ci-parity.sh` once before push). Engine A runs the sweep in a child whose `DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE` is STRIPPED (`push_gate.sweep_env`): the gate resolves its own flag through `ensure_django`, whose `setdefault` leaves that variable in `os.environ`, and pytest-django ranks it above the ini — so an inherited environment silently swaps the settings module out from under the sweep and collects the tree under settings CI never uses ([#3808](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3808)). And a failing sweep is REPORTED, never reduced to an exit code: `_run_doctests` returns a `DoctestOutcome` carrying the exit code and the run's own output, `run_push_gate` appends a note naming the targets, the code and the output tail, and `--emit-cmd` prints `sweep_command` — `sweep_argv` plus the visible `env -u…` strip — so the operator's hand-run is the run that failed. A gate that exits non-zero with no diagnostic is indistinguishable from an environmental flake and teaches its users to bypass it.

**The scoped lane is what the AGENT is told to run ([#3994](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3994)).** The selector above only helps if the prose a dispatched builder reads points at it. It did not: `skills/code` mandated `uv run pytest --no-cov -x -q` before any push and `skills/ship` mandated `dev/ci-parity.sh` (whose `dev/test-cov.sh` step is the whole suite plus the coverage floor) for any src-touching PR — so the full suite ran LOCALLY twice per ticket before CI ran it a third time, measured as most of a 3.5h cycle against a 30m target, on a box where two concurrent agents already peaked at 14.85 GB. Only the third run gates the merge. Every per-phase mandate now names the diff-scoped lane, and `teatree.quality.local_verification` pins it: each registered surface (the repo-root `CLAUDE.md` and `AGENTS.md`, `README.md`, `docs/contributing.md`, `tests/README.md`, `skills/code`, `skills/test`, `skills/ship`, `skills/contribute`, `skills/retro`'s fork pre-flight, `teatree.agents.coding_prompt`'s dispatch brief, `tests/CLAUDE.md`) must prescribe no unscoped whole-`testpaths` pytest AND must NAME a scoped lane — the second half because the first alone is absence-satisfied, so deleting the mandate would read as compliant. The matcher is `teatree.quality.full_suite_invocation`, extracted from `tests/test_no_full_suite_on_pre_push.py` so the push-hook guard and the mandate guard cannot drift. A surface that CITES a whole-suite command rather than prescribing one — "never a bare `pytest`", a runner-vs-runner count comparison — marks that line `local-verification: cited-not-prescribed`, because a prohibition is textually identical to a prescription and forcing correct prose to be mangled is the wrong trade. The escape is line-scoped and cannot reach the positive half, so it can silence one citation, never the guard. Nothing is weakened: the push gate, CI's sharded `test (3.13)` lane and the 93% whole-tree floor are untouched, and `classify_selection` is fail-safe TO FULL, so a migration / conftest / factories / settings / unclassifiable path still runs everything.

**One UNSCOPED lane survives on the push path: `tests/conformance`.** Everything else the push gate runs is diff-scoped, because an import graph can decide it. A conformance assertion cannot be scoped even in principle — its input is the whole tree, so a new `ScanSignal` kind with no dispatch/statusline route breaks `tests/conformance/test_signal_route_totality.py` whichever module the diff names. `tests/quality`'s always-run cousin was relocated to CI for cost (~420s); `tests/conformance` is 34s at `-n auto` and does not grow with the diff, so it stays. `dev/test-affected.sh` already force-keeps the dir (`FLOOR_DIRS`), but that lane is opt-in and the hand-picked directory lists agents substitute for it (`pytest tests/teatree_core tests/teatree_loop …`) exclude the dir — which is how the class reached CI twice ([#3787](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/pull/3787), [#3788](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/pull/3788)) behind green local runs. On the push path it is unconditional. Pinned by `tests/test_no_full_suite_on_pre_push.py::TestCiCriticalParityHook` in both directions: `tests/conformance` must be there, `tests/quality` must not.

**The order-dependence lane cannot report a false green.** CI's `test-shuffle` job and its local twin `dev/test-shuffle.sh` both run the curated order-safe set serially under `pytest-randomly`, which lives in the non-default `shuffle` dependency group (installing it into `dev` would shuffle every `uv run pytest` and destabilise the coverage lane). A hand-rolled `uv run pytest -p randomly … | tail` on a plain `dev` env therefore raises `ImportError: Error importing plugin "randomly"` while the PIPELINE exits 0 — a shell pipeline reports its LAST stage's status, so a lane that collected nothing reads green to anything gating on the exit code. The runner closes that with three layered guards: `set -euo pipefail` and no pipes; an `import pytest_randomly` preflight that fails loud with the fix command before pytest is asked to load the plugin; and `-o required_plugins=pytest-randomly`, pytest's own in-session assertion, so a degraded environment cannot yield an UNSHUFFLED green either. `tests/test_ci_shuffle_lane_scope.py` pins each guard against its own removal and pins byte-level directory parity between the local runner and the CI job, so the twin can neither drift narrower (a false local green) nor wider (asserting order-safety CI never verified).

**Docs are CLASSIFIED, not escalated ([#3645](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3645)).** `affected_tests.classify_selection` decided FULL *before* the tach graph was consulted for any changed path outside the modelled src/test roots — and the blueprint-sync gate REQUIRES a `BLUEPRINT.md` edit on exactly that class of commit, so every well-behaved implementer's diff forced the whole tree (measured: 59m32s / 30182 tests for a one-module import fix, plus 30 shared-box artifact failures that then had to be disproved). `teatree.quality.doc_impact` is a pure leaf (stdlib + filesystem) answering the two questions the selector could not ask — `is_doc_path`, `reference_tokens`, and a `disk_reference_reader_lookup` mapping those tokens to the tests that READ them. Docs are not assumed inert: `classify_selection` partitions doc paths out of the changed set *before* delegating to the shared `changed_set.classify` so a doc never reaches the out-of-root escalation, and `build_force_keep` then force-keeps the tests whose source names the changed doc (full path, basename, or any containing directory) as a `reference-read` reason — the textual analogue of the import scan that maps src modules to their tests. The classified set is markdown, the `docs/` tree and the mkdocs config, all outside `src/`/`tests/`, so a `.md` fixture under a code root is untouched and still forces FULL; everything executable keeps the old bias (conftest, `factories.py`, test settings, migrations, `.ast-grep` rules, config/toolchain, python outside the modelled roots, and any delete/rename of code — including one sitting beside a doc edit). The merge gate is untouched — the sharded CI run plus the combined coverage floor stays the authority, so a narrowed LOCAL classifier cannot create a false green. On this repo a `BLUEPRINT.md`-only diff now selects 17 reference-reader test files + 3 floor dirs instead of the whole tree.

**`dev/` lane runners are classified the same way; the lane's OWN machinery forces FULL ([#3817](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3817)).** `changed_set.CONFIG_PREFIXES` calls the whole `dev/` tree un-modellable toolchain. That is right for its two other consumers — `scripts/ci/changed_lanes.py` (CI lane routing) and `quality.push_gate` (the doctest + ast-grep sweep), both of which keep a whole-tree CI backstop — but for TEST SELECTION it made a one-line edit to `dev/push-gate.sh` select the whole tree (33952 collected tests on this repo), so the change that most needs a local run was the one change that could not get one. A shell / compose / Dockerfile asset under `dev/` is imported by nothing and executed by no test, so the only test that can observe an edit to it is one whose source NAMES it: `affected_tests.is_lane_runner` puts it in the same non-imported class as a doc and `is_reference_mapped` partitions both out before the shared classifier, mapping them to their readers. Python under `dev/` is excluded (importable ⇒ the out-of-root FULL still owns it), and `changed_set` itself is untouched, so CI-lane routing and the push gate stay exactly as conservative as before.

The safety this narrowing appears to remove never existed where it mattered, and is now explicit and strictly stronger. The `dev/` blanket covered only the shell runner; the selection logic proper — `changed_set.py`, `affected_tests.py`, `doc_impact.py`, `force_keep_plugin.py`, `test_path_mirror.py`, `cli/affected_tests_tools.py` — is `src/teatree/**/*.py`, which the shared classifier SCOPED like any other module, so the selector was already using its own edited logic to decide what to run. `affected_tests.SELECTION_DEFINING_PATHS` names all seven (the six modules plus `dev/test-affected.sh`) and `classify_selection` returns FULL for any of them BEFORE the doc/lane-runner partition and before the shared classifier — a selection cannot validate a change to the code that computes it. Membership is by exact path, so `test_every_pinned_path_exists_on_disk` fails on a stale entry a rename would otherwise drop silently. Measured on a one-line `dev/push-gate.sh` diff: FULL was 33904 tests in 890s at `-n auto`, peaking at 20.4 GB of system memory; SCOPED is 3825 tests in 205s (the 3 floor dirs plus the 65 test files that name `dev/`) — the same cost any other scoped diff pays. A FULL verdict is therefore long rather than impossible on a quiet box, but its ~3.7 GB of worker RSS is what OOM-kills a box already carrying several agents; `PYTEST_XDIST_AUTO_NUM_WORKERS=<n>` bounds `-n auto` so the run finishes slowly instead, and CI's sharded `test (3.13)` lane remains the authority.

**tach plugin is authoritative; force-keep preserves our escalations ([#3672](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3672)).** The impact engine for a SCOPED selection is tach's own **pytest plugin** (`--tach --tach-base origin/main`), which walks the import graph natively inside one normal pytest session and deselects the tests a diff cannot reach. `teatree.quality.affected_tests` owns only the ESCALATION policy the plugin cannot infer. `classify_selection` decides FULL-vs-SCOPED — a changed conftest / `factories.py` / test-settings / migration / any unclassifiable executable path forces FULL, a migration additionally requires `--create-db` — and a FULL verdict runs the whole suite with the plugin OFF, so nothing is deselected. On a SCOPED verdict `build_force_keep` computes the set the plugin's module graph cannot know — the floor dirs, the reference-reader tests (those naming a changed doc or `dev/` lane runner), the test-path-mirror path, and the changed test files themselves — and `teatree.quality.force_keep_plugin` applies it as a FORCE-KEEP layer over the plugin's deselection: a `pytest_collection_modifyitems` hook wrapper that hides the force-kept items before tach runs and re-inserts them after, in the SAME session, so no test runs twice. The force-keep layer reads the plugin's own `--tach-base` so both diff the identical ref, and fails SAFE — if the escalation set cannot be computed mid-run it protects the whole collected set rather than let tach deselect on an unproven scope. `cli/affected_tests_tools.py` emits the plugin invocation for a scoped run and reports the force-keep layer; `dev/test-affected.sh` runs it. The merge gate is untouched — the sharded CI run plus the combined coverage floor stays the authority, so this diff-scoped LOCAL selector never creates a false green.

**Behavioral eval harness ([#1160](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1160)).** `src/teatree/eval/` grades agent behaviour across free deterministic lanes (real-code-path regression corpus, skill-coverage, catalog-discovery, negative-control, transcript-replay, corpus-grade, skill-command-validity) and a metered AI lane (in-process `claude-agent-sdk`, weekly standalone `eval.yml`, authed on the credential `agent_harness_provider` selects — DEFAULT the subscription OAuth token, with the metered `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` selectable per run via `t3 eval run --credential api_key`). Bare `t3 eval` runs the whole suite in one table; subcommands target one lane. The architecture:

- **Doctrine and the corpus must grade the same command ([#4137](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4137)).** #3949 made `t3 push` the one supported push path from the worker container and three matchers kept asserting the retired `git push` spelling, so an agent following current doctrine EXACTLY was graded wrong for months — the skill and each matcher individually correct, the defect purely in the relationship. `eval/doctrine_agreement.py` derives both directions of the agreement from ONE structure, the *seam migration*: a fenced doctrine block whose `FORBIDDEN` form and mandated form are different PROGRAMS. That restriction is what makes the property honest — a same-program pair is an argument-level distinction (an emoji value, a `--draft` flag) a positive matcher legitimately spans, while a cross-program pair cannot be spanned by accident. `stale_matchers` flags any positive Bash-command matcher that matches the retired program and NO mandated form (a correctly-widened `(git|t3) push` therefore passes); `unpinned_mandates` flags any mandated command no scenario graded against that same `agent_path` pins. `tests/conformance/test_doctrine_eval_agreement.py` holds both, and each direction grades an exact named set rather than a "no worse than" floor: a new offender fails the lane, and a recorded one that gets fixed fails it too until its entry is deleted.
- **#550 3-tier skill model.** Tier-1 `skill-command-validity` (free) fails on a backticked `t3 …` that no longer resolves against the live CLI registry, over every human-authored prose doc — the skills tree, `agents/*.md`, `BLUEPRINT.md` and `docs/` (minus `docs/generated/`, which is rendered FROM that registry); Tier-2 is the metered behavioral scenarios; Tier-3 `skill-prose-judge` is **advisory** (scores SKILL.md prose, never fails the suite).
- **Under-load behavioural-drift lane ([#2346](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2346)).** `EvalSpec.lane` selects the harness mode: `clean_room` (default) loads one skill into an empty context; `under_load` reproduces real-session instruction drift — the skill bundle as system prompt plus an 8k–20k-token polluted `context_preamble` folded into the user prompt. The bundle is capped at a deterministic char budget (the scenario's own `agent_path` skill, the `rules` skill, and every small canonical-source skill always kept; only the largest peripheral skills shed) so a full bundle + a realistic preamble + a multi-tool scenario fits the model's input window instead of overflowing with "Prompt too long" before the model runs. A `--lane` filter slices the one catalog so the cheap PR-path anti-vacuity gate and the weekly metered lane run the right subset. There is **no known-red allowance and no shrink-only ratchet**: every `under_load` scenario must be GREEN, and a failing one reds the run **outright**, exactly like a failing `clean_room` scenario (`t3 eval run --trials k` exits non-zero on any red scenario, in either lane). The behavioural fix lever is the **skill prose** — a drifting rule must be made unmissable in the loaded `skills/*/SKILL.md` the lane sends the model, never added to a tolerated-red list. The known-red quarantine ([#4173](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4173), above) is not an exception to this and never reaches a verdict: it decides only which scenarios the bounded selective-PR lane SELECTS, so a quarantined scenario that runs still reds its run wherever it runs. Each scenario's matchers are proven anti-vacuous on every PR by `tests/eval_replay/test_scenarios_anti_vacuous.py` (its `_fail` fixture grades RED, `_pass` GREEN, a `_noop` transcript cannot satisfy it) before the metered lane (weekly `.gitlab-ci.yml` run + on-demand `eval.yml` workflow, off the per-PR path) ever runs.
- **Question-surface axis — the Slack round trip gates, the tool-call rendering does not ([#3855](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3855)).** `EvalSpec.surface` is ORTHOGONAL to `lane`: `lane` selects the harness mode, `surface` selects which question/answer surface the scenario grades. `headless` (the default) BLOCKS — it grades the contract teatree owns, that a question REACHES the user over Slack with a `DeferredQuestion` as the durable record and the answer comes back (`headless_question_contract.yaml` outbound, `askuserquestion_slack_resolution.yaml` inbound). `interactive` is ADVISORY — the scenario grades the Claude-interactive `AskUserQuestion` TOOL-CALL rendering, whose wire shape belongs to a bundled `claude` CLI generation, so it is still run and still reported (`N advisory failed` in the lane detail) but never flips a verdict. The verdicts it must reach are NAMED, never counted — `teatree.eval.surface.ADVISORY_EXEMPT_VERDICT_POINTS` is the canonical list and `tests/conformance/test_advisory_verdict_points.py` resolves every entry: `all._ai_lane_result` (the full-suite AI lane), `multi_trial.run_pass_at_k_lane`, `multi_trial.run_model_matrix_lane`, and both exits of the DEFAULT single-trial lane every metered CI leg actually drives — `run_modes.finalize_single_run` and `escalate.EscalationOutcome.is_hard_red` (`--escalate-on-fail`) — plus `green_proof.evaluate_green_proof`, the SECOND gate `eval-ci-heal` applies over the merged summary JSON after the shards finish. Counting these went stale twice ("three", then "four", both wrong and both omitting a real gate), which is why the list is a resolved-symbol enumeration rather than a number. The merged artifact carries each row's `surface` and the `advisory` flag derived from it, so the combine job and `loop.ci_eval_heal_advance.red_scenario_names` (the fixer-DISPATCH set) honour the exemption without re-deriving it; a row missing the flag reads as GATING. `t3 eval run --surface <headless|interactive>` slices the catalog outright, and `--surface` is forwarded across the `--docker` re-invocation like `--lane`/`--shard` (dropping it would silently run the full catalog in-container on metered spend). Absent means `headless`, so advisory status is always an explicit opt-in, and `tests/eval_replay/test_question_surface.py` reds when any scenario hard-requires the tool call while still gating — that gate is what REPLACED the `claude-agent-sdk` Dependabot quarantine, so Claude Code interactive stays graded without a rendering change ever reddening the headless lane. Generated scenarios DERIVE the label from their matcher shape rather than declaring it.
- **Production-hooks lane — measure the model+hook SYSTEM ([#107](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/107) cluster 5).** `EvalSpec.production_hooks: true` registers the SHIPPED teatree plugin (`hooks/hooks.json`; the repo root IS the plugin root, so a plugin-carried `hooks.json` fires despite the empty user-level `settings`) into the SDK child and sets `include_hook_events=True`, so a scenario whose behaviour is enforced by a Claude-Code hook (the #807 structured-question Stop gate, the #2665 completion-claim Stop gate) grades the model+hook system that ships instead of the raw hooks-stripped model. `hooked_env` additionally redirects the loop/hook state roots (`XDG_DATA_HOME`, `T3_HOOK_STATE_DIR`, `T3_LOOP_REGISTRY_DIR`, `TEATREE_CLAUDE_STATUSLINE_STATE_DIR`) into the sandbox home so `_session_drives_loop` sees a fresh owner-less registry and the Stop gate actually fires (else a developer's live loop-owner registry silently skips it). **Honesty is structural, never a spurious green:** gate-firing is a REPORT ANNOTATION (`pass (gate-assisted)` in `render_text`; `gate_assisted`/`gate_events` in the JSON report), NOT a per-scenario pass condition (a required "gate fired" matcher would wrongly RED a first-try-compliant model); a hooked run capturing ZERO hook events is a fail-loud `hooks_not_registered` error (the plugin failed to register → the lane would degrade to raw-model measurement); and the `harness_canary_stop_gate_fires` canary (a prose-only decision that can pass ONLY via the #807 bounce) REPORTS the wiring end-to-end each metered run. **What the canary no longer does is gate.** It can pass only via a captured `AskUserQuestion` call, so it is `surface: interactive` and therefore advisory — as are five of the other six `production_hooks` scenarios. The one whose behavioural VERDICT still gates on the shipped hook system is `done_only_on_deployed_dev_evidence` (the #2665 completion-claim Stop gate).
