- **Harness-failure axis — a run that measured NOTHING is not a verdict, so no surface exempts it ([#3922](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3922)).** `hooks_not_registered` used to ride a terminal `EvalRun` — a failing VERDICT — so the advisory exemption swallowed it: 6 of the 7 hooked scenarios are `surface: interactive`, the only gating one is `lane: under_load`, and the nightly runs `--lane clean_room`, so on that shard set the fail-loud could never gate at all. `teatree.eval.harness_failure` is now its own axis beside `teatree.eval.surface`: the surface weighs a verdict, and a run whose harness failed has none to weigh. The reason is read by `RunGuards.hooks_registered`, an unconditional guard that takes NO surface argument and runs BESIDE each lane's verdict (after the lane's artifacts are written, so a red run still drops its transcript + summary), which is what makes the exemption structurally unreachable rather than merely un-applied. The lanes that must call it are NAMED, never counted — `HARNESS_FAILURE_GUARD_POINTS`: `single_trial.run_single_trial`, `multi_trial.run_pass_at_k_lane`, `multi_trial.run_model_matrix_lane`, `all.run_full_suite`, `ladder.ladder`, `benchmark.benchmark` — and the two producers of the serialized `advisory` flag (`HARNESS_FAILURE_ADVISORY_CARVE_OUTS`: `summary_json._ScenarioRow.as_json`, `escalate.escalate_failures`) never set it for a row that measured nothing, so the `eval-ci-heal` combine job's merged-artifact gate cannot bless a shard the lane guard failed. A `--trials k` matrix cell carries the signal on `MatrixRow.harness_failed` (its `terminal_reason` holds only the CAP reason, which would drop it silently), folded from `PassAtKResult.harness_failed` — every executed trial, the way `is_error` folds. Calling the guard is not sufficient on its own, because the guard reads the ROW's flag: a fold that omits it hands over a clean row and that lane's call is vacuous, so every `PassAtKResult` → `MatrixRow` fold is NAMED too (`HARNESS_FAILURE_FOLD_POINTS`: `multi_trial._matrix_trial`, `ladder._row_from`) and must carry the field. `tests/conformance/test_advisory_verdict_points.py` resolves every name and asserts each lane actually calls the guard and each fold actually carries the flag, so a NEW lane that forgets reds there rather than in a metered CI leg. `done_only_on_deployed_dev_evidence` is `lane: under_load`, so a leg that does not run it — the nightly's `--lane clean_room` shards (whose four hooked scenarios are all advisory), the changed-scenarios PR lane, `--surface interactive`, a `--name` run — still loses that scenario's behavioural verdict, but no leg loses the harness fail-loud any more. Registration is run-wide, so the signal is never lost: read the run output rather than inferring registration from a green exit code. Where production enforcement is the `t3` CLI rather than a Claude hook, fidelity is a gate-aware stub instead (`cli_stubs: [t3@on_behalf_ask]` refuses colleague-surface posts with exit 1, parity-tested against the production block message). Additive: a scenario declaring none is byte-identical.
- **Model resolution by abstract tier ([eval redesign]).** Eval scenarios reference models by abstract TIER, not a concrete id: `EvalSpec.tier` (`frontier`/`balanced`/`cheap`) or `EvalSpec.phase` (an FSM phase name) resolve through the single `src/teatree/agents/model_tiering.TIER_MODELS` constant (`eval/model_resolution.resolve_eval_model`). The resolution precedence is `model:` (an explicit concrete-id escape hatch, `model@effort` preserved) > `tier` → `resolve_tier` > `phase` → `DEFAULT_PHASE_MODELS[phase]` → `resolve_tier` > `DEFAULT_TIER` (`balanced`). Adopting a new model is one edit to `TIER_MODELS` (or one `[agent.tier_models]` line) — every scenario and test follows, no edit. NO concrete model-id string literal appears in any scenario or test.
- **Non-Claude execution backend (`t3 eval run --backend pydantic_ai`).** A third `EvalRunner` (`eval/pydantic_ai_runner.py`, alongside the `api` SDK runner and the `transcript` replay runner) drives a NON-Claude model through the provider-agnostic `PydanticAiHarnessSession` (OrcaRouter BYOK, `x-lane: eval`), so a GPT/open-source swap is verifiable by the same scenarios — a config change (`agent_harness` + tier-model/router rows), not a code change. Grading stays runtime-neutral: the session adapts the model's streamed output into the shared `claude_agent_sdk` message vocabulary and `eval/message_mapping.eval_run_from_messages` folds it into an `EvalRun` unchanged — folding event dicts DIRECTLY via `StreamJsonEvent.from_obj` (no typed→JSON→dict round-trip). The scenario's declared tools become inert `pydantic_ai` stubs so tool-call behaviour is captured in the same `ToolUseBlock` vocabulary the SDK lane yields; the OrcaRouter credential resolves lazily inside `run`, so building the runner needs no live BYOK key.
- **CLI-free Claude execution backend (`t3 eval run --backend anthropic_api`, [#3222](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3222)).** A fourth `EvalRunner` (`eval/anthropic_api_runner.py`) runs the SAME Claude model as the `api` backend but through the Anthropic Messages API DIRECTLY — a `pydantic_ai` `AnthropicModel` over the `anthropic` package (the `[anthropic]` extra) → `api.anthropic.com`, spawning NO `claude` CLI child. So a downstream harness that forbids the Claude Code CLI can adopt teatree's `--backend api` eval scenarios while teatree's own default lane keeps the CLI-backed SDK transport. It shares the agentic loop + message-vocabulary mapping with the `pydantic_ai` backend (it resolves the `AnthropicModel` and delegates the drive to `PydanticAiRunner`, whose `model` is injectable), so grading is byte-identical; the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` credential (`AnthropicApiKeyCredential`) resolves LAZILY inside `run` (a missing key skips, or fails loud under `--require-executed` via `AnthropicApiKeyMissingError` — symmetric with the `api` runner's missing-`claude` skip). `backends.FRESH_CLAUDE_BACKENDS` = `{api, anthropic_api}` is the shared set both fresh-Claude lanes key on (require-executed arming, `--trials`/`--models` acceptance, docker credential forwarding). `eval.yml` gains a `backend` dispatch input (default `api`, so the weekly run is unchanged) that skips the Claude-CLI install step for the CLI-free lane; pair it with `credential: api_key` (the `anthropic_api` lane authenticates on `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`).
- **Selective-PR eval + weekly benchmark publish ([#2704](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2704)).** A PR runs the metered suite ONLY for the scenarios its diff touched: `scripts/eval/scenarios_for_changed.py` maps changed paths (STDIN) to the names of every spec whose `source_path` matches, and the PR workflow loops `t3 eval run <name>` per changed scenario. **A scenario is built from its YAML AND from the skill prose it grades** ([#3944](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3944)): a clean-room scenario's system prompt is `agent_path` narrowed to `agent_sections`, so keying selection on the YAML alone let an edit to the exact section a scenario grades select ZERO scenarios while the lane reported PASS — vacuous-green by construction. The selector therefore also matches a spec whose `agent_path` is in the changed set, and the optional `--diff-file` (the same range's `git diff --unified=0`, parsed by `teatree.eval.changed_sections`) supplies the section granularity a path list cannot carry: a spec declaring `agent_sections` is selected only when one of ITS sections moved, while a spec with no `agent_sections` grades the whole file and any edit selects it. Every uncertainty (no diff, unreadable post-image, a preamble-only edit, a heading-less file) degrades to selecting MORE, never fewer. Section matches are ranked ahead of whole-file ones so the cap below can never truncate away the tightest evidence (the positional NAME filter; the multi-name `--only` flag is removed). `.github/workflows/eval-pr.yml` (`on: pull_request`) detects-then-runs the changed scenarios against the BASELINE preset (`--preset baseline`, single trial) — the same cheapest-passing per-scenario tier the weekly cron pins, so a PR's eval verdict matches the weekly's (a brand-new scenario absent from `baseline.yaml` falls through to its own tier). Because that lane runs the selection SEQUENTIALLY in ONE job, the selector caps it at `MAX_SELECTIVE_PR_SCENARIOS` (= `lane_shard.MAX_SCENARIOS_PER_SHARD`): a corpus-wide mechanical edit (a `model:`→`tier:` backfill, a mass rename) that touches every scenario file is truncated to a deterministic sorted-name prefix so it stays inside the 80-min step cap — full-catalog coverage of such a change is the weekly SHARDED lane's job, not the PR lane's. When the cap bites, `changed_scenarios.ScenarioSelection.truncation_note()` emits a `selected N … capped to K … deferred M to the weekly sharded lane` line to stderr from both `t3 eval changed-scenarios` and `scripts/eval/scenarios_for_changed.py` ([#2737](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2737)), so a corpus-wide PR's truncated coverage is visible in the CI log instead of silently dropped. **Known-red quarantine ([#4173](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4173)).** Section-scoping is precise, and that precision has a cost: while a scenario is red, every PR touching the doctrine section it grades reds its eval lane on a failure it did not cause — and the `_BROAD` fail-safe widens that blast radius exactly when the diff is hardest to classify. `evals/quarantine.yaml` (`teatree.eval.quarantine`) is the registry that bounds it: each entry names a currently-failing scenario, the issue that will fix it, an ISO expiry date and a one-line reason, and `suppressed_scenario_names()` is what the selector drops AFTER banding — so the fail-safe `_BROAD` band cannot smuggle a tracked red back in. The suppression is SELECTION scope and nothing else: the scenario is still run, still graded, and still reds the weekly/heal lane and the green proof, so the no-known-red-allowance rule below is untouched and there is no tolerated-red list anywhere. Three staleness channels keep the list from rotting into decoration. **Expiry is self-enforcing** — past `until` the entry simply stops suppressing, the scenario re-arms and blocks again exactly as it did before quarantine, so the degraded state is the status quo rather than a silent pass and no permanent skip list is reachable. `t3 eval quarantine check` reds on an expired entry or one naming a scenario the catalog no longer defines; `t3 eval quarantine audit <merged-summary.json>` — the step `eval-ci-heal.yml` runs beside `green-proof`, `if: always()` — NAMES each quarantined scenario's outcome in the merged run (`STILL RED` / `NOT RUN`) and reds on an `ESCAPED` one, a quarantined scenario that PASSED and whose entry is now a lie. Every suppression is named on the selector's stderr (`ScenarioSelection.quarantine_note()`), so a shrunken PR lane is visible in the same CI log as a truncated one. The registry sits beside its scenarios dir (`evals/scenarios` → `evals/quarantine.yaml`, `quarantine_path_for`), so a consumer passing its own `--scenarios-dir` reaches its own registry with no extra flag. A missing registry is an EMPTY quarantine (an overlay with none of its own selects exactly as before), while a PRESENT but malformed one fails loud rather than degrading to "suppress nothing". Every metered run (PR + weekly) can emit two artifacts from THIS run's results: the PRIVATE per-trial `--transcript-html` (reasoning + tool calls), and the PUBLISH-SAFE `--summary-md` (`summary_markdown.render_summary_markdown` — total counts + total cost + model + a `scenario | lane | verdict | trials | cost` table built ONLY from spec identity + verdict + pass/trial counts + per-scenario `cost_usd`, NEVER a transcript; an anti-disclosure test pins a transcript sentinel absent). The weekly `eval.yml` uploads each shard's sanitized summary (`eval-summary-*`), and a `publish` job merges them (`scripts/eval/merge_summaries.py` — summing per-scenario cost into a dashboard total) into `docs/evals/index.md`, pushing to `main` so `docs.yml` serves the dashboard on Pages. A red weekly run is surfaced by GitHub's native failed-run email (no extra secret). The single-trial execution shape lives in `cli/eval/single_trial.py`, the multi-trial/matrix shapes in `cli/eval/multi_trial.py`.
- **Reusable eval-CI primitives + `workflow_call` workflows.** The selection / scheduled-guard / dashboard-merge logic is extracted into pure modules (`teatree.eval.{changed_scenarios,merged_prs,summaries}`) exposed both as reusable CLI subcommands (`t3 eval changed-scenarios` selects a PR's scenarios from a STDIN diff; `t3 eval merged-prs-since` is the weekly no-PR-merged cron guard; `t3 eval merge-summaries` builds the public dashboard) and consumed by the host's `scripts/eval/*.py` (now thin shims over the same core). The two reusable workflows `.github/workflows/{eval-pr-reusable,eval-weekly-reusable}.yml` (`on: workflow_call`) own the selective-PR and weekly metered flows once — with overlay host-checkout + scenario-assertion inputs and the no-silent-green enforcement — so a downstream overlay's eval CI becomes a thin caller of teatree's primitives instead of a duplicate. The anti-leak invariant is unchanged: the summary merger and the publish job read only the sanitized verdict rows, never transcripts. **Unmetered-shard publish guard.** The benchmark rides a subscription OAuth window; once exhausted mid-run the remaining shards still "complete" with every scenario force-FAILed at `$0.0000`, so publishing them would commit fake all-red matrices. `t3 eval verify-benchmark-publish <dir> --expected-shards <n>` (`teatree.eval.benchmark_publish_guard` over `teatree.eval.matrix_html_tally`, the reader counterpart to `render_matrix_html`) runs in the publish job BEFORE the dashboard is written and refuses the WHOLE publish when ANY single model in ANY shard records graded verdicts against zero metered cost — the partial case (one model metered, the rest zeroed) is the one an all-zero filter misses. A shard with no graded verdicts owed no spend and is legitimately empty. `--expected-shards` is REQUIRED, and carries the second refusal: it is the matrix leg count the run planned, so a shard whose leg timed out and uploaded nothing is a refusal rather than a smaller dashboard that looks complete (an empty directory is refused outright).
- **CI-eval heal primitives (workflow + machine-readable artifact + `gh` wrappers).** The workflow_dispatch-only `.github/workflows/eval-ci-heal.yml` runs the behavioral eval against a PR branch (a `scenarios` red-subset input — empty = full suite — plus `credential` and `pr_ref`), reusing the in-Docker `t3 eval run --backend api --docker --require-executed` body and uploading a PUBLISH-SAFE per-scenario JSON (`eval-heal-<sha>`) plus the PRIVATE transcript. That JSON is the `t3 eval run --summary-json <path>` artifact (`teatree.eval.summary_json.render_summary_json`, sibling of `--summary-md`): `generated_at`/`model`/`head_sha`/`totals` + a `scenarios[]` of spec identity + verdict + the triage discriminators (`is_error`/`terminal_reason`/`matcher_failed`/`judge_failed`) and a derived `triage_class` — publish-safe by construction (no transcript/tool-input/judge-rationale). The pure `teatree.eval.triage.classify_red` derives that class ONCE from the real `CAP_TERMINAL_REASONS` (`eval/models.py`) + throttle prefix (`eval/api_errors.py`) — `behavioral`/`judge` (fix), `infra_transport`/`infra_throttle`/`infra_cap` (retry), `no_coverage` (a skip under `--require-executed`, never green). The `t3 eval ci-trigger` / `ci-status` (non-blocking) commands wrap `gh workflow run` / `run list|view|download` through `teatree.backends.github.ci_eval_client.GhCiEvalClient` on the existing `_run_gh` seam (`GH_TOKEN` via env only, never argv/logs) — an operator dispatches + reads a run now; the heal loop drives them later.
- **CI-eval heal FSM + anti-cheat gate ([#3201](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3201) PR-2).** `CiEvalHealSession` (`core/models/ci_eval_heal_session.py`) is the durable state machine one PR branch walks through the heal loop: `pending → awaiting_ci → triaging → {green | fixing → pushed → awaiting_ci …}`, with `halted` as the escalation terminal. Two invariants are structural, not left to the loop. **Never suppress a red:** `mark_green` is guarded by a `_no_reds` condition, so a session carrying any red scenario cannot reach `green` — the only terminals are `green` (genuinely clean) and `halted` (escalated via a `DeferredQuestion`); the bounded `max_fix_attempts` budget makes "un-greenable" decidable (`fix_budget_exhausted` → halt, never an infinite loop). **Fix the code, never the test:** the `record_fix` transition runs the anti-cheat structural gate `eval_heal_anticheat` (`core/gates/eval_heal_anticheat_gate.py`, registered through the gate registry so the model → gate edge stays inverted like `forced_repro`). `classify_fix_diff` refuses any fix diff touching the scenario tree (`evals/scenarios/**`) or the eval harness that grades it (`src/teatree/eval/**`), raising `EvalHealCheatError` (an `InvalidTransitionError`) so a cheating fix rolls back to `fixing` with the attempt counter untouched — the healer must change the product (skill prose, hooks, core code), never the assertion that grades it. Both bans are DEFAULT-DENY prefixes ([#4220](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/4220)): the enumerated four-module denylist admitted `eval/report.py` — which computes `ScenarioResult.passed` — plus `loader.py`, `skip_guard.py`, `summary_json.py` and `green_proof.py` by omission, so a fixer could widen the verdict itself and pass the gate. Exceptions live in `EVAL_HARNESS_ALLOWED_PATHS` (empty by design) and the gate's conformance test refuses one that sits on the grading call graph, computed as the transitive import closure of the verdict seeds by `quality/eval_grading_surface.py`.
- **CI-eval self-healing loop — observe-only, default-OFF ([#3201](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3201) PR-3a).** The default-OFF `ci_eval_heal` mini-loop (`loops/ci_eval_heal/loop.py`, seeded `enabled=False`) that DRIVES the PR-2 FSM without ever writing a fix. An operator opens a session for a PR branch (`t3 eval ci-heal open --ref <branch>`, `cli/eval/ci_heal.py`) — the loop never discovers branches to touch on its own — and, once the row is enabled, the scanner (`loop/scanners/ci_eval_heal.py`) flags open sessions and the mechanical handler (`loop/mechanical_ci_eval_heal.py` → `loop/ci_eval_heal_advance.advance_open_sessions`) advances each one FSM step per 5m tick: `pending` → dispatch the `eval-ci-heal` workflow on the `$0` subscription credential (`GhCiEvalClient`); `awaiting_ci` → poll once (non-blocking); on CI `success` → GREEN; on any non-success conclusion the run is NEVER greened — a `failure` carrying parseable behavioral reds routes through `triaging` to `halted`, and any other conclusion (or a failure whose reds can't be confirmed) is an infra `halted`. Every `halted` escalates once to the human via a deduped `DeferredQuestion` (the §17.1 invariant-9 surface). **The anti-cheat invariant is preserved end-to-end:** `green` is reachable from exactly one place — a CI `success` (empty red set) — and the model's `_no_reds` guard independently refuses `mark_green` while a red remains, so a genuinely-failing eval can never self-certify green. The autonomous fixer dispatch (the `fixing`/`pushed` branch, bounded by `max_fix_attempts` and the `record_fix` anti-cheat gate) lands in PR-3b (below). Enabling the loop on the Hetzner `t3 worker` timer chain is a deliberate operator step (PR-3c), never flipped on in code.
- **CI-eval self-healing loop — autonomous fixer, default-OFF ([#3201](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/3201) PR-3b).** The bounded, anti-cheat-gated autonomous fixer that turns a behavioral `triaging` red into a fix instead of only escalating — the `fixing`/`pushed` branch of the PR-2 FSM. It is a DOUBLE opt-in: `loop/ci_eval_heal_fixer.autofix_armed` is true only when the DARK `ci_eval_heal_autofix_enabled` feature flag AND the `ci_eval_heal` `Loop` row are BOTH on, so observe-only (PR-3a) stays the default and even a by-hand `t3 eval ci-heal advance` cannot mutate CI unless the operator deliberately enabled the loop. When armed and the fix budget is not exhausted (`max_fix_attempts`, default 2 at open time), a behavioral red drives `begin_fix` → the fixer PROPOSES a fix (a bounded headless `claude` write turn in a THROWAWAY worktree of the PR branch, `loop/ci_eval_heal_fixer._HeadlessFixer`) and returns its changed paths WITHOUT pushing → the driver runs the #3282 `record_fix` anti-cheat gate over those paths → only on a clean gate is the fix PUBLISHED (pushed) and the eval re-triggered (`pushed` → `awaiting_ci`, the loop back-edge). **The gate runs strictly BEFORE the push**, so a fixer diff that edits `evals/scenarios/**` or the eval harness (`src/teatree/eval/**`) is REJECTED (`EvalHealCheatError`), DISCARDED, and the session `halted` + escalated — the cheating commit never reaches the branch. A rejected/empty fix, an exhausted budget, or a fixer failure all terminate at `halted` (never a loop-forever, never a false green); the anti-cheat invariant — `green` only from a real CI `success` — is untouched. The `claude` write turn is the single injected seam, so the whole worktree/gate/publish/re-trigger orchestration is tested with a fake (`test_ci_eval_heal_fixer.py`, `test_ci_eval_heal_advance.py`). Enabling the loop on the Hetzner `t3 worker` timer chain is a deliberate operator step (PR-3c), never flipped on in code.
- **`--benchmark` (the 3-tier matrix) + `--model` (force one model).** `t3 eval run --benchmark` runs every (filtered) scenario against ALL THREE tier models (`frontier`/`balanced`/`cheap`, each resolved through the single `TIER_MODELS` constant via `app_helpers.benchmark_models`) on the metered matrix lane and renders the comparison matrix + a self-contained HTML dashboard (`eval/matrix.render_matrix_html`) — the MANUAL benchmark entry (`eval-benchmark.yml` → `eval-weekly-reusable.yml`, workflow_dispatch); adopting a new model needs no flag edit. The scheduled WEEKLY cron (`eval.yml`) is NOT this fan — it runs the cheap `--preset baseline` leg (each scenario once at its baseline-pinned tier, single trial, no effort fan), so the shared OAuth window is never drained. `t3 eval run --model <m>` forces the whole suite onto one model (single-trial metered). `--benchmark`/`--model`/`--models` are mutually exclusive. The DEFAULT `t3 eval run` is per-scenario tier/phase, one trial, no pass@k, no escalation. **Escalate-on-fail (`t3 eval run --escalate-on-fail --escalate-trials N`, default N=3).** Still available off the default path for the PR lane: a single noisy LLM trial is not yet a hard red — `teatree.cli.eval.escalate.escalate_failures` re-runs ONLY the failed scenarios at N higher trials and classifies each `flaky` (recovered) vs `confirmed` (the lane reds). Single-trial only. **Effort matrix (`scripts/eval/lane_matrix.py --efforts low,medium,high`).** An optional axis that fans each `{lane, shard}` leg across the three reasoning-effort tiers, carried through `t3 eval run --effort <tier>`. The `t3 eval run` lane dispatch (matrix / `--model` force / pass@k / single-trial) lives in `cli/eval/run_dispatch.py`.
- **Ground-truth corpus + audit layer ([#2192](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/2192), [#1861](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1861)).** `eval/corpus/` pairs captured sessions with independently-authored labels under an independent-oracle guard (a matcher label whose author is the rule author is refused). The `SessionAuditRecord` ledger (parallel to `EvalScenarioResult`, ids/slugs only) is populated by the conversation-audit pass and rendered by a confusion-matrix renderer; the corpus/audit curation CLI (`t3 eval corpus|audit|label`) is the thin surface over these engines.
- **Cost + benchmark.** A per-scenario cost ledger (`EvalScenarioResult.cost_usd`) powers two cost gates and `t3 eval benchmark`. `--gate-cost-regression` is *relative* drift vs a mutable DB baseline run; `--gate-cost-bounds` is the *absolute* ceiling — each scenario's metered cost is checked against the CHECKED-IN `evals/cost_bounds.yaml` (calibrated `bound_usd` x `(1 + margin)`), a scenario over its ceiling or a configured-but-uncosted scenario fails loud (never skip-as-pass), and a ceiling set pinning NO scenario is RED rather than an inert flag reporting an unearned green, and the engine is the pure `teatree.eval.cost_bounds`. Both gates read the durable ledger, so they run on the persisted weekly/manual metered path, off the per-PR path; `--gate-cost-bounds` additionally requires a backend that RECORDS cost, and `t3 eval run` exits 2 rather than pairing it with one of `UNMETERED_FRESH_BACKENDS`, whose runs record $0 and would make every ceiling read MISSING. `t3 eval benchmark` runs the suite per `model@effort` variant and reports pass-rate, cost-per-pass, and honest cache-aware token metrics (billed cost is the headline).
- **Cheapest-green baseline + escalation ladder.** The file-backed `baseline` preset (`evals/presets/baseline.yaml`, `teatree.eval.presets`) records, per scenario, the cheapest model tier that PASSES it; `t3 eval set-baseline --from <matrix.json>` derives that map from a matrix run (a scenario failing every tier gets no entry and is surfaced, never tiered to frontier). `t3 eval ladder` (`teatree.eval.ladder`) is the CHEAP producer of that matrix: it escalates each scenario cheapest-first (`cheap → balanced → frontier`) and stops at the first tier it passes, so balanced runs only on `cheap`'s failures and frontier only on the scenarios that failed both — loss-free for the baseline question but a fraction of the full-matrix spend. It emits the same matrix JSON `set-baseline` consumes; a never-reached tier is an absent cell. Escalation is per-scenario and in-process, so a metered CI shard (`--shard i/n`) escalates its own subset inside one account's usage window with no cross-pass orchestration. `t3 eval benchmark` stays a full matrix (it exists to MEASURE per-model cost, so it runs every model on every scenario).

A skill's evals live in the central catalog `evals/scenarios/<skill>.yaml` (each spec carrying an explicit `agent_path: skills/<name>/SKILL.md`), never inline in the `skills/` tree (`tests/eval_replay/test_no_inline_skill_evals.py` enforces it); a skill with no eval declares `eval_exempt: <reason>`; `t3 eval coverage` is the per-skill coverage gate and EXITS NON-ZERO on a gap — the same verdict the `skill-coverage` lane and the `test_no_shipped_skill_is_an_uncovered_gap` pytest gate return for the same corpus. Every run ends with a plain-language closing verdict, and a setup-skipped lane reads as `NOT RUN`, never as validated. [evals/README.md](evals/README.md) is the SOT for all eval architecture detail.

---

Implementation details that previously lived in nine prose-of-code appendices have been folded into the sections above or moved to their true home — model and `OverlayBase` docstrings, typer `--help` text, `CLAUDE.md` / `AGENTS.md`, or kept in code where they were always canonical. See [#1128](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/1128).

## Maintenance

The BLUEPRINT stays architectural — not a prose mirror of the code — by **review-time judgment**, not a raw byte gate. There is no hard size cap and no per-PR byte-delta budget: a single hand-edited KB constant that every BLUEPRINT-touching PR had to bump made concurrent PRs re-break each other's CI endlessly, with no quality signal to show for it. Keeping the file tight is a reviewer responsibility — flag bloat, prose that restates code instead of capturing architecture, and stale or duplicated sections — captured in `skills/review/SKILL.md` § "Keep BLUEPRINT Tight" and in the periodic holistic review (`ac-reviewing-codebase`). When a section grows past appendix-class detail, split it into a linked appendix under [docs/blueprint/](docs/blueprint/); the top-level file holds the architecture, the appendices hold the depth.

Two structural pre-commit gates remain (these are sync/structure checks, not raw-size enforcement): `scripts/hooks/check_blueprint_sync.py` (BLUEPRINT updated alongside source changes) and `scripts/hooks/check_blueprint_invariant_numbering.py` ([#836](https://github.com/souliane/teatree/issues/836), the §17.1 invariants form a gapless `1..N`, with the CI `blueprint-cross-pr` job catching the concurrent-PR case).

Both gates read `src/` and `BLUEPRINT.md` as **teatree-root-relative**, and git reports staged paths relative to the WORKING-TREE root — the same root only in a plain clone. `check_blueprint_sync` therefore maps the staged set back through `_vendoring_prefix()` (`""` here, `vendor/teatree/` in a fork that vendors core), so in either layout a change to teatree's own `src/` is gated and teatree's own BLUEPRINT satisfies it. A path outside that tree — a vendoring fork's own overlay source — is neither, which is also what keeps a concurrent agent's staged file in a shared clone from moving an unrelated commit's verdict.

The auto-generated tach dependency graph lives in [docs/dependency-graph.md](docs/dependency-graph.md), outside the corpus, so structural growth never bloats this file.
